# Dominion takeaways — what every game we read teaches

**What this is.** The plain-words list of everything the Steam review study has learned that could shape
Dominion. One entry per finished game, plus one part that looks across all of them. Rico asked for it on
2026-10-03, with every usable detail squeezed out of each game. **A game is not Done until its entry is
here** (`CLAUDE.md`, `templates/README.md`). New entries use `templates/dominion-takeaways-entry.md`
and go at the top of "The games", newest finished first.

**Dominion, for this file:** a third-person, four-player co-op sci-fi PvE extraction and arena shooter
built on runs, hosted on one player's machine (a listen server), in Unreal Engine 5. **It starts at four
players on a listen server; a larger version, possibly 200 players, may come later** (Rico, 2026-10-03,
`Rico notes.md`). Lessons about dedicated servers or large player counts are for that later step.

**How to read it.**
- **Every number comes from the game's findings pages** in `findings/`, or, where an entry marks it,
  another findings page or `GAMES-TODO.md`. Each entry names its pages. "Reviews" counts reviews;
  "times it was said" counts single points (one review can make several); "per 100" means per 100
  reviews read.
- **Reviewers' claims are theirs.** Prices, drop rates, patches, bans and events are "reviewers say"
  unless the entry says it was checked against Steam or the studio.
- **"For Dominion — our reading" is our reading, not the reviews'.** Each lesson gives the evidence, what
  Dominion could do, and how strong the evidence is: **strong** (many reviews, clear), **medium**, or
  **weak** (a handful of reviews, or one game). They are suggestions, not decisions.
- **Store facts** (camera, group size) were checked against the game's Steam store page on 2026-10-03
  where an entry says so. Where the findings pages and the store are both silent, the entry says the
  fact is not known rather than filling it in.

**How it was built (2026-10-03).** Each entry was drafted from the game's findings pages and then
checked: every number of 10 or more was matched against the findings pages by script, and every fact
not on the pages was either checked against the Steam store or taken out. Where two pages disagree on a
number, the entry says so in its Limits part. The disagreements found while building this file are
listed in `tag-tree-open-gaps.md`, round 654.

**The parts:**
1. **Across all games** - the patterns that hold in more than one game, and the lessons that rest on
   them. Start here.
2. **The games** - one entry each, newest finished first, every entry in the same order: what it is,
   how close to Dominion, the short version, praise, complaints, change over time, co-op and online,
   combat and feel, enemies and difficulty, progression and luck, runs and content, money, tech, the
   studio, what players asked for, what only this game shows, the lessons for Dominion, and limits.

## Across all games

This part pulls together what the study learned by putting the games side by side. The study read
English Steam reviews of 29 games: 19 large games read as samples, and 10 small roguelike games
where every English review was read (751 of 751). In all, 28,613 English reviews were summarised
(29,375 in all languages). Each review was cut into short points. A "point" is one thing a reviewer
said, good or bad. One review can hold several points.

Source pages: findings/cross-game.md (sections 1 to 25), findings/roguelike-block.md (sections 1 to 3),
and "Rico notes.md". Section numbers below are cross-game.md sections unless named otherwise.

How to read the numbers:
- "Per 100 reviews" means: for every 100 reviews read, how many times this was said. One review can
  say two things, so a number can go over 100.
- Some later sections count "reviews per 100" instead: how many of every 100 reviews said it at least
  once. Where the page uses that count, this file says "of every 100 reviews".
- What reviewers say about a studio, a patch or a fact is what they SAY. The study did not check it,
  unless this file says it was checked.

### The comparison table (plain words)

What each column means:
- **Thumbs up** - the share of the reviews we read that recommend the game.
- **Praise per 100** - how many good things were said for every 100 reviews read.
- **Complaints per 100** - how many bad things were said for every 100 reviews read.
- **Praise to complaint** - good things divided by bad things. Above 1 means players said more good than
  bad. Below 1 means more bad than good.
- **Points per review** - how many separate things the average review said. A high number means people
  wrote longer, more detailed reviews.

**Main table - all counted the same way** (every good or bad point counted, after a counting fix in
round 470). Ten games come from section 25, the latest and largest table. Three more come from
section 20, which used the same count.

| Game | What kind of game | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | Praise to complaint | Points per review | From |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Deep Rock Galactic | co-op missions, paid | 97.1% | 175.9 | 23.7 | 7.4 : 1 | 2.12 | section 25 |
| Crab Champions | third-person runs, solo or co-op | 97.5% | 123.2 | 18.7 | 6.6 : 1 | 1.52 | section 25 |
| Risk of Rain 2 | third-person runs, co-op | 95.6% | 104.9 | 18.0 | 5.8 : 1 | 1.43 | section 25 |
| Gunfire Reborn | co-op runs, first person, seasons | 93.9% | 130.0 | 33.1 | 3.9 : 1 | 1.78 | section 25 |
| Earth Defense Force 5 | third-person co-op missions, hosted by one player | 95.7% | 140.2 | 38.9 | 3.6 : 1 | 1.89 | section 25 |
| Escape from Duckov | single-player PvE extraction | 90.9% | 134.6 | 40.4 | 3.3 : 1 | 1.92 | section 25 |
| Warframe | free, long-running co-op | 89.9% | 127.3 | 50.9 | 2.5 : 1 | 2.01 | section 25 |
| Helldivers 2 | co-op missions, live service | 83.3% | 108.0 | 55.5 | 1.95 : 1 | 1.79 | section 25 |
| Space Marine 2 | licensed third-person co-op | 87.6% | 116.0 | 61.9 | 1.87 : 1 | 1.99 | section 20 |
| Remnant II | third-person co-op sequel | 83.6% | 121.6 | 77.5 | 1.57 : 1 | 2.30 | section 20 |
| ARC Raiders | extraction where other players can shoot you | 79.7% | 86.8 | 56.7 | 1.53 : 1 | 1.56 | section 25 |
| Back 4 Blood | four-player co-op, paid | 69.2% | 118.0 | 137.2 | 0.86 : 1 | 2.63 | section 20 |
| The First Descendant | free third-person sci-fi looter, Unreal Engine 5 | 64.5% | 83.6 | 122.2 | 0.68 : 1 | 2.37 | section 25 |

What the main table says in plain words:
- The First Descendant is the only one of the section 25 games where complaints outnumber praise. It
  also has the lowest thumbs up and the longest reviews: "its reviewers argue at length" (section 25).
- The two run-based third-person games, Crab Champions and Risk of Rain 2, have the two lowest
  complaint rates on this count: 18.7 and 18.0 per 100 (section 24).
- Deep Rock Galactic has by far the most praise per 100 (175.9).

**Older table - NOT counted the same way, so do not line these up exactly with the table above.**
These come from the table at the top of cross-game.md, rebuilt on 2026-09-05 after an earlier counting
error, and before the round-470 counting fix. Section 20 says Deep Rock Galactic, Helldivers 2 and Back 4
Blood moved by under half a point between the two counts, but Risk of Rain 2 moved more, so treat these
as close, not equal.

| Game | Thumbs up, our sample | Thumbs up, Steam's own | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | Praise to complaint | Points per review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Aliens: Fireteam Elite | 81.3% | 79.7% | 123.7 | 140.2 | 0.88 : 1 | 2.93 |
| Immortal: Unchained (single-player) | 64.6% | 64.6% | 133.1 | 217.9 | 0.61 : 1 | 4.09 |
| Deep Rock Galactic: Rogue Core | 70.3% | 60.1% | 116.8 | 194.2 | 0.60 : 1 | 3.25 |
| The Anacrusis | 57.4% | 46.6% | 129.6 | 223.6 | 0.58 : 1 | 3.96 |
| Redfall | 48.6% | 38.5% | 116.5 | 241.6 | 0.48 : 1 | 3.76 |

- The ten small roguelike games together: 65.1% thumbs up, 2.43 points per review, praise to complaint
  0.56 : 1 (section 15). Per-100 rates for the block as a whole are not given.
- Why our sample and Steam disagree for Redfall and Rogue Core: most of their reviews were written in
  the launch month (65% for Redfall, 75% for Rogue Core), and the study reads every month about equally,
  so the angry launch month counts for less. Weighting Redfall's months back to their real size gives
  42.9%. The page does not explain the gap for The Anacrusis or Terminull Brigade.

### Patterns that hold across games

**1. Playing with friends is the praise every audience agrees on.**
- What players said: "it is much better with friends." It is the top item on the list of praise found
  in every early game (section 2).
- Numbers (times said per 100 reviews): Back 4 Blood 7.5, Deep Rock Galactic 10.1, Helldivers 2 3.8.
  Later games: Aliens: Fireteam Elite 10.4, Remnant II 10.1, Gunfire Reborn 9.6 (fourth in the study),
  Earth Defense Force 5 8.3 (section 22, 23).
- A separate claim showed up in The Anacrusis: "it is how I play with my family" - a different buyer,
  not a different friend (section 11).
- How sure: strong. Seven named games, rates near or above 5 to 10 per 100 in most of them.

**2. The short list every audience agrees on (section 2, first three games only).**
- Eight praises named in all three: play it with friends; it pulls you back; it is worth the money;
  the shooting feels good; it is hard in a good way; the difficulty steps are well built; the builds
  are deep; it works alone too.
- Three complaints named in all three: it gets repetitive; it punishes you if you play alone; the
  unlocks are a grind.
- "It gets repetitive" is the tightest match in the whole study: 1.8, 1.4 and 1.4 per 100 in a loved
  game, a hated game and a fought-over game.
- The same choice makes both sides. "Works alone" (1.4, 3.7, 0.9) sits next to "punishing alone" (1.1,
  1.3, 0.7). "Builds are deep" (5.8, 2.7, 1.0) sits next to "unlocks are a grind" (0.9, 0.7, 0.7).
- How sure: medium. The rates are small (about 1 to 6 per 100) and it rests on three games.

**3. A shared ritual or slogan grows only where players are - and it cannot be copied over.**
- What it is: a catchphrase, salute or in-joke players write without being asked, or a name they call
  themselves.
- Numbers (times said per 100 reviews): Deep Rock Galactic 28.6, Helldivers 2 24.5, Rogue Core 7.5,
  Back 4 Blood 0.0. Back 4 Blood has none at all in 1,663 English reviews and 712 Latin American
  Spanish ones. The study had the label ready long before it read Back 4 Blood, so the zero is real.
- Rogue Core is from the same studio and world as Deep Rock Galactic and kept the exact catchphrase. Its
  players use it at about 26% of the strength (7.5 against 28.6). "Inheriting one does not inherit its
  hold" (section 9).
- Space Marine 2: the slogan is the second most-said thing, 21.9 per 100. Two games (it and Helldivers 2)
  now have a story catchphrase in about one review in five (section 17).
- Talk about each other: the share of all points about the player community is Deep Rock Galactic
  28.8%, Helldivers 2 28.5%, Back 4 Blood 9.4%, Rogue Core 9.2%, Redfall 2.7%. This split the two
  surviving games from the three that did not do well, across five games - the page calls it its
  "strongest single line" (section 10).
- What players praise: community is 32.9% of Deep Rock's praise and 37.3% of Helldivers 2's, but 8.3%
  of Back 4 Blood's. Back 4 Blood's praise leans on the marketing (18.5%). "The failure praises the
  marketing. The survivors praise each other" (section 1).
- Small games: the player-culture topic is 1,325 points in the nine big early games and zero in all ten
  small roguelikes, including the four that are co-op. "Culture needs a population" (roguelike-block
  section 5).
- Things players can quote last longest. Earth Defense Force 5: quotable lines are 7.6 of every 100
  reviews, and 0.6 or less in every other game with 300 or more reviews; "cheesy on purpose" is 169
  points, 9.6 per 100, in no other game. Risk of Rain 2: praise for the music rises for seven years (3.3
  to 6.0 per 100), the only praise line in the study that rises, because players quote it ("his music
  was electric", eleven times in 2025-26) (sections 19, 23).
- Silly actions with no game effect (emotes, the beer, the barrel, the dance) were praised 111 times in
  Deep Rock Galactic (5.2 per 100), 6 times in Helldivers 2, never in Back 4 Blood (section 5).
- Friendly fire as a story maker: 37 points praise it in Helldivers 2 (2.3 per 100), against 3 people in
  the whole study who call it a cost. Remnant II: six say friendly fire is the fun (sections 5, 18). The
  page says these early counts miss some friendly-fire points, so they are a floor (section 6).
- How sure: strong that the pattern exists (many games, large counts). The page says plainly it cannot
  tell cause from effect: a game people stop playing has nobody to write about (sections 7, 10).

**4. Co-op connection problems: joining, hosting, dropping and scaling.**
- Earth Defense Force 5 is hosted by one player, like Dominion. Its online rules draw a list of small
  complaints (counts of reviews): solo and online progress kept separate (7), gear capped online (4), no
  joining a mission already going (4), a weapon cap per mission (2), difficulty that does not follow the
  head count evenly (2), the host leaving ends the game for everyone (1). One review praises the gear cap
  for keeping a mixed group even. "None is large, but they come from the same choice" (section 23).
- Crab Champions: online play draws almost no complaints - 1 point about a disconnect losing a run and
  2 about matchmaking, in 1,499 reviews (section 24).
- Aliens: Fireteam Elite (players connect to each other, no dedicated servers): in its last year the bad
  reviews gather on "the game is dead", "cannot find games" and "keeps disconnecting", not on the
  design (section 13).
- Space Marine 2: "keeps disconnecting" went 0.8 to 3.4 per 100 after an April 2025 update, then 0.0.
  Risk of Rain 2: disconnects 1.2 per 100 in 2024. The First Descendant: 15 cannot-connect complaints,
  all at launch (sections 17, 19, 25).
- ARC Raiders: matchmaking that sorts players by how they behave drew one praise and five kinds of
  failure (section 16).
- How sure: weak to medium. The counts are small (1 to 15). The page itself warns that "a large share
  of a small game is still small counts" (section 23). The page gives no wider count of rejoining after
  a drop.

**5. Patches are judged the same way everywhere - and fast fixes are praised even by people who dislike
the game.**
- Section 9 table (times said per 100 reviews): "the patch made it better" Back 4 Blood 0.0, Deep Rock
  1.6, Helldivers 2 1.2, Rogue Core 3.8. "It fixed what mattered" 3.0, 0.3, 1.6, 3.7. "The studio
  listens and acts" 0.4, 1.8, 4.7, 3.1. "The studio ignores feedback" 0.8, 0.0, 0.9, 1.8.
- Rogue Core leads both patch-praise lines while getting its worst scores. Praise for the response and
  a verdict on the game are separate things.
- Rogue Core's good-to-bad patch talk went 4.8 to 1 at launch, 5.8 to 1 in June, 1 to 1 in July after a
  hotfix. The thumb turned a month later.
- Redfall: the first big patch (June 2023) moved nothing. Game Update 2 (October 2023) moved thumbs up
  from 34.7% to 60.7% and it stayed. But it came five months in, when players were in single figures.
  "A recovery patch that lands after the audience leaves changes the reviews and not the business"
  (section 10).
- Patches that make it worse: Risk of Rain 2, 1.7 per 100 in 2024; Remnant II, 1.5 per 100 in 2024 for
  the DLC patches; Crab Champions, 7 points after its Island Update plus 3 who want the old version back
  (sections 18, 19, 24). The page has no separate count for weapon nerfs.
- How sure: medium. Four to seven games, rates of 1 to 5 per 100.

**6. The small-studio arc: praised for listening in year one, called abandoned by year three.**
- In the small roguelike block, "the developers listen" was said 23 times, every one in the launch
  window. "Updates stopped" was said 16 times, every one a year or more after launch.
- Five of the nine real block games made that turn (Zombie Girl, ZCREW, ArcRunner, Die After Sunset,
  VOIDCRISIS), often inside the same review, edited later. ArcRunner: 10 "they listen" and 9 "they
  left". One ArcRunner review in May 2023 says the devs "really listen", then the same review in
  January 2024 says they "seemed to have abandoned" it.
- Alien Dawn is the exception: 7 "they listen", 0 "they left", 2 "still supported", and its 2025 edits
  say so.
- Big games: "updates stopped" is 4.7 per 100 in Back 4 Blood and 0.0 in Deep Rock and Helldivers 2.
  "The studio earned my trust" is 0 in Back 4 Blood, 63 points in Deep Rock, 14 in Helldivers 2. "New
  content after launch is free" 1.5 per 100 in Deep Rock (section 5).
- Steady updates are praised most in Gunfire Reborn (3.9 per 100), Warframe (3.6) and Deep Rock (3.2)
  (section 22).
- How sure: medium. Many games, but the block counts are small (1 to 10 per game).

**7. Why the complaint line rises - five shapes, and only one is the game.**
- Helldivers 2: the game held and the studio's relationship with players collapsed.
- Space Marine 2: everything held and price complaints took over. Thumbs up 88.5% at launch and 87.8%
  two years later, while crashes went 3.2 to 0.0 per 100, "only buy it on sale" 0.5 to 5.1, and "too
  grindy" 0.7 to 2.6. "A studio that fixes its engineering does not earn a higher thumb - it earns a
  different complaint." 46% of all reviewers wrote in the launch month.
- Remnant II: nothing changed, the reviewer did. Complaints per 100 went 67 to 91; launch reviewers argued
  about upscaling, later ones about the first game and then the online guide (7.9 to 10.0 per 100).
- Risk of Rain 2: complaints fell for five years (22.5 to 13.0 to 9.2 per 100), then doubled to 18.3 in
  2024 when a new owner took over. Thumbs up went from 96-98% to 92.9% and 93.5%. "The publisher's name
  was the biggest single thing that ever happened to its score ... and it cost two points."
- Warframe: a flat line - a complaint built into the design that the people who stay accept.
- Also: Gunfire Reborn's complaints slid 20.9, 28.5, 36.1, 46.3 per 100 across four periods while praise
  stayed between 76.6 and 105.3 (section 22).
- How sure: medium. Five games, one shape each.

**8. Owners and publishers can hurt a game the studio made well.**
- Helldivers 2: "the owner puts me off" 2.8 per 100 (section 5).
- Risk of Rain 2: same complaint 1.7 per 100 in 2024; 11 complain about the new owner, 6 accept them.
  A user agreement added after purchase drew complaints; a later review changed back when it was removed
  (section 19).
- Redfall: the owning company, not the studio, ended it. Its always-online check outlived the studio:
  13 of 18 "cannot connect" reviews are after the closure, 7 of them by people with zero hours. Buyers
  posted the fix for each other in their reviews (section 10).
- Gunfire Reborn: one review about a user-agreement clause (91 found it helpful) (section 22).
- How sure: medium. Four games, small rates.

**9. Price: "fair" is praised everywhere; "wait for a sale" marks the games that struggled.**
- "The price is fair" (per 100): Back 4 Blood 1.6, Deep Rock 2.7, Helldivers 2 1.5. Warframe: 198
  points, its fourth most common tag (sections 2, 20).
- "Only buy it on sale" (per 100): Redfall 17.0, Aliens: Fireteam Elite 9.1, Back 4 Blood 8.6, Earth
  Defense Force 5 3.4, Deep Rock 0.3, Helldivers 2 0.0 (sections 5, 23).
- In the small roguelike block, one review in six (118 reviews, 15.7%) is about the price or the amount
  of game: fair price 38, too high for what it is 26, buy on sale 25, too little content 32. The
  recommendation reads the same in every small game: "it is short, it is janky, it is cheap, I liked it"
  (roguelike-block section 2).
- Immortal: Unchained: 63% of "too pricey for what it is" points come from people who still recommend
  it (section 14).
- How sure: strong for the sale line (several games, large rates in the failures).

**10. Money in free games: the fight is about time, and stacked chance reads as a sales trick.**
- Complaints about how the game makes money (of every 100 reviews): The First Descendant 14.6,
  Terminull Brigade 13.2, Warframe 2.8, Helldivers 2 1.8, every other game with 300 or more reviews 1.4
  or less (section 25).
- Warframe: praise about its money side beats complaints 134 to 103. The biggest money point is neutral:
  "paying only shortens the grind" (73). The player market makes the paid currency earnable (46).
  "I bought in to support the studio" is 0.93 per 100, the highest anywhere. "Paying affects play" is
  16 in 3,233 reviews (section 20).
- The First Descendant: grind for a box, roll it for a part, wait for a craft, pay to skip any step.
  "One random drop leads to another" 20, "the frustration is built to sell shortcuts" 58, "the shop
  charges far too much" 75 (the last two under 0.5 per 100 in Warframe). Then the game showed drop
  odds, added bad-luck protection and catch-up rewards; complaints fell from 128 to 84 per 100 across
  four periods, and grind and price complaints fell by more than half (section 25).
- How sure: medium. Strong contrast, but only three free games and the page says the model and the game
  cannot be separated (section 12).

**11. Randomness and grind.**
- "The unlocks are a grind" is small in the early games (0.9, 0.7, 0.7 per 100). In Warframe it is the
  top complaint: 264 reviews, 8.2 per 100, flat for thirteen years (6.8 to 9.6 by period), and 223 of
  the 264 recommend the game anyway. The same reviewers say everything can be earned (133), the grind
  pays off (111) and the price is fair (198) (section 20).
- "The item you need may never drop" (per 100): Escape from Duckov 2.0, the highest; Remnant II 1.7;
  Warframe 1.2; Risk of Rain 2 0.3. In Duckov nobody can trade you the part (section 21).
- Complaints about randomness in general (of every 100 reviews): The First Descendant 7.2, Rogue Core 6.6,
  Remnant II 2.5, Escape from Duckov 2.0, Warframe 1.3 (section 25).
- Remnant II: the reroll that never gives the item (24), the roll that decides which content you ever
  see (7) (section 18).
- Slow start because the good systems are still locked (per 100): Gunfire Reborn 1.2, Warframe 1.1 -
  both put the long-term unlocks before the fun build (section 22).
- Rico's note: a Deep Rock reviewer said "I'm never playing just to reach a goal" - the missions are
  fun in themselves. Rico: "I feel this way playing Helldivers 2." Its opposite, "nothing left to chase",
  came from four Deep Rock players (Rico notes, 2026-08-31).
- How sure: medium. Many games, but rates differ a lot by game.

**12. Feeling powerful wins; losing control loses.**
- "It makes you feel superhumanly strong" (per 100): Space Marine 2 4.5, Risk of Rain 2 3.8, Crab
  Champions 2.5 (section 24). In Space Marine 2, 63 of 64 praise points come from people who recommend
  it, and 12 of 13 "never as strong as the story says" complaints come from people who do not - the
  cleanest thumb split of any pair in the study (section 17).
- What breaks the power fantasy is always control taken away: stuns, a parry that fails when needed
  (section 17).
- Immortal: Unchained: faults about numbers are forgiven, faults about losing the controller are not.
  Recommended share: "some options are useless" 73%, "one option rules" 72%, but "no way to fight back"
  25%, "slow weapon handling" 30%, "stuns take control away" 31% (section 14).
- Too much power has a cost: Risk of Rain 2 has 73 praise points for becoming a god, one complaint that
  inputs stop mattering (247 hours), and "the effects block your view" is its second complaint (14)
  (section 19).
- Shooting that feels impactful (per 100): Back 4 Blood 5.8, Deep Rock 1.7, Helldivers 2 1.4. The
  Anacrusis's biggest complaint is weightless combat: 81 points, 3.4% of all its points (sections 2, 12).
- How sure: medium to strong. Four to six games, clear thumb splits.

**13. Movement is the feel players name - and only two games earn the praise.**
- Praise for movement (of every 100 reviews): Crab Champions 2.7, Warframe 2.6, every other game with
  300 or more reviews 0.5 or less - at most 1 review in 200 (section 24).
- The commonest movement complaint elsewhere is "sluggish": Gunfire Reborn 15, Rogue Core 11, Immortal:
  Unchained 10 (counts of points).
- How sure: medium. Clear gap, small counts.

**14. Content amount: the successful game's top complaint is "not enough of it".**
- Aliens: Fireteam Elite: the two largest points sit six apart and point opposite ways: "faithful to the
  films" 193 and "too little content" 187 (section 13).
- Space Marine 2: "too little" is the top complaint (84), and 66 of the 84 are from people who
  recommend it; median 17 hours. It was 7.6 per 100 at launch, 1.9 as missions were added, back to 5.1
  in 2026 ("thirteen missions after three years"). Deep Rock: the same complaint is 0.9 per 100
  (section 17).
- Deep Rock's whole complaint list (515 points) is mostly about design and content: 57% (section 3).
- The Anacrusis: "the potential is still there" by year 35, 33, 4, 2; "the game is dead" 8, 14, 15, 10.
  68 of 74 hopeful points are from the first two years (section 11).
- Small block: "too little content" 32; Banzai Escape's five "too short" points all recommend it.
- How sure: strong. Many games; the complaint comes from fans.

**15. Performance: one fault can be a whole game's story.**
- Terminull Brigade: stutter is 165 points, 7.3% of everything said, the largest single item in any game.
  40 of those reviews still recommend it; 18 say their verdict flips the day it is fixed (section 12).
- Helldivers 2: technical faults are 21.7% of complaints, more than design (19.9%) (section 3).
- Remnant II: four kinds of "my machine cannot run it": only works with upscaling (14), lowering settings
  does not help (7), only stable with the processor slowed (2), will not start at all (9; 8 of them
  thumbs down, median 2 hours). Its technical complaints went to zero "because the machines caught up,
  not the studio" (section 18).
- The First Descendant (Unreal Engine 5): performance complaints fell from 8.8 to 4.9 of every 100
  reviews (section 25).
- Space Marine 2: crashes 3.2 to 0.0 per 100, with no rise in thumbs up (section 17).
- How sure: strong. Many games, big counts in some.

**16. "One in five says nothing" - a floor that rises with approval.**
- Early games: a thumbs up with no reason given is 25.9 (Back 4 Blood), 20.6 (Deep Rock) and 22.5
  (Helldivers 2) per 100 reviews. The page first called this a constant (section 4).
- Later games broke the constant: ARC Raiders' bare thumbs up is 40% of its sample; Space Marine 2's is
  26% with 19% saying nothing at all; Risk of Rain 2 50.3 and Crab Champions 61.0 per 100. The small
  block is 10.9% (sections 16, 17, 24; roguelike-block section 2).
- A slogan soaks up the wordless praise: "a fan with nothing to say says For the Emperor" (section 17).
- "A game that almost everyone likes says the least about why." Crab Champions' mean review is 21.1
  words; no complaint reaches 10 (section 24).
- How sure: strong. Every game shows it; the size moves with how liked the game is.

**17. A thumbs up does not mean nothing is wrong.**
- Aliens: Fireteam Elite is 79.7% on Steam and Back 4 Blood 69.2%, ten points apart, yet their praise to
  complaint is 0.88 and 0.86. Its 1,221 thumbs-up reviews carry 1,187 complaints and 1,759 praise; its
  280 thumbs-down carry 918 complaints and 98 praise. "The thumb records whether they would buy it
  again; the text records what they would change" (section 13).
- Immortal: Unchained: a recommender writes 1.53 complaints, second to Redfall's 1.80 (section 14).
- Below about 0.9, the ratio stops following the thumb: Terminull (50.9% on Steam) has the worst ratio,
  Redfall (38.5%) does better (section 13).
- How sure: strong for the pattern; the ratio warning rests on six games.

**18. The words move before the score.**
- Rogue Core's ratio at 300 reviews predicted its thumb at 500; its July patch talk predicted its August
  thumb. Two of the first four games showed it. "A studio watching only the percentage is reading a
  lagging indicator" (section 9).
- Crab Champions: complaints went 12.7 to 24.8 per 100 after an update while thumbs up stayed at 97.8%
  (section 24).
- How sure: medium. Two to three games.

**19. Detail follows how MANY things people disagree about, not how strongly.**
- The most negative games were the most detailed for five games (Redfall 3.76 points per review). Then
  Terminull, the most negative, said less, because its complaint is one thing: "unplayable, fix the
  stutter" is a whole review (section 12).
- How sure: medium.

**20. Being compared with other games is a measured part of every game.**
- Back 4 Blood: arguing about which game it follows is 25.1 per 100, against Deep Rock 3.1 and
  Helldivers 2 0.9. "Accepts it as the successor" 14.5; "set a bar it could never clear" 8.4
  (sections 1, 5).
- The Anacrusis names another game in 263 points (10.9%), Redfall in 145 (3.8%). A third of that is plain
  description ("Left 4 Dead in space"), not a verdict (section 11).
- Immortal: Unchained: 17.5 per 100 describe it by other games (section 14).
- Remnant II: "accepts the sequel" 139 against "falls short of the studio's earlier games" 53; acceptance
  falls 12.7 to 4.7 per 100. "A sequel is reviewed against its predecessor for two years and then against
  itself" (section 18).
- Escape from Duckov: "beats its rivals" 6.1 per 100, the highest (The Anacrusis 5.6, Warframe 2.1);
  135 of 1,160 reviews name Tarkov, only 6 thumbs down (section 21).
- The First Descendant: 282 reviews (17.6%) name Warframe, half thumbs down; Destiny named in 186; "a
  named rival does it better" on 112 reviews here and 2 in the rest of the study (section 25).
- Crab Champions: 43 reviews name Risk of Rain; all 9 "beats its rivals" points are against it.
- Earth Defense Force 5: no review names Helldivers before 2024; 36 do after, none thumbs down;
  "beats its rivals" rose from 0.4 to 3.2 per 100. "The same game gets a new yardstick without
  changing" (section 23).
- How sure: strong. Nearly every game.

**21. Reviewers argue with the review page in games that did badly.**
- "This game is judged unfairly" (per 100): Rogue Core 10.2, Back 4 Blood 7.1, Helldivers 2 1.3, Deep
  Rock 0.2 (section 9). Redfall: 124 "judged unfairly" against 9 "deserved it" - 14 to 1 (section 10).
- How sure: medium.

**22. Reviews change with age.**
- Reviews shrink: Warframe's mean length went 67 to 28 words; short reviews (15 words or fewer) 51% to
  69%. A topic's fall over time only means something if it is much bigger than this shrink (section 20).
- Later reviewers have played longer and complain more: Space Marine 2 mean hours 29 to 74, complaints
  55 to 71 per 100. The First Descendant median hours 20 at launch to 50-70 later; its three most-helpful
  reviews (592, 554, 545 helpful) are thumbs down from players with 117 to 3,012 hours (sections 17, 25).
- When a loop is replaced, only long players notice: in Terminull Brigade, six reviewers who played 4,
  13, 73, 102, 147 and 402 hours (median player: five) said a season swapped the shape of play. "Nobody
  at zero hours raises it" (section 12).
- Edited reviews: Aliens 7.8% overall, falling from 12.1% (2021) to 1.9% (2026) - age, not disagreement;
  Risk of Rain 2 223 (11.8%); Warframe 581 (18.0%); Helldivers 2 18.4%; ARC Raiders 172; Space Marine 2
  155 (sections 13, 16, 17, 19, 20).
- How sure: strong. Many games.

**23. Things outside the game move the thumb.**
- Escape from Duckov: 96.7% up in November 2025, 74.4% in December - a mod dispute brought 13 of
  December's 34 thumbs down (reviewers' claims, not checked) (section 21).
- Terminull Brigade: a paid install campaign wrote 90 reviews, 76 at zero hours. Removing them moves the
  thumb 40.98% to 43.3%, only 2.3 points. Two waves of it: 36.6% up, then 9.1% up four months later
  (section 12).
- Crossovers: found in 25 reviews across five games; 8 up, 6 down in Terminull. "A crossover is a door,
  not a verdict."
- AI-made art: Gunfire Reborn 5 reviews, ARC Raiders 1 (section 22).
- How sure: medium.

**24. Fan service splits a review page in half.**
- Two small games (Zombie Girl, FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL): about one review in five each is this
  argument, and the two sides split the thumb almost perfectly. In FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL the twelve
  censorship reviews hold 52% of all helpful votes (section 15).
- The First Descendant: "the cast is built to titillate" is 9.3 of every 100 reviews, rising 6.6 to 16.8;
  Terminull 3.3; Earth Defense Force 5 0.8 (section 25).
- Politics: The Anacrusis drew both "put off by its politics" and "I like it for that", about the same
  cosmetic banners (section 11).
- How sure: medium.

**25. Small studios are graded against their size.**
- "I grade it against how small the team is": 3.2% of small-block reviews, 0.7% in the nine big early
  games (section 15).
- How sure: medium.

### Where games differ

**Runs versus missions.**
- Run-based third-person games (Crab Champions, Risk of Rain 2) read alike: lowest complaints (18.7 and
  18.0 per 100), most bare thumbs up (61.0 and 50.3 per 100), and both praise getting too strong (2.5 and
  3.8 per 100) (section 24).
- Rogue Core, the run-based spin-off, has two things no other game has: a clock that decides when you
  leave (13.8 per 100) and teammates who can take your things (5.8 per 100) (section 9). Five reviews
  across three games call the timer the thrill (section 19).
- Gunfire Reborn (co-op runs with seasons) is praised for seasons you can finish later (6 reviews) and
  for steady updates (3.9 per 100, the highest) (section 22).
- Mission games show "too little content" as their big late complaint (Aliens 187, Space Marine 2 84).
- Run games: in the small block, one game says the early floors become a chore you cannot skip (2
  sightings); not shown in a second game yet.

**Free versus paid.**
- Free games: Terminull Brigade 0.36 : 1 (older count), The First Descendant 0.68 : 1, Warframe 2.5 : 1.
  Money complaints 13.2 to 14.6 of every 100 reviews in two of them, 2.8 in Warframe.
- Paid games: the money complaint is "wait for a sale" (Redfall 17.0, Aliens 9.1, Back 4 Blood 8.6 per
  100).
- Hours, player counts and refund behaviour mean something different when the game is free (section 12).

**Extraction versus not.**
- Only two extraction games were read. Each one's top request is the other's format. ARC Raiders: 55
  "a mode I expected is missing" (3.7 per 100), 42 of them ask for PvE. Escape from Duckov: 39 (3.4 per
  100), most ask for co-op (section 21).
- "No PvP is a feature": Escape from Duckov 51 (4.4 per 100), ARC Raiders 0, next is Terminull at 0.8;
  no other game reaches 1 per 100. "Won over someone who avoids the genre": Duckov 16 (1.4 per 100),
  second only to Remnant II (1.5).
- ARC Raiders (other players can shoot you): the top 25 complaints are player behaviour 180, design 148
  (about half of that is "give me a mode without other players"), technical 42, the studio's response 32,
  live service 20. Player behaviour was in no earlier game's top ten; here it is five of the top eight.
- ARC Raiders: "not knowing who to trust is the thrill" 95 (6.4 per 100) against "says friendly then
  shoots you in the back" 38; 147 reviews carry only the praise, 158 only the complaint, 11 both.
- ARC Raiders: thumbs up fell 86% to 52% while "cheaters spoil matches" rose from 0.9 to 12.4 per 100 and
  "the studio does nothing about cheaters" from 0.2 to 7.1; design complaints fell in the same time
  (section 16).

**Small studio versus big.**
- Small games: price, amount and the studio as the ruler; the listen-then-leave arc; no player culture
  at all.
- Big games: owners and publishers enter the complaints (Helldivers 2, Risk of Rain 2, Redfall).
- Same studio, different game: Deep Rock (97.1%) and Rogue Core (60.1% on Steam) - "every variable
  except the game held constant" (section 9).

**Co-op versus single-player.**
- Immortal: Unchained (single-player): design is 53.6% of what is said, against 30.3% in the other eight;
  community 0.8% against 13.6%. How reviews are written (10.2% against 10.1%) did not change at all
  (section 14).

**Licensed and sequel games.**
- Aliens: Fireteam Elite: "faithful to the films" 193; "only worth it if you already love the films"
  76, written by people recommending it. Fans ask which part of a series it was faithful to (section 13).
- Space Marine 2: story is 9.2% of everything said, the highest (Redfall 8.2%, other co-op games under
  3%, ARC Raiders 0.5%) (section 17).
- Remnant II: its top complaint, unique to it, is "the best things are hidden behind a guide" (61, 46
  of them recommending) (section 18).

**Language.**
- Gunfire Reborn: "the English reads badly" is 36 reviews, 1.9 of every 100, ten times the next game
  (Escape from Duckov 0.2, Terminull 0.1, Back 4 Blood 0.1). It grows with each update (7 reviews in Early
  Access, 20 in 2025-26) (section 22).

**Long-running live service.**
- Warframe: "warns that it takes over your life" 74 points in 73 reviews (60 thumbs up), "interest comes
  and goes in waves" 44, "keeps playing while hating it" 9 - in no other game (section 20).

### For Dominion - our reading, across games

Dominion: third-person, four-player co-op, sci-fi, PvE extraction and arena shooter, built on runs,
hosted on one player's machine, Unreal Engine 5. Lessons marked "(page)" are "For Dominion" lines
already written in cross-game.md.

1. **Make the game best with friends, and make that easy.**
   - Evidence: the top praise in every early game; 3.8 to 10.4 per 100 across seven games (Back 4
     Blood, Deep Rock, Helldivers 2, Aliens, Remnant II, Gunfire Reborn, Earth Defense Force 5).
   - Dominion could: make inviting, joining and staying together the smoothest thing in the game.
   - Strength: strong.

2. **(page) Give the squad a voice and a line players can repeat.**
   - Evidence: Earth Defense Force 5 quotable lines 7.6 of every 100 reviews (0.6 or less elsewhere),
     cheesy on purpose 9.6 per 100; slogans in Deep Rock (28.6), Helldivers 2 (24.5), Space Marine 2
     (21.9); Risk of Rain 2's quoted music the only praise that rises with age.
   - Dominion could: "a squad that talks, and a line or chant players can repeat, cost little and are
     what this game's reviews are written about" (section 23).
   - Strength: strong that it matters. But it cannot be copied in: Rogue Core kept the exact catchphrase
     and got about a quarter of its pull. It has to be Dominion's own.

3. **(page) Decide early whether progress and gear carry between solo and online play, and say so on the
   store page.**
   - Evidence: Earth Defense Force 5 (hosted by one player): separate progress 7, gear capped 4, no
     joining mid-mission 4, weapon cap 2, uneven scaling 2, host leaving ends the game 1. Crab Champions
     shows online play can draw almost no complaints (1 disconnect, 2 matchmaking in 1,499).
   - Dominion could: let friends join a run already going; plan what happens when the host leaves;
     make difficulty follow head count evenly; state the rules up front.
   - Strength: medium on the rule, weak on each item (counts 1 to 7).

4. **Plan for the day the player count falls.**
   - Evidence: Aliens: Fireteam Elite's last-year bad reviews are about an empty game, not design; the
     small co-op games show no community at all; ARC Raiders' matchmaking got worse as players left;
     "works alone" praised in all three early games (1.4, 3.7, 0.9 per 100).
   - Dominion could: make solo and small-group runs worth playing, so the game survives a thin night.
   - Strength: medium.

5. **Never lock the game behind an online check the studio might not be around to answer.**
   - Evidence: Redfall: 13 of 18 "cannot connect" reviews after the studio closed, 7 at zero hours. Risk
     of Rain 2: a user agreement added after purchase locked buyers out (reviewers say).
   - Dominion could: keep listen-server hosting working without any studio server in the way.
   - Strength: medium-weak (two games).

6. **(page) Make movement feel quick; a third-person shooter is judged on how it moves.**
   - Evidence: movement praise 2.7 (Crab Champions) and 2.6 (Warframe) of every 100 reviews, 0.5 or less
     everywhere else; "sluggish" is the common complaint (Gunfire 15, Rogue Core 11, Immortal 10).
   - Dominion could: "a slide, a dash or a jump that feels quick is praised by name, and a slow one is
     the complaint" (section 24).
   - Strength: medium.

7. **Let players grow powerful, and never take control away from them.**
   - Evidence: "superhumanly strong" Space Marine 2 4.5, Risk of Rain 2 3.8, Crab Champions 2.5 per 100;
     Space Marine 2's thumb split 63 of 64 against 12 of 13; Immortal: Unchained keeps only 25-31% thumbs
     up on stuns and no-counterplay complaints but 72-73% on number problems.
   - Dominion could: tune enemies so stuns and lock-downs are rare and readable; let late runs feel
     huge, but watch that four players' effects do not hide the screen (Risk of Rain 2, 14).
   - Strength: medium-strong.

8. **(page) Do not stack chance on chance with money as the way out; show the odds and end bad luck.**
   - Evidence: The First Descendant 14.6 of every 100 reviews complain about money, randomness 7.2; it
     improved once it showed odds and added bad-luck protection (complaints 128 to 84 per 100). "The item
     you need may never drop": Duckov 2.0, Remnant II 1.7, Warframe 1.2 per 100.
   - Dominion could: "chance stacked on chance, with money as the exit, is what this audience reads as
     the design's purpose; showing the odds and ending bad luck are what it praises" (section 25). In an
     extraction game, give a sure path to the gear that matters.
   - Strength: medium.

9. **A grind is accepted when it is fair and the play itself is fun.**
   - Evidence: Warframe's top complaint (8.2 per 100) comes mostly from recommenders (223 of 264), next to
     "everything can be earned" (133) and "the grind pays off" (111). Deep Rock reviewer and Rico: the
     missions are fun with nothing to earn.
   - Dominion could: make each run worth playing even with no unlock at the end; avoid a slow start
     where the good systems are locked (Gunfire 1.2, Warframe 1.1 per 100).
   - Strength: medium.

10. **Expect "not enough content" to be the fans' main complaint, and fight repetition early.**
    - Evidence: Aliens 187, Space Marine 2 84 (66 from fans), small block 32; "it gets repetitive" 1.8,
      1.4, 1.4 per 100 in three very different games.
    - Dominion could: plan arenas, enemies and run variety as an ongoing supply, not a launch set.
    - Strength: strong.

11. **Performance faults can become the whole review page; Unreal Engine 5 games are watched for it.**
    - Evidence: Terminull stutter 165 points (7.3%); Helldivers 2 technical 21.7% of complaints; Remnant
      II's four "my machine cannot run it" types; The First Descendant (Unreal Engine 5) performance
      8.8 to 4.9 of every 100 reviews.
    - Dominion could: test on low-end machines, make settings actually help, avoid needing upscaling to
      play.
    - Strength: strong.

12. **Patch fast and say so; a late rescue does not bring players back.**
    - Evidence: patch praise in Rogue Core (3.8, 3.7) despite low scores; Helldivers 2 "listens and acts"
      4.7; Redfall's fix came five months late, 34.7% to 60.7%, with almost no players left.
    - Dominion could: plan a quick first-month fix cycle and show players what was fixed.
    - Strength: medium.

13. **Read the words, not just the thumb; weigh complaints by hours played.**
    - Evidence: Rogue Core's words moved a month before its thumb; Crab Champions' complaints doubled
      while thumbs stayed at 97.8%; Terminull's loop change was seen only by players with 4 to 402 hours.
      The page: "when you change the loop, complaint volume will not tell you. Weigh it by playtime or you
      will not see it at all" (section 12).
    - Dominion could: track playtest and review text by hours played, especially after big updates.
    - Strength: medium.

14. **A small studio's early listening is praised most and trusted least; keep it going.**
    - Evidence: 23 "they listen" all at launch, 16 "updates stopped" all a year or more later, five of
      nine small games; Alien Dawn the exception.
    - Dominion could: plan a support schedule it can keep for years, and say what it is.
    - Strength: medium.

15. **Price fairly for the amount of game; "wait for a sale" is a warning sign.**
    - Evidence: "fair price" in every early game; "buy on sale" Redfall 17.0, Aliens 9.1, Back 4 Blood
      8.6 per 100; one small-game review in six is about price or amount.
    - Strength: medium-strong.

16. **Expect to be compared; do not invite the comparison yourself.**
    - Evidence: Back 4 Blood's successor framing (25.1 per 100) and its "set a bar it could never
      clear" (8.4); The First Descendant against Warframe (282 reviews); Duckov wins against Tarkov (6.1
      per 100); Earth Defense Force 5 got compared to Helldivers 2 after 2024.
    - Dominion could: describe itself in its own words; know which rival it will be measured against
      (other sci-fi co-op and extraction shooters) and where it wins.
    - Strength: strong.

17. **PvE extraction is what both extraction audiences asked for.**
    - Evidence: 42 of 55 mode requests in ARC Raiders ask for PvE; Duckov's requests ask for co-op;
      "no PvP is a feature" 4.4 per 100 in Duckov.
    - Dominion could: say clearly on the store page that it is co-op PvE with no other players hunting
      you.
    - Strength: weak-medium (two games, and the page says it has read few extraction games).

18. **(page) Check upgrade text in English at every update.**
    - Evidence: Gunfire Reborn "reads badly" 1.9 of every 100, ten times the next game, and growing with
      each update; item descriptions "at least 3 lines long".
    - Dominion could: "any system that asks the player to read upgrade text mid-run needs its English
      checked at every update, not once" (section 22).
    - Strength: weak (mostly one game; others 0.1 to 0.2).

19. **Let players make their own stories and fun.**
    - Evidence: friendly fire praised 37 times in Helldivers 2 against 3 costs; six in Remnant II say it
      is the fun; silly actions praised 111 times in Deep Rock.
    - Dominion could: consider friendly fire and emotes as story-makers, not just risks.
    - Strength: weak-medium.

20. **Let players say what they want from a run before it starts (Rico's note).**
    - Evidence: Rico's note on a 1,790-hour reviewer who hand-picks teammates by goal; the cheap fix is
      outside the game (named Discord channels: for fun, for rewards, for clears), the costly one a lobby
      flag (Rico notes, 2026-08-31).
    - Strength: weak as evidence (one review plus Rico's call); cheap to try.

21. **Owners, user agreements and fan-service choices can sink a good game; decide them on purpose.**
    - Evidence: owner complaints in Helldivers 2 (2.8), Risk of Rain 2 (1.7, cost two points); fan service
      as The First Descendant's loudest subject (9.3 of every 100); the small fan-service fights.
    - Strength: medium.

22. **Do not read a dip in the thumb as a verdict until the words say so.**
    - Evidence: Duckov's mod dispute (96.7% to 74.4%); Terminull's reward campaign (2.3 points). "The
      monthly thumb needs the round notes beside it" (sections 21, 22).
    - Strength: medium.

### What the study cannot tell us yet

- **Only English.** Non-English reviews are not read. The page gives two old totals that do not match
  (329,785 at the top; 320,920 in section 7) and says both are out of date. Later games add about
  164,000 (ARC Raiders), 100,000 (Space Marine 2), 381,000 (Warframe), 93,000 (Duckov), 60,000 (Gunfire
  Reborn), 61,000 (The First Descendant) and more, none pulled.
- **Few extraction games, and no successful co-op PvE extraction game.** Only two were read: ARC Raiders
  (other players can shoot you) and Escape from Duckov (single-player, co-op only by mods). For co-op in
  Duckov, "the request is recorded, the experience is not" (section 21).
- **Hosting and servers.** Aliens is peer-to-peer and "nothing in this corpus has a dedicated-server
  analogue" (section 13). Listen-server counts come mostly from Earth Defense Force 5, with 1 to 7
  reviews each.
- **Runs and third-person feel.** Gunfire Reborn is first person; Warframe, Earth Defense Force 5 and The
  First Descendant have no runs; Crab Champions is a cartoon, not serious sci-fi.
- **Cause.** The study counts what was said, not why. It cannot say if a missing player culture caused
  a failure or came from it (section 7). A complaint's count is not its cause: The Anacrusis's "no
  personality" came from players with a median of one hour (section 11).
- **Claims not checked.** No patch notes were pulled for ARC Raiders, Space Marine 2 or others; the mod
  dispute and studio actions are what reviewers say.
- **Free versus paid.** Nothing separates the money model from the game (section 12); The First
  Descendant's money finding "may not carry to a game sold once" (section 25).
- **Thin months.** Aliens' launch month held 25.4% of its reviews but was read at 1.62%. Remnant II's
  launch week was read at 0.9%. Immortal: Unchained has 31 reviews for 2024-2026. Crab Champions' update
  period is 230 reviews (about plus or minus 7). The First Descendant: 62% of reviews in its launch month.
- **Edited reviews.** Reviews edited later are filed on one date; this moves numbers in some games (Risk
  of Rain 2, ARC Raiders). It waits on Rico.
- **Counting faults.** Two silent counting faults were found and fixed (sections 6, 13), and a third in
  round 470 (section 20). The page says each was found by accident. Friendly-fire counts in section 5 are
  still a floor.
- **Wrong-game reviews.** One review described a different game; how often this happens is unknown in
  every game (section 12).
- **One-game claims.** Roguelite early-floor chores, single-use checkpoints, conduct-based matchmaking and
  translation all rest on one game.
- **The tag tree has not been reviewed by Rico** (section 7), and several structure questions are open
  (loot, friendly fire, publisher talk, "the first game had X").
- **Whether a licence pays.** Aliens bought 193 points of goodwill and 76 "only for fans" warnings;
  nothing nets them out (section 13).

---

# The games

## The First Descendant

**Read:** 1,600 of 51,208 English reviews (3.1%; 1,604 were read and 4 were not about the game), ±2.50%,
written June 2024 to October 2026. **64.5% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam, all languages: 57%, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/the-first-descendant-english.md`, `findings/the-first-descendant.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 25.

**What it is:** A free third-person sci-fi looter shooter. You play alone or with up to four players in
online co-op. Strangers can be matched into your mission. The pages do not say who hosts a game. You play
missions and grind over and over to build characters and guns; there are no runs that end and no
extraction. It is free, with a shop, a battle pass and seasons. Built on Unreal Engine 5. Made by NEXON
Games and published by NEXON. Out on Steam on 2024-06-30. (Store facts checked against Steam.)

**How close to Dominion:** Close on the surface. It shares the third-person camera, the sci-fi setting,
four-player online co-op, power that builds up over time, and Unreal Engine 5. It is not built on runs,
has no extraction, and is free with a shop instead of sold once. Its long-term progress is a grind for
characters, not a run that resets.

### The short version (plain words)
This is the only game of the ten counted this way where complaints outnumber praise: 122.2 complaints
against 83.6 praise per 100 reviews. Most of the anger is about how the game is sold, not how it plays.
Getting what you need means chance stacked on chance, and the shop sells a way out; 294 reviews carry a
complaint or remark about the shop and 125 talk about luck. Players also judge it against Warframe all
the time: 282 reviews (17.6%) name Warframe, and half of those are thumbs down. The game did get better:
complaints per 100 reviews fell from 128.0 at launch to 84.3 in 2026, once it showed drop odds, added
bad-luck protection and cut the grind. But 62% of all English reviews were written in the first month,
so the launch verdict still sets its Steam score.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts here are times it was said (bullets). 369 more thumbs-up reviews said nothing specific.
1. **The grind feels earned.** Said 71 times (4.4 per 100 reviews).
2. **It looks great.** Said 64 times (4.0 per 100).
3. **Everything can be earned by playing, without paying.** Said 56 times (3.5 per 100).
4. **The characters are appealing.** Said 46 times (2.9 per 100).
5. **The studio listens and acts.** Said 45 times (2.8 per 100). It peaked at 6.5 per 100 in the rest of
   2024, and was 1.1 in 2026. One review says season 1's content was moved earlier after feedback
   (173145523).
6. **It beats its rivals.** Said 44 times (2.8 per 100). Reasons: the shooting and respect for your time.
   *"The shooter experience ... feels worst in WF and in my opinion best in TFD"* (178128680). A
   500-hour Warframe player says it *"respects my time more"* (229267499).
7. **The price is fair** (it is free). Said 43 times (2.7 per 100).
8. **Combat hits hard.** Said 42 times (2.6 per 100).
9. **Builds are deep and varied.** Said 34 times (2.1 per 100).
10. **An update made it better.** Said 31 times (1.9 per 100). Rose from 1.5 to 5.9 per 100 across the
    periods.
11. **The story is worth following.** Said 29 times (1.8 per 100). Rose to 4.3 per 100 in 2026.
12. **It keeps pulling you back.** Said 28 times (1.8 per 100).
13. **Each character plays its own way.** Said 27 times (1.7 per 100).
14. **Much better with friends.** Said 24 times (1.5 per 100).
15. **Movement feels quick and responsive.** Said 19 times (1.2 per 100). The grapple and double jump are
    named.
16. **The base design works and the rest can still come.** Said 18 times (1.1 per 100).
17. **Progress feels satisfying.** Said 18 times (1.1 per 100).
18. **A game you can relax to.** Said 15 times (0.9 per 100).
19. **The bosses are a highlight.** Said 15 times (0.9 per 100).
20. **Buying things to support the studio.** Said 12 times (0.8 per 100).
21. **Runs well on my machine.** Said 12 times (0.8 per 100).
22. **The shop only sells looks.** Said 12 times (0.8 per 100).
23. **Plenty of content.** Said 11 times (0.7 per 100).
24. **A fair way of making money.** Said 11 times (0.7 per 100).
25. **The drop chances feel fair.** 4 times (only in this game).
26. **The odds are shown.** 3 reviews; **bad-luck protection exists**, 2 reviews. Both come only in
    2025-2026.
27. **Free items are handed out often** (free top characters, login events, catch-up mail). Rose from 0.1
    to 2.7 per 100 across the periods.
28. **Coming back after a break, you get catch-up rewards.** Named by a returning player (229267499).
29. **Seen as better now than at launch.** *"after a streak of high-quality monthly updates, TFD is
    actually finally a very good game"* (226202933, 43 found it helpful).

Neither praise nor complaint: 149 reviews (9.3 per 100) say the characters are designed to look sexy
(fan service). Some like it, some find it embarrassing, many only joke. 124 times reviewers described the
game by naming other games.

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are times it was said (bullets) unless it says reviews. **547 of the 1,955 complaint bullets
(28.0%) come from reviews that recommend the game**; the rest come from thumbs-down reviews. The
thumbs-down reviews have a median of 15.6 hours played, the thumbs-up 39.2.
1. **A named rival does it better** (almost always Warframe). Said 113 times (7.1 per 100), on 112
   reviews.
2. **Too grindy.** Said 100 times (6.2 per 100).
3. **The shop costs far too much.** Said 75 times (4.7 per 100).
4. **Thumbs down with no reason given.** 75 times (4.7 per 100).
5. **Repetitive.** Said 67 times (4.2 per 100).
6. **The thing you need may never drop.** Said 67 times (4.2 per 100), on 66 reviews.
7. **The grind is made frustrating to sell shortcuts.** Said 59 times (3.7 per 100), on 58 reviews.
8. **Feels like a cash grab.** Said 56 times (3.5 per 100).
9. **A copy of an older game.** Said 54 times (3.4 per 100).
10. **The story is thin or forgettable.** Said 47 times (2.9 per 100). The repeated word "Ironheart" and
    the names Greg and Jeremy are mocked.
11. **Poorly optimised.** Said 41 times (2.6 per 100).
12. **The stated drop chances feel rigged.** Said 34 times (2.1 per 100).
13. **Locked behind real-world waiting time.** Said 25 times (1.6 per 100).
14. **Paying affects play** (pay to win). Said 23 times (1.4 per 100). Rose from 1.0 to 2.7 per 100.
15. **The studio teamed up with another studio the reviewer dislikes.** Said 21 times (1.3 per 100).
16. **One random drop only leads to another random drop.** Said 20 times (1.2 per 100). Built for this
    game.
17. **Crashes again and again.** Said 19 times (1.2 per 100).
18. **Long load times.** Said 18 times (1.1 per 100).
19. **Crashes at a certain moment.** Said 16 times (1.0 per 100).
20. **Bought colours are used up when you apply them.** Said 16 times (1.0 per 100). Built for this game.
21. **Needs a strong PC.** Said 16 times (1.0 per 100).
22. **One character or option is far stronger than the rest.** Said 16 times (1.0 per 100). Rose from 0.5
    to 3.2 per 100. Bunny, then Serena and Raven, are named.
23. **Cannot connect.** 15 times (0.9 per 100), every one at launch.
24. **Too little content.** 14 times (0.9 per 100).
25. **Servers are poor.** 13 times (0.8 per 100).
26. **Paying only shortens the grind** (said as a plain fact, neither good nor bad). 21 times.
27. **One strong player kills everything first** while the others follow. 10.
28. **The battle pass rewards are poor.** 7 (built here).
29. **Power creep**: new gear keeps beating old gear. 6.
30. **Skins can only be bought, not earned.** 5 (built here).
31. **Wants a token that guarantees the drop.** 5 reviews (built here).
32. **Abilities make the guns pointless.** 4 (built here).
33. **One gun type uses the scarcest ammo** without hitting hard enough. 4 (built here).
34. **A boss can only be beaten by a rule the game never hints at.** 4 (built here).
35. **Runs worse than it used to on the same PC.** 4 (built here).
36. **No guilds or clans.** 4 (built here).
37. **Locked out of my own account.** 4. Account linking that wiped progress (176109233, 236515665) and a
    support ticket closed without help (231111022). Reviewers' accounts, not checked.
38. **The studio took back a promise** (after months of "no nerfs"). 4.
39. **No fix for a purchase that went wrong.** 4. **The store would not take my money.** 3.
40. **Failed tries do not raise the chance.** 3 reviews (built here).
41. **Players bump into each other.** 2 (built here).
42. **Smaller ones, counts not given:** a bought character comes without its upgrades; some items only
    come in bundles; a skin must be bought again for each character; prices are not set for poorer
    regions (208632208, 325 found it helpful, about African countries); regret over money spent; skins
    that break the mood of a battle; male characters weaker and less dressed up; nerfs of loved
    characters (Ines in April 2025, by the reviewers' account); players who idle and leave the work to
    others; one wipe ends a party; a matched fight that starts before you load in; each player rolls
    their own drops; enemies that switch off your abilities; no crouch or slide; an unreliable grapple;
    loading screens between small areas; detail that pops in close up; shader compiling at launch; "now
    more about cosmetics than play" (rose from 0.1 to 2.7 per 100).

### How it changed over time
Four periods. Margins are about ±3 (launch), ±6, ±6 and ±7, so read for direction.

| Period | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch (June-July 2024) | 864 | 63.1% | 57.8 | 128.0 |
| Rest of 2024 | 310 | 66.1% | 69.4 | 119.7 |
| 2025 | 241 | 63.9% | 56.0 | 102.5 |
| 2026 (to October) | 185 | 69.2% | 64.9 | 84.3 |

- **Complaints fell every period.** Grind 6.7 → 2.7 per 100. Shop prices 6.0 → 1.6. Drops that never
  come 4.3 → 2.2. "A copy of an older game" 4.2 → 1.1. Poor optimisation 3.1 → 0.5. Performance
  reviews 8.8 → 4.9 per 100. Server reviews 4.3 → 0.5. Luck reviews 8.3 → 4.9. Performance, crash and
  server reviews together 18.2 → 12.4 per 100.
- **What grew:** fan service 6.6 → 16.8 reviews per 100; one character too strong 0.5 → 3.2; pay to win
  1.0 → 2.7; "now more about cosmetics than play" 0.1 → 2.7; "an update made it better" 1.5 → 5.9; free
  items 0.1 → 2.7.
- **What players tie the turn to:** shown drop odds and bad-luck protection (praised only in 2025-2026,
  5 reviews); 2026 reviewers say a run of updates removed the container chance and cut the grind
  (232874117, 236591847).
- **Who writes later:** the median hours on a review rise from 20 at launch to 50-70 later. Late
  reviewers are veterans.
- **Warframe fades as a yardstick:** named in 172 reviews at launch, 47 in the rest of 2024, 42 in 2025,
  21 in 2026.
- **But the most-helpful reviews are angry veterans from 2025-2026:**
  - 592 helpful, thumbs down, 1,546 hours (186252899): a character nerfed right after her first buffs,
    while another stays too strong.
  - 554 helpful, thumbs down, 117 hours (231111022): a friend lost a 120-hour account when support
    closed his ticket.
  - 545 helpful, thumbs down, 3,012 hours (233515312, 2026-08): season 4's top weapons, weekly reroll
    limits and sold drop boosts are *"an open push to pay for power"*.
  - 325 helpful, thumbs up, 278 hours (208632208): premium currency too expensive in African countries.
  - 298 helpful, thumbs down, 1 hour (213357044): *"It's Warframe, but with more microtransactions and
    more micromanagement"*.
  - 289 helpful, thumbs down, 615 hours (208640843): collaboration skins at $80 and $35 (the reviewer
    says); each update makes enemies spongier.

### Co-op and online play
- **Better with friends:** said 24 times.
- **Strangers cause friction.** One strong player clears everything while three follow (10). Players
  idle and leave the work to others. One wipe ends a party. Matchmaking can drop you into a fight that
  started before you loaded in. Players bump into each other's bodies (2).
- **Loot:** each player rolls their own drops, so one player's luck does nothing for the group.
- **Servers:** cannot connect was said 15 times, all at launch; server reviews fell from 4.3 to 0.5 per
  100. Poor servers 13.
- **Social:** no guilds or clans (4).
- The pages say nothing about hosting type, joining a game already running (other than being matched in
  late), bots, friendly fire or crossplay.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Combat hits hard:** 42. **Each character plays its own way:** 27. Characters are the draw; guns come
  second.
- **Abilities vs guns:** abilities that clear the map make guns pointless (4). Enemies or fights that
  switch off your abilities leave only the gun, so the character feels pointless.
- **One gun type uses the scarcest ammo** without enough damage to be worth it (4).
- **Movement is mixed.** Responsive movement 19 (grapple and double jump). Complaints: no crouch or
  slide, and an unreliable grapple. Players bump into each other (2).
- **Balance:** one character far stronger than the rest (16, rising to 3.2 per 100); power creep (6);
  nerfs of loved characters, with the most-helpful review in the sample (592) about one.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Bosses are a highlight:** 15.
- **A boss rule is never hinted:** 4 (an order or target you must guess).
- **Enemies get spongier each update** (one 289-helpful review says so).
- **Enemies that switch off your abilities** draw complaints.
- The pages give no other counts on difficulty settings or enemy variety.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **The grind is the game.** Too grindy 100; the grind feels earned 71; everything can be earned 56;
  progress feels satisfying 18; locked behind real-world waiting time 25.
- **The chain of chances.** Characters and guns are crafted from parts. Parts come from containers. A
  container drops at one chance and opens to the part at another. Reviewers do the maths: *"a 25% chance
  ... then roll a separate reactor at 10%, about 2.5% each"* (176109236); *"52 runs for a material for 52
  runs of a mission, still no code"* (176097031).
- **Luck counts (reviews):** may never drop 66; chances feel rigged 34; one random drop leads to another
  20; wants a guaranteed-drop token 5; failed tries do not raise the chance 3; odds shown (praise) 3;
  bad-luck protection (praise) 2. Drop chances feel fair (praise) 4.
- **Randomness complaints are 7.2 reviews per 100, the highest in the corpus** (Warframe 1.3).
- **Builds are deep:** 34. **Power creep:** 6.
- **Returning players** got endgame materials to catch up (229267499).

### Runs, content and replay value
- No runs. Missions are repeated to farm parts.
- **Repetitive:** 67. **Too little content:** 14. **Plenty of content:** 11. **Keeps pulling you back:**
  28. **A game you can relax to:** 15. **The potential is still there:** 18.
- **The story:** thin or forgettable 47; worth following 29 (rising to 4.3 per 100 in 2026).

### Money and price
- **This is where the reviews live.** 294 reviews (18.4%) carry a complaint or remark about the shop.
  **234 reviews (14.6 per 100) carry a shop complaint, the highest in the corpus; Warframe, also free, is
  at 2.8.** Terminull Brigade is 13.2 and Helldivers 2 1.8; every other English group of 300 or more
  reviews is at 1.4 or less.
- **Complaints:** too expensive 75; frustration built to sell shortcuts 58 reviews; cash grab 56; pay to
  win 23 (rising 1.0 → 2.7 per 100); colours used up when applied 16; battle pass rewards poor 7; skins
  only for money 5; paying only shortens the grind 21 (said as neutral).
- **Specific grievances (counts not given):** a bought character without its upgrades; items only in
  bundles; a skin bought again for each character; prices not adjusted for a region; regret over money
  spent; collaboration skins at $80 and $35 (the reviewer says, 208640843).
- **Purchases gone wrong:** no fix for a purchase that went wrong (4); store would not take their money
  (3).
- **Praise:** fair price 43; buys to support the studio 12; shop only sells looks 12; a fair business
  model 11; free items handed out often (rising to 2.7 per 100).
- **What players will not accept:** chance stacked on chance with money as the exit, and power for sale
  (233515312, 545 helpful).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- **Unreal Engine 5 costs it on PC.** Poorly optimised 41; needs a strong PC 16; long load times 18;
  crashes again and again 19; crashes at a certain moment 16; shader compiling and crashes at launch.
- **Built for this game:** loading screens split small areas; detail pops in close up; runs worse than
  it used to (4).
- **Runs well:** 12.
- **It got better:** performance reviews 8.8 → 4.9 per 100; poor optimisation 3.1 → 0.5.
- **Accounts:** locked out of my own account 4 (account linking that overwrote progress; a ticket
  closed). Not checked.

### The studio, updates and community
- **Listens and acts:** 45, most in the first months (6.5 per 100 in the rest of 2024, 1.1 in 2026).
- **Updates made it better:** 31, rising to 5.9 per 100 in 2026. Seen as better now than at launch.
- **Nerfs:** nerfs of loved characters (Ines, April 2025, by the reviewers' account) draw complaints;
  the studio took back a "no nerfs" promise (4). The most-helpful review (592) is about a nerf.
- **Teamed up with a studio players dislike:** 21.
- **Free items handed out often** rose to 2.7 per 100.
- **Rivals:** some came from Helldivers 2 after its nerfs and from Arc Raiders; others left for
  Warframe, Destiny or Anthem.
- **Played a test before release:** 11 reviews name a beta or test as how they came to the game.
- **Fan service** is the loudest single subject: 149 reviews, 9.3 per 100 (next game Terminull Brigade
  3.3, EDF 5 0.8), rising to 16.8 per 100 in 2026.

### What players asked for
- **A token that guarantees the drop:** 5 reviews.
- **Failed tries that raise the chance** (complaint that they do not): 3.
- **Guilds or clans:** 4.
- **Crouch and slide** (complaint that they are missing; count not given).
- **Prices set for their region** (208632208, 325 helpful).
- The pages give no other counted requests.

### Only in this game
82 tags appear in no other game. The ones that matter for design:
- One random drop leads to another: 20.
- Bought colours used up when applied: 16.
- Played a test before release: 11.
- Battle pass rewards poor: 7.
- Skins only for money: 5.
- Wants a token that guarantees the drop: 5.
- Drop chances feel fair: 4.
- Abilities make guns pointless: 4.
- One gun type uses the scarcest ammo: 4.
- A boss rule is never hinted: 4.
- Runs worse than it used to: 4.
- No guilds or clans: 4.
- Also built here: failed tries do not raise the chance (3), odds shown (3), bad-luck protection (2),
  returning players get a catch-up, players idle and leave the work, the fight starts before you load
  in, each player rolls their own drops, enemies switch off your abilities, players bump into each other
  (2), loading screens split small areas, detail pops in close up.
- "A named rival does it better" is almost only this game's: 112 of the 114 reviews on it in the corpus.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Do not stack chance on chance, with money as the way out.**
   Evidence: shop complaints are 14.6 reviews per 100, the highest in the corpus; Warframe, also free, is
   2.8. Luck complaints are 7.2 per 100, also the highest. One random drop leads to another (20),
   frustration built to sell shortcuts (58 reviews), shop too expensive (75).
   Dominion could: keep each reward to one roll at most. Never put a random box inside a random box. If
   Dominion ever sells anything, sell nothing that skips a luck step.
   Strength: strong.
2. **Show the odds and end bad luck from day one.**
   Evidence: shown odds and bad-luck protection were praised only after the game added them (5 reviews,
   2025-2026); 5 reviews ask for a guaranteed-drop token; 3 say failed tries do not help; 66 reviews say
   the thing they need may never drop; 34 say the odds feel rigged. Luck reviews fell from 8.3 to 4.9 per
   100 once it changed.
   Dominion could: show drop chances in the run summary or codex; give a pity counter that guarantees the
   item after a set number of tries; let players trade duplicates for a token.
   Strength: medium (the praise is small; the complaints are large).
3. **Grind is fine if it feels earned and everything is earnable by play.**
   Evidence: too grindy 100 against grind feels earned 71 and everything earnable 56; real-world wait
   timers 25; grind complaints fell 6.7 → 2.7 per 100 once the grind was cut.
   Dominion could: make meta-progress between runs steady and visible; avoid real-time timers on crafting
   or unlocks.
   Strength: strong.
4. **Do not sell power. Pay to win is the rising complaint even in a free game.**
   Evidence: pay affects play 23, rising 1.0 → 2.7 per 100; the third most-helpful review (545) calls
   season 4 *"an open push to pay for power"*; "now more about cosmetics than play" 0.1 → 2.7.
   Dominion could: keep anything bought to looks only, and keep the game about play.
   Strength: strong.
5. **If Dominion sells looks, make them permanent and fair.**
   Evidence: colours used up when applied (16), skins only for money (5), battle pass rewards poor (7),
   a skin bought again for each character, items only in bundles, no prices for poorer regions (325
   helpful). Praise: shop only sells looks (12), fair business model (11), buying to support the studio
   (12), free items often (rising to 2.7 per 100).
   Dominion could: make bought colours and skins unlock for good and for every character; set regional
   prices; give some skins for play.
   Strength: medium.
6. **Unreal Engine 5 performance is judged hard at launch.**
   Evidence: poorly optimised 41, strong PC needed 16, long loads 18, crashes 19 and 16, shader compiling
   at launch; loading screens between small areas; detail popping in close; runs worse after updates (4).
   Performance reviews fell 8.8 → 4.9 per 100 once fixed.
   Dominion could: precompile shaders before the first match; set a frame budget for low PCs; watch
   pop-in and load screens; test that updates do not slow old machines.
   Strength: strong (same engine).
7. **The launch month sets the score for years.**
   Evidence: 62% of all English reviews were written in the first month. Complaints fell from 128 to 84
   per 100 later, yet Steam still shows 57%, Mixed. Cannot connect (15) was all at launch.
   Dominion could: fix the economy, performance and joining before launch, not after; run a public test
   first (11 reviews here came through a test before release).
   Strength: strong.
8. **Know your yardstick and win on one clear thing.**
   Evidence: 282 reviews (17.6%) name Warframe, half of them thumbs down; Destiny in 186. A named rival
   does it better 113; a copy of an older game 54; beats its rivals 44. When it won, it won on the
   shooting and on respecting time.
   Dominion could: expect to be compared to the co-op shooters near it, and make sure one thing is
   plainly better (feel, run length, fairness of drops).
   Strength: strong for the pattern; medium for which rival.
9. **Strangers in co-op need guard rails.**
   Evidence: one strong player kills everything first (10); players idle; one wipe ends a party; you can
   be matched into a fight already started; players bump into each other (2). Better with friends 24.
   Dominion could: give a late joiner a safe spawn and a moment of control before they can be hurt; make
   power gaps in a lobby smaller (scale or cap); let players walk through each other; reward taking part,
   not only kills.
   Strength: medium.
10. **Decide whether loot is shared or rolled per player, and say so.**
    Evidence: each player rolls their own drops, so one player's luck does nothing for the group (built
    here; count not given).
    Dominion could: make some drops shared or tradeable inside a team, so co-op helps everyone's luck.
    Strength: weak.
11. **Keep guns worth using next to abilities.**
    Evidence: abilities make the guns pointless (4); enemies that switch off abilities make the character
    pointless; one gun type uses the scarcest ammo (4).
    Dominion could: let abilities set up gun damage rather than replace it; avoid bosses that turn off
    the whole kit; keep ammo types fair.
    Strength: weak to medium.
12. **Buff the weak more than you nerf the loved, and do not promise "no nerfs".**
    Evidence: one character too strong (16, rising 0.5 → 3.2 per 100); the most-helpful review (592)
    is about a nerf right after buffs; took back a promise (4); power creep (6).
    Dominion could: balance by raising weak options; never promise you will not nerf.
    Strength: medium.
13. **Telegraph boss rules.**
    Evidence: a boss rule is never hinted (4); bosses a highlight (15).
    Dominion could: show the needed order or target with clear visual or voice cues.
    Strength: weak.
14. **Movement needs the modern moves.**
    Evidence: responsive movement 19 (grapple, double jump); complaints about no crouch or slide and an
    unreliable grapple.
    Dominion could: include a slide or dodge, and make any traversal tool reliable.
    Strength: medium (it agrees with Crab Champions).
15. **Protect accounts and purchases.**
    Evidence: locked out of own account 4 (linking wiped progress; a ticket closed); the second
    most-helpful review (554) is about a lost account; purchase that went wrong 4; store would not take
    money 3.
    Dominion could: never let linking a platform overwrite progress; back up saves; have a clear support
    path for lost progress.
    Strength: medium (few reviews, very high helpful votes).
16. **Choose the tone and keep looks inside it.**
    Evidence: fan service is 149 reviews (9.3 per 100) and rising to 16.8 per 100; skins break the mood;
    male characters weaker and less dressed up.
    Dominion could: decide the tone of its sci-fi look early, keep skins in that mood, and treat all
    characters with the same care.
    Strength: medium.
17. **A thin story is skipped; a light one can still win people later.**
    Evidence: thin or forgettable 47 vs worth following 29 (rising to 4.3 per 100 in 2026); a repeated
    word and names mocked.
    Dominion could: keep story short and avoid catchwords repeated too often.
    Strength: weak to medium.
18. **Listen early, keep saying so, and help returners catch up.**
    Evidence: listens and acts 45 (peak 6.5 per 100 in the first months, 1.1 in 2026); updates made it
    better rising to 5.9; returning player got catch-up rewards (229267499).
    Dominion could: post what changed and why; give returning players a catch-up.
    Strength: medium.
19. **Late reviews come from veterans, and they are read most.**
    Evidence: median hours rise from 20 to 50-70; the top three most-helpful reviews are thumbs down from
    players with 117 to 3,012 hours.
    Dominion could: watch long-time players' complaints (balance, power sales) as closely as new players'.
    Strength: medium.
20. **Missions farmed over and over get called repetitive.**
    Evidence: repetitive 67; too little content 14.
    Dominion could: lean on runs that change, so repeating does not feel the same.
    Strength: medium.
21. **Guilds or clans are wanted in a long-running game.**
    Evidence: no guilds or clans (4).
    Dominion could: consider a light group feature if it goes live-service.
    Strength: weak.

### Limits
- The sample is 3.1% of English reviews, ±2.50% on whole-sample shares; the periods carry about ±3 to
  ±7.
- The launch month holds 31,555 of 51,208 English reviews (62%), and 864 of the 1,600 kept reviews are
  from the launch period. From 2024-10 on most months are read at 20 reviews (2026-10 at 6).
- 199 reviews (12.4%) were edited later and are counted on the date they were made; many edits judge
  later seasons.
- Drop rates, lawsuits or fines, account losses, prices and what each season added are the reviewers'
  words, not checked. Season and nerf dates are as reviewers give them; no Steam news was read.
- Only English was read; about 61,000 reviews in other languages were not. 16 English-tagged reviews are
  in other languages.
- Counts in the ranked tables are times it was said; a few on the page are given as reviews (112 vs 113,
  66 vs 67, 58 vs 59).
- "A named rival does it better" is partly how this game was tagged; other games file a similar idea
  differently.

---

## Crab Champions

**Read:** 1,499 of 27,193 English reviews (5.6%; 1,512 were read and 13 were not about the game),
