4. **The story is thin or forgettable** - 47 times, 3.4 per 100.
5. **The item you need may never drop** - 24 times, 1.7 per 100. *"I had a buddy grind for two weeks
   for a ring"* (202191613); *"a 3 hour long pure rng based quest to get a useless gun"* (216597954).
6. **Buy it on sale only** - 20 times, steady at 1.7 to 1.9 per 100 every year after launch.
7. **Needs a strong PC** - 17 times.
8. **Too little content** - 16. **Price too high** - 16. **Repetitive** - 16 (2.9 per 100 in 2025).
9. **Grindy** - 15. **Buggy** - 15. **Build choices shallow** - 15 (0.0 by 2026).
10. **The ending lets it down** - 14 times.
11. **Only runs right with upscaling on** - 14 times (1.5 per 100 at launch, 0.0 by 2026).
12. **Hard and worse alone** - 14 times. *"solo is genuine torture"* (216597954).
13. **A patch made it worse** - 13 times (the DLC patches: *"these new updates ruined most of the game's
    performance"*, 183452969).
14. **Menus and map hard to use** - 13 times (the minimap has no compass directions).
15. **Some options are useless** - 12. In this game it is mostly rings: nine reviews call them filler,
    *"2/3 of all loot in the game, most of them useless"*.
16. **Lost saves** - 12 times; two got them back. One review (187410273, 31 votes) says the save bug
    went unfixed for a year and names a launcher workaround players found themselves.
17. **Bosses are a chore** - 12 times (0.2 per 100 at launch, 2.5 in 2025).
18. **Characters flat or annoying** - 12 (0.4 to 2.4 per 100).
19. **One-hit kills** - 11. *"insta-kills are the worst and laziest kind of game design"* (144291151).
20. **Cannot connect** - 11. **Missing basic comforts in the menus** - 11.
21. **No way to talk to other players** (no voice, no text, only a ping) - 11.
22. **Upgrading makes the game harder** - 9 times. Enemies scale with your gear: *"upgrading your weapon
    often makes your enemies deal more damage and take less"* (209854285); *"you're better off not
    upgrading them"* (223833959).
23. **The game will not start at all** - 9 reviews, 8 thumbs down, median 2 hours played.
24. **One area drags the rest down** - 7 (the Labyrinth in 3, N'Erud in 4).
25. **Lowering the settings does not help** - 7.
26. **The roll decides which content you ever see** - 7. *"I'm not even guaranteed to see the other
    bosses or areas"* (202739424); asks for a *"true-playthrough"* option.
27. **The final boss cannot be read** - 7. *"a visual tragedy to read"*; *"turn the in-game music off
    and listen for the sound cues"*.
28. **A new weapon starts the grind again** - 6.
29. **Slow doors and lifts pad the run** - 4 (*"20 elevators in 2 hours"*).
30. **No way to save a loadout** - 4 (later added, reviewers say).
31. **Three-each complaints:** some rewards need a second player (*"many unlocks are available only
    through multiplayer mechanics"*, 234695004); invisible walls; jumping sections that do not belong
    in a shooter; the boss hides behind its adds; attacks land beyond their visible reach; healing too
    slow for the pace; levelling up changes nothing; a guest can decide your campaign; a dead player
    watches until the next checkpoint; the guest fights at a disadvantage.
32. **Two-each complaints:** the game only stable with the processor slowed down; attacks timed to bait
    your dodge; stamina runs out too fast; melee has no parry or block; only the host keeps progress;
    a third-party tool to beat the reroll; the sequel changes too little.
33. **One:** *"I do wish that there was a way in-game to see what you are missing"* (182187651).
34. **Named, with no count on the pages:** stuns that take control away; spaces built too big; stuck
    in aim-down-sights; the game talks too much; drab colours; the generated levels add nothing; no
    cloud save; a reward that never arrives; equipped items vanishing; no ping shown before joining;
    a guest can play the DLC but not keep its loot.

Mostly from people who stayed: the hidden-content complaint (46 of 61 thumbs up). Mostly from people
who left: "it falls short of the first game" (30 of 53 thumbs down) and "it will not start"
(8 of 9). The pages give no split for the rest.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch quarter (2023-07 to 09) | 465 | 86.5% | 126.2 | 67.3 |
| Late 2023 | 239 | 82.0% | 118.8 | 60.3 |
| 2024 | 268 | 84.7% | 126.1 | 81.3 |
| 2025 | 240 | 79.6% | 122.1 | 91.2 |
| 2026 (to 09) | 170 | 82.4% | 104.1 | 91.2 |

- **Half of all English reviews (19,047 of 38,120) came in the first ten weeks.** The second biggest
  month is 2023-11 (4,397), which reviewers tie to the first DLC. After 2025-02 no month passes 350.
- **Three different reviewers, one after another, and the game stood still.** The launch reviewer
  liked the first game and had a PC that struggled (upscaling 1.5 per 100, waiting for promised
  content 2.2). The 2024 reviewer owned the DLCs and missed the old game (falls short 7.1 per 100,
  patch made it worse 1.5). The 2025-26 reviewer never played the first game (sequel praise 4.7), plays
  with friends (12.4), and cannot find the hidden classes (7.9 then 10.0).
- **The tech complaints went to zero** because, the page says, players' machines caught up - not
  because the studio fixed them.
- **By 2025-26 the hidden classes had been dug out of the game files by players.** Three reviews from
  then say the studio meant it: *"the developers fully intended on players data-mining solutions"*
  (209854285). The complaint is not that secrets exist; it is that a new player is handed the answer
  key and told to go look.
- Bad single months: 2024-10 (59.3% up), 2025-09 (50.0%), but these are 20-review months - noise.
- Reviews written in other languages under the English tag rose from 0.0 to 2.9 per 100.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends:** much better with friends 139, rising every year to 12.4 per 100.
- **Alone:** works alone 25, hard alone 14; some rewards need a second player 3.
- **Hosting and the guest are the weak point.** A guest can decide your campaign (3): *"if the other
  person does not talk to the NPC you have to Alt+F4... some random can control your progress in a
  game you paid for"* (222191203). Only the host keeps the progress (2). The guest fights at a
  disadvantage - worse timing on dodges than the host (3). A dead player watches until the next
  checkpoint, sometimes a whole boss (3). A guest can play the DLC but not keep its loot (no count).
  No ping shown before joining (no count). Map exploration not shared in co-op, and a launcher that
  decides which friends you can see (one review each, waiting in the gaps file).
- **Connection:** cannot connect 11.
- **Talking:** no voice, no text, only a ping - 11. The only community complaint in double figures.
- **Cheating:** one review says a cheater could corrupt a co-op partner's save (one sighting).
- **Friendly fire:** 6 say it is the fun; one says having none *"saves friendships"* (201175078).
- No player-behaviour argument and no slogan in this game's reviews.

### Combat, movement and feel
- The shooting has punch (63); no one says the guns feel weak.
- **The complaints are all about control taken away:** one-hit kills 11; stuns; the boss hides behind
  its adds 3; attacks land beyond their visible reach 3; attacks timed to bait your dodge 2; stamina
  runs out too fast 2; healing too slow for the pace 3; melee has no parry or block 2; stuck in
  aim-down-sights (no count).
- The final fight cannot be read (7): too much on screen, music drowns the sound cues.
- Jumping sections that do not belong in a shooter (3).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Memorable special enemies 44. Bosses a chore 12, rising in 2025 (much of it one boss).
- Hard in a good way 71; clear difficulty steps 24; won over someone who avoids the genre 21.
- Upgrading makes the game harder (9) - the enemies scale with your gear.
- One review each: difficulty cannot be changed mid-campaign; the top difficulty is locked until the
  story is done; four reviews say the first boss is the hardest part.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Deep builds 98 (two classes at once, mods, rings) against shallow builds 15.
- The trait cap (9 reviews; *"uncap trait points"*, 174 votes). Armour set bonuses missed (7).
- Grindy 15; a new weapon starts the grind again 6; levelling up changes nothing 3; upgrading makes it
  harder 9. The page sums it up: the builds are loved, the small stat upgrades are invisible or harmful.
- Rings as filler (9); currency always short (3); two praise the drops being staged.
- Randomness: the thing you need may never drop 24; the roll decides which content you see 7; a
  third-party "world analyzer" tool to beat the reroll 2.
- No way to save a loadout 4, then loadout slots praised.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Not a run game: a campaign with checkpoints, rerolled to see other content.
- Keeps pulling you back 75; varied worlds 33; plenty 17. Repetitive 16; too little 16; the
  generation adds nothing (no count).
- **The hidden-content cluster** - hidden behind a guide 61, never drops 24, roll decides content 7,
  needs a second player 3, reroll tool 2 - is about seven complaints per 100 reviews, the biggest
  cluster.
- Level pacing: one area drags the rest down 7; slow doors and lifts 4; invisible walls 3; menus and map
  hard to use 13; spaces built too big; audio logs that make you stand still (one review).

### Money and price
- Price fair 19 (falls to 0.0 in 2026); too high 16; buy on sale only 20, steady every year. The page
  reads it as a game that was already a sale purchase in 2023.
- DLC fair 13 (2.1 per 100 in late 2023). One review each: a new player is started inside the DLC.
- Price and sales are 36 of the top-25 complaint remarks.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- **The PC that cannot run it, said four ways:** needs a strong PC 17; only runs right with upscaling
  14; lowering settings does not help 7; only stable with the processor slowed down 2. None is a crash
  complaint.
- **Will not start at all:** 9 reviews, 8 thumbs down, median 2 hours; fixes ranged from slowing the
  processor to Windows 7 compatibility mode.
- Lost saves 12; no cloud save; equipped items vanish; a reward never arrives (no counts for the last
  three). Buggy 15. Cannot connect 11.
- One review each: performance gets worse as the save file grows; sound in one ear only.
- Tech is 7.8% of all remarks; it falls to near zero by 2026.

### The studio, updates and community
- Studio earned my trust 11. A patch made it worse 13 (peaks with the 2024 DLC patches).
- Waiting for promised content, including DLSS and roadmap items, 2.2 per 100 at launch, gone by 2025.
- Two companies are named: Gunfire Games for the design, Arc Games for the unfixed save corruption
  (*"Arc Games is breaking the law"*, 187410273, 31 votes - the reviewer's claim). Seven communication
  complaints do not say which.
- No slogan, no culture, no player-conduct fight.

### What players asked for
- Uncap the traits (9; 174 votes on the top review).
- Bring back armour set bonuses (7), Survival mode (6), the old worlds and biomes.
- An in-game way to see what you are missing (1) and a "true-playthrough" that guarantees content (in
  the 7 reviews saying the roll decides what you see).
- Voice or text chat (in the 11 "no way to talk" complaints).
- A way to save loadouts (4; reviewers say it was added).
- DLSS and roadmap content (2.2 per 100 at launch).
- A ping shown before joining; cloud saves (no counts).

### Only in this game
- **Content hidden on purpose as the top complaint** - the mode was built for this game, and the line
  rises in every period. 38 of the 363 kinds of remark used here appear in no other game.
- **Upgrading makes the game harder** (9) - no other game has it.
- **The trait cap** - one of nine modes this game built that no other uses.
- **The guest-control complaints** (a guest can decide your campaign; a dead player spectates until
  the checkpoint) were first built here.
- **The four-way "my PC cannot run it"** set (upscaling, settings do not help, slowed processor, will
  not start).
- **The final fight cannot be read**, sitting beside "the final fight is a pushover" as the two ways a
  final boss fails.
- **A sequel judged against the first game for two years, then against itself.**

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Hide secrets, but never hide the core: let players see in the game what is left to find.**
   Evidence: hidden behind a guide 61 (4.4 per 100, rising to 10.0 in 2026), 46 of them from fans; the
   one ask for an in-game list (182187651).
   Dominion could: keep secrets for cosmetics and lore; put every class, perk and major item on an
   in-game codex that shows "not yet found" and hints where; never gate a class on a hidden puzzle.
   Strength: **strong**.
2. **Do not let the dice decide whether a player ever sees content or gets a key item.**
   Evidence: never drops 24; roll decides content 7; a third-party tool to beat the reroll 2; a buddy
   grinding two weeks for a ring.
   Dominion could: use bad-luck protection and "pity" counters; let players choose or pin a map type
   or boss for a run; show drop sources.
   Strength: **medium**.
3. **Never make upgrading gear make the game harder.**
   Evidence: upgrading makes the game harder 9; *"you're better off not upgrading"*; levelling up
   changes nothing 3; a new weapon restarts the grind 6.
   Dominion could: scale run difficulty by the chosen tier, not by the player's gear; make every
   upgrade felt in play.
   Strength: **medium**.
4. **Do not cap the thing that makes builds fun.**
   Evidence: *"uncap trait points"*, 174 votes, the most-helpful review; 9 trait-cap reviews; armour
   set bonuses missed 7.
   Dominion could: let players max a build's key stats over time; if there is a cap, let it be raised.
   Strength: **medium**.
5. **Build-making is the praise; protect it.**
   Evidence: deep builds 98; each class plays its own way 12; two classes at once named as the thing
   the sequel added; firing range and loadout slots thanked after 4 asked.
   Dominion could: offer class combos or sub-classes, a firing range in the hub, and saved loadouts
   from day one.
   Strength: **strong**.
6. **The guest must never lose out to the host.**
   Evidence: a guest can decide your campaign 3; only the host keeps progress 2; the guest fights at a
   disadvantage 3; a guest cannot keep DLC loot; no ping shown before joining.
   Dominion could: give every player their own progress and loot from a run; test dodge and hit timing
   on the client, not only the host; show ping before joining; never let one player's choice lock
   another's progress.
   Strength: **medium** (small counts, but this is exactly Dominion's listen-server setup).
7. **Do not make a downed player watch for minutes.**
   Evidence: a dead player spectates until the next checkpoint 3 (sometimes a whole boss).
   Dominion could: allow revives, a timed respawn or a way to help while down.
   Strength: **weak**.
8. **Take control away from the player as little as possible, and make every attack readable.**
   Evidence: one-hit kills 11; stuns; attacks beyond their visible reach 3; baited dodges 2; boss
   hiding behind adds 3; slow healing 3; stamina 2; no parry 2; the final fight cannot be read 7,
   with music drowning the cues.
   Dominion could: telegraph big hits, match hitboxes to visuals, keep stuns short, mix boss music
   under attack sounds.
   Strength: **medium**.
9. **Test low-end PCs early and make settings matter.**
   Evidence: needs a strong PC 17; only right with upscaling 14; lowering settings does not help 7;
   slowed processor 2; will not start 9 (8 thumbs down, median 2 hours).
   Dominion could: set a minimum spec and test against it, make each setting visibly lighten the load,
   support upscalers but do not require them, catch launch failures.
   Strength: **medium** (many reviews; the pages do not name the engine, so whether Dominion would meet the same load is not shown here).
10. **Never lose a player's save.**
    Evidence: lost saves 12; the save bug a reviewer says went a year unfixed; no cloud save.
    Dominion could: use cloud saves and backups; never let a guest or cheater write the host's save.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **Give players a way to talk.**
    Evidence: no voice, no text, only a ping - 11, the top community complaint.
    Dominion could: ship voice chat, text chat and a rich ping wheel.
    Strength: **medium**.
12. **Spend on the world, keep the story short.**
    Evidence: world worth exploring 92 and places with mood 20, against a thin story 47, the ending 14,
    flat characters 12 and the game talks too much; story worth following only 23.
    Dominion could: tell the story through places and short lines; do not make players stand still to
    listen.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **A companion players can pet earns affection cheaply.**
    Evidence: the dog - 11, the only character named with love.
    Dominion could: give a class or the hub a creature or drone players can interact with.
    Strength: **weak**.
14. **Keep levels moving.**
    Evidence: one area drags the rest down 7; slow lifts 4; invisible walls 3; jumping sections 3; hard
    to use map 13 (no compass on the minimap).
    Dominion could: cut long lifts and doors from runs; give the minimap compass directions; avoid
    platforming.
    Strength: **medium**.
15. **Price for a sale world, and keep DLC fair.**
    Evidence: buy on sale 20, steady every year; price fair falls to 0.0; DLC fair 13.
    Strength: **weak**.
16. **Friends grow in importance as a game ages.**
    Evidence: better with friends rises 7.3 to 12.4 per 100 while sequel praise falls.
    Dominion could: plan long-term around group play (invites, friend lists, group rewards).
    Strength: **medium**.
17. **Do not lock difficulty choices.**
    Evidence: one review each for no mid-campaign change and top difficulty locked until the story is
    done; won over someone who avoids the genre 21.
    Dominion could: let players change difficulty between runs freely.
    Strength: **weak**.

### Limits
- A 3.6% sample at ±3.27%; 32 of 39 months are the 20-review floor. Read the five periods, not months.
- The launch week is under-sampled: 70 of 7,906 reviews (0.89%), because the sampling grid's first
  three July windows ended before the launch day.
- 79 reviews (5.7%) were edited later and counted on the day they were first written.
- No patch notes were pulled. The DLCs, boss rush, loadout slots, region filter and save fixes are the
  reviewers' word; the page cannot say whether the hidden-content problem was ever addressed.
- English only; about 30,000 reviews in other languages were not pulled. 12 of the "English" reviews
  were in other languages.
- 21% of remarks said nothing specific.
- The pages give no largest group size and no engine.
- The cross-game recount gives praise 121.6 and complaints 77.5 per 100 and a ratio of 1.57 : 1; the
  game's own pages give 1.60 : 1 and the Risk of Rain 2 page cites 76 complaints per 100.

---

## Warhammer 40,000: Space Marine 2

**Read:** 1,418 of 124,849 English reviews (1.14%), ±2.60%, written September 2024 to September 2026.
**87.6% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam, all languages: 84.0%, Very Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/space-marine-2-english.md`, `findings/space-marine-2.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 17.

**What it is:** A third-person co-op shooter against hordes of enemies (PvE), with heavy melee: a parry,
and finishing moves on stunned enemies. Made by Saber Interactive, published by Focus Entertainment. It
is a licensed game, set in Games Workshop's Warhammer 40,000 sci-fi world - the first licensed game in
the study. It has a short story campaign, a co-op mode called Operations built on six classes, and a
player-versus-player (PvP) mode; reviewers say a Siege mode came in 2026. Computer teammates (bots) fill
empty spots. Crossplay covers the co-op but not the PvP. Sessions are missions picked from a hub, not runs that
reset and not extraction. There is no loot to find. Paid, full price; reviewers say the paid
extras are only for looks. Released 2024-09-09; the pages name no early access. The pages do not say how many players
are in a squad, who hosts the game, or what engine it runs on.

**How close to Dominion:** Close in shape. It is third person, co-op, PvE, sci-fi, built on classes, and
lives or dies on how strong the combat makes you feel. It is not built on runs, has no extraction and no
loot, has a story campaign and a PvP mode Dominion does not plan, and leans on a famous licence that
Dominion does not have. The pages say nothing about hosting, so nothing here speaks to a listen server
directly.

### The short version (plain words)
People love this game for what it is and complain about how little of it there is. The top complaint is
"not enough game" (said 84 times, 5.9 per 100 reviews) - and 66 of those 84 come from people who still
recommend it; the median reviewer who says it has played 17 hours. The top praise, after the war cry
"For the Emperor" (said 310 times, about one review in five), is the power fantasy: "it makes me feel
superhumanly strong" (64 times, 63 of them from thumbs-up reviews). The flip side, "it never makes me
feel as strong as the story says" (13 times), comes 12 of 13 times from thumbs-down reviews, and the
cause is always a stun or a swarm that takes control away. The thumb barely moved in two years (88.5%
in the launch month, 87.8% in 2026), but what people complained about changed completely: crashes fell
to zero and "wait for a sale" rose about ten-fold.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are times it was said (one review can say more than one thing). Per 100 = per 100 reviews read.

1. **The war cry.** Reviews shout the game's slogan, "For the Emperor" - 310 times, 21.9 per 100. One
   review in five says nothing but the slogan. The page counts this as praise, the same shape as
   Helldivers 2's "for Democracy". A fan with nothing to say says "For the Emperor."
2. **The story is worth following** - 74 times (5.2 per 100), steady at about 5 per 100 every year.
3. **It looks great** - 64 times (4.5 per 100), rising to 6.1 per 100 in 2026.
4. **It makes you feel superhumanly strong** - 64 times (4.5 per 100); 63 of the 64 from thumbs-up
   reviews. "Every fibre calls out for death to heresy" (218331878); "peak testosterone gaming"
   (221527851); "the power fantasy of being a Space Marine done right" (222216060). The study had to make
   a new label for this; before this game it could only say combat felt heavy or light.
5. **It is faithful to the Warhammer 40,000 source** - 62 times (4.4 per 100). This one fades: 5.9 per
   100 in the launch month, 1.0 by 2026. It is what a fan notices in the first week.
6. **The world is worth exploring** - 54 times (3.8 per 100). Much of it is "this game got me into
   Warhammer 40,000".
7. **A good choice of modes** (campaign, Operations, PvP) - 51 times (3.6 per 100).
8. **Combat hits hard** - 45 times (3.2 per 100), rising to 4.6 in 2026. "So satisfying and incredibly
   visceral" (218905901). Only 6 say the guns feel weightless.
9. **The sequel was worth the wait** - 40 times (2.8 per 100). It came thirteen years after the first
   game. "Here we are thirteen years later with more honour, blood, faith and testosterone than ever"
   (175772969, 68 people found it helpful). Only 2 say the sequel claim backfired.
10. **Much better with friends** - 32 times (2.3 per 100), rising from 2.2 to 3.6 per 100 by 2026.
11. **Patches fixed what mattered** - 25 times (1.8 per 100), mostly early: 2.5 per 100 in the launch
    month (reviewers name the ultrawide screen fix, then weapon and parry fixes), 0.5 by 2026.
12. **It keeps pulling you back** - 23 times (1.6 per 100).
13. **Old-fashioned, and better for it** - 21 times (1.5 per 100). "A return to the glorious feel other
    shooter franchises have lost" (218332641).
14. **Hard in a good way** - 20 times (1.4 per 100), rising from 1.1 to 3.6 per 100 by 2026, as the
    average reviewer piled up hours.
15. **Made for its fans, and better for it** - 18 times (1.3 per 100).
16. **The politics of the setting drew me in** - 16 times (1.1 per 100), launch only (1.6 per 100 in
    2024, 0.0 in 2026).
17. **The price is fair** - 16 times (1.1 per 100). By 2026 nobody says it (1.3 per 100 in 2024 and 2025,
    0.0 in 2026).
18. **Best game of its kind** - 14 times (1.0 per 100).
19. **Unlocking things feels good** - 13 times (0.9 per 100); 1.2 per 100 in 2024, 0.0 in 2026.
20. **You can make your Space Marine look how you want** - 13 times (0.9 per 100). This did not exist at
    launch: 0.0 in 2024, 2.0 in 2025, 3.6 in 2026, after the 2025 looks updates. All 13 thanks come from
    2025-03 on.
21. **The mood draws you in** - 13 times (0.9 per 100).
22. **It runs well** - 12 times (0.8 per 100).
23. **The shop sells only looks** - 11 times (0.8 per 100).
24. **Hits look powerful (effects and gore)** - 11 times (0.8 per 100).
25. **It ends before it wears out** - 11 times (0.8 per 100). This is the answer to the top complaint:
    11 say it ends at the right time, 84 say it ends too soon.
26. **A studio that listens** - 11 times.
27. **A welcoming community** - 7 times.
28. **It beats its rivals** - 6 times, naming Call of Duty and Helldivers 2. "With Black Ops' marketing
    budget it would blow it out of the water" (179597075, 622 helpful, the second most-voted review).
29. **The finishing moves are a highlight** - 3 times. "The executions - especially the newer Power Axe
    ones - make you feel like a real warrior" (206151338, said inside a thumbs-down review).
30. **Reviews that teach you how to play** - 3 times.
31. **Bots that help** - 2 times (against 10 that say they are useless).
32. **No friendly fire, named as a plus** - 1 review: "Helldivers without accidentally shooting your
    friends" (220778360).

Also: 62 reviews explain the game by naming another game, and half of those name Gears of War (31) - as
praise. Helldivers 2 is named in 12, Darktide 7, Doom 7, Vermintide 4, Halo 3, Destiny 3. "Reminds me of
Gears of War - worth the playthrough" (220783067).

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are times it was said. Per 100 = per 100 reviews read. The year columns on the page are per 100
reviews in 2024 (921 reviews), 2025 (301) and 2026 (196).

1. **Not enough game** - 84 times (5.9 per 100). **Mostly from people who stayed: 66 of the 84 are
   thumbs up.** 43 of the 81 reviews that say it have under 20 hours; 6 have over 100. The median
   reviewer who says it has 17 hours. At launch Operations had six missions (the page says). 7.1 per 100
   in 2024, 3.0 in 2025 as Operations grew, back to 5.1 in 2026 from the other side: "thirteen missions
   after three years, PvP dead" (224995971).
2. **It repeats itself** - 37 times (2.6 per 100); 2.9 in 2024, 1.0 in 2025, 3.6 in 2026.
3. **Too expensive for what it is** - 28 times (2.0 per 100), flat at about 2 per 100 every year.
4. **It crashes again and again** - 26 times (1.8 per 100). A launch problem: 3.2 per 100 in the launch
   month, 0.0 in 2025, 1.0 in 2026.
5. **Only buy it on sale** - 22 times (1.6 per 100). **14 of the 22 from people who recommend it.** The
   only complaint that rises without stopping: 0.5, 0.9, 2.1, 1.9, then 5.1 per 100 across the five
   periods. By 2026 one review in twenty says wait for a sale.
6. **Long loading times** - 20 times (1.4 per 100).
7. **The story is thin or forgettable** - 18 times (1.3 per 100).
8. **A patch made it worse** - 16 times (1.1 per 100).
9. **One mode falls flat** - 15 times (1.1 per 100). It is almost always the PvP: "PvP is buns, like
   Destiny 2's Crucible without the random-roll guns" (234734108).
10. **Too grindy** - 15 times (1.1 per 100); 0.5 per 100 in 2024, 2.6 in 2026. Reviewers name perk
    points and weapon mastery. One says "only about 20% of players have maxed a gun" (220131655) - as
    praise.
11. **Another game does it better** - 15 times (1.1 per 100). Mostly Darktide, peaking in 2025 (2.3 per
    100). "Play Darktide if you are in it for the gameplay" (206151338, 38 helpful, 212 hours).
12. **Only one of each class per squad, so someone loses the class they wanted** - 13 times (0.9 per
    100). "One Bulwark per squad, so if two of you want it one of you loses." It turns a review to
    thumbs down on its own (202744724), and players asked for it to be lifted in the 2026 Siege mode
    (219488514). Seen in no other game.
13. **It never makes you feel as strong as the story says** - 13 times (0.9 per 100). **12 of the 13 are
    thumbs down - people who left.** Always the same cause: a stun, a knock-down or a swarm takes control
    away from a hero who is supposed to be unstoppable.
14. **Disconnects** - 12 times (0.8 per 100). 0.5 per 100 in 2024, 2.3 in 2025 (the April 2025 update),
    0.0 in 2026.
15. **Buggy** - 11 times (0.8 per 100).
16. **Bots are useless in a fight** - 10 times (0.7 per 100). "Playing alone on higher levels the bots
    assigned to you become dolts" (222211857).
17. **A famous thing from Warhammer 40,000 is missing** - 10 times (0.7 per 100): a Dreadnought to play,
    the Necrons, the T'au, Lightning Claws.
18. **You cannot change how you look** - 10 times (0.7 per 100). 8 asks in 2024, 2 in 2025, 0 in 2026 -
    it went away as the looks options came in (see praise 20).
19. **Some weapons or options are useless** - 9 times (0.6 per 100).
20. **Health does not come back between fights** - 9 times (0.6 per 100).
21. **The parry fails when you need it** - 9 times (0.6 per 100).
22. **The narrow view is a handicap** - 9 times (0.6 per 100). This is the dark edge (vignette) that
    closes in on the screen.
23. **Crashes at one certain moment** - 9 times (0.6 per 100).
24. **Stuns take control away** - 9 times (0.6 per 100). "Clips exist of players unable to act until
    they die."
25. **Progress not saved** - 8 times (0.6 per 100).
26. **Ranged enemies hit you from anywhere while you are swarmed** - 5 times (the Rubric Marines and
    Tzaangor archers of the Chaos side).
27. **You cannot tell what the threat is** - 5 times. "Chaos fights are visual clutter with misleading
    cues"; the kinds of Chaos Spawn cannot be told apart.
28. **One part is far harder than the rest** - 4 times (the Chaos and Thousand Sons missions, and the
    campaign's last chapter - one reviewer dropped to normal to finish it, 222211857).
29. **Levels pad themselves with lifts and walks** - 4 times.
30. **Every mission starts with dead time** - 2 times. "Select a mission, see the captain, go to the
    armoury, go to the launch bay, and it repeats" (222215927).
31. **The game forces melee on you when you wanted to shoot** - 2 times.
32. **A whole loadout cannot hit the Zoanthrope** - 2 times. It hovers above every melee build.
33. **The guns feel weightless** - 6 times (against 45 that say combat hits hard).
34. **Slow-motion on every interaction** - named in 206151338: "it slows your perception, not the game."

Also named by this game, with no count on the pages: the controls take hours to get used to; the default
button layout is awkward (a parry in a bad spot); the finishing moves repeat until they are stale; the
game runs your computer hot; you cannot start your own lobby, only join ones the game picks; paid content
still has to be earned in the game; the story mode leaves out the class system; a class is weaker in one
mode than another; there is no way to replay one chapter; updates go to parts of the game the reviewer
does not care about; the studio does not play its own game; crossplay covers co-op but not PvP. And seen
only once each (left open in the study): progress does not carry between modes, a PvP that feels like
playing bots, a colour sold behind a paywall, flashing lights, a mission modifier that switches off part
of your kit, and a fan fix that beat the studio's.

**Who could have stopped these?** Of the top 25 complaints: how much game there is, 121 times; game
design, 121; engineering (crashes, loads, disconnects, bugs, saves), 86; the price, 50; the studio's
reputation and patches, 31; the story, 28. There is no "other players" complaint in the top 25 - in the
whole sample, player conduct comes up only 21 times. Nobody can shoot you.

### How it changed over time

| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | Mean hours played |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Launch month (2024-09) | 591 | 88.5% | 106.8 | 55.2 | 29 |
| Rest of 2024 | 330 | 87.0% | 122.1 | 61.8 | 55 |
| 2025, Jan to Jun | 146 | 87.7% | 122.6 | 60.3 | 63 |
| 2025, Jul to Dec | 155 | 85.2% | 113.5 | 61.3 | 47 |
| 2026, Jan to Sep | 196 | 87.8% | 127.6 | 70.9 | 74 |

- **The thumb stayed inside three points for two years.** Praise and complaints both rose; later
  reviewers say more, good and bad, and land in the same place. The verdict was set in the launch month,
  which alone holds 45.8% of all English reviews ever written (57,134 of 124,849). Two thirds (67.6%) came
  in the first four months.
- **Each period argues about something new.** The launch month argues about crashes; the rest of 2024
  about content; 2025 about the April update and Darktide; 2026 about the price and the grind.
- **Fell:** crashes (3.2 per 100 in the launch month, 0.0 by 2025); "patches fixed what mattered" (2.5 to
  0.5); "faithful to the source" (5.9 to 1.0); "the price is fair" (1.3 to 0.0 by year); "you cannot change
  how you look" (0.9 to 0.0).
- **Rose:** "buy on sale only" (0.5 to 5.1); "hard in a good way" (0.7 to 3.6); "you can look how you
  want" (0.0 to 3.6); "too grindy" (0.7 to 2.6); "one mode falls flat" (1.2 to 2.0); "it is just average"
  (0.5 to 2.6).
- **Peaked in the middle:** "not enough game" peaked in the rest of 2024 (7.6 per 100), fell to 1.9 in late
  2025, came back to 5.1 in 2026. "Makes you feel superhumanly strong" peaked in the rest of 2024 (7.6),
  then halved to 3.6 by 2026, when reviewers had 74 hours on average and wrote about difficulty instead.
  Disconnects peaked at 3.4 per 100 in early 2025 and went to 0.0. "Another game does it better" peaked
  at 2.6 in late 2025.
- **The April 2025 update** (called "Trygon" by reviewers) broke servers, added stutter and caused a false
  anti-cheat kick, which reviewers say was fixed on 2025-04-24 (193373353). No patch notes were pulled,
  so this is what reviewers say.
- **People kept reviewing.** Every full month from 2025-01 on has 1,300 to 3,100 English reviews on Steam,
  with no slope down. The page reads this as a game that keeps selling on sale.
- **Edits run both ways.** 155 reviews (10.9%) were edited later but are counted on the day first written:
  "got my cape 10/10" (226907394) and "just awesome nowadays" sit on 2024 dates next to launch complaints
  that were later taken back.

### Co-op and online play
- **Better with friends:** 32 times (2.3 per 100), rising to 3.6 per 100 by 2026.
- **The class lock** (one of each class per squad) is the co-op complaint this game owns: 13 times, and
  it flips reviews to thumbs down by itself (202744724). Players asked for it off in the Siege mode
  (219488514).
- **Bots:** 10 say they are useless in a fight, 2 say they help. They get worse on higher difficulty when
  you play alone (222211857).
- **Disconnects:** 12 times, mostly after the April 2025 update (2.3 per 100 in 2025), gone in 2026.
- **Strangers:** no player-conduct fight here - 21 times in the whole sample, against 360 in ARC Raiders.
  There is no friendly fire, and one review names that as a plus (220778360). A welcoming community: 7.
- **Lobbies:** the study added "you cannot start your own lobby, only join ones the game picks" from this
  game (count not given).
- **Crossplay:** covers co-op but not PvP (count not given).
- **Progress not carrying between modes** was seen once and left open.
- The pages do not say who hosts a match, how joining a running mission works, or how enemies scale to
  the number of players.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **The power fantasy decides the thumb.** 64 say it makes you feel superhumanly strong (63 thumbs up);
  13 say it never does (12 thumbs down). The page calls this the cleanest thumb split of any pair in the
  study. A lifelong fan bounced at launch and came back when the weapon and parry fixes made it click
  (225612951).
- **Impact:** 45 say combat hits hard; 6 say weightless. Hits look powerful: 11. Nobody says the guns feel
  weak.
- **What breaks it is always lost control:** the parry fails when needed (9), stuns take control away (9),
  the dark edge that narrows the view (9), slow-motion on every interaction ("it slows your perception,
  not the game", 206151338), melee forced on someone who wanted to shoot (2). Seen once each: three
  shapes of the "defence" complaint - the slow-motion, having to watch for warning symbols, having to
  parry all the time.
- **The hated enemy is the Chaos Spawn, and the reason is the stun.** Two reviews name it as the enemy
  they will not fight, both for a grab that takes control away (206151338, 206819849).
- **Finishing moves:** 3 call them a highlight; the study also built "they repeat until they are stale"
  from this game (count not given).
- **Controls:** "take hours to get used to" and "the default layout is awkward" were built from this game
  (counts not given). Game feel as a whole (combat and controls) was said 220 times, the biggest design
  subject.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **The Chaos problem.** The most-helpful review in the sample (217297755, 2026-01-31, 116 hours, 632
  helpful, thumbs down) says the Chaos missions are "a full difficulty tier above Tyranid missions, not
  by design but through clutter, misleading cues, flinching and stun-lock - so players avoid them,
  halving the map pool to six." The reviewer quotes the studio's own numbers: 35 billion Tyranid
  Warriors killed, over triple the Chaos Spawn count; the top three missions all Tyranid; Chaos missions
  unranked for lack of plays. And: "Saber has stayed silent despite overwhelming feedback... not
  recommended, with a heavy heart." The same complaint shows up 4 ways in the sample: one part far
  harder than the rest (4), threats you cannot read (5), stuns (9), ranged enemies hitting you while you
  are swarmed (5).
- Whether it was fixed is unknown: of the 169 reviews from 2026-02 on, one mentions Chaos.
- **The Zoanthrope** hovers above every melee build, so a whole loadout cannot hit it (2).
- **Hard in a good way:** 20, rising to 3.6 per 100 in 2026.
- **The campaign's last chapter** is too hard for one reviewer, who dropped to normal (222211857).
- **Balance:** some options are useless (9); health does not come back between fights (9).
- Difficulty was said 57 times, enemies 49, power balance 41.
- Bosses are not named on the pages.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **No loot in this game** (the page says so). Progress is classes, perks, weapon mastery and looks.
- **Grind:** 15 say it is grindy, rising with hours (0.7 per 100 at launch to 2.6 in late 2025 and 2026).
  Perk points and weapon mastery are named. "Only about 20% of players have maxed a gun" (220131655, said
  as praise).
- **Unlocks feel good:** 13, but gone by 2026 (1.2 to 0.0 per 100).
- **Looks:** the clearest "asked and delivered" in the sample. 10 asked to change how they look (8 in
  2024, 2 in 2025, 0 in 2026); 13 thank the studio for it from 2025-03 on, after the 2025 looks updates
  and the Heretic Astartes unlocks. A cape for the Bulwark was asked in a thumbs-down (183521531) and
  later "Got my cape 10/10" (226907394). Players want to look like their own chapter.
- **Paid but locked:** "paid content still has to be earned in the game" was built from this game (count
  not given). A colour sold behind a paywall: seen once.
- **The story mode leaves out the class system**, and a class can be weaker in one mode than another
  (counts not given). Progression was said 123 times in all.

### Runs, content and replay value
- This is not a run-based game. Content is a short campaign plus Operations missions.
- **Too little:** 84 (see complaints). Six Operations missions at launch; one 2026 reviewer counts
  "thirteen missions after three years" (224995971).
- **Repetitive:** 37. **Keeps pulling you back:** 23. **Ends before it wears out:** 11.
- **Modes:** a good choice of modes, 51; one mode falls flat (the PvP), 15. "PvP dead" (224995971).
- **Dead time:** the hub walk before every mission (2) and lifts and walks inside missions (4).
- **No way to replay one chapter** of the campaign (built from this game, count not given).
- **Story and setting:** the story division is 9.2% of everything said, the highest in the study (Redfall
  8.2%, the other co-op games under 3%, ARC Raiders 0.5%). Story worth following 74, thin 18; world worth
  exploring 54; the politics drew me in 16; a famous thing is missing 10.

### Money and price
- **Too expensive for what it is:** 28, flat at about 2 per 100.
- **Only buy on sale:** 22, rising from 0.5 to 5.1 per 100; 14 of the 22 from recommenders. **The 2026
  reviewer likes the game and thinks it is not worth full price two years on.**
- **Fair price:** 16, falling to 0.0 in 2026.
- **The shop sells only looks:** 11 say so, as praise.
- **Bought it but still have to earn it:** built from this game (count not given).
- **A colour behind a paywall:** seen once.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- **Crashes again and again:** 26 (3.2 per 100 in the launch month, 0.0 in 2025, 1.0 in 2026).
- **Long loads:** 20 (1.4 per 100), steady.
- **Disconnects:** 12, tied to the April 2025 update.
- **Buggy:** 11. **Crashes at one moment:** 9. **Progress not saved:** 8 - a 2024 and April 2025
  complaint that goes to zero afterwards.
- **Runs well:** 12. **Runs the machine hot:** built from this game (count not given).
- Reviewers name an ultrawide fix (2024-10) and the April 2025 update with its 2025-04-24 fix. No patch
  notes were pulled. The engine is not named or blamed on the pages.
- The page sums it up: the engineering complaints were fixed, and the price complaint took their place.

### The studio, updates and community
- **The war cry:** 310 times, 21.9 per 100. The community division is mostly this slogan (311 of 395).
  A few reviews go further and write the whole review in the voice of a Space Marine.
- **Patches:** fixed what mattered 25 (mostly early), made it worse 16, a studio that listens 11. Waiting
  on promised content: 23.
- **Updates go to the wrong place:** built from this game - updates keep coming, but to cosmetics or a
  mode the reviewer did not come for (count not given). "The studio does not play its own game": built
  from this game (count not given).
- **Silence:** the 632-vote Chaos review says the studio stayed silent.
- **Publisher:** reviewers name Saber, never Focus.
- **Reputation:** old-fashioned and better for it 21; made for its fans 18; best of its kind 14; another
  game does it better 15 (mostly Darktide); it beats its rivals 6 (Call of Duty, Helldivers 2); the
  sequel was worth the wait 40 (backfired 2); "it is just average" rose from 0.5 to 2.6 per 100.
- A fan fix beat the studio's (seen once).
- Other review habits built from this game: a reviewer promoting their own stream; a reviewer saying they
  are the target audience; a reviewer who finished the game and blames their own computer for problems.

### What players asked for
- **More missions and more game** - the 84 "not enough game", and 23 waiting on promised content.
- **Famous Warhammer 40,000 things** - 10: a Dreadnought to play, Necrons, T'au, Lightning Claws.
- **Looks** - to change how they look (10 asks, all before 2025-03), to look like their own chapter, a
  cape for the Bulwark. Delivered, then thanked 13 times.
- **No class lock** in the Siege mode (219488514), and in general (13 complain about it).
- **A fix for the Chaos missions** (217297755 and the 4 kinds of complaint above).
- **Your own lobby** instead of only the game's matchmaking; **crossplay in PvP**; **chapter select** for
  the campaign; **progress that carries between modes** (counts not given or seen once).
- **Better bots** (10 useless).
- **A lower price, or wait for a sale** (22 say buy on sale only).

### Only in this game
29 of the 365 labels used here appear in no other game. The ones that matter for design:
- Feeling superhumanly strong, and its flip, never feeling as strong as the story says - the cleanest
  thumb split in the study.
- One of each class per squad, so someone loses theirs (13).
- A class weaker in one mode than another; the story mode leaving out the class system.
- Ranged enemies hitting you from anywhere while you are swarmed (5); an enemy a whole loadout cannot hit
  (2).
- The parry fails when you need it (9); melee forced on you (2).
- Health does not come back between fights (9).
- No way to replay one chapter; levels padded with lifts and walks (4).
- Finishing moves as a highlight (3), and repeating until stale.
- Controls that take hours to learn; an awkward default layout.
- You cannot start your own lobby; crossplay in one mode only.
- Paid content you still have to earn; updates that go to what the player did not come for; the studio
  does not play its own game; the game runs the machine hot.
- Old-fashioned and better for it (21); made for its fans (18); praised just for being a good game.

### For Dominion - our reading

1. **Lesson:** In a power-fantasy shooter, the moment you lose control is the moment you leave.
   **Evidence:** 64 say the game makes them feel superhumanly strong, 63 of them thumbs up; 13 say it never
   does, 12 of them thumbs down. The cause is always a stun, knock-down, grab or swarm: stuns take control
   away (9), the parry fails when needed (9). **Dominion could:** keep hard stuns on players rare and short;
   give a way to break out (a dodge or shove with a cooldown); never chain-stun a player; make the
   parry or block window forgiving; test every enemy grab against "can I still act?". **Strength:** strong.

2. **Lesson:** One enemy faction that is hard through clutter, not design, empties half the game.
   **Evidence:** the most-helpful review (632 helpful) says the Chaos missions are a tier harder "through
   clutter, misleading cues, flinching and stun-lock", players avoid them, and the map pool halves; the
   studio's own numbers show Tyranid kills over triple Chaos ones. The sample backs it 4 ways: unreadable
   threats (5), stuns (9), ranged enemies hitting you while swarmed (5), one part far harder (4).
   **Dominion could:** do a readability pass per enemy faction (clear warning signs before big hits,
   distinct looks for each enemy kind, less screen clutter); track how often each faction or map is
   picked and how often squads lose there; keep ranged enemies from hitting players who are locked in
   melee. **Strength:** medium (one review with huge votes, plus about 23 times in the sample).

3. **Lesson:** "Not enough game" can be the top complaint even from fans, and it comes back later.
   **Evidence:** 84 times, 66 from thumbs-up reviews, median 17 hours; six missions at launch; 7.6 per 100
   in late 2024, down to 1.9 as missions were added, back to 5.1 in 2026 ("thirteen missions after three
   years"). Repetitive: 37. Only 11 say it ends at the right time. **Dominion could:** plan how much there
   is to do at launch against how many hours a fan will play; use runs, mixed objectives and mixed
   arenas to stretch the same content; keep a steady flow of new maps or enemy sets after launch.
   **Strength:** strong.

4. **Lesson:** The launch month sets the score, so launch stability matters most.
   **Evidence:** the launch month holds 45.8% of all English reviews; the thumb was 88.5% then and 87.8%
   two years later. Crashes were 3.2 per 100 in the launch month and fixing them later did not move the
   thumb - it just changed what people complained about. **Dominion could:** spend on stability, loading
   and save safety before launch, not after. **Strength:** strong.

5. **Lesson:** A big update can break online play, so test networking after every update.
   **Evidence:** disconnects jumped to 3.4 per 100 in early 2025 after the April update (servers broken,
   stutter, a false anti-cheat kick, which reviewers say was fixed 2025-04-24), then fell to 0.0. "A patch
   made it worse": 16. **Dominion could:** run a host-migration and listen-server smoke test (host, join,
   leave, rejoin, finish a run) before every patch. **Strength:** medium.

6. **Lesson:** Locking classes to one per squad makes someone lose the class they love.
   **Evidence:** 13 times; it flips a review to thumbs down on its own (202744724); players asked for it off
   in the Siege mode (219488514). **Dominion could:** allow duplicate classes, or lock only in a mode where
   roles really matter, or make the lock optional in the lobby. With four players, more people
   compete for the same class. **Strength:** medium.

7. **Lesson:** Price complaints rise as the game ages, even when players like it.
   **Evidence:** "buy on sale only" rose from 0.5 to 5.1 per 100, 14 of 22 from recommenders; "the price is
   fair" fell from 1.3 to 0.0; "too expensive for what it is" steady at 28. **Dominion could:** set the
   launch price against hours of content, and plan sales and content drops together so value keeps up
   with age. **Strength:** medium.

8. **Lesson:** Letting players change their look turns a complaint into praise.
   **Evidence:** 10 asks to change their look (all before 2025-03, none in 2026); 13 thanks from 2025-03 on,
   rising to 3.6 per 100; "got my cape 10/10" (226907394); the shop selling only looks praised 11 times.
   But "paid content still has to be earned" and a paywalled colour were complaints. **Dominion could:**
   ship a real look-customisation system early; keep paid items looks-only; never make a bought item
   still need grinding; do not sell colours. **Strength:** medium.

9. **Lesson:** Grind complaints grow with hours.
   **Evidence:** "too grindy" 15 times, 0.7 per 100 at launch to 2.6 in 2026 as mean hours went from 29 to
   74; "unlocks feel good" fell from 1.2 to 0.0. Perk points and weapon mastery are named. **Dominion
   could:** check unlock speed for a 70-hour player, not just a 20-hour one. **Strength:** medium-weak.

10. **Lesson:** Bots must stay useful on hard difficulty if solo play uses them.
    **Evidence:** 10 say bots are useless in a fight, 2 say they help; "on higher levels the bots ... become
    dolts" (222211857). **Dominion could:** if bots fill empty slots, scale their skill with difficulty, or
    scale enemies down for smaller squads instead. **Strength:** medium-weak.

11. **Lesson:** The third-person camera must not shrink the view.
    **Evidence:** the dark edge that narrows the view, 9; slow-motion on every interaction ("it slows your
    perception, not the game", 206151338). **Dominion could:** keep screen effects for damage and
    low health light, and give a setting to turn them down. **Strength:** medium-weak.

12. **Lesson:** Dead time before every mission adds up.
    **Evidence:** the hub walk (2: "select a mission, see the captain, go to the armoury, go to the launch
    bay, and it repeats", 222215927); lifts and walks inside levels (4). **Dominion could:** let players
    start the next run from a menu or a short walk; cut lifts and walks inside arenas. **Strength:** weak.

13. **Lesson:** A slogan players can shout is free marketing and glue.
    **Evidence:** "For the Emperor" 310 times, one review in five; Helldivers 2 has the same with "for
    Democracy". **Dominion could:** give the world a short, sayable battle cry and put it in the game
    (voice lines, emotes, the end-of-run screen). **Strength:** medium (two games show it).

14. **Lesson:** Comparing to Gears of War is praise; an old-fashioned, complete game is welcome.
    **Evidence:** 31 reviews name Gears of War; old-fashioned and better for it 21; made for its fans 18.
    **Dominion could:** lean into weighty third-person shooting as a selling point. **Strength:** medium.

15. **Lesson:** A side mode that falls flat costs reviews.
    **Evidence:** one mode falls flat, 15 - almost always the PvP; "PvP dead" (224995971). **Dominion
    could:** stay with PvE and arenas rather than splitting work into PvP. **Strength:** medium-weak.

16. **Lesson:** Combat impact is easy to praise, but finishing moves need variety.
    **Evidence:** 45 say combat hits hard, only 6 weightless; finishing moves are a highlight (3) but also
    "repeat until stale" (built from this game). **Dominion could:** invest in hit feedback; if there are
    takedowns, make many and rotate them. **Strength:** weak-medium.

17. **Lesson:** Balance holes that players notice: dead weapons and health that does not refill.
    **Evidence:** some options are useless, 9; health does not come back between fights, 9. **Dominion
    could:** track weapon pick rates and buff dead picks; decide on purpose how health returns between
    arena waves or rooms and tell players. **Strength:** weak-medium.

18. **Lesson:** Loading times and lost progress are steady background complaints.
    **Evidence:** long loads 20; progress not saved 8. **Dominion could:** keep loads short between hub and
    run; save run rewards for every player when the run ends, even if the host drops. **Strength:**
    medium-weak.

19. **Lesson:** Let players start their own lobby.
    **Evidence:** "you cannot start your own lobby" was built from this game (count not given). **Dominion
    could:** a listen-server game naturally lets any player host; keep a "host my own" button next to
    quick match. **Strength:** weak.

20. **Lesson:** Silence on a known problem shows up in the top review.
    **Evidence:** "Saber has stayed silent despite overwhelming feedback" in the 632-vote review; "a studio
    that listens" was praised 11 times; patches that fixed what mattered 25. **Dominion could:** answer
    big community threads in public, even when the fix is not ready. **Strength:** weak-medium.

21. **Lesson:** Clutter makes it hard to track things; flashing light hurts some players.
    **Evidence:** two sightings toward a memory-and-attention problem (the story lost during combat,
    189003013; tracking on a cluttered screen, 196658368); flashing lights seen once. **Dominion could:**
    keep key story lines out of heavy fights; offer a reduce-flashing option. **Strength:** weak.

22. **Lesson:** A rich world makes people care - but Dominion has no licence to lean on.
    **Evidence:** story is 9.2% of everything said, the most in the study; world worth exploring 54; story
    worth following 74; but story thin or forgettable 18. **Dominion could:** do not expect the world to
    carry reviews the way a famous licence does; keep the setting clear and the story short and
    strong. **Strength:** weak for Dominion (the licence does the work here).

### Limits
- A 1.14% sample, ±2.60% on whole-sample shares, wider on any one month. 21 of the 25 months have fewer
  than 40 reviews; one review in 2026-09 moves that month by ten points. Read the five periods, not months.
- 591 of the 1,418 reviews are from the launch month and 921 from 2024; 2025 rests on 301 reviews and
  2026 on 196.
- 155 reviews (10.9%) were edited later and are counted on the day first written; here edits run both
  ways, so the error could go either way.
- No patch notes were pulled. The ultrawide fix, the April 2025 update and its fix, the weapon and parry
  changes and the 2026 Siege mode are what reviewers say.
- Whether the Chaos problem was fixed, or the players who cared left, cannot be told.
- Whether the PvP is dead cannot be counted from reviews.
- English only; about 100,000 reviews in other languages were not pulled.
- 19% of what was said was too vague to file; 363 of those are a bare thumbs up.
- Squad size, hosting, engine and how the game scales with players are not on the pages.
- The cross-game page gives a recomputed praise-to-complaint ratio of 1.87 : 1 (116.0 praise and 61.9
  complaints per 100); section 17 gives 1.92 : 1 from an earlier count. Use the recomputed one to compare
  games.

---

## ARC Raiders

**Read:** 1,473 of 251,588 English reviews (0.59%), ±2.69%, written October 2025 to September 2026.
**79.7% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam, all languages: 82.0%, Very Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/arc-raiders-english.md`, `findings/arc-raiders.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 16
(and section 21, which sets it beside Escape from Duckov).

**What it is:** A third-person sci-fi extraction shooter where players fight both the world's robot
machines (the ARC) and other players (PvPvE). Players drop in solo, as a duo or as a trio, loot, and
try to get out alive; gear you bring can be lost, and you can also go in with a free loadout. Matches
are filled by a matchmaker that sorts players by how they behaved in past matches (how often they
attacked other players) instead of by skill. There is an optional reset you can choose (it trades what
you built for a reward) and the space between runs is a menu, not a shared place. Made and published by
Embark Studios. Paid, $40. Released 2025-10-30. The pages do not name the engine or say who hosts a
match.

**How close to Dominion:** The closest game on Steam to Dominion's shape, the page says: third person,
sci-fi, extraction, runs that end, shooting robot enemies. The big difference is that other players can
shoot you here; Dominion is PvE, four players on the same side, hosted on one player's machine. Reviewers
mention playing as duos and trios; the pages do not state the largest squad. No arenas. A paid game with
no shop named on the pages.

### The short version (plain words)
The best thing and the worst thing about this game are the same thing: the other players. The most-said
specific praise is "not knowing who to trust is the thrill" (said 95 times, 6.4 per 100 reviews), and
five of the top eight complaints are about other players: no mode without them (55), griefers (42), the
one who says "friendly" and then shoots you in the back (38), cheaters (38), and players who camp the
exit (27). Over eleven months the thumb fell from 86% to 52%, and what rose was cheating: from 0.9 to
12.4 per 100 reviews, with "the studio does nothing about the cheaters" close behind (0.2 to 7.1).
Design complaints fell over the same time. The game's answer to all this - a matchmaker that sorts
players by behaviour - is praised 23 times and attacked in five different ways.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are times it was said (one review can say more than one thing). Per 100 = per 100 reviews read.
The year columns on the page are per 100 reviews in 2025 (896 reviews) and 2026 (577).

1. **Not knowing who to trust is the thrill** - 95 times (6.4 per 100). It does not fade: 5.7, 7.5, 7.4,
   4.4 per 100 across the four periods. "Be careful who you trust"; "gave me permanent trust issues";
   "I love being a rat - 10/10"; "met a lot of cool people and betrayed a lot of people too."
2. **It keeps pulling you back** - 65 times (4.4 per 100).
3. **Strangers team up instead of fighting** - 41 times (2.8 per 100). Highest at launch (4.1 per 100),
   down to 0.9 by mid-2026.
4. **Best game of its kind** - 37 times (2.5 per 100); 3.1 in 2025, 1.6 in 2026.
5. **The stakes are worth the risk** - 32 times (2.2 per 100); 3.0 in 2025, 0.9 in 2026. "A successful
   extraction is a real adrenaline rush" (209802131, 87 helpful, the most-helpful review in the sample).
6. **The runs turn into stories you retell** - 26 times (1.8 per 100). "Incredibly cinematic - players,
   ARC machines and chaos everywhere, like scenes from a sci-fi movie" (220132764).
7. **The enemy AI behaves well** - 25 times (1.7 per 100); 2.3 in 2025, 0.7 in 2026. "Robots that move
   like nothing he has seen" (223339137).
8. **Much better with friends** - 25 times (1.7 per 100).
9. **Easy to start** - 25 times (1.7 per 100), rising to 2.4 in 2026. 25 people say this was their first
   extraction shooter and it took them.
10. **The matchmaker sorts you by how you play, and it works** - 23 times (1.6 per 100). The only praise
    that rises: 1.0 in 2025, 2.4 in 2026 (0.8, 2.0, 2.5, 2.7 by period). "If you are stuck in lobbies
    that do not fit your playstyle, rate the match poorly and the aggression-based matchmaking changes who
    you play against" (220131270).
11. **The mood draws you in** - 22 times (1.5 per 100); 2.1 in 2025, 0.5 in 2026.
12. **It runs well** - 18 times (1.2 per 100); 1.7 in 2025, 0.5 in 2026.
13. **Punchy sound** - 17 times (1.2 per 100); 1.9 in 2025, 0.0 in 2026.
14. **A welcoming community** - 17 times (1.2 per 100).
15. **It won over someone who avoids this kind of game** - 15 times (1.0 per 100); 1.7 in 2025, 0.0 in
    2026. The cross-game page gives 16 at 1.4 per 100 on a later count, second only to Remnant II.
16. **Unlocking things feels good** - 14 times (1.0 per 100); 1.6 in 2025, 0.0 in 2026.
17. **A good choice of modes** - 14 times (1.0 per 100).
18. **The price is fair** - 12 times (0.8 per 100).
19. **Combat hits hard** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
20. **Plenty to do** - 8 times (0.5 per 100).
21. **A shared threat turns strangers into allies** - 8 times (0.5 per 100). Built for this game.
22. **It works solo** - 7 times (0.5 per 100).
23. **Made with care** - 7 times (0.5 per 100).
24. **Dying costs little and you are back fast** - 7 times (0.5 per 100).
25. **Hard in a good way** - 7 times (0.5 per 100).

The launch-window praise - the sound, the unlocks, winning over newcomers - goes to zero in 2026, when
the average reviewer has 150 to 300 hours. Also: 48 reviews explain the game by naming another one -
Tarkov in 19, then Fortnite 7, Helldivers 2 5, The Division 4.

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are times it was said. Per 100 = per 100 reviews read.

1. **The mode I expected is missing** - 55 times (3.7 per 100); 42 of the 55 ask for a PvE-only mode.
   Rising: 2.7, 2.9, 6.2, 8.0 per 100 across the four periods - it triples, and by the last period it is
   the most-said complaint after cheating.
2. **Trolls and griefers** - 42 times (2.9 per 100); 1.6 in 2025, 4.9 in 2026 (5.8 per 100 in Feb-Apr
   2026).
3. **Says "friendly" then shoots you in the back** - 38 times (2.6 per 100). **21 of the 38 are thumbs
   down.** "I was sharing blueprints and healing a man, then hunted and gunned down for three matches
   straight" (223339137, 7 helpful). Peaks at 4.1 per 100 in Feb-Apr 2026, then 0.9 - the page reads this
   as those reviewers leaving or moving on to cheaters, not the betrayal stopping.
4. **Cheaters spoil matches** - 38 times (2.6 per 100). The finding of the page: 0.9, 2.4, 2.9, 12.4 per
   100 across the four periods - fourteen-fold in eleven months. "In the beginning it was good, but now
   there are a lot of cheaters" (226905811, 30 helpful, 936 hours). Reviewers say cheat accounts sell for
   "fifty cents to four dollars" (221530335).
5. **Thumbs down with no reason given** - 33 times (2.2 per 100).
6. **Players camp the exit** - 27 times (1.8 per 100), steady.
7. **The studio does nothing about the cheaters** - 22 times (1.5 per 100); 0.2, 1.5, 2.5, 7.1 per 100 by
   period. "They refuse to put an anticheat and only ban a small fraction" (234982159, 887 hours) - the
   reviewer's claim.
8. **The players turned hostile after launch** - 22 times (1.5 per 100). Shows 2.1 in 2025 and 0.5 in
   2026, but the page says this reads backwards: most were written at launch and edited later.
9. **Nothing left to chase** - everything is unlocked and no new goal is set - 18 times (1.2 per 100). The
   first complaint about the game's own systems.
10. **Dropped into a match already underway** - 13 times (0.9 per 100).
11. **A third player swoops on your fight** - 13 times (0.9 per 100); 1.1 in 2025, 0.5 in 2026.
12. **The design sets players against each other** - 13 times (0.9 per 100); 1.3 in 2025, 0.2 in 2026.
13. **Updates come too slowly** - 12 times (0.8 per 100); 0.4 in 2025, 1.4 in 2026.
14. **Crashes again and again** - 11 times (0.7 per 100). Row 14 here; row 5 in Helldivers 2.
15. **Dying costs nothing** - 11 times (0.7 per 100). "Nobody tries to progress, the group always runs
    free loadouts, looting loses its appeal" (219490113). 1.0 in 2025, 0.3 in 2026.
16. **The praise is undeserved** - the game is not as good as its name - 10 times (0.7 per 100).
17. **Lag and desync** - 10 times (0.7 per 100); 1.0 in 2025, 0.2 in 2026.
18. **Too grindy** - 10 times (0.7 per 100).
19. **An attack you cannot dodge, block or answer** - 9 times (0.6 per 100); 0.9 in 2025, 0.2 in 2026.
20. **The studio weakened things players liked** - 8 times (0.5 per 100). (The page files some loot and
    drop-rate complaints here because the study has no loot heading yet.)
21. **Failing costs a lot of progress or time** - 8 times (0.5 per 100).
22. **One option beats all the rest** - 8 times (0.5 per 100).
23. **Buggy** - 8 times (0.5 per 100).
24. **Wants a price on attacking other players** - a karma flag, a bounty, a penalty - 8 times (0.5 per
    100).
25. **Too afraid to use the good gear** - 8 times (0.5 per 100). "Why would he risk a Bobcat when he can
    lose a Stitcher for a quarter of the price" (223877027). Built for this game.
26. **An exploit ruins the game** - 8 times; 0.1 per 100 in 2025, 1.2 in 2026. The duplication bug and an
    unprotected API that let anyone "mass-duplicate loot with a single request" (reviewers' account).
27. **Solo is friendly and squads shoot on sight** - 12 times (filed as neutral). "Solo you can find a
    friendly lobby... duo and trio are instantly kill-on-sight even for a non-PvP player - do not bother
    playing with friends, family or kids" (223340527). The page: team size predicts other players'
    behaviour better than the matchmaker does.
28. **The player count is falling** - 0.0, 0.0, 0.8, 1.8 per 100 by period.
29. **Wants to see a hostile player before they strike** - 2 times.
30. **Wants a private base where progress shows** - 3 sightings, left open.

**The matchmaker's five failures** (all built from this game):
- **Shooting back once sorts you with the killers** - 7. "500 hours with almost no PvP beyond
  self-defence, and one kill puts you instantly in kill-on-sight lobbies" (223340527, 9 helpful).
- **The fighters' lobbies are where the cheaters are** - 6. Playing PvP means playing cheaters.
- **Peaceful players still land with the killers** - 5. "You will only be put in PVE lobby's if you do
  not do any PVP, well that's a lie" (232925246).
- **The sorting used to work and no longer does** - 5. "Something has changed recently. Matchmaking no
  longer works" (227450454, 556 hours, 8 helpful). Five reviewers tie this to the falling player count.
- **Killers play nice to get sorted in with the peaceful** - 2. "People abuse it to get put in easy,
  friendlier lobbies just to shoot friendly people in the back of the head" (220786008).
- Plus **wants players sorted by how they play** - 4, from reviewers who did not know it existed or did
  not believe it did.

**Also built from this game, with no count on the pages:** smaller teams matched against full squads; the
gear gap decides the fight; whoever shoots first wins; the PvP feels bolted onto a PvE game; the optional
reset is not worth what it costs; players spawn on top of each other; looting, reviving or healing locks
you in an animation and that is when you die; managing the inventory is a chore; what a cheater took is
never given back; support replied with a form letter; no shared place between runs; the studio wiped
what I had earned; banned with no reason given; you get stuck on the scenery; the voices are made by AI
and it shows (four voices); you cannot tell if a sound is above or below you; the game's rules reward the
opposite of what its story says; abuse and slurs over voice chat. **Seen once each, left open:** the
third-person camera and peeking round corners, players lying in wait at quest spots, one region getting
content first (a China-first release, 228603070), a frame rate that locks until you switch windows.

**Who could have stopped these?** Of the top 25 complaints: the other players' conduct, 180 times; game
design and production, 148 (about half of it "give me a mode without other players" and the fights; take
the other players out and design falls to about 70); engineering (matchmaking, crashes, lag, bugs), 42;
the studio's response and reputation, 32; live-ops, 20; said nothing specific, 33. In Helldivers 2 the
top 25 were led by live-ops and publisher decisions; in Deep Rock Galactic by the game itself.

### How it changed over time

| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 | Praise to complaint | Mean hours played |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Oct-Nov 2025 | 661 | 85.8% | 100.3 | 46.3 | 2.2 : 1 | 58 |
| Dec 2025-Jan 2026 | 456 | 81.1% | 79.6 | 50.4 | 1.6 : 1 | 89 |
| Feb-Apr 2026 | 243 | 73.3% | 75.7 | 67.1 | 1.1 : 1 | 140 |
| May-Sep 2026 | 113 | 52.2% | 59.3 | 112.4 | 1 : 1.9 | 282 |

- **The line bends in April 2026.** Thumbs up drops from 76% to 64%; complaints pass praise for the first
  time (88 against 78 per 100); the mean reviewer has 175 hours; cheating becomes a top-three complaint.
  From May 2026 on, complaints per 100 are above 100 every month. June 2026 is the low: 48.0% thumbs up.
- **Rose:** cheaters (0.9 to 12.4); the studio does nothing about cheaters (0.2 to 7.1); the missing PvE
  mode (2.7 to 8.0); griefers (1.7 to 5.3, peak 5.8); the player count is falling (0.0 to 1.8); exploits
  (0.1 to 1.2 per 100 by year); updates too slow (0.4 to 1.4 by year); the matchmaker works (0.8 to 2.7).
- **Fell:** design complaints - the third player swooping (1.1 to 0.5), the design sets players against
  each other (1.3 to 0.2), dying costs nothing (1.0 to 0.3), lag (1.0 to 0.2), no counterplay (0.9 to
  0.2); strangers team up (4.1 to 0.9); stakes worth the risk (3.0 to 0.9 by year); the AI behaves well
  (2.3 to 0.7); sound, unlocks and "won me over" to 0.0.
- **"The 2026 reviewer has stopped arguing with the design and started arguing with the lobby."**
- **The reviewer changes.** Mean hours at review go 58, 105, 175, 293 across the year. "A November review
  is written after a week; a June review after a season."
- **Volume:** 62.8% of all English reviews came in the first three months (157,893 of 251,588), 42% in
  November 2025 alone. Steam's Very Positive is the launch, and will stay the launch for years. The 50%
  thumb in 2026-09 is 811 people on Steam.
- **Events reviewers name:** a 2026-03-31 balance patch ("too hard for solo players just starting out",
  222215525), the duplication exploit, and a currency wipe around April 2026 that took honest players'
  currency along with the duplicated (223877518, 230568877). No patch notes were pulled; none of this is
  confirmed from the studio's side.
- **Edits:** 172 reviews (11.7%) were edited later but are counted on the day first written - mostly
  launch reviews rewritten to say "now full of cheaters". So the cheating rise is understated, not
  overstated.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends:** much better with friends, 25 (1.7 per 100). But "duo and trio are instantly kill-on-sight
  ... do not bother playing with friends, family or kids" (223340527): solo is friendly and squads shoot
  on sight, 12.
- **Strangers:** the heart of the game. One review in five (305 of 1,473) talks about how strangers
  behaved. 147 reviews carry only praise about other players, 158 only complaints, 11 both - two groups
  of people who each think they are describing the game. The page puts the ratio at about 1 : 1 and
  falling. Player conduct is 360 bullets, 15.7% of everything said.
- **Praise:** not knowing who to trust (95), strangers team up (41), a shared threat turns strangers into
  allies (8), welcoming community (17).
- **Complaints:** griefers (42), betrayal (38), cheaters (38), exit campers (27), third-party swoops (13),
  the players turned hostile after launch (22), abuse over voice chat (no count).
- **Matchmaking:** sorted by behaviour (23 praise, five failure shapes, see complaints); dropped into a
  match already underway (13); smaller teams matched against full squads (no count); players spawn on top
  of each other (no count).
- **Lag and desync:** 10, falling. **Disconnects** are not on the lists.
- **Solo:** works solo 7; a reviewer says the 2026-03-31 patch made it "too hard for solo players just
  starting out" (222215525).
- **Social space:** no shared place between runs; wants a private base (3 sightings).
- **Regions:** the review text mentions Oceania lobbies and a China-first content release (228603070).
- **Cheaters and the studio:** see the studio section.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Combat hits hard:** 11. **Punchy sound:** 17, all in 2025.
- **PvP feel:** whoever shoots first wins; the gear gap decides the fight (both built here, no counts).
- **Locked in an animation:** looting, reviving or healing freezes you, and that is when you die (no
  count).
- **No counterplay:** 9 say an attack cannot be dodged, blocked or answered.
- **Sound direction:** you cannot tell above from below (no count).
- **Getting stuck on scenery** (no count). **Third-person camera and corner-peeking:** seen once.
- **Inventory:** managing it is a chore (no count).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **The robot AI is praised:** 25 (2.3 per 100 in 2025, 0.7 in 2026). "Robots that move like nothing he
  has seen" (223339137).
- **The shared threat is what makes strangers cooperate** (8).
- **Runs that become stories:** 26 - "players, ARC machines and chaos everywhere".
- **No counterplay:** 9, falling to 0.2 per 100 in 2026.
- **Hard in a good way:** 7. **One option beats the rest:** 8.
- **Difficulty change:** a reviewer says the 2026-03-31 patch made it too hard for new solo players
  (222215525).
- The pages name no bosses.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Loot is the core, but the study has no heading for it yet** (gap 331, Rico's call); loot rooms,
  blueprint drop rates and the duplication economy are filed elsewhere. "This game is where the evidence
  lives."
- **Nothing left to chase:** 18, rising (1.0 to 1.6 per 100 by year).
- **Unlocking feels good:** 14, gone in 2026. **Grindy:** 10.
- **The optional reset is not worth what it costs** (no count).
- **Gear risk, both ways:** too afraid to use the good gear, 8; dying costs nothing, 11 (free loadouts mean
  "nobody tries to progress"). "Eleven people say death is too cheap and eight say gear is too precious
  to use - both in a game with one punishment model." Failing costs too much: 8. Dying costs little and
  you are back fast: 7. Stakes worth the risk: 32.
- **Wipes:** the studio wiped what I had earned (built here; the currency wipe around April 2026, as
  reviewers tell it). What a cheater took is never given back (no count).
- **The rules contradict the story:** the game rewards the opposite of what its fiction says you are
  there to do (no count).

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Keeps pulling you back:** 65 (4.8 per 100 in 2025, 3.8 in 2026).
- **Runs become stories you retell:** 26. **Stakes worth the risk:** 32.
- **Nothing left to chase:** 18. **Plenty to do:** 8. **A good choice of modes:** 14.
- **Updates too slow:** 12, rising.
- **The missing PvE mode:** 55, 42 of them asking for PvE. The cross-game page: the two extraction games
  read so far each ask for the other's format - ARC Raiders players want PvE, Escape from Duckov (PvE
  alone) players want co-op. "No PvP is a feature" is said 51 times in Escape from Duckov and 0 times
  here.
- **Easy to start:** 25 - first extraction shooter for many.

### Money and price
- Paid, $40. **The price is fair:** 12 (0.8 per 100 both years).
- Publishing is only 1.7% of everything said. The pages name no shop complaints.
- Cheat accounts at "fifty cents to four dollars" (221530335, the reviewer's claim) - cheap accounts make
  bans cheap to dodge.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is 7.9% of everything said, and "this game's engineering is not what people argue about".
- **Runs well:** 18 (1.7 per 100 in 2025, 0.5 in 2026).
- **Crashes:** 11. **Lag and desync:** 10, falling to 0.2. **Buggy:** 8.
- **Exploits:** 8, rising in 2026 - duplication and an unprotected API (reviewers' account).
- **Stuck on scenery; banned with no reason; a frame rate that locks until you switch windows** (no
  counts or seen once).
- No patch notes were pulled. The engine is not named on the pages.

### The studio, updates and community
- **Cheating is the studio's test, and players say it failed.** "Does nothing about the cheaters" 22,
  rising to 7.1 per 100; "what a cheater took is never given back"; "support replied with a form letter";
  "banned with no reason given". "They refuse to put an anticheat and only ban a small fraction"
  (234982159) - the reviewer's claim.
- **The wipe:** reviewers say a currency wipe after the duplication exploit also took honest players'
  currency (223877518, 230568877).
- **Weakened what players liked:** 8. **Updates too slow:** 12.
- **Live-ops** is only 3.2% of everything said (Helldivers 2: 7.7%) - patches are not the argument here.
- **Reputation:** best of its kind 37; the praise is undeserved 10; won over someone who avoids the genre
  15.
- **AI voices:** four voices; "the voices are made by AI and it shows" (built here). An ethics complaint
  about AI voices is left open.
- **Embark is both studio and publisher**, so there is no studio-versus-publisher split here.
- **The other players are the community:** player conduct is 78% of the community division.
- **Wordless thumbs up:** 591 reviews, 40% of the sample - the highest in the study.

### What players asked for
- **A PvE-only mode** - 42 of the 55 "expected mode missing", rising to 8.0 per 100.
- **Real anti-cheat and bans** - "does nothing about the cheaters" 22.
- **A price on attacking other players** - a karma flag, a bounty, a penalty - 8.
- **A way to see a hostile player before they strike** - 2.
- **Players sorted by how they play** - 4 (not knowing the matchmaker already does it).
- **Something left to chase** - 18.
- **Faster updates** - 12.
- **A private base / a shared place between runs** - 3 sightings, and the "no shared place" label.
- **Lost items given back after cheating** (no count).

### Only in this game
37 of the 279 labels used here appear in no other game, and 33 of those 37 are about the other players or
the matchmaker. 38 labels were built in this run - the most of any game. Before this game the study had
no way to say a stranger could shoot you. The ones that matter for Dominion:
- The behaviour-sorting matchmaker: works (23), and five ways it fails.
- Too afraid to use the good gear (8).
- Looting, reviving or healing locks you in an animation and that is when you die.
- Players spawn on top of each other; dropped into a match underway (13); smaller teams matched against
  full squads.
- A shared threat turns strangers into allies (8).
- The runs turn into stories you retell (26).
- Managing the inventory is a chore.
- No shared place between runs.
- The optional reset is not worth what it costs.
- The studio wiped what I had earned; what a cheater took is never given back.
- You cannot tell above from below by sound.
- The voices are made by AI and it shows.
