- The game's rules reward the opposite of its story.

### For Dominion - our reading

1. **Lesson:** Players who come for extraction tension often do not want other players shooting them.
   **Evidence:** the top complaint is the missing mode (55), 42 asking for PvE, tripling to 8.0 per 100 by
   the last period; five of the top eight complaints are about other players; Escape from Duckov players
   praise having no PvP 51 times. **Dominion could:** treat being PvE-only as a selling point and say it
   plainly on the store page; get the risk and tension from the world and the clock, not from other
   squads. **Strength:** strong.

2. **Lesson:** Cheating kills a game slowly, and "the studio does nothing" follows it.
   **Evidence:** cheaters 0.9 to 12.4 per 100 in eleven months, the thumb 86% to 52%; "does nothing"
   0.2 to 7.1; exploits 0.1 to 1.2; the wipe that hit honest players. **Dominion could:** in PvE on a
   listen server, cheating mostly hurts the host's own squad - but any shared progress, trading or
   leaderboards need server-side checks; never trust the client for item grants; if a fix wipes items,
   protect players who did nothing wrong. **Strength:** strong (for anything shared between players).

3. **Lesson:** Gear risk must sit in the middle: too cheap and nothing matters, too dear and nobody uses it.
   **Evidence:** dying costs nothing 11; too afraid to use the good gear 8; stakes worth the risk 32 (the
   most-helpful review: "a successful extraction is a real adrenaline rush"); failing costs too much 8;
   quick recovery praised 7. **Dominion could:** let players lose some of what they carry, but keep a
   safety net (an insured slot, a cheap standard kit that still feels good, rewards for using good gear);
   test how often players actually bring their best gear. **Strength:** medium-strong.

4. **Lesson:** Runs that turn into stories are the praise to aim for.
   **Evidence:** runs become stories you retell 26; keeps pulling you back 65; "like scenes from a sci-fi
   movie" (220132764). **Dominion could:** build runs around surprises (a big machine arrives, a squad
   member goes down at the exit) and make it easy to clip and share. **Strength:** medium.

5. **Lesson:** Smart, readable robot enemies win praise, and a shared threat bonds players.
   **Evidence:** good AI 25; "robots that move like nothing he has seen"; a shared threat turns strangers
   into allies 8. No counterplay complaints 9. **Dominion could:** invest in enemy movement and behaviour;
   give every big attack a way to dodge, block or answer it. **Strength:** medium.

6. **Lesson:** Do not lock players in long animations when danger is near.
   **Evidence:** "interacting freezes you and that is when you die" built from this game (no count).
   **Dominion could:** let players cancel looting, reviving and healing, or make them short; give a squad
   mate a way to cover. **Strength:** weak (no count), but cheap to act on.

7. **Lesson:** Joining a match in progress and spawning beside others both annoy.
   **Evidence:** dropped into a match already underway 13; players spawn on top of each other (no count);
   smaller teams matched against full squads (no count). **Dominion could:** if late joining exists, tell
   the joiner how far along the run is and give them a fair start; spread spawns; scale enemies to squad
   size. **Strength:** medium-weak.

8. **Lesson:** Launch praise fades as reviewers get hundreds of hours; plan goals for the long tail.
   **Evidence:** mean hours went 58 to 293; sound, unlocks and "won me over" fell to 0.0 in 2026; nothing
   left to chase 18 and rising; updates too slow 12 and rising. **Dominion could:** plan end-game goals
   and a steady update pace before launch, aimed at the 200-hour player. **Strength:** medium.

9. **Lesson:** Easy to start brings in new players.
   **Evidence:** easy to start 25, rising to 2.4 per 100; won over someone who avoids the genre 15; many
   say it was their first extraction shooter. **Dominion could:** keep the first runs simple and safe,
   teach extraction step by step. **Strength:** medium.

10. **Lesson:** A system that sorts players needs to be clear and hard to game.
    **Evidence:** the behaviour matchmaker: works 23; misreads self-defence 7; puts cheaters with fighters
    6; peaceful players still meet killers 5; stopped working as the player count fell 5; gamed by killers
    2; 4 did not know it existed. **Dominion could:** for public PvE matchmaking, keep any sorting
    (skill, play style, voice chat) simple, shown to the player, and safe at low player counts.
    **Strength:** medium.

11. **Lesson:** Sell the game's community feel early; strangers teaming up is a launch effect.
    **Evidence:** strangers team up 4.1 per 100 at launch, 0.9 by mid-2026; welcoming community 17;
    much better with friends 25. **Dominion could:** reward strangers for helping (shared rewards for
    revives, emotes, pings). **Strength:** medium-weak.

12. **Lesson:** Inventory and between-run spaces matter.
    **Evidence:** inventory is a chore; no shared place between runs; a private base asked for (3).
    **Dominion could:** keep inventory quick with sorting and bulk actions; consider a hub where the squad
    meets between runs. **Strength:** weak.

13. **Lesson:** Sound must tell up from down in 3D spaces.
    **Evidence:** cannot tell above from below (built here, no count); punchy sound praised 17 at launch.
    **Dominion could:** test sound positioning on multi-level maps. **Strength:** weak.

14. **Lesson:** AI-made voices get noticed.
    **Evidence:** "the voices are made by AI and it shows" (four voices, built here); an ethics complaint
    left open. **Dominion could:** use recorded actors for key voices, or expect the comment. **Strength:**
    weak.

15. **Lesson:** Support and bans must explain themselves.
    **Evidence:** support replied with a form letter; banned with no reason given; what a cheater took is
    never given back (all built here). **Dominion could:** give real replies and a ban reason; restore
    items lost to bugs or cheats. **Strength:** weak.

16. **Lesson:** The fiction and the rules should agree.
    **Evidence:** "the rules contradict the story" built here. **Dominion could:** make what earns rewards
    match what the story says the squad is there to do. **Strength:** weak.

17. **Lesson:** A strong launch sets the score for years.
    **Evidence:** 62.8% of English reviews in the first three months; Steam's Very Positive "is the launch,
    and it will stay the launch for years" even as the late thumb fell to about 50%. **Dominion could:**
    put the most polish into the first three months. **Strength:** strong.

### Limits
- A 0.59% sample, ±2.69% on whole-sample shares, wider on any month. The last five months are 113 reviews
  together; one review moves 2026-09 by ten points. They are reported only because all five point the
  same way.
- The launch month is two days and 19 reviews; November 2025 is the launch month in practice.
- 172 reviews (11.7%) are counted on the day first written, not last edited; mostly negative edits landing
  on 2025-11, so the cheating rise is understated.
- No patch notes were pulled: the 2026-03-31 patch, the duplication exploit and the currency wipe are what
  reviewers say.
- Whether the matchmaker broke or the player count fell below what it needs cannot be told apart.
- English only; about 164,000 reviews in other languages were not pulled (Oceania lobbies, a China-first
  release).
- 31% of what was said was too vague to file; 591 reviews are a bare thumbs up.
- All six matchmaker labels rest on this one game.
- Section 16 gives praise to complaint as 1.55 : 1; the later recomputed table gives 1.53 : 1 (86.8 praise
  and 56.7 complaints per 100). The "won over someone who avoids the genre" count is 15 on the game pages
  and 16 on the cross-game page.
- Engine, hosting and squad size are not stated on the pages (the duo and trio quote and the label for
  "full three-player squads" imply three).

---

## Immortal: Unchained

**Read:** 435 of 435 English reviews (100%, every review - so there is no margin of error), written
September 2018 to August 2026 (all 77 months). **64.6% thumbs up** (281 up, 154 down; Steam's own
English count is the same) (Steam, all languages: Mixed, 890 reviews).
**Pages:** `findings/immortal-unchained-english.md`, `findings/immortal-unchained.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 14.

**What it is:** A single-player "soulslike" third-person shooter from 2018: hard, lock-on combat in
the style of Dark Souls, but with guns. Players level and customise weapons. The world is
built from large, linked areas ("biomes") full of paths and secrets. Paid, finished, and no longer
updated: no seasons, no roadmap, no shop. No co-op, no online play. Engine not named on the pages.
It is in the study as a test: does a way of sorting reviews built from co-op shooters still work on a
single-player game? (It did.)

**How close to Dominion:** Half close. It shares the third-person camera, shooting as the main
action, enemies with weak points, builds and weapon levelling, and a hard,
skill-based tone. It does not share co-op, hosting, runs, extraction or arenas: it is one player,
one long campaign. Its lessons for Dominion are about how a third-person shooter feels in the hands,
how aim help and weak points fight each other, what players forgive, and what they do not. It is a
middling game, not a disaster, but many of its lessons are "what went wrong".

### The short version (plain words)
People who like this game still list its faults: a thumbs-up reviewer writes 1.53 complaints on
average, and 45.5% of all complaints (431 of 948) come from people who recommend it. They forgive
faults about numbers - bad balance, one weapon that beats the rest, the price. They do not forgive
the game taking control away from them: stuns, slow weapon handling, attacks you cannot answer. The
level design is the most-praised thing (46 times), and hard-but-fair difficulty is next (39). The
lock-on, borrowed from sword games, grabs the enemy's body instead of its weak point, and that
clashes with shooting. Nobody can describe the game without naming another game (76 times).

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The levels are well built** - 46 times. Praised by both camps. Even a review that calls the game
   *"an exercise in frustration"* says the areas are *"multi-levelled, sprawling areas full of
   criss-crossing paths and secrets to discover"* (233349028). Another game's page sums it up: this
   game was praised for its levels.
2. **Hard in a way that feels good** - 39 times.
3. **Many builds to try** - 31 times.
4. **Nothing else plays like it** - 27 times. *"If you want a soulslike with guns, this is it. There is
   no other option."* (218154663)
5. **The mood pulls you in** - 25 times.
6. **It gets good once you learn it** - 24 times. Most often the lock-on, or how to level a weapon.
   *"This game became enjoyable after I started using lock-on. I hated the game until I learned this
   trick."* (78807510)
7. **The studio listens and acts** - 9 times.
8. Praise overall: 579 positive things said, against 948 negative and 254 neutral.

### What players complained about, most to least
"Recommenders" means the share written by people who gave a thumbs up (the people who stayed).
1. **Costs too much for what it is** - 38 times. **63% from recommenders.**
2. **Enemies appear with no chance to react** - 30 times. 37% from recommenders: mostly people who
   left.
3. **Some choices are never worth taking** - 26 times. **73% from recommenders.**
4. **One choice beats all the others** - 25 times. **72% from recommenders.**
5. **Another named game does it better** - 25 times. *"Poor mans remnant... don't bother"*
   (171161481). (Remnant is the game reviewers name.)
6. **Weapon handling is slow** (swapping, reloading, readying) - 23 times. **30% from recommenders:**
   mostly people who left.
7. **Every enemy is fought the same way** - 18 times: *"every enemy has a head and a back"*.
8. **Buggy** - 18 times. 56% from recommenders.
9. **The jump between difficulty levels is wrong** - 17 times. 59% from recommenders.
10. **Attacks you cannot dodge, block or answer** - 16 times. **25% from recommenders:** mostly people
    who left.
11. **Shots go where they want** (spread beats aim) - 14 times.
12. **Hits stun you and take control away** - 13 times. **31% from recommenders:** mostly people who
    left.
13. **Moving feels slow and heavy** - 10 times (from the cross-game page, which lists it beside
    Gunfire Reborn at 15 and DRG: Rogue Core at 11 as the commonest movement complaint).
14. **One setting causes the slowdown** - 6 times (players found the single graphics option behind
    the poor frame rate).
15. **The lock-on holds the body, not the weak point** - 4 times.
16. **The camera gets in the way** - 1 time.
17. **A shot that should pass is stopped by the scenery** - 1 time.
18. **The game makes you remember things with no text to help** - 1 review (an accessibility point,
    filed with a later game's matching review).
19. **You cannot skip what the game plays at you** (scenes you must sit through) - found while
    reading this game; four sightings across two games, this game's share not given.

**The dividing line is control.** Faults about numbers (balance, a top weapon, price) are mostly
written by people who still recommend the game. Faults about the game taking the controller away
(stuns, slow handling, no counterplay, unfair spawns) are mostly written by people who do not.

### How it changed over time
Thumbs up by the year the review was written:

| Year | Up | Down | Reviews | % up |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2018 | 118 | 45 | 163 | 72.4% |
| 2019 | 69 | 33 | 102 | 67.6% |
| 2020 | 16 | 19 | 35 | 45.7% |
| 2021 | 16 | 11 | 27 | 59.3% |
| 2022 | 25 | 11 | 36 | 69.4% |
| 2023 | 25 | 16 | 41 | 61.0% |
| 2024 | 4 | 10 | 14 | 28.6% |
| 2025 | 5 | 4 | 9 | 55.6% |
| 2026 | 3 | 5 | 8 | 37.5% |

- **61% of every English review came in the first sixteen months** (163 in 2018, 102 in 2019). The
  review rate never came back.
- **Do not read a trend into 2024-2026**: only 31 reviews across three years. 2024 is four up and ten
  down; one more review either way moves it eight points.
- **The score is flat because the game is finished.** Talk about updates is 0.5% of everything said,
  against 4.1% in the co-op games. No patches, seasons or roadmap to argue about. Only who wanders in
  changes.
- 58 reviews (13.3%) were edited after posting.

### Co-op and online play
None: it is a single-player game. The pages show what that does: talk about other players and the
community is 0.8% of everything said, against 13.6% in the eight co-op games. 12 whole topics that
co-op games fill (co-op design, bots, solo play, roles, friends, player behaviour, population,
matchmaking, servers, netcode, crossplay, social features) were never mentioned. The pages do not
record players asking for co-op.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Guns and souls-style lock-on clash.** Lock-on comes from sword games, where the enemy is one
  target. Put it in a shooter with weak points and the aim help fights the player.
  - Lock-on holds the body, not the weak point: 4 times. *"when you lock on, your reticule becomes
    suddenly glued to the enemy's torso, making targeting the weak spots nigh on impossible"*
    (233349028). *"Lock on does not autotarget weak points"* (45153643).
  - Shots go where they want: 14 times.
  - Every enemy fought the same way (head and back): 18 times.
  - 39 word hits for "lock-on" and "aim assist" across the whole study; 22 are this game.
- **Loss of control is what makes people quit.** Slow weapon handling 23 (30% recommenders); stuns
  take control away 13 (31%); no counterplay 16 (25%).
- **Moving feels slow and heavy**: 10 times.
- **Learning it pays off**: 24 say the game became good once they learned something, most often the
  lock-on (78807510).
- **The camera gets in the way**: 1 time. **The scenery swallows a shot**: 1 time (a third-person
  camera fault later seen in other games).
- **The spaces are too small** for the game's own moves (no room to dodge or use the camera) - first
  named in this game; no count on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Hard in a good way**: 39 times. **Gets good once learned**: 24 times.
- **Unfair spawns** (enemies appear with no chance to react): 30 times, mostly from people who left.
- **Attacks with no answer**: 16 times, 25% from recommenders.
- **Every enemy fought the same way**: 18 times.
- **The jump between difficulty levels is wrong**: 17 times, 59% from recommenders.
- One review says the player used a cheat to get past a hard part (one of two such reviews in the
  study).
- Bosses are not counted on the pages.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Many builds to try**: 31 times.
- **Some choices are useless**: 26 (73% recommenders). **One choice dominates**: 25 (72%
  recommenders). Players forgive these, but they are among the most-said complaints.
- **Levelling a weapon** is one of the things players say they had to learn before the game clicked.
- The game does not show what is left to earn: a complaint first named for this game (no count on the
  Immortal page; the Remnant II page says the idea was built here).
- Loot luck and grind are not counted on the pages.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Well-built levels**: 46, the top praise; sprawling, layered, full of secrets.
- **The spaces can be too tight** for dodging and camera (first named here, no count).
- **Players asked for a checklist of secrets** (the Remnant II section of the cross-game page says
  this game "had the checklist ask"; no count).
- No counts on length or repeat play.

### Money and price
- **Costs too much for what it is**: 38 times, the fourth most-said thing, and **63% of it comes from
  people who still recommend it.** A fair price fault is forgiven.
- A finished game bought years later, often on sale: the pages note it has no shop, no currency and no
  refund or pre-order talk at all.
- One reviewer says they got the game free from the studio (one of three such reviews across three
  games in the study). One says they played it for a Steam platform reward like trading cards (one of
  two such reviews in the study).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- **Buggy**: 18 times, 56% from recommenders.
- **One graphics setting causes the slowdown**: 6 times, only in this game. Players found the setting
  and turned it down.
- **Falling through the floor** was first named while reading this game (nine sightings across six
  games in the study; the page does not give this game's share).
- Tech talk is 5.4% of everything said, against 7.3% for the eight co-op games.

### The studio, updates and community
- The game is finished and abandoned. Talk about updates is 0.5% of everything said.
- **Talk about the studio**: 13 of the 14 community mentions. The studio listens and acts 9; open
  about plans 1; working on the wrong thing first 1; talks but never about the problem 1; **replied
  to my review on the store page** 1; unclear 1.
- **Grading against the studio's small size**: 34 times, one review in thirteen, the fifth most-said
  thing. The reviewer tells you which ruler they used.
- **Reviewers state their own record in the genre first**: 8 times, only in this game.
- **Comparisons everywhere**: describing the game by naming other games is the top thing said, 76
  times (17.5 per 100 reviews, the biggest by a factor of 1.6). Its two neighbours are the same claim
  with opposite signs: "nothing else plays like it" 27, "Remnant did it better" 25.
- Where the talk goes: how the game plays 53.6% (co-op games 30.3%); reputation and marketing 12.5%
  (10.0%); the review itself 10.2% (10.1%); art 5.2% (4.0%); how it was made and shipped 2.0% (7.4%);
  other people 0.8% (13.6%); updates 0.5% (4.1%).

### What players asked for
- **A checklist of what is left to find or earn** (the cross-game page notes the ask; no count).
- **Lock-on that targets weak points** (4 + quotes).
No other counted requests on the pages.

### Only in this game
- **Lock-on that grabs the body, not the weak point** - 4 times, only here.
- **One setting causes the slowdown** - 6 times, only here.
- **Reviewers posting their genre record before the verdict** - 8 times, only here.
- **The camera gets in the way** - 1 time, only here. **Trading-card talk** - 1 time, only here.
- **The clearest split between forgiven and unforgiven faults**: numbers forgiven, loss of control
  not.
- **The most game-design talk** of any game read before it (53.6%), because there are no other players to
  talk about.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Never take the controller away for long.**
   Evidence: stuns 13 (31% recommenders), slow weapon handling 23 (30%), no counterplay 16 (25%),
   unfair spawns 30 (37%). These are the complaints that make people leave; balance and price faults
   are forgiven.
   Dominion could: keep stuns and knockdowns short and rare, always with a tell and a way out (dodge,
   break-free, teammate save). Every enemy attack should have an answer by skill. Make reload, swap
   and ready times quick, and let actions cancel into a dodge.
   Strength: **strong** (clear split across several complaints).
2. **Aim help must respect weak points.**
   Evidence: lock-on holds the body 4 (only here); shots go where they want 14; every enemy fought the
   same way 18; 22 of 39 lock-on and aim-assist mentions in the study are this game.
   Dominion could: if it adds aim help or lock-on for pad players, let it snap to weak points or
   release near them; keep spread small enough that aim matters; give enemies weak points that need
   different answers, not just "head and back".
   Strength: **medium**.
3. **Spawns must give the player a chance.**
   Evidence: unfair spawns 30 times, mostly from people who left.
   Dominion could: spawn enemies out of sight with an audio or visual tell, never right behind a
   player with no warning; test spawn points in every arena.
   Strength: **medium to strong**.
4. **Third-person movement must feel quick.**
   Evidence: moving feels slow and heavy 10 times; the cross-game page says a third-person shooter is
   judged on how it moves, and a slow dash or slide is the complaint.
   Dominion could: tune sprint, slide and dodge for speed and quick response before release.
   Strength: **medium**.
5. **Hard is good when it is fair, and when learning pays off.**
   Evidence: satisfyingly hard 39; gets good once learned 24; but badly scaled difficulty 17.
   Dominion could: offer hard modes that reward skill, with even steps between levels, and teach the
   key trick (dodge, weak points) early so the "click" comes in the first session.
   Strength: **medium**.
6. **Teach the main trick early.**
   Evidence: *"I hated the game until I learned this trick"* (78807510); 24 say it only got good once
   learned.
   Dominion could: make the first run show the core moves clearly, so players do not quit before the
   game clicks.
   Strength: **medium**.
7. **Balance faults are forgiven, but they are said a lot.**
   Evidence: some choices useless 26 (73% recommenders); one choice dominates 25 (72%); many builds
   praised 31.
   Dominion could: test that every gun and ability has a use; nerf or buff in patches; do not panic,
   but do not ignore it.
   Strength: **medium**.
8. **Level design is a strong praise point, even for critics.**
   Evidence: well-built levels 46, the top praise, from both camps.
   Dominion could: make arenas layered with routes and secrets, and leave room to dodge and use the
   camera (the "spaces too small" complaint was first named here).
   Strength: **medium**.
9. **Show players what is left to earn.**
   Evidence: the "does not show what is left to earn" complaint was first named here; a checklist was
   asked for (no counts).
   Dominion could: give a codex or unlock list showing locked items and how to get them.
   Strength: **weak**.
10. **One graphics option should never wreck performance.**
    Evidence: one setting causes the slowdown, 6 times; players had to find it.
    Dominion could: test each high setting for cost; flag the expensive ones in the menu; give
    sensible defaults.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **A third-person camera must not block the view or the shot.**
    Evidence: camera gets in the way 1; scenery swallows a shot 1 (later seen in more third-person
    games).
    Dominion could: test camera collision and shots near cover, rails and fences.
    Strength: **weak** here (stronger across the study).
12. **Expect to be compared; be clear what you are.**
    Evidence: describing by other games 76 (17.5 per 100); "nothing like it" 27 vs "Remnant did it
    better" 25.
    Dominion could: know the games it will be named next to, and be better at one clear thing.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **Price faults are forgiven if the game is liked, but they still cost.**
    Evidence: too pricey 38 times, 63% from recommenders.
    Dominion could: price to content; know that even fans will say it.
    Strength: **medium**.
14. **Answering reviews and showing you listen is noticed even in a solo game.**
    Evidence: listens and acts 9; replied to my review 1.
    Dominion could: reply to store reviews and patch what players name.
    Strength: **weak**.
15. **Co-op and live updates fill a lot of what players talk about.**
    Evidence: with no other players, game-design talk rose to 53.6% (30.3% in co-op games), and talk
    about players fell to 0.8% (13.6%).
    Dominion could: expect its co-op, matchmaking and update talk to be a big share of its reviews,
    and plan for it.
    Strength: **medium** (one game, clear numbers).

### Limits
- English only: 455 non-English reviews (51% of the total) are unread.
- Census: no sampling error, but small numbers after 2023 (31 reviews in 2024-2026).
- 58 reviews (13.3%) were edited after posting and are filed under the date first written.
- One review labelled English is in Portuguese (177878863); it was read on its content.
- The game is finished; these numbers change only if someone writes a new review.
- Single-player: co-op and live-service lessons cannot come from this game.
- The pages give no counts for bosses, loot luck, grind or content length.
- Claims about a free copy and platform rewards are what reviewers say.

---

## The small-game block (ten small games read whole)

**Read:** 751 of 751 English reviews across ten games (100%). Every English review was read, so there
is no margin of error on any game. Checked against Steam's own English totals. **65.1% thumbs up**
across the block. Read on 2026-09-10, the same day Rico cleared it.
**Pages:** `findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 15, one page per game for the
eight with more than forty reviews (`findings/<slug>-english.md`). VOIDCRISIS and Town Of The Dead Life
have no page of their own.

### What the block was, and why it was read
The block is every small third-person game sold as a roguelike that had 100 or more Steam reviews and
had failed or been forgotten. They were measured on 2026-09-02; Rico put them off that day (*"We could
touch upon these at a later time"*), then cleared all ten as one block on 2026-09-10 (*"start the
roguelike block"*). Most of them have very few English reviewers: Zombie Girl has 979 reviews and 89 in
English; Town Of The Dead Life has 166 and one.

**The question the block was chosen to answer:** can the study say *why* a game with a hundred reviews
failed, when the honest answer is usually "nobody showed up" and not "the combat was bad"?

Not all ten are third-person roguelikes in the end: ZCREW is top-down, Banzai Escape is an on-rails
phone port, Alien Dawn is VR open-world survival, SCP: Abhorrent is a free thirty-minute horror game.
Four are co-op: ZCREW, ArcRunner, Alien Dawn and VOIDCRISIS.

| Game | Reviews read | Thumbs up | Things said per review | Praise for each complaint |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Zombie Girl | 89 | 73.0% | 2.97 | 0.53 |
| ZCREW | 77 | 49.4% | 2.78 | 0.42 |
| ArcRunner | 177 | 68.4% | 2.55 | 0.60 |
| FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL | 116 | 69.8% | 2.65 | 0.45 |
| Banzai Escape | 80 | 63.8% | 2.25 | 0.50 |
| SCP: Abhorrent | 69 | 63.8% | 1.41 | 1.18 (mostly ironic praise) |
| Alien Dawn | 74 | 63.5% | 2.03 | 1.03 (mostly praise for the two developers) |
| Die After Sunset | 51 | 58.8% | 2.33 | 0.38 |
| VOIDCRISIS | 17 | 64.7% | 2.18 | 0.29 |
| Town Of The Dead Life | 1 | (one thumb up) | 4 things said | none against |
| **Whole block** | **751** | **65.1%** | **2.43** (1,823 things said) | **0.56** |

### The short version (plain words)
In small games, one review in six is about the price or how much game there is, and one in nine is a
thumbs up that says nothing. Players grade small games against the size of the team. Five of the nine
real games were praised for "the developers listen" in year one and called abandoned within two years;
only Alien Dawn kept its players' trust. Two of the ten have review pools that are mostly a fight about
fan service, not play. And in the four co-op games nobody talked about other players at all: there was
nobody there to talk about.

### Block-wide findings

**1. Price and amount are what small games are judged on.**
| What players said | Reviews | Share of 751 |
|---|---|---|
| Price or amount, in total | 118 | 15.7% |
| - the price is fair | 38 | |
| - there is too little game | 32 | |
| - the price is too high for what it is | 26 | |
| - buy it only on sale | 25 | |
| A thumbs up that says nothing | 82 | 10.9% |
| Described by naming other games | 53 | 7.1% |
| Graded against the size of the studio | 24 | 3.2% |
| The developers listen and act | 23 | 3.1% |

The recommenders write the same sentence in every game: *it is short, it is janky, it is cheap, I liked
it.* Examples: 7 FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL reviewers name the bundle price they paid; all 5 of Banzai
Escape's "too short" comments are thumbs up; all 14 of Zombie Girl's "price is fair" comments are
thumbs up.

**2. The studio is the ruler.** 24 reviews say outright they are judging a one- or two-person team, and
another 12 are written to the studio rather than to a buyer. In the nine big games of the study,
judging by studio size came up in 0.7% of reviews; in this block it is 3.2%.

**3. The year-one-to-year-three arc.** "The developers listen and act" was said 23 times, every one in
the game's launch window (2018 to 2023). "Updates stopped" was said 16 times, every one a year or more
after launch (2021 to 2026). "Still supported" was said 3 times (Alien Dawn 2, Zombie Girl 1).

| Game | "The developers listen" | "The developers left" | "Still supported" |
|---|---|---|---|
| ArcRunner | 10 | 9 | 0 |
| Zombie Girl | 3 | 2 | 1 |
| ZCREW | 1 | 2 | 0 |
| Die After Sunset | 1 | 1 | 0 |
| VOIDCRISIS | 0 | 2 | 0 |
| Alien Dawn | 7 | 0 | 2 |

Five of the nine real games turned from "the developers listen" to "the developers left" inside two
years, and the same review that praised the developers is often the one later edited to say they left:
*"the devs really listen to the community, insane the speed they're putting patches out"* became
*"absolute GEM that the devs seemed to have abandoned"* (ArcRunner, 137512385, May 2023 then January
2024). Alien Dawn is the exception, and its reviews say so in 2025. Banzai Escape is the exception the
other way: never praised for updates, never called abandoned, and the same ammo complaint every year
from 2016 to 2026.

**4. Two review pools that are fights about fan service.**
| | Zombie Girl | FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL |
|---|---|---|
| The argument | the store's adult-content tags lied | the black shadow under the skirt is censorship |
| Reviews making the claim | 12 (9 thumbs down) | 12 (9 thumbs down) |
| Reviews rejecting it | 8 (all 8 thumbs up) | 11 (all 11 thumbs up) |
| Share of the game's helpful votes on the claim | not given | 52% |

19 of Zombie Girl's 89 reviews and 22 of FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL's 116 are this argument, and the two
sides split the thumb almost perfectly. Take them out and the rest are 77.1% and 72.3% thumbs up, with
little to fight about. The study records both sides and does not judge either.

**5. No players, no community talk.** Subjects the nine big games talk about a lot never came up in
the block at all:
| What players talk about | Times said in the nine big games | In the block |
|---|---|---|
| The player community's habits, in-jokes and rituals | 1,325 | 0 |
| How other players behave | 315 | 0 |
| How they found the game | 235 | 0 |
| Friends lists, chat and other social features | 181 | 0 |
| The moment a reward drops | 82 | 0 |

The four co-op games are included in those zeros. A game with 80 players has no shared ritual, no
in-joke, no bad behaviour to complain about. The block's answer to its question: **the study can tell a
game failed for lack of players, because the places where players would be are empty.** The only
community topic that survives a game with no players is how the developers talk to people.

**6. Small games supply the second sighting.** Eight new kinds of comment were added to the study's
list during the block; six were claims already scattered in bigger games one at a time, which a small
game read whole finally confirmed (for example, shots stopped by scenery in third person: one each in
Helldivers 2 and Immortal: Unchained, then four in ArcRunner). Four kinds of comment are used only by
block games. The small games did not find new kinds of complaint; they confirmed ones the big samples
could not.

**7. Open questions the block left (each seen in one game only, not yet confirmed):**
- The thumbs up given as encouragement to the studio, not as a verdict (Zombie Girl, 1 review).
- Permanent upgrades make early floors trivial and nothing lets you skip them (ArcRunner, 2 times).
- The game changes your aim sensitivity by itself near traps (FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL, 5 times, patched).
- AI teammates cost in-game currency every run (VOIDCRISIS, 3 times).
- A new study topic was created for players who need text instead of memory (from one ArcRunner
  review); the first comment filed under it is that the game relies on remembering icons with no text.

### VOIDCRISIS (no page of its own)
17 English reviews read of 141 in all languages; 64.7% thumbs up in English (Steam, all languages:
53.9%, Mixed). 37 things said, 2.18 per review, 0.29 praise for each complaint: the most negative text
in the block. A four-player co-op mech game in early access from August 2022; reviewers argue whether
it is tower defence or a mech battle game without human opponents (*"Gundam mixed with League of
Legends"*, 120923709). From the block page and the open-questions file:
- **Abandoned fast.** 2 reviews: *"not even three months in early access and they abandon ship"*
  (125209428, who says it was due to poor sales; not checked).
- **AI teammates cost currency every run.** 3 times it was said, all this game. *"you have to buy VA's
  (Mech suit) for the CPU's with in game currency each time to join you on solo play"* (120026161).
  One player's counter-advice: *"hire an AI's (CPU's) to fill the slots, don't turn the game into solo
  slog"* (149271537).
From the review notes (no counts on the pages for these): solo was *"a forty-minute slog"* while one
human with two AI teammates took eight minutes (120026122); the AI teammates run in and die (120026161,
120056760), though a 2023 reviewer found them *"surprisingly competent"* (149271537); four players who
split roles well make a strong team (120058013, 120279267); the tutorial is poor (120026122, 120279267,
120376388); network errors in multiplayer meant one player never finished the fifth mission
(120221788); failing a mission restarts you from the first and makes you rebuy the mechs (125209428);
bosses have cheap instant deaths (125209428); one map, lightly randomised (149271537); by 2023 it was
*"almost impossible to find anyone to play with"* (149271537). One reviewer refunded it as too little for
the price (120026161); another says it should have been priced at $15 to $20 (120047206).

### Town Of The Dead Life (no page of its own)
1 English review of 166 in all languages (Steam, all languages: 68.1%, Mostly Positive). The one
reviewer (95049171, thumbs up) says it mixes strategy, crafting, survival, city building, tower
defence and roguelike well, the resource management is deep, it has an English translation and it is
cheap for what you get. He also links his own curator page. Nothing to learn for Dominion from one
review; its English audience barely exists.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A small game is forgiven for being short or rough, never for not working.**
   Evidence: ZCREW's game-stopping bugs were said 11 times, 10 thumbs down; Zombie Girl's "too short"
   14 times, 12 thumbs up; Banzai Escape's "too short" 5, all thumbs up.
   Dominion could: put "every mission and every extraction can always finish" ahead of new content.
   Strength: medium to strong (clear in two games, matches the rest of the block).
2. **Plan the after-launch years, not just the launch month.**
   Evidence: "the developers listen" 23 times, all in launch windows; "updates stopped" 16 times, all a
   year or more later; five of nine games turned within two years; Alien Dawn kept updating and kept
   its praise (7 listen, 0 left, 2 still supported).
   Dominion could: set an update rhythm the team can keep for years; when it must slow, say so openly.
   Strength: strong for this block (five games, same shape); medium as a general rule.
3. **Price for the hours and the polish.**
   Evidence: 118 of 751 reviews (15.7%) are about price or amount; 26 too high, 25 sale only, 38 fair,
   32 too little. FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL at $49.99 drew 22 "too high", 14 of them from fans.
   Dominion could: test how many hours a team gets out of the game before setting the price; expect
   sales to be how many players arrive.
   Strength: strong (the biggest single theme in the block).
4. **A co-op game needs a way to play when nobody is online.**
   Evidence: the four co-op games produced zero comments about other players; VOIDCRISIS: *"almost
   impossible to find anyone to play with"*, solo *"a forty-minute slog"*; its AI teammates cost
   currency every run (3).
   Dominion could: make solo and two-player runs fair (scale enemies to team size), offer free AI
   teammates if it has them, and make inviting friends to a listen-server game very easy, since
   strangers may not be there.
   Strength: medium (the zeros are clear; the VOIDCRISIS details are a few reviews).
5. **Never charge players for basics or bots.**
   Evidence: Banzai Escape's pay-for-ammo rule, 17 times, 11 thumbs down, for ten years; VOIDCRISIS's
   paid AI teammates, 3; FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL's used-up floor keys, 9.
   Dominion could: keep ammo, AI help and checkpoints free inside a run; put costs on choices, not on
   being able to play.
   Strength: medium.
6. **The store page must match the game exactly.**
   Evidence: two of ten games have review pools that are mostly a fight over fan service (19 and 22
   reviews); in FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL 12 reviews carry 52% of all helpful votes.
   Dominion could: check its own and player-added tags before and after launch, and say plainly what
   the game is.
   Strength: medium.
7. **Expect to be described by the genre leader's name.**
   Evidence: 53 of 751 reviews (7.1%) describe a game by naming another; in ArcRunner it is tied for
   the most common thing said, and the most-helpful review is three words naming Risk of Rain 2.
   Dominion could: decide which game players will compare it to, and make the difference obvious in
   the first run and on the store page.
   Strength: medium.
8. **Be visible as people.**
   Evidence: Alien Dawn, 21 of 74 reviews about the two developers, 20 warm; 24 block reviews grade
   against studio size and 12 more write to the studio; replies on reviews changed one verdict (SCP:
   Abhorrent) and became a three-year scorecard in another (ZCREW).
   Dominion could: answer reviews where a fix is coming, keep every public promise, and be present in
   its community.
   Strength: medium.
9. **Third-person shots must not be eaten by scenery.**
   Evidence: 6 times across three games, 4 in ArcRunner.
   Dominion could: check the shot from the camera and from the gun, and show when the gun cannot reach
   the aim point.
   Strength: medium.
10. **Movement and dodge feel are where roguelite shooters lose players.**
    Evidence: ArcRunner "too slow" 17 times (largest complaint), Die After Sunset 7; VOIDCRISIS "too
    slow and boring for a mecha shooter" (125209428); Die After Sunset's dodge unreliable 3.
    Dominion could: playtest sprint, dodge and speed early and often.
    Strength: medium.
11. **Full release must not be worse than early access.**
    Evidence: Die After Sunset fell from 71% to 40% to 17% thumbs up across early access, 1.0 year and
    after; ZCREW's bug reports are all after 1.0.
    Dominion could: lock features well before 1.0 and test the first run end to end.
    Strength: medium.
12. **Long runs need a save, and earned skips should not be used up.**
    Evidence: FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL, runs too long 8, no save 4, used-up keys 9; ArcRunner, no way to
    skip outgrown floors 2 and upgrades wiped on finishing 12.
    Dominion could: keep runs to a session, let the host save between stages, keep earned progress.
    Strength: weak to medium.
13. **Readability basics: brightness, landmarks, words on icons.**
    Evidence: SCP: Abhorrent too dark 6 (a brightness slider flipped one thumb); empty spaces with
    nothing to remember in Alien Dawn (4), ArcRunner and Zombie Girl; one ArcRunner reviewer could not
    remember icons.
    Dominion could: ship brightness calibration, give each map strong landmarks, add text to icons.
    Strength: weak to medium.

### Limits
- Every English review was read, so there is no margin of error, but every year-by-year figure rests
  on small numbers; each page states its counts.
- 1,849 reviews in other languages across the ten were not read. Zombie Girl's English reviewers are
  only 9% of its audience and 22 points more positive than the rest.
- Three games were first counted from their full-release date and missed early-access reviews (ZCREW
  35, Die After Sunset 40, VOIDCRISIS 11). This was fixed before reading.
- Two games (Banzai Escape, SCP: Abhorrent) have gained one review each since the pull.
- 9 reviews marked English across the block were in other languages; they were read from content.
- The VOIDCRISIS notes beyond the block page come from the per-review notes, not a findings page; they
  carry no counts.
- The Die After Sunset page says its studio later made ArcRunner; the ArcRunner page names a different
  studio. Not settled here.
- Claims about patches, prices paid and why a studio stopped are what reviewers say, not checked.

---

## ArcRunner

**Read:** all 177 of 177 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin
of error. Written April 2023 to July 2026 (30 months, every month read). **68.4% thumbs up** in the
English reviews, 121 up and 56 down (Steam, all languages: 69.1% of 236 reviews, Mostly Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/arcrunner-english.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 15. There is no separate `findings/arcrunner.md` page.

**What it is:** a co-op roguelite shooter (runs that end, with some upgrades that stay between runs),
third person, up to three players. Made by Trickjump Games. Released 2023-04-27 at $20, with a free
demo. Sci-fi, cyberpunk look. The engine is not named on the page. How it is hosted is not on the
page. It is the largest game in the small-game block and the only one with a real launch: 137 of its
177 English reviews came in 2023.

**How close to Dominion:** very close in shape. Third person, co-op, sci-fi, built on runs, a shooter.
It is three players, not four. It has no extraction. Hosting is not recorded.

### The short version (plain words)
Players liked it at launch and liked how fast the studio patched it, then watched the studio go quiet.
The thumbs-up share fell from 74% in 2023 to 56% in 2024, 45% in 2025 and 0% in 2026 (2 reviews). Ten
reviews praised the studio for listening, all in 2023; nine called the game abandoned, from 2024 on.
Almost everyone describes it by naming Risk of Rain 2. The biggest design complaints are that you move
slowly (17 times it was said) and that finishing the game on normal wipes all your permanent upgrades
(12 times it was said, 7 of them by people who still recommend it).

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The studio listened and patched fast at launch.** 10 reviews, all in 2023. *"the devs really
   listen to the community, insane the speed they're putting patches out"* (137512385, May 2023).
2. **A slow, calm pace.** Three recommenders say the slow speed is the reason to buy. *"risk of rain
   was to fast for me, if ur older with slower reflexes give this a shot"* (137464145).
3. **It is not just a copy of Risk of Rain 2**, some say. *"This reminds me of Mass Effect
   multiplayer"* (137415655, 77 people found it helpful). *"arcrunner is more like its parents, doom
   and returnal"* (139378615).
4. Many thumbs-up reviews say nothing: 23 of the 121 thumbs-up reviews (19%) give no reason at all.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **You move too slowly.** 17 times it was said, the largest complaint, 7 from thumbs-up reviews and
   10 from thumbs-down. *"For a game with 'runner' in the title, you move remarkably slow, even after
   the patch to speed you up."* (138076943). *"Arc-Casual-Jogger"* (154220887). So the studio did
   patch speed, and it was still called slow.
2. **Finishing the game wipes your permanent upgrades.** 12 times it was said, 7 from thumbs-up
   reviews, so both people who stayed and people who left name it. Finishing the campaign on normal
   resets every permanent upgrade. *"Had lots of fun.... Until the first clear on normal and all your
   meta progression is wiped"* (138323944, thumbs up). *"None of the problems I mentioned until now
   are even remotely as bad as the fact that finishing the game resets all of your metaprogression."*
   (138076943, thumbs down). One reviewer says the studio patched it into a different reset instead
   of removing it (137459084).
3. **It is worse than Risk of Rain 2 (or another game).** 11 times it was said, 10 from thumbs-down
   reviews. *"like playing Risk of Rain 2 and Gunfire Reborn at the same time, but without the fun"*
   (137666125).
4. **Key tools are locked at the start.** 10 times it was said: the second weapon and the ultimate
   ability are locked behind the currency you earn across runs, so the first hours are weak.
5. **The studio left the game.** 9 reviews from 2024 on. *"absolute GEM that the devs seemed to have
   abandoned"* (137512385, the same review that praised the studio, edited in January 2024).
6. **Your shots hit the scenery.** 4 times it was said in this game: shots stopped by fences, rails,
   lamp posts, ankle-high ledges, and once by something behind the camera.
7. **The early floors become a boring chore you cannot skip.** 2 times it was said (see Progression).
8. **You have to remember what each gun and skill does from its picture**; there is no text. One
   reviewer.

### How it changed over time
| | 2023 | 2024 | 2025 | 2026 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reviews | 137 | 27 | 11 | 2 |
| Thumbs up | 74% | 56% | 45% | 0% |
| "The studio listens" | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| "Updates stopped" | 3 | 3 | 3 | 0 |

The three "updates stopped" reviews listed under 2023 were all edited in 2024 or later, so they
really belong to 2024 or later. The turn players name is the studio going quiet. One reviewer says
the game is fun and they would give it a thumbs up, but *"as of right now I believe that this is
abandonware"* (189831000), so the thumb down is a protest against the studio, not a verdict on play.
2025 and 2026 together are only 13 reviews, so those percentages are shaky.

### Co-op and online play
The page records nothing about hosting, joining, matchmaking, lag or how it scales to player count.
The block page notes that ArcRunner, a co-op game, has zero reviews about a player community, its
habits or its in-jokes, which it reads as a sign there were too few players to form one.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Movement is slow: 17 times it was said, even after a speed patch (see complaints).
- Third-person camera problem: shots blocked by fences, rails, lamp posts, ankle-high ledges and once
  by something behind the camera. 4 times in ArcRunner; 6 times across three games in the whole
  study (one each in Helldivers 2 and Immortal: Unchained). The study had no name for this until
  ArcRunner supplied the count.
- The Die After Sunset page says ArcRunner's reviewers called its upgrades ("augments") bland. No
  count is given.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
The page has nothing on enemy types or bosses. The one difficulty point is that permanent upgrades
make the first world too easy later on: *"By the time you reach world 3, the world 1 that was once
challenging and fun becomes just a chore as you are over level for those enemies"* (138105024, from
the open-gaps file). One reviewer says they used a cheat to get through; before this, only one other game in the
study (Immortal: Unchained) had a reviewer say that.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Finishing on normal wipes permanent upgrades: 12 times it was said, 7 from people who still
  recommend it. It is the one design choice in the game named on both sides of the thumb.
- The second weapon and the ultimate are locked behind the between-run currency: 10 times it was said.
- Using a weapon enough lets you start runs with it, and permanent upgrades keep growing, so the early
  areas become *"trivial time-wasters. Roguelites with metaprogression need floor skip options"*
  (138076943). 2 times it was said, both in this game; the study expects it to come up again in
  other roguelites.

### Runs, content and replay value
The page gives no run length or content count. The replay problem it records is the forced replay of
early floors you have outgrown (2 times) and the wipe on finishing (12 times). The Alien Dawn page
says ArcRunner's reviewers said its arenas have nothing in them to remember; no count is given.

### Money and price
$20 with a demo. The page records no price complaints or price praise for this game.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
Only the shots-hitting-scenery problem (4 times). The page records no crash or performance findings.

### The studio, updates and community
- 10 reviews praise fast patches and listening, all in the first five weeks of 2023.
- 9 reviews call it abandoned, from 2024 on. Often the same reviewer, editing the same review.
- One reviewer gives a thumbs down only to protest the studio leaving (189831000).
- Speed was patched once; still called slow. The upgrade wipe was patched into a different wipe, the
  reviewer says (137459084).
- The Die After Sunset page calls ArcRunner the next game from the Die After Sunset studio. The
  ArcRunner page names a different studio. See Limits.

### What players asked for
- A way to skip early floors once you have outgrown them (138076943).
- Faster movement (17 times it was said that it is slow).
- Keep permanent upgrades after finishing the game (12 times the wipe was named).
- Text that says what each gun and skill does, not just a picture (one reviewer).
- Continued updates (9 reviews say they stopped).

### Only in this game
- ArcRunner supplied most of the sightings (4 of 6) for shots stopped by scenery in third person.
- A new topic in the study, "memory and attention" for players who need on-screen text instead of
  remembering icons, was created because of one ArcRunner review.
- The early-floors-become-a-chore problem (2 times) has only been seen here.
- A group of reviewers who push back on the Risk of Rain comparison. *"maybe I'd simply enjoy one
  thing without constantly being upset that it isn't something else"* (137916317).
- The most-helpful review of the game is three words of comparison: *"Risk of Cyberpunk Rain 2"*
  (137389141, 118 helpful). 18 of the reviews that name another game name Risk of Rain, and naming
  other games is tied for the most common thing said (23 times).

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Make sure shots are not eaten by scenery in third person.**
   Evidence: 4 times in ArcRunner, 6 times across three third-person games.
   Dominion could: test the shot from the camera against the shot from the gun, so low ledges,
   rails and things behind the camera do not block a shot the crosshair says is clear; show a
   "blocked" marker on the crosshair when the gun cannot reach the aim point.
   Strength: medium (small counts, but in three games and a known third-person fault).
2. **Do not wipe permanent upgrades when players finish the game.**
   Evidence: 12 times it was said, 7 by players who still recommend it; one reviewer calls it worse
   than every other problem combined (138076943).
   Dominion could: keep earned upgrades after a "win"; if a reset is wanted, make it a choice the
   player opts into, with a clear reward.
   Strength: medium.
3. **Let players skip content they have outgrown.**
   Evidence: 2 times it was said, one game: early floors become a chore once upgrades stack up.
   Dominion could: let a team start later in a run, or scale early areas up, once they have proven
   they can clear them.
   Strength: weak.
4. **Do not lock core kit behind the between-run currency.**
   Evidence: 10 times it was said that the second weapon and the ultimate were locked at first.
   Dominion could: give every player their core tools from the first run; put side options, not core
   tools, behind unlocks.
   Strength: medium.
5. **Movement speed decides the feel of a shooter built on runs.**
   Evidence: 17 times it was said that it is too slow, the largest complaint, even after a speed
   patch; 3 recommenders liked the slow pace.
   Dominion could: tune sprint and dodge early with playtests; if a slower pace is part of the
   identity, say so on the store page.
   Strength: medium.
6. **Fast patches at launch win praise, but going quiet later costs more than the praise earned.**
   Evidence: 10 reviews praise listening (all 2023), 9 call it abandoned (2024 on); thumbs up fell
   from 74% to 45% by 2025. One thumbs down is a protest about the studio, not the game.
   Dominion could: plan updates at a pace that can last for years, not a burst at launch; tell
   players when updates will slow down rather than going silent.
   Strength: medium in this game; strong when read with the rest of the block (five games show it).
7. **Expect to be judged against the genre leader.**
   Evidence: naming other games is tied for the most common thing said (23 times); 11 say it is worse
   than another game, 10 of them thumbs down; the most-helpful review is "Risk of Cyberpunk Rain 2".
   Dominion could: decide early which game players will name, and make the difference clear in the
   first minutes and on the store page.
   Strength: medium.
8. **Put words next to icons.**
   Evidence: one reviewer could not remember what each gun and skill did from the picture.
   Dominion could: add short text to every gun, skill and upgrade icon, on hover or hold.
   Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. But 2025 and 2026 are only 13 reviews together,
  so their percentages are shaky.
- 17 reviews (9.6%) were edited after posting and are counted on the date they were first written;
  three of them belong to a later year than they are filed under.
- 59 reviews in other languages (25% of the audience) were not read.
- The page says nothing on co-op, hosting, enemies, bosses, price or crashes, so those sections are
  thin because the page is thin, not because players said nothing.
- Patches and the upgrade-wipe change are what reviewers say; the page did not check them.
- The studio name does not match across pages: this page says Trickjump Games; the Die After Sunset
  page says ArcRunner is the next game from Die After Sunset's studio (Playstark). Not settled here.

---

## Zombie Girl (末世少女 Zombie Girl)

**Read:** all 89 of 89 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin
of error. Written October 2023 to August 2026 (27 months, every month read). **73.0% thumbs up** in
the English reviews, 65 up and 24 down (Steam, all languages: 51.0% of 979 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/zombie-girl-english.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 15. There is no separate `findings/zombie-girl.md` page.

**What it is:** a single-player third-person zombie shooter made by one person in Unreal Engine 5.
Released October 2023 at about $7. Floors are generated by the game. Short: about two hours by most
accounts. The store page carries the tags *Sexual Content*, *Nudity* and *NSFW*. All voice audio is
Chinese or Japanese. English speakers are only 9% of its reviewers.

**How close to Dominion:** shares the third-person camera, the shooting and Unreal Engine 5. It is
single-player, has no co-op, no runs in Dominion's sense, and is not sci-fi.

### The short version (plain words)
Most of this game's review pool is a fight about fan service, not about play. 19 of the 89 reviews
(one in five) argue about whether the adult store tags lied, and the two sides split the thumb almost
perfectly. Take those out and the other 70 reviews are 77.1% thumbs up. The rest of the case is "short
but cheap": 14 say the price is fair (all thumbs up) and 14 say there is too little game (12 of them
still recommend it). The studio was praised for quick updates in 2023 and called gone by 2026.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The price is fair.** 14 times it was said, all 14 thumbs up. *"its not bad enough to not
   recommend it at the price its being sold at"* (148299530).
2. **"The buyers did not read the tags."** 8 times it was said, all 8 thumbs up: the game never
   promised adult content. *"Game literally does not say that is has sex or nudity in it, if you read
   the description."* (148267026). Three reviewers say the tags were added by Steam users, not the
   studio (*"the 'user tags'"*, 168049101). One argues it cannot be adult content because it is sold
   in Germany (163896162). The page did not check this.
3. **Quick updates and a studio that listens, in 2023.** 3 reviews say updates came steadily and 3 say
   the developer listens: *"already got multiple QoL updates... a minimap, new weapons, a melee
   attack"*. One 2024 review says a patch made it better.
4. **Graded kindly because one person made it.** 8 reviews (9%) say so outright, 7 of them thumbs up.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **The store tags promise adult content the game does not have.** 12 times it was said, 9 from
   thumbs-down reviews (people who left), 3 from thumbs up. *"It promises nudity, sexual content, NSFW
   and fails to deliver."* (148135531, 147 helpful, the most-helpful review of the game).
2. **Too little game.** 14 times it was said, 12 from thumbs-up reviews (people who stayed). Most say
   about two hours; one reviewer says they finished the story in thirteen minutes (154333287).
3. **Not clear where to go.** 7 times it was said, 4 from thumbs-up reviews.
4. **Enemies take too many bullets.** 4 times it was said, 2 from thumbs-up reviews.
5. **The developer left.** 2 reviews in 2026: *"the developer has run away"* (221730277).
6. **No English voices.** All in-game audio is Chinese or Japanese. 2 times it was said.
7. **Spiders.** One reviewer flags spider sections as a problem for people afraid of spiders.
8. **The generated floors have nothing memorable in them.** The Alien Dawn page says Zombie Girl's
   reviewers said this; no count is given.

Overall the text is far more negative than the thumb: 136 complaints against 72 pieces of praise
(0.53 praise for each complaint), and 86 of the 136 complaints (63.2%) were written by people who
recommend the game. The page reads the recommenders as writing a wish list to the developer.

### How it changed over time
| Year | Reviews | Thumbs up | What the update talk says |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2023 | 36 | 78% | steady updates (3), the developer listens (3) |
| 2024 | 36 | 64% | a patch made it better (1); waiting on a promised skill tree (2) |
| 2025 | 8 | 100% | *"hasn't received any updates in 2025 atm, I hope it hasn't been abandoned"* |
| 2026 | 9 | 67% | updates stopped (2): *"the developer has run away"* |

2025 and 2026 are 17 reviews together, so their percentages are shaky. The turn players name is the
promised skill tree not arriving and updates stopping.

### Co-op and online play
Single-player. The page records no requests for co-op.

### Combat, movement and feel
The 2023 updates added a melee attack and new weapons, players say. The only combat complaint with a
count is enemies that soak up bullets (4 times). Nothing else on the page.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
Bullet-sponge enemies (4 times, 2 from thumbs up). Spider sections (1). Nothing on bosses.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
A skill tree was announced and players waited for it (2 times in 2024). Nothing else on the page.

### Runs, content and replay value
About two hours of game; too little content was said 14 times, but 12 of those 14 still recommend it.
Not clear where to go was said 7 times; a minimap was added in an early update, a reviewer says.

### Money and price
About $7. Every one of the 14 price comments says the price is fair. *"I beat the game in 2 hours, so
i wouldnt necessarily recommend it, but its not bad enough to not recommend it at the price"*
(148299530). Low price is what makes a short game acceptable here.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
The page records no crash or performance findings.

### The studio, updates and community
- One person made it. 8 reviews grade it against that.
- 5 reviews are written to the developer, not to a buyer. One says the thumbs up is encouragement,
  not a verdict: *"give a good review first... a little encouragement to the production team"*
  (148210236). This kind of review is new to the study and not yet named.
- Steady updates and listening in 2023 (3 and 3), a promised skill tree in 2024 (2), quiet in 2025,
  *"the developer has run away"* in 2026 (2).

### What players asked for
- More content (14 say too little).
- Clearer direction on where to go (7).
- Fewer bullet-sponge enemies (4).
- The promised skill tree (2).
- English voice audio (2).
- Clear store labels that match what is in the game (12 and 8 on the two sides).

### Only in this game
- The fight over whether the adult store tags lied, which is most of the argument in the review pool.
  (FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL has the same shape of fight, about a different thing.)
- No English voice audio (2 times), used by no other game in the study.
- A spider warning (1 time), used by no other game in the study.
- English reviewers are 22 points more positive than the other 91% of its audience (73.0% against
  51.0% for all languages). The page cannot say why.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **The store page must match what is in the game.**
   Evidence: 12 times players said the store tags promised something the game does not have, 9 of
   them thumbs down; the most-helpful review (147 helpful) is this claim. 8 thumbs-up reviews defended
   it. 19 of 89 reviews (one in five) are this argument.
   Dominion could: check the store tags (including the ones players add) before launch and after; if
   a user tag is wrong, say plainly on the store page what the game is and is not.
   Strength: medium (one game, but clear and one-sided on the thumb).
2. **A short game is forgiven when the price matches.**
   Evidence: 14 say too little content, 12 of them still recommend; 14 say the price is fair, all
   thumbs up.
   Dominion could: set the launch price against the hours a team will really get out of it.
   Strength: medium.
3. **Promising a feature starts a clock.**
   Evidence: 2 reviews in 2024 waiting on a promised skill tree; by 2026, 2 say the developer left.
   Dominion could: only announce features with a date it can keep; if plans change, say so.
   Strength: weak.
4. **Show players where to go.**
   Evidence: 7 times it was said, 4 from people who still recommend it.
   Dominion could: always show the next goal and the extraction point on screen and on the map.
   Strength: weak to medium.
5. **Enemies that soak bullets read as lazy difficulty.**
   Evidence: 4 times it was said.
   Dominion could: make harder enemies harder by what they do, not only by more health; show hits
   landing.
   Strength: weak.
6. **Voice audio in the players' language matters.**
   Evidence: 2 times it was said that there are no English voices.
   Dominion could: plan which languages get voices, and make sure key callouts also show as text.
   Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. But English is only 9% of this game's audience;
  the other 890 reviews (91%) were not read and are far less positive. The page cannot say why.
- 2025 and 2026 are 17 reviews together.
- 11 reviews (12.4%) were edited after posting and are counted on the date first written.
- Whether the adult tags were the studio's or players' cannot be settled from reviews; the page
  records both sides and does not judge.
- 3 reviews marked English were in Chinese or Thai; they were read from their content.

---

## ZCREW

**Read:** all 77 of 77 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin of
error. Written December 2020 to July 2026 (31 months, every month read). **49.4% thumbs up** in the
English reviews, 38 up and 39 down (Steam, all languages: 52.6% of 363 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/zcrew-english.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 15. There is no separate `findings/zcrew.md` page.

**What it is:** a top-down twin-stick co-op shooter against zombies, made by Snail Games. In early
access from December 2020; full release (1.0) in September 2022. Missions with objectives and an
evacuation helicopter at the end. Paid; one reviewer recommends it "at $5" (the page does not give the full price). The engine and the
hosting model are not on the page. Player count is not on the page.

**How close to Dominion:** shares co-op, PvE, missions that end in getting out by helicopter (close to
extraction), and classes. It is top-down, not third person, and zombies, not sci-fi.

### The short version (plain words)
This game's thumbs-down reviews are about whether it works, not how it plays. "A bug stops me from
playing" was said 11 times, and 10 of those were thumbs down. Mission goals that get stuck, an
evacuation helicopter that will not come, enemies that stop spawning. The only real case for buying it
is that it is cheap: 11 say the price is fair, all thumbs up. The studio was praised for updates in
early access in 2021; after full release the reviews are bug reports, and some call it abandoned.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The price is fair.** 11 times it was said, all thumbs up; tied for the most common thing said
   about the game. *"At $20 or more, I'd be giving it a negative review. But at $5? Yes, I can
   absolutely recommend it."* (122276388). *"its cheaper than the £7 they charge at the cinema for
   popcorn"* (197864285).
2. **Steady updates in early access (2021).** 4 times it was said. *"an example to All Early Access
   games"* (87208986).
3. **Early access grew into what it promised.** 3 times it was said, all 2021.
4. **"Nothing does what it does."** One reviewer (122526677).
5. The developer listens: 1 review.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **A bug stops the game from being played.** 11 times it was said: 10 from thumbs-down reviews
   (people who left), 1 from thumbs up. A mission goal that sticks at 8 of 10, an evacuation helicopter
   that does not respond, enemies that stop spawning, triggers that never fire. *"after the tutorial
   mission, you cannot progress any further due to: crashes, game not spawning any enemies, game not
   giving you any missions... there is no excuse for this after 4 years"* (158283309, 2024).
2. **It feels unfinished.** 6 times it was said, all 6 thumbs down. *"probably not totally finished
   despite it not saying early access on steam"* (122526677).
3. **It is worse than another game, mostly Helldivers.** 4 times it was said; 10 of the 77 reviews
   name another game and 5 of those name Helldivers. *"Zcrew is trying to be some kind of Helldivers
   mixed with zombies. The problem is it never even comes close"* (108187929). *"It's a lot like a
   favorite game of mine called helldivers, but with all the soul/charm sucked out"* (122276388). One
   calls it a reskin of Kill Squad (118117616).
4. **It crashes again and again.** 3 times it was said, all thumbs down.
5. **The frame rate jumps around.** 3 times it was said, 2 thumbs down.
6. **The studio left it.** 2 to 3 reviews from 2023 on (the block page counts 2 as "updates stopped";
   this game's page counts 3 about abandonment in 2023-2026). *"another forsaken child of the game
   industry"* (230608709).
7. **The class you pick decides your character's gender.** Said in ZCREW and one other game (4 times
   across the two).
8. **Achievements do not unlock when you do the thing** (once here).
9. **Promised controller support never came** (one review, see Studio).
10. **Barren at early-access launch:** *"an interesting idea and a good foundation, BUT... so barren"*.

Overall: 132 complaints against 56 pieces of praise (0.42 praise for each complaint), the second most
negative text of any game in the study. Bugs and tech are 15.0% of everything said about ZCREW, against
7.3% for eight larger co-op games: the highest share of any game read.

### How it changed over time
| Period | Reviews | Thumbs up | What they say |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dec 2020, early access opens | 9 | 56% | a good start, but barren |
| 2021 | 20 | 45% | steady updates (4), it grew into its promise (3) |
| 2022 (full release in September) | 30 | 53% | not really finished |
| 2023 to 2026 | 18 | 44% | a bug stops play (7), abandoned (3) |

All the praise for update pace is from 2021. All the bug reports are from after full release. "It
grew into its promise" (3) and "it never did" (1) both appear: the same game judged a year apart.
These periods are 9, 20, 30 and 18 reviews, so the percentages are shaky.

### Co-op and online play
The page records nothing about hosting, joining, matchmaking, lag or scaling. The block page notes that
ZCREW, a co-op game, has zero reviews about a player community, which it reads as a sign there were
too few players to form one.

### Combat, movement and feel
The page records only the Helldivers comparison (the "soul" and "charm" are missing) and the frame rate
jumps (3 times). Nothing else on combat or movement.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
Enemies that stop spawning, so a mission cannot finish (part of the 11 game-stopping bug reports).
Nothing on enemy types or bosses.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- The class decides the character's gender (said in ZCREW and one other game, 4 times across both).
- Achievements that do not track what you did (once here; 3 times across three games).
Nothing else on the page.

### Runs, content and replay value
"Barren" at the start of early access. Mission goals that stick (8 of 10) and an evacuation that will
not come stop a mission from ending at all.

### Money and price
11 say the price is fair, all thumbs up; the argument is "not good, but cheap". Recommenders write
1.16 complaints each. One reviewer says they got it for a platform reward (Steam trading cards); one
says the studio gave them a free copy.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Game-stopping bugs: 11 times, 10 thumbs down. Stuck goals, a helicopter that will not respond,
  enemies that stop spawning, triggers that never fire.
- Feels unfinished: 6, all thumbs down.
- Crashes again and again: 3, all thumbs down.
- Frame rate jumps: 3, 2 thumbs down.
Small games are forgiven for being small. This one is not forgiven for not working.

### The studio, updates and community
- 2021: steady updates (4), early access grew into its promise (3), *"an example to All Early Access
  games"* (87208986).
- **The studio answered a review on the store page.** In November 2021 it promised *"full, intuitive
  controller support"*. The reviewer (96754160) then edited the review in September 2022, January
  2024 and May 2024 to say it never came. A reply on the store page became a promise the review kept
  score against.
- After 2023: reports that it was abandoned (2 to 3).

### What players asked for
- Fix the bugs that stop missions (11).
- Full controller support, which the studio promised (1 review, edited three times).
- Character looks not tied to the class (part of 4 across two games).
- Achievements that track correctly (1).
- More in it at the start of early access ("barren").

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by ZCREW alone. But ZCREW tipped four claims that had been scattered in the study one
at a time: the class decides your gender; a reviewer says they got a free copy from the studio;
achievements do not track what you did; a reviewer played it for a Steam platform reward. And it is
the clearest case in the study of a studio's reply on the store page turning into a broken promise.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A bug that stops a mission is the one thing a small game is not forgiven for.**
   Evidence: 11 times it was said, 10 thumbs down; stuck goals, an evacuation helicopter that will not
   respond, enemies that stop spawning. Compare Zombie Girl, where 12 of 14 "too short" comments still
   recommend.
   Dominion could: give every objective and every extraction a fail-safe (a timer or a host command
   that forces the next step), log stuck states, and test extraction under host lag and client
   disconnects on a listen server.
   Strength: medium for this game alone; the contrast with forgiven "too short" makes it stronger.
2. **The extraction moment must always work.**
   Evidence: the evacuation helicopter that does not respond is named among the 11 game-stopping bugs.
   Dominion could: make extraction its most tested system, with a backup path if the main trigger
   fails.
   Strength: weak by count, high stakes for an extraction game.
3. **Full release raises the bar; bugs after 1.0 are judged harder.**
   Evidence: praise for updates is all 2021; game-stopping bugs are 7 of the 18 reviews after 2023;
   *"no excuse for this after 4 years"* (158283309).
   Dominion could: do not call it 1.0 until missions finish reliably.
   Strength: medium.
4. **Anything the studio promises in a reply becomes a scorecard.**
   Evidence: one review edited three times over two and a half years, counting the missing controller
   support.
   Dominion could: only promise in public what is scheduled; follow up on the same thread when it ships
   or is dropped.
   Strength: weak (one review), clear.
5. **Do not tie a character's look or gender to the class.**
   Evidence: said in ZCREW and one other game, 4 times across the two.
   Dominion could: let any player pick any look for any role.
   Strength: weak to medium.
6. **Expect comparison to the big co-op name, and to lose on "soul".**
   Evidence: 10 of 77 reviews name another game, 5 name Helldivers; 4 say it is worse.
   Dominion could: give the game its own personality, voice lines and moments so it is not "the same
   but with the charm sucked out".
   Strength: weak to medium.
7. **Make achievements track reliably.**
   Evidence: once here, 3 times across three games.
   Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. The periods are 9, 20, 30 and 18 reviews.
- 13 reviews (16.9%) were edited after posting and are counted on the date first written.
- 286 reviews in other languages (79% of the audience) were not read.
- The first count of this game missed its 35 early-access reviews; it was fixed before reading.
- The abandonment count differs a little between pages (3 on this page, 2 on the block page).
- The page says nothing on co-op, hosting or enemy design, so those sections are thin because the page
  is thin.
- 2 reviews marked English were in Chinese and Portuguese; they were read from their content.

---

## FULL METAL SCHOOLGIRL

**Read:** all 116 of 116 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin
of error. Written October 2025 to August 2026 (11 months, every month read). **69.8% thumbs up** in
the English reviews, 81 up and 35 down (Steam, all languages: 69.8% of 235 reviews, Mostly Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/full-metal-schoolgirl-english.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 15. There is no separate `findings/full-metal-schoolgirl.md` page.

**What it is:** a single-player third-person roguelite shooter from YUKE'S, published by D3Publisher.
Released 2025-10-23 at $49.99. You climb a hundred-floor tower as one of two schoolgirl cyborgs, framed
as a livestream. Runs of two to seven hours, no save mid-run, and keys that skip floors but are used up
when you use them. A black shadow is drawn under every skirt. The engine is not on the page.

**How close to Dominion:** shares the third-person camera, the shooting and runs that end. It is
single-player, not co-op, and has no extraction.

### The short version (plain words)
Most of the loudest reviews are a fight about fan service, not about play. 12 reviews say the game
censors itself (the shadow under the skirt), 9 of them thumbs down, and those 12 carry 52% of every
helpful vote on the game. The seven most-helpful English reviews are all this claim. On the other side,
11 reviews say buyers were wrong to expect more, all 11 thumbs up. Under the fight, the real complaint
is the price (22 times it was said, 14 from people who still recommend it), and the real design
complaint is the floor-skipping key that is used up.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **Buyers were wrong to expect more** (the other side of the fan-service fight). 11 times it was
   said, all 11 thumbs up. *"a game that is identical across all platforms and regions"* (207594888).
   *"It's not a H-game. It was never advertised as one"* (208277404). Four more say the negative
   reviews are not about the game.
2. **Funny, memorable writing and characters.** 9 times it was said, all thumbs up.
3. **Shooting that hits hard.** 6 times it was said, all thumbs up.
4. **A game you can relax to.** 6 times it was said, all thumbs up.
5. **It runs well.** 6 times it was said, all thumbs up.
6. **Cheap-looking in a good way.** 11 reviews describe it by naming budget games (EDF, Onechanbara,
   Simple 2000, Lollipop Chainsaw, Hi-Fi Rush). *"It's slop but it's good slop."* (218057177).

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **The price is too high for what it is.** 22 times it was said, the most common thing said about
   the game; 14 from thumbs-up reviews (people who stayed). *"an easy recommendation... at like
   $20-30. 50 is complete nuts lol. Do not pay that."* (207594888).
