2. **The game visibly censors itself.** 12 times it was said, 9 from thumbs-down reviews (people who
   left). Many are one word long: *"Censored"* (207472674, 36 helpful). *"The game is censored. Because
   of that, I cannot recommend the game."* (207503610, 97 helpful).
3. **Buy it only on sale.** 10 times it was said, 6 from thumbs up.
4. **The floor-skipping key is used up when you use it.** 9 times it was said, 5 thumbs up and 4
   down. A second death costs more than the first. *"smarter players will never use their higher
   level keys, because you need to build up your arsenal so you don't get smoked"* (217406229). *"If
   you only have one 85 key and die, your starting at the lobby."* (208277404).
5. **Runs take too long.** 8 times it was said; runs of two to seven hours.
6. **The game changes your aim sensitivity by itself near traps.** 5 times it was said, all this game;
   one says it was patched. (*"When you're near traps it doubles your sensitivity"*, 207491180, from
   the open-gaps file.)
7. **You cannot save and come back.** 4 times it was said. *"desperately needs a quicksave between
   floors"*.
8. Repetitive: part of the recommenders' usual line ("overpriced, repetitive, and I liked it"); no
   count on the page.

Overall: 181 complaints against 81 pieces of praise (0.45 praise for each complaint). 47.5% of the
complaints are on thumbs-up reviews; a recommending reviewer writes 1.06 complaints.

### How it changed over time
The page gives no month-by-month trend. All reviews are from the first eleven months. The one change
named is a patch to the aim-sensitivity bug.

### Co-op and online play
Single-player. The page records no co-op requests.

### Combat, movement and feel
- The shooting hits hard: 6, all thumbs up.
- Aim sensitivity changes by itself near traps and pits (doubles or resets): 5, one says patched.
- Relaxed pace: 6, all thumbs up.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
The page records nothing on enemy types or bosses. The danger it records is losing a long run: the
used-up key means the second death costs more than the first, so players hoard keys to build up first.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Keys skip floors and are spent when used: 9 (5 up, 4 down).
- Players hold back their best keys to build up gear first (217406229).
- Nothing on loot or randomness.

### Runs, content and replay value
A hundred floors. Runs of two to seven hours (8 say too long). No save between floors (4). The page
says every review that reached the top says some version of this: a hundred floors, no mid-run save,
and a checkpoint you spend.

### Money and price
- $49.99 at launch. Too high: 22 times (14 from people who still recommend it). Sale only: 10 (6 up).
- 7 reviewers say they paid bundle prices: $2.77, $10, $25 for nine games, and judge it worth that.
  *"I paid $2.77, that was a 9 games bundle for $25"* (215897327). *"the value of the bundle was
  probably almost $100 since these games are so overpriced"* (219672669).
- Talk about price is 11.1% of everything said about this game, the highest of any game in the study;
  the co-op average is 7.1%.
- The 22 price reviews carry 19% of the helpful votes.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- It runs well: 6, all thumbs up.
- Aim sensitivity changes near traps: 5, reportedly patched.
Nothing else on the page.

### The studio, updates and community
The page records nothing on update pace or studio talk beyond the sensitivity patch. The community
story is the fan-service fight: 12 against and 11 defending, plus 4 who say the bad reviews are not
about the game.

### What players asked for
- A lower price, or wait for a sale (22 and 10).
- A save between floors (4: *"desperately needs a quicksave between floors"*).
- Keys that are not used up (9).
- Shorter runs (8).
- No shadow under the skirt (12).
- Aim sensitivity that stays put (5).

### Only in this game
- The claim that the game visibly censors itself (12) is used by this game alone, and it decided the
  public face of the game: 750 helpful votes, 52% of all helpful votes; the seven most-helpful reviews
  are all this claim, and five of them are under ten words. A buyer sorting by "most helpful" sees
  nothing else.
- The used-up floor key (9) is used by this game alone.
- The aim sensitivity that changes near traps (5) has been seen only here.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A few short, angry reviews can become the whole store page.**
   Evidence: 12 reviews carry 52% of all helpful votes; the seven most-helpful are one claim.
   Dominion could: find out before launch what one-line complaint could top the store page, and
   either fix it or address it plainly on the page.
   Strength: medium (one game, but the numbers are stark).
2. **Do not make the reward for progress something you lose by using it.**
   Evidence: 9 say the floor key is used up, so smart players hoard instead of using it.
   Dominion could: make checkpoints, skips or saved progress in a run permanent once earned, or
   rebuyable at a fair cost, so using them never feels like a trap.
   Strength: weak to medium.
3. **Long runs need a way to stop and come back.**
   Evidence: 8 say runs are too long (two to seven hours), 4 ask for a save between floors.
   Dominion could: keep runs short, or allow the host to save the team's run between stages and
   resume with the same group; for a listen server, decide what happens if the host has to leave.
   Strength: medium.
4. **The game must never change the player's input settings on its own.**
   Evidence: 5 times it was said that sensitivity doubled near traps; patched.
   Dominion could: never scale mouse or stick input in special zones; test aim with every camera mode.
   Strength: weak (one game, one bug), but a cheap rule to keep.
5. **Price shapes the whole conversation.**
   Evidence: 22 too high (14 from recommenders), 10 sale only, 7 paid bundle prices and found it fair;
   price talk is 11.1% of everything said, the highest in the study.
   Dominion could: price for the hours and polish players will get; a full price on a budget-feel
   game is judged harshly even by fans.
   Strength: medium.
6. **Punchy shooting and a sense of humour win fans even in a cheap-looking game.**
   Evidence: hard-hitting shots 6, funny writing 9, all thumbs up.
   Dominion could: spend on hit feel and personality before spending on extra content.
   Strength: weak to medium.

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. Only eleven months of reviews; no trend is drawn.
- 10 reviews (8.6%) were edited after posting and are counted on the date first written.
- 119 reviews in other languages (51% of the audience) were not read.
- Whether the shadow is censorship cannot be settled from reviews; the page records both sides.
- 15 of 81 thumbs-up reviews (19%) say nothing.
- The sensitivity patch is what a reviewer says; the page did not check it.

---

## Banzai Escape

**Read:** all 80 of 80 English reviews there were when the reviews were pulled (100%). Every English
review was read, so there is no margin of error; one more review has arrived since. Written March 2016
to July 2026 (48 months with reviews, across ten years, every one read). **63.8% thumbs up** in the
English reviews, 51 up and 29 down (Steam, all languages: 68.5% of 168 reviews, Mostly Positive).
**Pages:** `findings/banzai-escape-english.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 15. There is no separate `findings/banzai-escape.md` page.

**What it is:** a single-player on-rails cover shooter (like the arcade game Time Crisis, played with a
keyboard and mouse) from 2016, made by one person and ported from Android phones. About $6. Missions
you replay to earn money; money buys guns and ammunition. Not a roguelike and not a third-person
shooter. The oldest game in the block: 26 of its 80 reviews are from 2016 and no later year has more
than eight. The engine is not on the page.

**How close to Dominion:** not close. It shares shooting, cover and boss fights. It is single-player,
on rails, made for phones first, and has no runs, co-op or extraction. Its lessons are about economy,
fairness and controls.

### The short version (plain words)
One rule is the whole complaint: you have to buy your ammunition. It was said 17 times, twice as often
as anything else, 11 of them thumbs down, and it shows up in every year from 2016 to 2021 and again in
2024 and 2026. The other half of the complaints come from it being a phone game moved to PC. The
people who like it say it is small, cheap, funny and hard, and five of them want a sequel.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **Want a sequel.** 5 times it was said, all thumbs up; more, for the game's size, than any other
   game in the study. *"I'll be waiting for Episode 2!"* (35074164).
2. **Hard in a good way.** 5 times it was said, 4 thumbs up.
3. **Funny, memorable writing and characters.** 5 times it was said, 4 thumbs up.
4. **A likeable cast of characters.** 4 times it was said, all thumbs up.
5. **Made with passion.** 3 reviewers in 2016 and 2017 name the developer and say so; one grades it as
   a first game.
6. **Good voice acting**, some say: *"pretty good for a low cost game. Well done."* (25319033). (See
   complaints: others say the opposite about the same voices.)

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **You must buy your ammunition, so you replay old levels to afford it.** 17 times it was said, 11
   from thumbs-down reviews (people who left). Run out of ammo mid-mission and you fail.
   *"Playing the same mission over, and over, and over again to earn money to buy ammo to use a good
   gun in order to complete the mission is not a good gameplay mechanic."* (25319033). *"This is the
   first shooter I've seen do this, and I can see why."* (55038865). *"grinding the same level again,
   and again... to eventually have enough money to buy enough ammo to fight a boss for 5min
   straight"* (226313875, 2026). This one rule is 9% of everything said about the game and 16% of all
   complaints.
2. **Controls do not respond.** 9 times it was said. The laser room is steered by dragging the mouse:
   *"the character moves everywhere except where you want"*.
3. **Deaths feel unfair.** 8 times it was said: hit while behind cover, no warning markers, melee
   attacks that kill instantly.
4. **A control is missing.** 5 times it was said: there is no weapon switch, so you may have to fight a
   boss with the pistol.
5. **Bad voice acting.** Voice acting comes up 8 times, 5 of them thumbs down: *"6th grade school
   theater level of bad"* (77141318). Some praise the same voices.
6. **It is a phone game.** 4 times it was said. *"a mobile port... the HUD and all the point and click
   stuff instead of a proper keyboard binding"*.
7. **Too short.** 5 times it was said, all 5 thumbs up: a complaint from people who still recommend it.

Overall: 105 complaints against 53 pieces of praise (0.50 praise for each complaint). A recommending
reviewer writes 0.86 complaints, the lowest in the block. Fans write short; critics write about ammo.

### How it changed over time
No trend is claimed: 26 reviews in 2016 and under nine in every later year. The ammo complaint is the
same in every year from 2016 to 2021 and again in 2024 and 2026. The developer told a 2018 reviewer it
would be fixed in the sequel (47975155, the reviewer says); this game was never changed.

### Co-op and online play
Single-player. The page records no co-op requests.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Controls that do not respond (9), mostly the mouse-drag laser room.
- No weapon switch (5), so the gun you have is the gun you fight the boss with.
- Interface and controls built for touch, not for a keyboard (4).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Unfair deaths (8): hits through cover, no warning markers before attacks, instant-kill melee.
- Hard in a good way (5, 4 thumbs up). So players liked the challenge but not the unfair parts.
- Bosses that take five minutes of ammo you had to grind for (226313875).

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Money buys guns and ammo; running out of ammo fails the mission; the fix is to replay old missions
  for money. 17 times it was said, 11 thumbs down. The single largest complaint, by a factor of two.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Too short (5), all from people who recommend it.
- The replaying it does have is forced replaying for money, which players hate (17).

### Money and price
About $6. The page records no price complaints. The money problem is inside the game (the ammo
economy), not the store price.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
The page records no crash or performance findings. The tech problems are the port: four complaints
(unresponsive controls, unfair losses, missing controls, built for phones) the page says share one
cause, the move from phone to PC.

### The studio, updates and community
- One developer, named by 3 early reviewers who say it was made with passion.
- Never praised for updates, never called abandoned. The developer told a 2018 reviewer the ammo issue
  would be fixed in a sequel (47975155); this game kept it for ten years.

### What players asked for
- A sequel (5).
- No paying for ammo, or no forced grinding to afford it (17).
- A weapon switch key (5).
- Proper keyboard and mouse controls instead of phone controls (4, plus 9 on unresponsive controls).
- Warning markers before attacks; cover that works (8).
- More game (5).

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by this game alone. What stands out: one economy rule complained about for ten years
without change, and four separate complaints that the page says all come from one cause (the phone
port). The page notes the study files them as four feelings on purpose; adding them up to one cause is
the developer's job.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Never make players pay for the basics they need to finish content.**
   Evidence: 17 times it was said, 11 thumbs down, every year for ten years; 16% of all complaints.
   Dominion could: keep ammo found or refilled in the run; if ammo is a resource, never let running
   out mean grinding old content to come back; if extraction rewards buy gear, keep a free basic kit.
   Strength: medium (one small game, but the largest and longest-lasting complaint in it).
2. **Cover must protect and attacks must warn.**
   Evidence: 8 times it was said that deaths felt unfair: hit behind cover, no warning markers,
   instant-kill melee.
   Dominion could: make cover rules exact and readable; give every big enemy attack a wind-up and a
   visual or sound warning; avoid instant kills without a tell.
   Strength: medium.
3. **Players expect to switch weapons whenever they want.**
   Evidence: 5 times it was said; a boss fought with only a pistol.
   Dominion could: always allow switching and rebinding.
   Strength: weak to medium.
4. **Build controls for the platform players are on.**
   Evidence: 4 say it is a phone port, 9 say controls do not respond, all from one cause.
   Dominion could: design for keyboard and mouse and for controller from the start, with full
   rebinding.
   Strength: medium.
5. **A small, funny, hard game earns a loyal few.**
   Evidence: 5 want a sequel; funny writing 5, likeable cast 4, satisfyingly hard 5.
   Dominion could: give characters a voice and humour; it buys goodwill that bugs and roughness do not
   erase.
   Strength: weak.
6. **When a fix is promised for "the sequel", players remember.**
   Evidence: the 2018 reply (47975155); the complaint continued to 2026.
   Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read at the time of the pull; one more has arrived since.
- 26 reviews in 2016 and under nine in every other year: no trend is claimed.
- 6 reviews (7.5%) were edited after posting.
- 88 reviews in other languages (52% of the audience) were not read.
- 19% of the reviews say nothing (9 thumbs up, 6 thumbs down).
- The developer's 2018 reply is what the reviewer says; the page did not check it.
- 2 reviews marked English were in Spanish and Indonesian; they were read from their content.

---

## SCP: Abhorrent

**Read:** all 69 of 69 English reviews there were when the reviews were pulled (100%). Every English
review was read, so there is no margin of error; one more review has arrived since. Written March 2022
to July 2026 (25 months with reviews, every one read). **63.8% thumbs up** in the English reviews, 44
up and 25 down (Steam, all languages: 57.7% of 111 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/scp-abhorrent-english.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 15. There is no separate `findings/scp-abhorrent.md` page.

**What it is:** a free, thirty-minute horror game set in the SCP world, from 2022, made by students. A
pitch-black corridor built by the game, a pistol that only kills with headshots, a flamethrower, and an
opening cutscene people cannot stop talking about. The page does not say how many players it takes,
and nothing on it mentions co-op. The camera, engine and hosting are not on the page. 42 of its 69 reviews are from 2022.

**How close to Dominion:** not close. It shares shooting and a corridor the game builds for you. It is
free horror, very short, with no runs, co-op or extraction.

### The short version (plain words)
The praise is for how bad it is. The most common real praise is funny writing (11 times it was said),
but 4 of those 11 are on thumbs-down reviews: people are laughing at the game. *"greatest opening
cutscene of all time. completely unplayable tho"* (158938519). The one honest complaint is that it is
too dark to see (6 times). The studio fixed that with a brightness slider after a reviewer complained,
and the reviewer changed his thumb to up. 19 of the 69 reviews (28%) say nothing at all.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The writing is funny or memorable** (mostly as a joke). 11 times it was said, 4 of them on
   thumbs-down reviews. *"I was excited because the intro was so bad it's good, then I was
   disappointed because the game was so bad it's bad"* (131402396). *"It's free, so get it to get
   omegalulz out of how bad it is."* (112645927, thumbs up).
2. **The music fits.** 7 times it was said, all thumbs up: *"Music slaps"*. The only praise the page
   reads as sincere.
3. **The developers fixed a problem after a review** (see Studio).

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **Too dark to see.** 6 times it was said, 4 thumbs down. *"Visibility was too limited for me to
   continue playing."* (117699810).
2. **Unplayable / bad** in general, wrapped in jokes: *"completely unplayable tho"* (158938519); *"so
   bad it's bad"* (131402396). The page gives no separate count.

Overall: 39 complaints against 46 pieces of praise (1.18 praise for each complaint), the only game in
the block with more praise than complaint. The page warns that this number is a joke told straight:
a quarter of the bullets are ironic praise. It does not mean the game is good.

### How it changed over time
No trend is claimed: 42 of 69 reviews are from 2022 and no later year has more than ten. The one
change named is the brightness slider.

### Co-op and online play
The page records nothing on co-op or online play, and no requests for it.

### Combat, movement and feel
A pistol that only kills with a headshot, and a flamethrower. The page records no counted findings on
how it feels.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
The page records nothing counted. One reviewer jokes that the true ending can only be reached by NOT
typing the god-mode cheat: *"the true ending which you can 100% only get by NOT pressing tilda and
typing in God"* (116897590). That is the third time in the study a reviewer mentions using a cheat to
get through.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
Nothing on the page. It is a thirty-minute game.

### Runs, content and replay value
Thirty minutes, in a corridor the game builds. The page records no counted findings on length.

### Money and price
Free. The free price is part of the joke recommendation: *"It's free, so get it..."* (112645927).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
Too dark to see (6). One reviewer's monitor showed nothing; a brightness slider fixed it (112105387).

### The studio, updates and community
**The studio answered a review and fixed the problem.** 112105387 did not recommend the game because
his monitor showed nothing. *"As you can see from the comments to my review, the developers quickly
released a patch fixing this issue using a brightness slider."* He changed his thumb to up. The page counts this as the
fourth time in the block a reviewer says the studio replied to their review, and the second time the
reply changed the verdict.

### What players asked for
- A way to see in the dark (6): a brightness setting, which came.
The page records no other requests.

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by this game alone. What stands out: the only game in the study whose praise is mostly
ironic, and the fewest things said per review of any game read (1.41), with 37 different kinds of
comment, the fewest of any game. The page calls it a floor: below about 1.5 comments per review, the
reviews read more like a poll than text.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Darkness must never stop players from seeing.**
   Evidence: 6 times it was said that it is too dark, 4 thumbs down; a brightness slider turned one
   thumbs down into a thumbs up.
   Dominion could: ship with a brightness and gamma setting and a calibration screen; test dark areas
   on cheap monitors; keep enemies readable in the dark (outlines, glow, sound).
   Strength: medium (small count, but a cheap fix that flipped a verdict).
2. **A fast, visible fix after a public complaint can change the review.**
   Evidence: 112105387 flipped to thumbs up after the studio replied and patched.
   Dominion could: read reviews in the launch weeks, reply where a fix is coming, and point to the
   patch when it lands.
   Strength: weak (one review), but repeated across the block (see the block entry).
3. **Laughs are not the same as liking.**
   Evidence: 4 of 11 "funny writing" comments are on thumbs-down reviews; the high praise number is
   ironic.
   Dominion could: when reading its own playtest or review praise, check whether people laugh with the
   game or at it.
   Strength: weak.
4. **Music can carry a weak game.**
   Evidence: 7 times it was said that the music fits, all thumbs up, the only sincere praise.
   Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read at the time of the pull; one more has arrived since.
- 42 of 69 reviews are from 2022; no trend is claimed.
- 4 reviews (5.8%) were edited after posting.
- 42 reviews in other languages were not read.
- 19 of 69 reviews (28%) say nothing: 12 thumbs up, 7 thumbs down.
- The patch is what the reviewer says; the page did not check it.
- The page cannot record why players laugh; the study has no way to mark praise as ironic.
- 1 review marked English was in Spanish; it was read from its content.

---

## Alien Dawn

**Read:** all 74 of 74 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin of
error. Written October 2021 to August 2026 (21 months with reviews, every one read). **63.5% thumbs
up** in the English reviews, 47 up and 27 down (Steam, all languages: 64.0% of 100 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/alien-dawn-english.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 15. There is no separate `findings/alien-dawn.md` page.

**What it is:** a co-op open-world survival shooter against aliens, playable in VR and on a normal
screen, and VR players and normal-screen players share the same server. Made by two people. In early
access since October 2021 at about $15, still in early access. A session: crash-land, loot farmhouses,
avoid the alien mothership, reach the helicopter to get out. One reviewer describes a change of engine;
the page does not name the engines. Player count and hosting model are not on the page.

**How close to Dominion:** fairly close in shape. Co-op, sci-fi (aliens), PvE, a session that ends by
reaching a helicopter to leave (close to extraction). It differs in being open world, survival, VR-first
and not built on runs. The camera is first person in VR; the page does not say what the flat-screen
camera is.

### The short version (plain words)
The two developers are the most-praised thing about this game. 21 of the 74 reviews say something
about them, and 20 of those 21 are warm. 6 say they bought it to fund the studio. It is the one game in
the block that players did not call abandoned: a 2025 edit says the new update was *"proof the devs
have been doing their jobs"*. VR is the fault line: playing VR and normal-screen players together is
loved (5 times, all thumbs up), but VR that will not launch or misreads your hands is the main reason
people leave (8 times, 7 thumbs down).

### What players praised, most to least
1. **The developers listen and act.** 7 times it was said, all 7 thumbs up. *"invite those who have
   negative experiences to the discord to chat about where the problems lie"* (101695010).
2. **Bought it to support the studio.** 6 times it was said, 4 thumbs up. *"Buy to support
   development, but not to play a game yet as it is still very WIP."* (104979629, a thumbs down
   *"only because Steam has no sideways thumbs"*).
3. **VR and normal-screen players can play together.** 5 times it was said, all thumbs up. *"The fact
   that you can play with friends that don't have VR is amazing."* (101852717).
4. **Graded kindly because the studio is small.** 4 times it was said, 3 thumbs up.
5. **Players know the developers by name.** 3 times it was said, all thumbs up. *"I had a lot of fun
   running with the developers (Christian and Jes) the day after writing this review"* (101584057).
6. **Still supported.** 2 reviews, 2022 (*"Devs are NOT abandoning this game"*) and a 2025 edit.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **VR controls do not respond well.** 10 times it was said, 5 thumbs up and 5 down: grabbing,
   holsters, the circle menus for the inventory.
2. **It does not work on my VR setup.** 8 times it was said, 7 from thumbs-down reviews (people who
   left): will not launch in VR, hands misaligned on Valve Index controllers, launches in the wrong VR
   system (Windows Mixed Reality), player height stuck. *"Wouldn't buy in its current state if you
   intend on playing VR."* (103293267).
3. **The world has nothing to remember or find your way by.** 4 times it was said. *"there are no
   unique landmarks that are important in open world games for pathfinding... Everything is designed
   on a flat axis plane"* (104979629).
4. **Motion sickness in VR.** 2 times it was said, both thumbs down.
5. **Too early to play** ("still very WIP"), from people who bought to support it.
6. One support request went unanswered (the one cold comment out of 21 about the developers).

Overall: 68 complaints against 70 pieces of praise (1.03 praise for each complaint). The page says
this is real, not ironic, and mostly praise for the developers. It warns that it does not make this a
better game than larger ones; it is a smaller game with a closer studio.

### How it changed over time
63 of the 74 reviews are from 2021 and 2022. The game after the engine change one reviewer describes
is covered by only eleven reviews, so no trend is claimed. What the page does show: "still supported"
in 2022 and again in a 2025 edit; never "abandoned".

### Co-op and online play
- VR players and normal-screen players on one server: 5 times praised, all thumbs up. The page notes
  this is the reason to buy and, when VR breaks, the reason it does not work.
- The page records nothing about hosting, joining, lag or scaling with player count.
- Players played alongside the developers (101584057).

### Combat, movement and feel
- VR handling of guns and gear (grabbing, holsters, inventory circles) does not respond well: 10.
- Motion sickness: 2.
Nothing on the normal-screen feel.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
The page records nothing counted on enemies, the mothership or difficulty.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
Looting farmhouses is part of the session. The page records nothing counted on loot or progression.

### Runs, content and replay value
The open world has no landmarks and is flat (4). Nothing counted on session length or replay.

### Money and price
About $15 in early access. 6 bought it to fund the studio. The page records no price complaints.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
VR setup failures (8, 7 thumbs down): will not launch in VR, Index hands misaligned, launches in
Windows Mixed Reality, height stuck. Nothing else on the page.

### The studio, updates and community
- 21 of 74 reviews mention the two developers; 20 are warm.
- The developers invite unhappy players to their Discord to talk (101695010).
- Players know them by name and played with them (3; 101584057).
- Still updating in 2025, players say; called "not abandoned" in 2022 and 2025; never called
  abandoned (0).
- The only block game whose biggest praise group is the studio itself.

### What players asked for
- VR that launches and tracks correctly on every headset (8).
- Better VR grabbing, holsters and menus (10).
- Landmarks in the world (4).
- Comfort settings against motion sickness (2).
- A finished game (the "support it, but wait" reviews).

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by this game alone. It is the first VR game in the study, and the first where playing
VR and normal-screen players together came up. It is the one game in the block that kept being
supported, and its reviews say so in 2025.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A small studio that talks to players, by name, earns praise that outweighs rough edges.**
   Evidence: 21 of 74 reviews mention the two developers, 20 warmly; 7 say they listen and act; 3 name
   them; 6 bought to fund them.
   Dominion could: be present in a Discord, invite unhappy players to talk, play with the community,
   and let players know who the team is.
   Strength: medium.
2. **Keeping updates going is the difference from the rest of the block.**
   Evidence: 0 abandonment comments here against 9 in ArcRunner; "still supported" in 2022 and 2025.
   Dominion could: plan a long, steady update rhythm rather than a launch burst.
   Strength: medium (read with the block).
3. **Letting players on different setups play together is loved; a broken setup is fatal.**
   Evidence: crossplay of VR and normal screens praised 5 times (all thumbs up); setup failures 8
   times (7 thumbs down).
   Dominion could: if it lets mixed inputs or platforms play together, test every combination; a
   player whose setup does not launch writes a thumbs down.
   Strength: medium.
4. **Open spaces need landmarks.**
   Evidence: 4 times it was said; the same complaint about empty spaces came up for ArcRunner's arenas
   and Zombie Girl's floors.
   Dominion could: give every arena and extraction map tall, unique landmarks to find your way and
   call out positions to teammates.
   Strength: weak to medium (small counts, three games).
5. **Players will pay to support a studio they trust, but say so in the thumb.**
   Evidence: 6 bought to support; one gave a thumbs down only because there is no middle option.
   Dominion could: be honest about early access state on the store page so supporters are not
   reviewing a promise.
   Strength: weak.

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. 63 of 74 reviews are from 2021 and 2022; the game
  today is covered by eleven reviews. No trend is claimed.
- 7 reviews (9.5%) were edited after posting; one moves praise from 2023 to 2025.
- 26 reviews in other languages were not read.
- 7 thumbs-up and 2 thumbs-down reviews say nothing.
- The engine change and the 2025 update are what reviewers say; the page did not check them.
- The page does not give player count, hosting model or the flat-screen camera.

---

## Die After Sunset

**Read:** all 51 of 51 English reviews (100%). Every English review was read, so there is no margin of
error. Written February 2022 to August 2026 (19 months with reviews, every one read). **58.8% thumbs
up** in the English reviews, 30 up and 21 down (Steam, all languages: 65.0% of 103 reviews, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/die-after-sunset-english.md`, `findings/roguelike-block.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 15. There is no separate `findings/die-after-sunset.md` page.

**What it is:** a third-person roguelite shooter (runs that end) from Playstark. In early access from
February 2022; full release (1.0) in August 2023 at about €20. Enemies get stronger as the sun sets, so
you keep the battlefield lit. Single-player: reviewers say co-op was promised and never came. One
reviewer thanks the publisher PQube for a free copy. The engine is not on the page. The page says
Playstark later made ArcRunner, but the ArcRunner page names a different studio (see Limits).

**How close to Dominion:** close in shape: third person, shooter, built on runs, with enemies that
grow stronger as the sun sets. The setting is not described on the page. It has no co-op (players call
that a broken promise) and no extraction.

### The short version (plain words)
The sharpest early-access fall in the block. 71% thumbs up during early access in 2022 (35 reviews),
40% in the year of full release (10 reviews), and 17% from 2024 to 2026 (6 reviews). Players say full
release made it worse, broke the first boss, and then the studio left. The broken promise is co-op: 5
reviews say less was delivered than promised, and 4 of them are about missing co-op. It is the most
negative text in the block: 80 complaints against 30 pieces of praise.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **Devoted developers, in early access.** *"the devs are extremely devoted"* (2022). The block page
   counts 1 review that the developers listen.
2. **Better than Risk of Rain 2, early on.** *"I prefer this to Risk of Rain 2 and I think it can be
   bigger!"* (110055549, 2022; the same reviewer later changed his mind).
3. The page gives no counted praise list; overall there are 30 pieces of praise.

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **Movement is slow and clumsy.** 7 times it was said, 5 from thumbs-up reviews (people who stayed),
   2 down. *"The movement is one of the weakest points of this experience, being clunky and
   inaccurate, I doubt it will be fixed."* (119558048, thumbs up).
2. **Co-op was promised and never came.** 5 times it was said that less was delivered than promised;
   4 of the 5 are about co-op. *"Never gave us multiplayer"* (110085235). *"No Koop (YET)"*
   (134003294). *"Would be super cool to have the possibility to play with friends co-op."*
   (113712549). One more asks for it with a crude word (144492573).
3. **Builds barely change anything.** 4 times it was said, 2 up, 2 down. *"The roguelike tag on the
   store page is really a stretch at this point as there is no real difference in builds or
   upgrades"* (118300691).
4. **Guns feel weightless.** 4 times it was said, 1 up, 3 down.
5. **The dodge does not work reliably.** 3 times it was said, all 3 thumbs up.
6. **Early access never grew into what it promised.** 3 times it was said, all from 2023 on.
7. **Not worth it yet (wait).** 2 times it was said, both 2022.
8. **It is not really a roguelite.** 2 times it was said.
9. **Full release made it worse.** *"the game at 1.0 is worse than it was in early access"*; a patch
   broke the first boss. *"Within 5 minutes the gun i was holding was sideways... and i fell through
   the ground"*.
10. **Abandoned.** *"There are more bugs then features. Its also abandoned."* The block page counts 1
    review that updates stopped.

### How it changed over time
| | 2022 (early access) | 2023 (1.0 in August) | 2024 to 2026 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reviews | 35 | 10 | 6 |
| Thumbs up | 71% | 40% | 17% |
| What they say | *"the devs are extremely devoted"* | *"the game at 1.0 is worse than it was in early access"* | *"more bugs then features. Its also abandoned."* |

The same reviewer who wrote *"I prefer this to
Risk of Rain 2"* in 2022 edited it: *"2 years later, the content didn't pick up much whereas Risk of Rain
2 turned into a master piece, I wouldn't recommend it now, its a below average buggy game."*
(110055549). The turn players name is the 1.0 release. Later periods are 10 and 6 reviews, so the
percentages are shaky.

### Co-op and online play
None. Co-op was promised, players say, and never came: 4 of the 5 broken-promise comments. The page
notes that the studio's roadmap is not in the study, so "co-op was promised" is the reviewers' claim.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Slow, clumsy movement: 7 (5 from people who still recommend it).
- Weightless guns: 4 (3 thumbs down).
- An unreliable dodge: 3 (all thumbs up).
- A gun held sideways after full release (bug).

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Enemies strengthen as the sun sets (the core idea).
- A patch at full release broke the first boss.
Nothing else counted.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Builds and upgrades barely differ: 4.
- "Not really a roguelite": 2.

### Runs, content and replay value
"The content didn't pick up much" over two years (110055549). Thin builds (4) are the replay problem
named.

### Money and price
About €20 at full release. The page records no price findings.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Falling through the floor: *"i fell through the ground to the endless void"* (the twelfth time in
  the study, in its seventh game).
- A gun held sideways; the first boss broken by the 1.0 patch.
- *"more bugs then features"*.

### The studio, updates and community
- Praised as devoted in early access; one reviewer says the developer commented on his review
  (118300691).
- One reviewer got a free copy from the publisher: *"Thanks Laurence from PQube for the copy!"*
  (119909633).
- 1.0 made it worse; then called abandoned.
- The page says this studio went on to make ArcRunner, whose reviews repeat the same arc and the
  same two complaints (slow, thin upgrades) a year later. The ArcRunner page names a different studio.

### What players asked for
- Co-op (4 of 5 broken-promise comments).
- Faster, cleaner movement and a reliable dodge (7 and 3).
- Builds that matter (4).
- Guns with punch (4).
- More content after release.

### Only in this game
Nothing is used by this game alone. What stands out: the sharpest fall from early access to full
release in the block, and a missing co-op mode as the broken promise.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Full release must be better than early access, not worse.**
   Evidence: 71% thumbs up in early access (35 reviews), 40% in the 1.0 year (10), 17% after (6);
   *"the game at 1.0 is worse than it was in early access"*; a 1.0 patch broke the first boss.
   Dominion could: freeze features well before 1.0, test the first run and first boss above all, and
   do not ship 1.0 with new breaking changes.
   Strength: medium (small numbers, clear direction).
2. **If co-op is promised, it must ship, and early.**
   Evidence: 4 of 5 broken-promise comments are missing co-op.
   Dominion already is co-op; the lesson is to have four-player co-op and hosting working from the
   first public build, not added later.
   Strength: weak by count, high stakes for Dominion.
3. **Movement and dodge feel decide a roguelite shooter, again.**
   Evidence: slow movement 7 (5 from recommenders), unreliable dodge 3 (all recommenders), weightless
   guns 4. ArcRunner got the same complaint.
   Dominion could: make the dodge always fire when pressed, give clear hit feedback, and tune speed
   with playtests.
   Strength: medium (two games, same complaints).
4. **Builds must feel different, or players say it is not a roguelite.**
   Evidence: shallow builds 4, "not a roguelite" 2.
   Dominion could: make upgrades change how you play (new behaviour), not just small number bumps.
   Strength: weak to medium.
5. **The early-access-to-abandoned arc repeats across a studio's games.**
   Evidence: this game and ArcRunner show the same arc a year apart (the page says same studio).
   Dominion could: plan the after-launch years before launch; the block shows small studios are
   praised early and blamed later.
   Strength: medium (read with the block).

### Limits
- Every English review was read: no margin of error. 2023 is 10 reviews and 2024 to 2026 is 6, so the
  later percentages are shaky.
- 3 reviews (5.9%) were edited after posting; one moves from 2022 praise to later criticism.
- 52 reviews in other languages (50% of the audience) were not read.
- The first count of this game missed its 40 early-access reviews; it was fixed before reading.
- That co-op was promised is the reviewers' claim; the roadmap is not in the study.
- The page's heading says every review since 1.0 is a thumbs down, but its own table gives 17% thumbs
  up for 2024 to 2026; this entry uses the table.
- Studio names do not match: this page says Playstark made this game and later ArcRunner; the
  ArcRunner page names Trickjump Games. Not settled here.
- The last review (232026981, August 2026) is a 700-word essay unlike any other review of the game;
  the page notes it reads as if it may be machine-written and tagged it on its content anyway.

---

## Aliens: Fireteam Elite

**Read:** 1,501 of 17,551 English reviews (8.6%), ±3.56%, written 2021-08 to 2026-09 (62 months; the
last review read is dated 2026-09-01). **81.3% thumbs up** in the sample (83.42% when each month is
weighted back to its true size). Steam, all languages: 79.7%, Mostly Positive.
**Pages:** `findings/aliens-fireteam-elite-english.md`, `findings/aliens-fireteam-elite.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 13 (also the table at the top of the page, and short mentions in
sections 22 and 23).

**What it is:** A third-person, class-based, ability-driven co-op PvE shooter for three players, based
on the Aliens films. Made by Cold Iron Studios, published by Focus Entertainment. Released 2021-08-23,
with no Early Access. Paid, with no free tier, plus paid add-ons and a season pass. Online play is peer
to peer: one player hosts, with host migration. The campaign is twelve missions across four areas. Bots
fill empty seats. It shipped with no text chat and no voice chat. The studio later stopped updating it.
The pages do not give the engine.

**How close to Dominion:** The study calls it the closest match to Dominion in the whole corpus, and the
only close match that worked: third person, class-based, ability-driven PvE, paid, and hosted by one
player. It differs in team size (three, not four), in being missions rather than runs, in having no
extraction or arena mode, and in being a licensed game (Dominion's setting is its own).

### The short version (plain words)
This is the study's one "ordinary success": it was good, it sold, it ran five years and it stopped.
Players loved how faithful it was to the films (193 reviews) and nearly as many said there was not
enough of it (187). Praise is about the feel - the world, the friends, the builds, the guns; complaints
are about supply - how much there is, how fast it repeats, and whether anyone is left to play with. The
co-op broke three ways at once: nobody to match with, no way to talk to the people you got, and bots
that did not cover the gap. 137 reviews (9.1%) say wait for a sale, and the page reads that as the game
being worth about ten dollars, not forty.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **Faithful to the films it adapts.** 193 reviews, the largest item after "good game, nothing else".
   One fan: *"You'll note this is not an Alien game. This is an Aliens game. The developers have really
   picked up on what made James Cameron's vision of the Alien universe unique."* (234223542).
2. **Much better with friends.** 156. At 10.4 per 100 reviews, the highest in the corpus (cross-game
   section 22).
3. **Builds are deep and varied.** 112.
4. **Shooting hits with weight.** 81.
5. **Keeps pulling you back.** 54.
6. **The atmosphere draws you in.** 51.
7. **Looks great.** 38.
8. **Each class plays its own way.** 38.
9. **Fair price.** 35.
10. **Bots make solo play possible.** 28. One reviewer (234027899) has 130 hours and clears Intense
    difficulty with one friend and a bot.
11. **Paid add-ons are fair.** 11 (against 14 saying they are not worth it).
12. **Friendly fire makes funny stories.** Present (no count): *"it helps you set new records for
    friendly fire damage. It's awesome."* (the last review read).
13. "Good game" with nothing else: 334 (22.3% of reviews), the single largest item. With the negative
    version, 358 reviews (23.9%) say nothing about the game at all.

### What players complained about, most to least
Thumbs-up reviews complain too: the 1,221 thumbs-up reviews carry 1,187 complaints (and 1,759 praises);
the 280 thumbs-down reviews carry 918 complaints (and 98 praises). That is 0.97 complaints for each
recommending review (Immortal: Unchained page).
1. **Too little content.** 187. Twelve missions; median play 13 hours. A thumbs-down that opens
   *"faithful recreation of aliens"* complains about the twelve-mission campaign (132762903).
2. **Buy it on sale only.** 137 (9.1% of all reviews), the fifth largest item and the second largest
   complaint. 9.1 per 100 reviews is second in the corpus after Redfall (17.0). *"This is a good horde
   shooter, for the $10 I paid. Heck, this is a good $40 horde shooter."* (224397410, a minority view).
3. **Repetitive.** 101.
4. **Only worth it if you already love the films.** 76, the fourth largest complaint, written mostly by
   people recommending the game ("for fans, everyone else stay away").
5. **Dead game.** 73.
6. **Bots useless in combat.** 60. Also: you cannot set your bots' class (they default to Gunner); they
   shoot you in the back and stand in your line of fire; the game itself warns you not to rely on them
   above a certain difficulty (no counts given for these three).
7. **No way to talk to teammates.** 51. *"why the hell is there no text or voice chat??"* (222497258,
   the whole review).
8. **Matchmaking finds no game.** 41.
9. **Nobody to play with.** 37.
10. **Price too high for what it is.** 30.
11. **Does not feel like the films.** 28.
12. **Paid add-ons not worth it.** 14. Paid add-ons and how the game makes money: 58 points across 15
    items, both ways.
13. **Film details missing or fenced off** (no counts): the pulse rifle without its grenade launcher; the
    Queen you cannot fight; the power loader behind glass; the pulse rifle's 99-round magazine locked
    behind a grind.
14. **The monsters are not scary any more.** *"the aliens look as scary as a dog"* (no count).
15. **Other licence complaints** (no counts): it carries over a part of the films the reviewer dislikes;
    none of the films' characters are here; the additions do not belong.
16. **Friendly fire is just a cost.** Present (no count).
17. **Disconnects:** players freezing and dropping from the host; "frequent disconnects" is one of the
    three main 2026 complaints (no count).
18. **Achievements do not track what you actually did.** Seen here (3 sightings across this game,
    ZCREW and The Anacrusis; ZCREW page).

### How it changed over time
| Year written | Reviews read | % thumbs up |
|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 355 | 81.4% |
| 2022 | 256 | 80.5% |
| 2023 | 248 | 81.9% |
| 2024 | 240 | 85.4% |
| 2025 | 240 | 81.7% |
| 2026 | 162 | 75.3% |
- Flat for five years, best in 2024, then a drop in 2026.
- The 2026 reviews are the least happy and the shortest (the last two batches are the second and seventh
  lowest of thirty on points per review).
- 2026 complaints cluster on "dead game", "can't find a game" and "frequent disconnects" - not on
  design. The page: "Nothing about the game got worse. The thing that got worse is that there is nobody
  in it."
- The cost of waiting for a sale: *"Enjoyable game when it reached my price point, too bad it took so
  long to be discounted. As the playerbase is dead now."* (229190054, thumbs down).
- The launch month (2021-08) read 88.9% thumbs up, but on only 72 reviews; it holds 25.4% of all
  English reviews.
- Edited reviews fall with age: 12.1% of 2021 reviews were edited later, 8.6% (2022), 8.9% (2023),
  5.8% (2024), 5.4% (2025), 1.9% (2026). Older reviews have simply had longer to be edited.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends make it:** 156 say much better with friends (10.4 per 100, top of the corpus).
- **Three faults chained:**
  1. Nobody to match with: dead game 73, matchmaking finds no game 41, nobody to play with 37.
  2. No way to talk: 51. No text chat, no voice chat at launch.
  3. Bots do not fill in: useless 60, against 28 who say bots make solo work; bots cannot be set to a
     class, shoot you, and the game warns you off them at higher difficulty.
- One careful thumbs-down for newcomers (228598427) walks the whole chain: no lobby, or a lobby of
  over-levelled strangers who leave or steamroll, or bots worse than nothing. He says the bot option
  should be removed, because it promises a solo game that is not there.
- **Hosting:** peer to peer with host migration. Network complaints are players freezing and dropping
  from the host.
- **Friendly fire:** both sides present - a cost to some, a source of stories to others.
- Across the whole corpus, bots being useless in combat is said 128 times and "no bots at all" only 2.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting hits with weight:** 81.
- **Each class plays its own way:** 38.
- **Atmosphere:** 51. **Looks great:** 38. Audio is 1.7% of all points (76).
- **The monsters are not scary:** *"the aliens look as scary as a dog"*.
- Nothing on movement, camera or controls on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- The Queen is in the game but cannot be fought (a complaint, no count).
- Higher difficulties: the game warns you off its bots; one player clears Intense with a friend and a
  bot (234027899).
- Strangers in matchmaking can be far stronger and steamroll new players (228598427).
- The pages give no other findings on enemy variety or difficulty.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Builds deep and varied:** 112, the third largest praise.
- **Each class its own way:** 38.
- **An iconic gun part locked behind a grind:** the pulse rifle's 99-round magazine.
- **Over-levelled strangers** in public games (228598427).
- Nothing on random drops on the pages; the study notes it has no heading for loot the game hands you
  (3 sightings across games, parked in three places).

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Too little:** 187. **Repetitive:** 101. **Keeps pulling you back:** 54.
- Twelve missions across four areas. Median hours 13 (about the campaign's length), 90th percentile 69,
  longest 2,928.

### Money and price
- **Wait for a sale:** 137 (9.1%). **Fair price:** 35. **Too high:** 30. The verdict: worth about ten
  dollars, not forty.
- **A co-op game that waits for sales loses its players:** 229190054 waited, and by then the lobbies were
  empty.
- **Paid add-ons:** 58 points across 15 items; not worth it 14, fair 11. No review reads as a full
  verdict on the season pass.
- 26 reviews (1.7%) were marked as received free. 52 (3.5%) have zero hours (Terminull Brigade, a free
  game: 30.4%).

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is 6.8% of all points (301).
- Disconnects and freezes dropping players from the host; "frequent disconnects" is one of the three main
  2026 complaints.
- No other specific bugs named on the pages.

### The studio, updates and community
- **Updates stopped and nobody talked about it:** live service talk is 1.7% of points (73), the smallest
  share of any game that ran five years. A finished game makes no update talk, good or bad.
- **Community talk** is 10.4% of points (459).
- **Some players want a sequel** (a heading built for this game; no count on the pages).

### What players asked for
- **Text and voice chat** (51 complain it is missing).
- **More missions and more variety** (187 too little, 101 repetitive).
- **Bots you can set up and trust** (60 useless; can't choose their class).
- **The film things they expected:** the grenade launcher on the pulse rifle, a Queen fight, the power
  loader in use.
- **A lower price or a sale** (137).
- **A sequel** (present, no count).

### Only in this game
- **The only licensed game in the corpus.** Nine headings exist only because of it: faithful (193) / not
  faithful (28); only for fans (76); an iconic thing is missing; an iconic thing is there but unused; the
  monster is no longer scary; faithful to one part of the series; carries over a disliked part; none of
  the characters are here; the additions do not belong.
- **"The game itself warns you off its bots."**
- **The only "ordinary success":** good, sold, ran five years, stopped. Its ending was the population,
  not the design.
- Same praise-to-complaint balance as Back 4 Blood (0.88 to 1 against 0.86 to 1) while ten points
  higher on Steam: the thumb records "would buy again"; the text records "what I would change".

### For Dominion - our reading
These include every lesson the game's own "for Dominion" page gives, marked (page).
1. **You can nail the feel and still lose on content and population.** (page) Evidence: faithful 193,
   too little content 187; the 2026 drop is about empty lobbies, not design. Dominion could: plan content
   volume and repeat-play (runs, arenas, extraction maps, modifiers) as hard as the feel; for a small
   team, systems that make many runs from few parts matter more than hand-built missions. Strength:
   strong.
2. **Build chat with the session, not after it.** (page) Evidence: 51 say there is no way to talk; no
   text or voice chat at launch; chat is the cheapest of the three co-op faults to fix and the one this
   game skipped. Dominion could: ship text chat, voice chat and a ping/marker system on day one, with
   mute and report. Strength: strong.
3. **A three- or four-player game must win its matchmaking.** (page) Evidence: dead game 73, can't find
   a game 41, nobody to play with 37; 2026 complaints are about this. Dominion could: make joining easy
   (quick join, open lobbies, friend invites, joining a run in progress), keep one shared pool rather
   than many split queues, and add crossplay if possible. Strength: strong.
4. **A bad bot is worse than no bot; decide early what bots are for.** (page) Evidence: useless 60
   against 28 who say bots make solo work; bots can't be set, shoot you, and the game warns you off them;
   across the corpus bad bots 128, missing bots 2; 228598427 wants them removed. Dominion could: either
   make bots a real supported way to play (choose their class, they revive, they never hurt you, they hold
   up on high difficulty) or scale the run to the number of players and leave bots out. Say which in the
   design, not in a warning box. Strength: strong.
5. **A co-op game's price plan is a population plan.** (page) Evidence: 137 say wait for a sale (9.1%);
   the verdict is "worth ten, not forty"; 229190054 waited and found the game empty. Dominion could:
   price so launch buyers feel it is fair, and plan early discounts, bundles or friend passes to keep
   lobbies full, rather than a long wait for a deep sale. Strength: strong.
6. **Expect a quarter of reviews to say nothing.** (page) Evidence: 358 (23.9%) carry no detail; good
   games get short reviews (2.93 points each). Dominion could: plan feedback reading knowing about one in
   four reviews is noise. Strength: strong.
7. **A licence buys an audience and narrows it; an original setting pays neither cost.** (page)
   Evidence: 193 faithful, 76 "only for fans", plus the missing-iconic-thing complaints. Dominion could:
   earn the attention a licence hands out - a clear look, a strong first hour and a reason to tell
   friends - since it has no film to lean on. Strength: medium.
8. **Fans ask "which part were you faithful to".** (page) Evidence: "not an Alien game, an Aliens
   game" (234223542). Dominion could: since it draws on its own source books for lore, pick which part of
   them it follows and say so. Strength: weak (one review), but sharp.
9. **Do not fence off the iconic thing.** Evidence: the Queen you cannot fight, the power loader behind
   glass, the grenade launcher missing, the 99-round magazine locked behind a grind. Dominion could: if
   the world has a famous threat or tool, let players fight it or use it, and do not lock the signature
   version of a gun behind a long grind. Strength: weak (no counts), clear pattern.
10. **Thumbs up does not mean nothing is wrong.** Evidence: thumbs-up reviews carry 1,187 complaints
    (about one each). Dominion could: read the complaints in positive reviews as the to-do list. Strength:
    strong.
11. **Peer-to-peer hosting needs good drop handling.** Evidence: players freeze and drop from the host;
    frequent disconnects is a main 2026 complaint; the game has host migration. Dominion could, as a
    listen-server game: plan host migration or a quick rejoin, save run progress so a dropped player can
    come back, and test bad connections. Strength: medium.
12. **Strangers far above your level ruin the first games.** Evidence: 228598427 - over-levelled
    strangers who leave or steamroll. Dominion could: match by level or difficulty, and let new players
    find a team at their level. Strength: weak to medium.
13. **Deep builds and distinct classes are what players praise.** Evidence: builds 112, guns 81, classes
    38. Dominion could: keep classes clearly different and builds deep. Strength: strong.
14. **Monsters must stay scary.** Evidence: "as scary as a dog". Dominion could: keep the main enemy's
    threat clear with sound, size and behaviour, and do not let it become just a crowd. Strength: weak.
15. **When updates stop, the game goes quiet, not angry.** Evidence: live service talk only 1.7%.
    Dominion could: expect that ending a game's support will not draw a storm, but the population will
    fade. Strength: medium.
16. **Paid add-ons split players.** Evidence: 14 not worth it, 11 fair, 58 points in all. Dominion could:
    if it sells add-ons, keep maps and modes free so the player pool stays whole. Strength: weak.
17. **Friendly fire splits people.** Evidence: present both as a cost and as fun. Dominion could: make it
    a lobby option or a difficulty rule. Strength: weak.
18. **The page's network note:** the game's page wrote its lessons for a Dominion with a 200-player
    dedicated-server target, and says nothing in the corpus speaks to that. For a four-player listen
    server, this game's peer-to-peer complaints are the right comparison.

### Limits
- 1,501 reviews, ±3.56%, not ±2.5%. The launch month (2021-08) holds 25.4% of English reviews and was
  read at 1.62% (72 reviews); it carries two thirds of the uncertainty. Nothing here speaks with
  confidence about the first weeks. Rico chose not to re-pull it.
- English only: 9,505 other-language reviews unread.
- 117 reviews (7.8%) were edited more than a day after posting.
- The study cannot tell "the matchmaking is bad" from "not enough people bought it".
- Nothing measures sales; "sold well" comes from the review count against its rating.
- It cannot see people who stopped without reviewing.
- Several sharp complaints (bots can't be set, film details missing) have no counts on the pages.
- The game's page compares it to a Dominion of "3-4" players with a 200-player dedicated-server target;
  the current brief is a four-player listen server.

---

## The Anacrusis

**Read:** 611 of 1,290 English reviews (47.4%), ±2.85%, written January 2022 to August 2026 (55 of
the 56 months; 49 of those 55 months were read in full, every review). **57.45% thumbs up** in the
sample; weighted to each month's true size, 58.49% (Steam, all languages: 46.6%, Mixed). The English
figure and Steam's all-language, all-time figure are not the same thing; do not set them side by side.
**Pages:** `findings/the-anacrusis-english.md`, `findings/the-anacrusis.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 11.

**What it is:** A co-op horde shooter for one to four players, built on purpose as a rival to Left 4
Dead, by Stray Bombay, a small independent studio whose lead wrote Left 4 Dead. Set on a huge space
ship overrun by aliens. Played as episodes: reviewers name 5 episodes and 4 to 6 hours of content.
An AI "director" moves the difficulty up or down by how well you are doing. Bots take over a player's
character when they step away. Custom maps through a map workshop (one reviewer says the map tools
taught him Unreal Engine). Paid, and also shipped on Game Pass. Early access from January 2022, full
release (1.0) December 2023. The pages do not say who hosts a game, but the studio's chat server
(Discord) is how many players find a full team. The Steam store page calls it *"a 1-8 player, sci-fi first-person shooter"* with *"4 player co-op"*
(checked 2026-10-03).

**How close to Dominion:** Close. It is a four-player co-op PvE shooter, sci-fi, on a space ship,
made by a small team, paid, with bots and an adaptive director. It differs in camera (first person,
not third), in shape (fixed episodes in a horde game, not runs, arenas or extraction), and it has no
loot or build system the pages talk about. It is a failure case: a small game that was liked,
defended, released, and then slowly emptied.

### The short version (plain words)
The thing players do most - shoot - feels like it hits nothing, and that never got fixed. "Shots feel
weightless" is the biggest single complaint (81 times), and its share of each year's complaints grew
every year: 4.8%, 5.9%, 6.6%, 8.6%. At first, players defended the game: "the potential is still
there" was said 74 times, 68 of them in 2022 and 2023. After full release that hope just stopped (4,
then 1, then 1). The players left, the lobbies emptied ("dead game" 47 times), and the studio argued
with players about the player count. The art and the map-making tools were liked the whole time.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **"The potential is still there"** - 74 times, the third biggest single thing said. This is hope
   more than praise of what is there: the design works and the missing parts can still come. 35 in
   2022, 33 in 2023, 4 in 2024, 1 in 2025, 1 in 2026.
2. **The art style looks well directed** - 58 times.
3. **The studio listens and acts, and is open about its plans** - 34 times together, mostly in 2022
   and 2023.
4. **The game is better than its rivals** - 34 times. As a rate, 5.6 reviews per 100; when Escape from
   Duckov was read, only Duckov (6.1) was higher.
5. **The map workshop** (custom maps) - 30 times. Nobody argues about it. *"honestly helped me out
   with my career in making custom maps & level design in general! This was one of my first ways of
   going into Unreal Engine."* (197189537, 110 hours)
6. **The special enemies** - 25 times.
7. **The environment art** - 23 times.
8. **Playing with friends** - 23 times.
9. **Shooting hits hard** - 21 times (against 137 saying it does not).
10. **The best game of its kind** - 9 times.
11. **The cast is funny or memorable** - 9 times (these reviewers played a median of 6 hours).
12. **Nothing else plays like it** - 8 times.
13. **Bots take over when you step away** - 3 times, all praise, even from one reviewer who does not
    recommend the game: *"It has one good innovation that when you go afk in co-op session AI takes
    control of the character."*
14. Players who buy extra things just to support the studio: 0.82 reviews per 100. When Warframe was
    read, only Warframe (0.93) was higher.

### What players complained about, most to least
The pages do not give a thumbs-up / thumbs-down split for each complaint. They do say a thumbs-up
reviewer here writes 1.16 complaints and 1.93 praises on average.
1. **Shooting feels weightless** - 81 times (3.4% of everything said), the biggest single complaint.
   With **hits look weak** (32) and **weak, thin sound** (24) it makes a group of **137 (5.7% of
   everything said) against 21 that say shooting hits hard: six and a half to one.** *"this game is
   99% shooting and that aspect feels worse in every way than L4D, so I can't recommend it over
   that."* (131738483, 30 helpful) *"They have received complaints about this since it arrived on
   Early Access and have done absolutely nothing to rectify it."* (154491765)
2. **Another named game does it better** - 77 times. (The First Descendant page gives 73 for this.)
3. **It copies an older game** - 63 times.
4. **Dead game, nobody to play with** - 47 times. With "only the studio's chat server fills a lobby"
   (8), "nobody ever heard of it" (7), "dead in my region" and "the numbers are falling", the group is
   70.
5. **Too little content** - 42 times (reviewers name 4-6 hours, 5 episodes).
6. **Wayfinding: cannot tell where to go** - 22 times on its own. With "cannot spot what you need",
   "the screen hides information" and "nothing in the level shows what happened here", the group is
   45, which would put it fifth.
7. **The characters are flat or annoying** - 32 times. But see below: these reviewers played a median
   of 1 hour.
8. **The studio talks badly to players** (punishes criticism, disputes the player count, fights
   players, ignores feedback, and more) - 28 times together, almost all from 2024 on.
9. **The studio lead's politics drove me away** - 4 reviews in 2025, two of them the most helpful of
   their batch (41 and 45 helpful). Neither talks about play.
10. **The game's world politics put me off** (cosmetic banners) - 3 times. One reviewer says the
    opposite: *"I love the pride banners"* (181915055); the study has no way yet to count that side.
11. Other games do it better, with none named - 1 time.

### How it changed over time
- **Hope died, and the complaint stayed.** "The potential is still there" by year: 2022 **35**,
  2023 **33**, 2024 **4**, 2025 **1**, 2026 **1**. "Dead game" by year: 8, 14, 15, 5, 5. After the
  December 2023 full release, people stopped saying it would get better. They did not say it got
  worse.
- **Weightless shooting grew as a share** of each year's complaints: 2022 37 of 767 (4.8%), 2023 51 of
  869 (5.9%), 2024 33 of 502 (6.6%), 2025 14 of 162 (8.6%). Named in the first month of early access,
  still the top complaint two years after full release.
- **The studio's talk turned.** Praise for how the studio talks (34) clusters in 2022-2023. Almost all
  the negative talk (28) is 2024 or later. Three reviewers tell the same story on their own: the
  public charts show almost nobody, the studio says the charts are wrong, the studio removes people
  who post them. One quotes a ban notice for linking the game's own public player-count page, with no
  reason given (139919128, the reviewer says). Later: *"the devs have now finally admitted that there
  is a low player count."* (141244492)
- **2025 is mostly not about the game.** 2025 reads 37.7% up, and its two most-helpful reviews (41
  and 45 helpful) are about the studio lead's public politics.
- The months read least were 2022-06 (64 of 345, 18.6%) and 2023-01 (79 of 299, 26.4%). The page
  calls these the early access launch month and the free weekend: months where the game reached
  people who had not chosen it.
- 125 reviews (20.5%) were edited after posting; some reviewers kept their review as a running list
  of what the studio fixed (the page gives no count).

### Co-op and online play
- **Not enough players.** 70 times said in the population group: dead game 47, only the studio chat
  fills a lobby 8, nobody ever heard of it 7, plus dead in my region and numbers falling. Reviewers
  bring their own numbers (what they say, not checked):
  - *"the monthly average players is 3.4 per day and even on a Saturday night it only peaks at 7"*
    (157013622)
  - *"the player count displayed a pitiful '1.' Yes, you guessed it, that lone soul was none other
    than myself."* (160509575)
  - *"This game had maybe 11 players before the f2p weekend"* (130514365)
- **The studio's Discord became the matchmaker.** 8 times said it is the only sure way to fill a
  lobby, one as friendly advice, one quoting the studio telling players to arrange games there. The
  cost: *"Their Discord server members are not very mature, and people get easily pissed when you have
  different opinions."* (219373901) A player who cannot get along there cannot play.
- **Bots take over when someone steps away**: 3 times, all praise. The least argued-about feature.
- Playing with friends: 23 praise. A new idea first seen here: people who play it with a family
  member (no count).
- Reviewers disagree on characters: two say the game picks your character for you even in single
  player (153599609, 194024444); another, fourteen months apart, says *"you get to pick your character
  from the menu"* (159107507). The page says to check the patch notes.
- One reviewer says fewer than 10% of owners hold the achievement for opening the game (176679912,
  the reviewer's reading of a public stat).
- Hosting, joining mid-game, crossplay and lag are not counted on the pages.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting is the core, and it feels like nothing.** Weightless 81, hits look weak 32, weak sound 24
  = 137, against 21 for "hits hard".
- **Only one reviewer of 611 gives a fix list instead of a verdict** (165340291): *"add viewbob, do a
  whole pass on animations, remake Commons from scratch, add bullet penetration on pellets / consider
  replacing hitscan, either implement physics or just death animations (enemies just sort of slowly
  lump on ground, no momentum on ragdolls)"*. He also argues that the bad sound is a red herring:
  fix only the audio, and *"The game would still have a lot more important problems at hand."* His
  view: sound (24) and visual impact (32) are both symptoms of one missing layer - no hit reaction,
  no variety in death animations, no momentum when enemies fall. One reviewer, but the only cause
  anyone gives.
- Movement, camera and controls are not counted on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Special enemies are liked**: 25 praise.
- **The basic enemies ("Commons") need remaking**, says the one fix-list reviewer (165340291).
- **The AI director** that moves the difficulty by how you are doing was first named in this game,
  and the page says players judged it both ways (no count).
- No count for bosses, unfair attacks or difficulty settings on the pages.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
Nothing on the pages. The Anacrusis pages give no counts for unlocks, loot or grind.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Too little content**: 42 times. Reviewers name **4-6 hours** and **5 episodes**.
- **Wayfinding fails twice.** 45 times about not knowing where to go. The sharpest one lists what is
  missing: *"there are no indications of other survivors than yourselves, no gore-splatter, no
  signage, no unpossessed corpses, no hi-vis paint, no HUD element"* (159128455, 19 helpful). The same
  empty rooms fail to tell the story and fail to show the way. Another reviewer (156652417) reaches
  the same point from the story side.
- **The game's own fix became a complaint.** The game auto-paths you when you wander. The reviewer
  who could not find his way then said: *"the game will just path you to where you need to go... this
  felt more like an annoyance and like the game was treating me like an idiot."* (130889109)
- **Custom maps** (30 praise) add to what there is to play.
- Median playtime across all 611 reviews: **2 hours**; 46.2% under two hours.

### Money and price
- Too little content for the money: 42 times (see above).
- Players buying extras just to support the studio: 0.82 per 100 reviews, second in the study.
- Game Pass: the game shipped there, so Steam's hours do not show everyone's play.
- 23 reviews say outright they are grading the game against the team's small size: three lower the
  bar for that, one raises it because of who the lead is, and one refuses the excuse: *"I understand
  that the team working on this game is small, but there are incredible games made by small teams."*
- No price-verdict counts on the pages.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
Nothing counted on the pages. Tech does not appear among the game's named problems. The only tech
note: one review says the achievements do not track what you actually did (one of three such reviews
in the study, each from a different game).

### The studio, updates and community
- **The studio listened early and fought later.** Praise for listening and being open: 34. Negative
  (punishes criticism, disputes the player count, fights players, ignores feedback, others): 28. The
  praise is 2022-2023; the negatives are almost all 2024 on.
- **Disputing the player count** was first named here: the studio said the public numbers were
  wrong; players could check them.
- **Bans for posting player counts** (reviewer says, 139919128).
- **The big complaint was never fixed**, and reviewers said so (154491765).
- **The workshop and the community chat are the two things nobody argues about** - though the chat
  is also a trap, see co-op.
- **A political row in 2025** about the studio lead: 4 reviews, top helpful counts (41, 45).
- **Some reviews say the game reads as machine-made**, a new idea first seen here (no count).
- **Some reviewers say the gap between what they expected and what they got was their own fault**,
  another new idea here (no count).
- **Comparison talk is huge.** 263 of 2,411 things said (10.9%) name another game, nearly three
  times Redfall (3.8%). 141 say it loses or copies; 51 say it wins or stands alone; 71 just use Left 4
  Dead to say what the game is (*"Left 4 Dead in space"*), with no verdict. Marketing and comparison
  talk is 14.4% of everything said, the highest of any game when read.

### What players asked for
- **Make the shooting land**: one detailed list (165340291): view bob, an animation pass, remade
  common enemies, bullet penetration for shotgun pellets or moving off hitscan, physics or death
  animations, ragdoll momentum.
- **Signs and set dressing so the place tells you where to go**: survivors, gore, signage, corpses,
  hi-vis paint, a screen marker (159128455).
- **More content**: 42 say too little.
- **Honesty about the player count** (implied by the 28 negative studio-talk mentions and the
  dispute story).
The pages give no other counted requests.

### Only in this game
- **Hope as its own measurable thing, and its collapse.** No other game shows optimism dying apart
  from the score falling.
- **The studio's chat server as the only real matchmaker**, and its social mood as a gate to play.
- **The studio publicly disputing the player count.**
- **Bots taking over when you step away** - praised by everyone who mentions it.
- **The AI director** judged both ways.
- **Grading against the studio's size**, a review kept as a ledger of fixes, a game that "reads as
  machine-made", "the mismatch was my fault", playing with family - all first named here.
- **The character complaint may be about delivery, not writing.** 32 say the cast is flat. One
  reviewer (166892773, 21 hours) says the lines that show character are weighted to play rarely, so
  *"you're not gonna get this all from just one hour"*. The test fits: the 31 "flat" reviewers played a
  median of **1 hour** (52% under 2 hours); the 9 "funny or memorable" reviewers played a median of
  **6** (22% under 2 hours).
- **The highest comparison rate** of any game when it was read (10.9%).
- **The study could only record one side of a political argument** (dislike of the banners, not
  liking them).

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **The action players do most must feel great first, before anything else.**
   Evidence: weightless shooting 81 times, the top complaint; with weak hits (32) and weak sound (24),
   137 against 21 for "hits hard". Its share grew every year (4.8% to 8.6%). *"this game is 99%
   shooting"* (131738483).
   Dominion could: make gun feel a gate for any public build. Give every hit a reaction (flinch,
   stagger, part damage), several death animations, and ragdolls that keep the bullet's momentum.
   Then tune sound and effects on top of that, not instead of it.
   Strength: **strong**.
2. **Sound and visual impact may be symptoms, not the cause.**
   Evidence: one reviewer (165340291) argues sound (24) and visual (32) complaints both come from no
   hit reaction, no death variety, no momentum; fixing audio alone would not help.
   Dominion could: when "feels weak" comes in, check enemy hit reactions and death motion first, then
   audio.
   Strength: **weak** (one reviewer), but it is the only cause given.
3. **An unanswered top complaint slowly turns defenders into ex-players.**
   Evidence: "the potential is still there" fell from 35 and 33 a year to 4, 1, 1 after full release,
   while the weightless complaint grew. *"They have received complaints about this since it arrived on
   Early Access and have done absolutely nothing"* (154491765).
   Dominion could: if early access or playtests name one top complaint, fix it or say clearly why not,
   before full release. Track hope ("it could be great") as a signal; when it drops, time is short.
   Strength: **strong**.
4. **A four-player co-op game needs players or a way to play without them.**
   Evidence: 70 times about too few players; 47 "dead game"; reviewers quoting player counts of 1 to
   11.
   Dominion could: make solo with bots and two- or three-player runs fully good, so an empty
   matchmaking pool does not end the game; scale enemies to party size.
   Strength: **strong**.
5. **Let bots take over a player who steps away.**
   Evidence: 3 mentions, all praise, even in a thumbs-down review; the least argued-about feature.
   Dominion could: when a player goes idle or drops on a listen server, let a bot run their character
   and hand it back when they return.
   Strength: **weak to medium** (few mentions, but none against).
6. **Do not make a chat server the matchmaker.**
   Evidence: 8 say the studio Discord is the only way to fill a lobby; one says its members are
   unpleasant; a player who cannot fit in there cannot play.
   Dominion could: build in-game public lobbies and quick join, so outside chat is a bonus, not a
   requirement.
   Strength: **medium**.
7. **Never argue with players about the player count; never ban for posting it.**
   Evidence: 28 negative studio-talk mentions, almost all 2024 on; three reviewers tell the same story
   of denial and bans; the studio later admitted the low count (141244492).
   Dominion could: be honest about numbers; answer criticism, do not remove it.
   Strength: **medium**.
8. **Levels should show the way with props and story, not with a nav arrow.**
   Evidence: 45 times about wayfinding; *"no signage, no unpossessed corpses, no hi-vis paint, no HUD
   element"* (159128455); the auto-path felt like *"treating me like an idiot"* (130889109).
   Dominion could: use lighting, signs, damage, bodies and paint to lead players through each arena
   and extraction route; keep any marker as a light help, not the main guide.
   Strength: **medium**.
9. **Small-team excuses do not save a core flaw.**
   Evidence: 23 reviews grade against the team's size, split both ways; one says great small-team
   games exist.
   Dominion could: expect players to judge the shooting by the standard of the best in the genre, not
   by team size.
   Strength: **medium**.
