10. **Content length matters for a paid co-op game.**
    Evidence: 42 say too little; 4-6 hours, 5 episodes named.
    Dominion could: lean on runs that play out differently each time, so a small number of arenas
    gives many hours.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **Count a complaint, then check who is making it.**
    Evidence: 32 say the cast is flat; those reviewers played a median of 1 hour, the fans played 6.
    One reviewer says key lines are set to play rarely.
    Dominion could: let the best character lines play early and often enough to be heard in a first
    hour; when reading feedback, look at hours played before rewriting.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
12. **A custom map workshop builds goodwill.**
    Evidence: 30 praise, uncontested; one reviewer says it started his Unreal level-design career.
    Dominion could: later, consider letting players make arenas (one reviewer says the map tools taught him Unreal Engine). A long-term nice
    to have, not a launch need.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **Art direction is the safest praise, but it does not hold players.**
    Evidence: art direction 58, environment art 23 - yet the game still emptied.
    Dominion could: keep a strong look, but spend first on feel.
    Strength: **medium**.
14. **Adaptive difficulty splits players.**
    Evidence: the AI director was judged both ways (no count on the page).
    Dominion could: if difficulty moves under players, let them see or choose it.
    Strength: **weak** (no count).
15. **Studio politics can swamp reviews.**
    Evidence: 4 reviews in 2025 with the top helpful votes (41, 45), about the studio lead, none about
    play; 3 about the game's banners.
    Dominion could: know that the studio's public voice is part of the product page.
    Strength: **weak**.
16. **Being compared to a giant is unavoidable; make it a short label, not a contest.**
    Evidence: 263 of 2,411 things said (10.9%) name another game; 77 say a named game is better; 63
    say it copies; 71 just say "Left 4 Dead in space".
    Dominion could: know which games it will be measured against and beat them on at least one clear
    thing.
    Strength: **medium**.

### Limits
- English only: 596 reviews in other languages are unread. Spanish reviews showed up in the English
  group, so Steam's language tag is imperfect.
- 611 of 1,290 read; ±2.85%. Two big months were read thin: 2022-06 at 18.6% and 2023-01 at 26.4%.
- 125 reviews (20.5%) were edited after posting.
- **Playtime is a weak guide here.** Median 2 hours; 46.2% under 2 hours; **163 reviews (26.7%) show
  zero hours, 113 of them thumbs down.** Game Pass play is not counted by Steam.
- 2025's 37.7% is a political row, not a verdict on the game.
- The line-weighting claim (why the cast seems flat) is one reviewer; the playtime test fits it but
  does not prove it.
- Player counts, bans and the character-pick question are what reviewers say; none was checked.
- The pages give no counts for tech, loot, progression, movement or hosting; those sections are thin
  because the pages are.
- Pages differ on small numbers: 2,411 things said and 3.95 per review (game page) vs 2,419 and 3.96
  (cross-game table).

---

## Terminull Brigade

**Read:** 737 of 2,818 English reviews (26.2%), ±3.05%, written 2025-07 to 2026-09 (15 of 15 months;
the 8 months from 2026-02 on read in full). **41.0% thumbs up** in the sample (40.63% when each month
is weighted back to its true size). Steam, all languages: 50.9%, Mixed. The page warns the English
41.0% and Steam's all-language 50.9% are not the same measure and should not be set side by side.
**Pages:** `findings/terminull-brigade-english.md`, `findings/terminull-brigade.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` section 12 (also short mentions in sections 21, 22 and 25).

**What it is:** A free-to-play third-person roguelike shooter for four players, built on runs and on
character abilities. Made by Pew Pew Games, published by Level Infinite. Released July 2025; a public
test came before launch. It is the only free game in the first part of the study. Online play goes
through the studio's servers: reviewers report the studio saying its stutter is a server-side problem.
Its second season changed the main mode from runs to farming gear with a fixed build. The pages do not
give the engine.

**How close to Dominion:** The closest in shape of any game read: run-based, third person, ability-
driven, four players - and failing. It differs in price model (free, with a shop for looks) and, as far
as reviewers say, in hosting (studio servers, not one player's machine). No extraction mode is described.

### The short version (plain words)
One fault decided this game: a stutter that freezes play. It is named 165 times, more than three times
the next real complaint, and with its close neighbours (frame rate swings, freezes, lag) it is 225 of
2,248 points made (10.0%). It was there in the first month, the last month and, two reviewers say, the
public test. Yet 40 of the 165 who name it still recommend the game, and 18 say they will flip their
verdict the day it is fixed. People who named the stutter recommend the game 25.0% of the time; people
who named neither the stutter nor the install reward recommend it 50.8%. A paid install campaign and a
season that replaced the run mode added smaller, sharper damage.

### What players praised, most to least
(The share that are thumbs up is in brackets where the page gives it.)
1. **Builds are deep and varied.** 27 times (77% thumbs up). The strongest praise.
2. **The game is better than its reviews say.** 19 times, all 19 thumbs up.
3. **Shooting hits with weight.** 18 times (66% up). The second-strongest praise about play.
4. **Explained by naming other games** ("X meets Y"), used as a plain description. 17 times (76% up).
5. **Looks great.** 15 times (86% up).
6. **Keeps pulling you back for another run.** 15 times (73% up).
7. **The art style works.** 15 times (73% up).
8. **Paid extras are only looks; they never change play.** 13 times (76% up).
9. **Free, so easy to try first.** 13 times (61% up).
10. **Fun under the stutter.** Many stutter reports are conditional praise: *"Besides the stutter
    issuees its a reeally fun rogue like game"*, *"lotta stuttering but kinda fun nonetheless"*,
    *"Excellent free roguelike 3PS, once they fix those lag spikes!"*, *"The bloody Stuttering in this
    game. Otherwise good."* (no ids given on the page).
11. **The design underneath works, and only content is missing.** 28 times in 2025, 3 in 2026.
12. **No player-versus-player is a plus.** 0.8 per 100 reviews (cross-game section 21; the highest
    after Escape from Duckov).
13. **The game's politics (or lack of them) drew them in.** A praise built during this read; no count
    given.
14. **Checked the "spyware" claim and found it false.** 3 reviewers (see Tech).
Overall, only 21.7% of all points made were praise once weighted (59.7% complaints, 18.6% neutral).

### What players complained about, most to least
1. **Stutter.** 165 times, the largest single complaint of any game in the study (7.3% of everything
   said about this game). 40 of the 165 are thumbs up. Neighbours: frame rate swings 23, freezes or
   hangs 17, lag and desync 20. All together 225 (10.0%).
2. **Repetitive.** 50 times - the next real complaint, a third the size of the stutter.
3. **Paid extras and the shop.** Reviews with a complaint about how the game makes money are 13.2 per
   100 here, the second highest in the study after The First Descendant (14.6) (cross-game section 25).
   How the game sells itself is 10.6% of all points made (Warframe page).
4. **Spyware fears.** 21 times: admin prompts, an anti-cheat driver, claims of spying or mining.
5. **Matchmaking finds no game.** 21 times.
6. **A copy of an older game.** 19 times (21% up). *"a copy of a copy of a copy with nothing interesting
   carried over"*, *"a clone of five other games"*, *"the characters look like knock-offs of two named
   popular games"*.
7. **Another game does it better.** 15 times (13% up). *"a named competitor twenty dollars cheaper"*.
8. **The season replaced the run mode.** 6 reviewers, 5 of them thumbs down, 4 with over 70 hours (see
   Studio).
9. **The crossover came before the fix.** 3 reviewers wrote to the licence holder, not the studio:
   *"the fact these devs decided to do a crossover without fixing the biggest thing that so many
   negatives are pointing it out is just bad"* (217246854).
10. **Updates stopped.** Appears in 2026 (no count given).
11. **The characters are designed to be sexy.** 3.3 reviews per 100 (the page files this as mixed, not
    as a complaint; cross-game section 25).
12. **Sorting worthless drops.** 2 reviewers.
13. **Motion sickness and headache from the stutter.** 2 reviewers (217246854, 219828133).
14. **The first stages of every run are dull.** 1 review here (Risk of Rain 2 page).
Each thumbs-up review carries 0.96 complaints and 1.25 praises (Immortal: Unchained page).
Thumbs split by group:
| Group | Reviews | Share | % up |
|---|---|---|---|
| Named the stutter, not the reward | 188 | 25.5% | 25.0% |
| Named the outside reward, not the stutter | 80 | 10.9% | 25.0% |
| Named both | 10 | 1.4% | 20.0% |
| Named neither | 459 | 62.3% | 50.8% |
This split held across nine batches: the stutter group stayed 24.7% to 27.3% of reviews and its
thumbs-up 25.0% to 29.0%. The page warns the 50.8% is not the game's score.

### How it changed over time
| Month | Reviews read | Named the stutter | Share |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-07 | 50 | 7 | 14.0% |
| 2025-08 | 424 | 96 | 22.6% |
| 2025-09 | 45 | 21 | 46.7% |
| 2025-12 | 97 | 12 | 12.4% |
| 2026-01 | 19 | 6 | 31.6% |
| 2026-02 | 16 | 5 | 31.2% |
- December's drop is not a fix: 33 of its 97 reviews were reward arrivals with zero hours, who never
  played long enough to see a stutter. January and February go back to about 31%.
- Two reviewers say the stutter was there from the public test (218520118, 217246854) - nine months.
- **2026 is worse than the lifetime figure.** The 62 reviews from 2026-02 to 2026-09 (every one read)
  are 32.3% thumbs up; all 81 reviews from 2026 are 34.6%; the whole read is 41.0%.
- "The design works, the content will come" fell from 28 times in 2025 to 3 in 2026, and "updates
  stopped" appeared.
- The install-reward campaign hit twice: 2025-08 (41 reviews, 36.6% up, 31 at zero hours) and 2025-12
  (33 reviews, 9.1% up, 32 at zero hours). A smaller first wave in 2025-07: 12 reviews, 25.0% up, 10 at
  zero hours.
- Season 2 changed the main mode (see Studio).
- 2025-07 and 2025-08 hold 2,193 of the 2,818 English reviews (78%).
- Population (reviewer claims from an outside tracker, not checked): four reviewers over five weeks put
  live players between 100 and 657; one (202932618) gives 466 now, 657 peak, just under 6,000 at launch.

### Co-op and online play
- **Lag and desync:** 20 times. **Matchmaking finds no game:** 21 times. The page says to lean on this
  count, not on player-count claims, when talking about population.
- **Nobody agrees what causes the stutter.** Reviewers name the servers, frame rate tied to network
  delay, the netcode, and the studio's own server-side statement. One reviewer (202002088) reports the
  studio saying so, and that the stutter freezes the whole world at once, *"so it does not put anyone at
  a disadvantage."*
- The pages say nothing about friends versus strangers, hosting by a player, joining mid-run, bots,
  friendly fire or crossplay.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting hits with weight:** 18 (66% up).
- **Stutter freezes play:** 165, plus frame rate swings 23 and freezes 17.
- **Motion sickness and headache:** 2 reviewers, both tied to the stutter.
- Nothing on movement, camera or controls on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
The pages give no findings on enemies, bosses or difficulty. The talk was taken up by the stutter, the
reward crowd and the season change.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Deep and varied builds:** 27, the top praise (77% up).
- **Season 2 locked earned gear to the new modes.** *"The first season you could equip the gear you've
  earned and play the now 'classic' mode with it... Now the gear you earn can only be used in the
  repetitive modes."* (220803108, 402 hours).
- **Worthless drops to sort:** 2 reviewers (216458142, 223751951).

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Keeps pulling you back:** 15 (73% up). **Repetitive:** 50.
- **Season 2 replaced runs with gear farming:** *"In S2, they replaced the run-based format with a
  build-based format where you play too-short levels with a fixed build to farm gear. The whole gameplay
  loop that made the game fun for me is gone - it's not the same game any more."* (219464772, 102 hours).
- **Early stages of a run are dull:** 1 review.

### Money and price
- Zero-hour reviews are 30.4% of the read (Aliens: Fireteam Elite page; that game, which is paid, has
  3.5%).
- Free to play. **Easy to try first:** 13 (61% up). **Paid extras only change looks:** 13 (76% up).
- **But complaints about the shop are high:** 13.2 reviews per 100, second in the study.
- **The install-reward campaign:** a chat platform paid its users to install and play fifteen minutes.
  It put 90 reviews in the read (the second largest single item), 76 of them at zero hours. Reward
  reviews: 24.4% up, median 0 hours. Everyone else: 43.3% up, median 5 hours. Removing all 90 lifts the
  score from 40.98% to 43.3% - 2.3 points - and the praise-to-complaint balance from 0.36 to 1 only to
  0.37 to 1. The campaign is not why the game scores 41%.
- **A crossover brought people in:** 14 times, 8 up and 6 down. A door, not a verdict.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- **Stutter:** 165; frame rate swings 23; freezes 17; lag 20. 225 in all (10.0% of all points).
- **18 reviewers say the verdict flips the day it is fixed.** One asks only to be told: *"tell me when
  you fix the stutters, or at least drop some english patch notes... so that i can better know when that
  deal braking problem for this otherwise good game is fixed"* (234031384).
- **Spyware fears:** 21 times, from an admin prompt, an anti-cheat driver and a launcher file with a
  confusing name. Three reviewers checked: *"It asks for admin to access its own launcher, which is
  unfortunately names 'rs_launcher.exe'. If you actually look at the details you can see that it is
  their own launcher inside of their own directory."* (214226524). The page's reading: a badly named file
  is a support cost.

### The studio, updates and community
- **The season replaced the game, and only the long-time players saw it.** 6 reviewers: 4, 13, 73,
  102, 147 and 402 hours; the median for all reviewers is 5 hours. Five of the six are thumbs down.
  Nobody at zero hours raised it. Six reviews against 165 for the stutter: a simple count buries it.
- **The crossover on top of an unfixed fault** read to 3 reviewers as choosing marketing over repair.
- **No English patch notes** is named by the reviewer above (234031384).
- **Updates stopped** appears in 2026.
- **Defenders are real fans:** all 19 "judged unfairly" reviews are thumbs up.
- **Copied reviews:** a script found five copies across four months; the earliest is the original.
- **"The beta was better":** five hits, two of them say the opposite.

### What players asked for
The pages give no list of requests. The one clear ask: fix the stutter, and say when it is fixed (18
say their verdict flips on the fix; 234031384 asks for English patch notes). The long-time players ask
for the run mode back and for earned gear to work in it (219464772, 220803108).

### Only in this game
- **The largest single complaint of any game in the study** (stutter, 165, 7.3%).
- **The install-for-a-reward campaign** (90 reviews, 76 at zero hours) - built as a new item here.
- **Players comparing the game to its own past self** (the season change), not to another game.
- **Spyware fears from a badly named launcher file**, with the corrections in the same pile.
- **The most negative text in the study** at 0.36 praise per complaint, yet only the third least
  detailed (3.05 points per review): one complaint does not take many words.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A single unfixed performance fault can become the whole game.** Evidence: stutter 165 times, 225
   with its neighbours (10.0% of all points), from public test to the last month; stutter-namers
   recommend the game 25.0% of the time against 50.8% for those who named neither. Dominion could: treat
   hitches and freezes as a launch blocker; test a full four-player match with the host on a mid-range
   machine, since a listen server puts the host's frame rate on everyone; hunt hitches (shader
   compiling, loading, garbage collection, network spikes) before content. Strength: strong.
2. **Players who report a fault are often fans waiting to come back - tell them when it is fixed.**
   Evidence: 40 of 165 stutter reviews are thumbs up; 18 say the verdict flips on the fix; 234031384
   asks only to be told. Dominion could: keep a public known-issues list and write short, plain patch
   notes that say "fixed: X". Strength: strong.
3. **Know and say where a hitch comes from.** Evidence: reviewers name four causes and the studio a
   fifth; no one knows. Dominion could: build in its own timing and network logs so the team can say
   whether a hitch is the host, the client or the network, and tell players. Strength: medium.
4. **Do not replace the loop players came for; add beside it.** Evidence: 6 reviewers, 4 with over 70
   hours, 5 thumbs down, say Season 2 swapped runs for gear farming and locked earned gear out of the old
   mode. Dominion could: keep the run mode as the home mode, let earned gear or unlocks work in every
   mode, and weigh feedback by hours played after any big change. Strength: medium (few reviews, but the
   most committed players).
5. **Do not pay for installs.** Evidence: 90 reward reviews, 76 at zero hours, 24.4% up; the second wave
   was 9.1% up with 32 of 33 at zero hours. Dominion could: skip "install and play 15 minutes" deals;
   if it runs promotions, aim them at people who will play. Strength: strong.
6. **But do not blame the score on outsiders.** Evidence: removing every reward review moves the score
   only 2.3 points. Dominion could: always check how much a suspected cause really moves the number
   before acting on it. Strength: strong.
7. **A crossover is a door, not a fix.** Evidence: 14 crossover reviews, 8 up and 6 down; 3 say a
   crossover on top of an unfixed fault reads as choosing marketing over repair. Dominion could: run
   collaborations or events only after the main faults are fixed. Strength: weak to medium.
8. **Name every file and permission prompt plainly.** Evidence: 21 spyware fears from an admin prompt,
   an anti-cheat driver and a launcher file with a confusing name; 3 found it was harmless. Dominion
   could: name its files after the game, avoid admin prompts, explain any anti-cheat on the store page.
   Strength: medium.
9. **Builds and shooting are what players will praise if the game runs.** Evidence: deep builds 27
   (77% up), shooting weight 18 (66% up). Dominion could: keep run builds deep and hits weighty; they
   were the strongest praise even here. Strength: medium.
10. **Do not let the game read as a copy.** Evidence: 19 call it a copy of an older game (21% up), 15
    send people to a rival (13% up); when players name other games just to describe it, 76% are up.
    Dominion could: have one clear thing of its own (for example extraction plus arena runs in a
    listen-server team) and lead with it. Strength: medium.
11. **Matchmaking must find a game even when few are online.** Evidence: 21 say they could not find a
    game. Dominion could: let players host and start alone or with bots, and fill games as people join.
    Strength: medium.
12. **Shop praise and shop complaints can live side by side.** Evidence: 13 praise looks-only extras,
    yet shop complaints run 13.2 reviews per 100. Dominion could: if it sells looks, keep prices fair and
    never tie them to play. Strength: medium.
13. **Watch the late years, not only the lifetime score.** Evidence: 2026 reads 32.3% up against 41.0%
    lifetime; "the potential is still there" fell from 28 to 3. Dominion could: track recent reviews and
    hope-type comments as an early sign that players are giving up. Strength: medium.
14. **Screen motion can make players sick.** Evidence: 2 reviewers report motion sickness or headache.
    Dominion could: offer camera shake and blur toggles and a field-of-view slider. Strength: weak.
15. **Loot nobody wants is a chore.** Evidence: 2 reviewers complain about sorting worthless drops.
    Dominion could: auto-sell or auto-skip junk. Strength: weak.

### Limits
- 737 of 2,818 English reviews, ±3.05%. The two launch months hold 78% of English reviews and were read
  at 21.6% together (July at 8.7%, so one July review counts for 11.6). The weighted and raw scores agree
  to 0.35 points.
- Free to play: hours, zero-hour share and population talk are not comparable to paid games. Nothing here
  separates "free" from "this game".
- English only: 2,855 other-language reviews unread.
- 46 reviews (6.2%) have more than one date.
- The cause of the stutter is not known; the corpus records claims only.
- Player counts are reviewer claims from an outside tracker.
- The reward wave's December figure was printed wrong twice before it settled (5.3%, then 10.3%, then
  9.1%); the gap between waves is the safe finding.
- One review (229578207, 1,470 hours) was about a different game; only its two stutter sentences were
  used. How often this happens is unknown.
- Some numbers here come from other games' pages (shop complaints 13.2 per 100 and the "designed to be
  sexy" 3.3 per 100 from The First Descendant's section; publishing share 10.6% from Warframe's page;
  no-PvP 0.8 from section 21).

---

## Redfall

**Read:** 1,015 of 3,071 English reviews (33.0%), ±2.52 points, written May 2023 to August 2026.
**48.6% thumbs up** in the sample (493 of 1,014) (Steam, all languages: 38.5%, Mostly Negative).
The sample reads high on purpose: the method reads every month at about the same depth, and 65% of all
English reviews came in the launch month. Weighted back to each month's real size, the sample reads
**42.9%** up. Never quote the 48.6% as Steam's number.
**Pages:** `findings/redfall-english.md`, `findings/redfall.md`, `findings/cross-game.md` section 10.

**What it is:** A first-person shooter for one to four players, played online (the Steam store page: *"an open-world, single player and co-op FPS"*, *"up to four player co-op"*, checked 2026-10-03). Vampires take over a
small island town, and you clear it in an open world. Made by Arkane Austin, a studio owned by
Microsoft. Paid, and also shipped on Game Pass and given away with graphics cards; a "Bite Back
Edition" was sold too. Released 2 May 2023. The game checks in with a server when it first starts.
An offline mode was added on 30 May 2024. Microsoft closed the studio on 7 May 2024. The engine is
not named on the pages. Reviewers say co-op has no public
matchmaking (you can only join by invite) and that only the host keeps story progress; the pages built
a name for each of these but give no count.

**How close to Dominion:** Close in the parts that hurt most. It is a four-player co-op shooter with
PvE enemies, a paid game, built by a studio for friends to play online, and it failed. It differs in
camera (first person, not third), shape (an open world, not runs, arenas or extraction), setting
(vampires in a modern town, not sci-fi) and size (a big studio owned by a giant company). It is the
corpus's disaster case, so most lessons here are "what went wrong".

### The short version (plain words)
Redfall's reviews are mostly about a purchase, a reputation and a company, not about how the game
plays. The two most common things said are "buy it on sale" (172 times) and "it costs too much for
what it is" (144 times). The game got much better after a patch in October 2023: reviews from
May to August 2023 are 34.7% thumbs up, and reviews from September 2023 on are 60.7% up. But Steam
shows one score for both, and that score is built mostly out of launch month. Then Microsoft closed
the studio, and today the game often will not start because it waits for a server: one in ten of the
2026 reviews read say so. The biggest complaint about the game itself is dumb enemies (126 times).

### What players praised, most to least
1. **"The game is better than its bad name"** - 124 times. This is the top praise, and it is not
   about the game. It is people arguing that other reviewers are wrong. Only 9 times does someone
   say the bad name is fair. That is 14 to 1. It ran at 13.0% of reviews in the launch half and 11.6%
   after, so people argued it during the pile-on, not just years later.
2. **The patches made it better** - 61 times.
3. **Much better with friends** - 54 times.
4. **The story is worth following** - 53 times.
5. **Shooting hits hard and feels good** - 53 times.
6. **The world is worth exploring** - 52 times.
7. **The mood pulls you in** - 51 times.
8. **The art style looks well directed** - 44 times.
9. **Bugs are rare and small** - 39 times.
10. **The places are worth looking at** - 39 times.

Four of the top ten (items 6, 7, 8, 10) are about the town and how it looks. **The art held up when
nothing else did**, and it was praised even inside thumbs-down reviews at launch. Also: 129 thumbs-up
reviews say nothing about why.

### What players complained about, most to least
Raw count first. Where the page gives it, the "weighted" share is the share of all English reviewers
once the launch month is counted at its true size.
1. **Only buy it on sale** - 172 times (12.7% weighted). Said most often, but voted least helpful:
   15 helpful votes per time said.
2. **Costs too much for what it is** - 144 times (18.1% weighted, the top complaint once weighted).
   It carries 6,107 helpful votes, 42 per time said.
3. **The enemies are dumb** - 126 times (15.2% weighted). The top complaint about the game itself.
   It carries 3,448 helpful votes. *"how are vampires so stupid in A VAMPIRE GAME"*. *"Rather than
   stalking, flanking, or overwhelming players in clever ways, many enemies simply run forward or
   stand in place."*
4. **It shipped broken** - 99 times (12.0% weighted). 4,289 helpful votes, 43 per time said.
5. **The story is thin and forgettable** - 72 times (7.2% weighted).
6. **It is buggy** - 70 times (8.5% weighted).
7. **It falls short of the studio's earlier games** - 65 times (5.9% weighted).
8. **The owner pulled the plug** (Microsoft closed the studio) - 60 times.
9. **Bugs break the session** - 60 times (5.1% weighted).
10. **It wasted its potential** - 53 times.
11. **Every session feels the same** - 52 times (4.9% weighted).
12. **The frame rate jumps around** - 49 times (6.4% weighted).
13. **Too little content** - 41 times.
14. **Another game does this better** - 41 times.
15. **The cutscenes are badly made** - 41 times.

Also named: the store page hides a deal-breaker (8 times); wanted a refund and was refused or out of
time (34 times); two reviewers ask for the game to be pulled from sale.

The page does not give the thumbs-up / thumbs-down split for each complaint. It does say a
thumbs-up reviewer here writes 1.80 complaints and 2.10 praises on average, the most complaints per
thumbs-up review of the nine games compared on the Immortal: Unchained page.

**The most helpful-voted complaint per time said is about a free copy.** "It came free with my
graphics card" is said only 50 times, but carries 3,935 helpful votes, 79 per time said, the highest
of anything said in the Redfall read. The single most helpful review in the whole Redfall read (1,070 helpful,
thumbs down, one hour played) says three things: it shipped broken, it costs too much, and it came
free with hardware.

### How it changed over time
Reviews by period (patch dates are from Steam's own news feed, so they are checked):

| When | What happened | Reviews |
|---|---|---|
| 2 May 2023 | Release | May 2023: 34.9% up, 404 reviews read |
| 8 May 2023 | Small hotfix | no change |
| 12 June 2023 | First big patch: enemy AI, graphics, stability | June 34.6%, July 30.0%, August 35.0%: **flat** |
| Sept 2023 | - | 18.8% up, the worst month (only 16 reviews) |
| 6 Oct 2023 | **Game Update 2**: performance mode, open-world rework | October jumps to 55.6% and **never goes back** |
| 16 Nov 2023 | Game Update 3: weapons, character animation | November 64.3%, December 67.9% |
| Jan 2024 | two hotfixes | holds around 60% |
| 7 May 2024 | **Microsoft closes the studio** | "the owner pulled the plug" appears, reaching 60 times said |
| 30 May 2024 | **Game Update 4**: pause, **offline mode**, DLSS 3 | "it plays offline" appears: 21 times, none before |
| 8 June 2024 | Refunds of the price gap for the Bite Back Edition | refund talk reaches 55 times |

**Two halves, two different games.** May-August 2023: 470 reviews, 34.7% up (range 30.4 to 39.0).
September 2023 to August 2026: 544 reviews, 60.7% up (range 56.6 to 64.8). The ranges never touched
in nine rounds of reading.

Share of reviews, launch half then later half:
- Price too high: 21.3% then 8.1% (fell by two thirds).
- Dumb enemies: 17.0% then 8.5% (halved).
- Shipped broken: 14.7% then 5.5%.
- Frame rate jumps: 8.5% then 1.7% (nearly gone).
- Buy on sale only: 8.7% then 24.1% (tripled).
- The owner pulled the plug: 0.2% then 10.8%.
- Crashes again and again: 1.7% then 5.5% (tripled).
- Plays offline (praise): 0.0% then 3.9%.
- Cannot reach the server: 0.6% then 2.8%.

**The game that exists now crashes more than the one that shipped.** Frame rate was fixed; stability
was not, and then the people who could fix it were let go.

Two plain points from the timeline: **the first big patch did nothing to the score**; the turn came
four months later, at Game Update 2. And **the best update shipped three weeks after the studio was
closed**: the offline mode is now the only reason a new buyer can play at all. The cross-game page
says that by the time of that patch the number of people playing at once was in single figures
(it does not say how it checked this), so the recovery changed the reviews and not the business.

The helpful vote keeps the old verdict on top: the launch-week price and broken-launch complaints are
voted about three times harder per time said than the later "buy on sale" advice. That is one reason
Steam's score stays at 38.5% while the reviews under it move.

### Co-op and online play
- **Players barely talk about each other.** Only 2.7% of everything said is about other players or
  the community. Deep Rock Galactic is 28.8% and Helldivers 2 is 28.5%; the next lowest co-op game,
  Rogue Core, is 9.2%. **A four-player co-op game whose reviewers almost never mention playing with
  anyone.** The cross-game page notes the two games with lots of "other people" talk survived, and
  the low ones did not, but says this does not prove which way it runs.
- **Friends help a lot when it happens:** "much better with friends" is the third praise, 54 times.
- **Co-op setup:** reviewers say there is no public matchmaking (invite only) and only the host
  keeps progress. The pages name both but give no count.
- **The online check at first start is the big online story.** Cannot reach the server: 18 reviews.
  3 at launch in 2023 (none at zero hours, 56 helpful votes). **13 after the studio closed (from
  November 2024), 7 of them with zero hours played, carrying 593 helpful votes.** Seven people
  reviewed a game they could never start.
  - *"Don't buy, game just is stuck on a connecting to server page."* (39 helpful, 0 hours)
  - *"offline mode doesn't exist? it won't let me start the game just gets stuck trying to connect
    to non existent servers"* (54 helpful, 0 hours)
  - *"It still tries to connect to a server that isn't there anymore and doesn't boot up cuz of it...
    Was refused a refund and the game is still for sale despite being unplayable."* (77 helpful)
- **How it breaks, from the reviews:** on first start a "connecting to server" box sits on top of the
  licence agreement. You must accept the agreement to connect, but the box covers it. The offline
  mode that would skip all of this is behind the same screen for anyone who did not turn it on
  before the servers went quiet.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Shooting is praised as hitting hard: 53 times (tied fifth praise).
- Game Update 3 (November 2023) changed weapons and character animation; the months after it read
  64.3% and 67.9% up. The page does not split out a gun-feel complaint count.
- The page gives no counts for movement, camera or controls.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Dumb enemies are the top complaint about the game itself**: 126 times, 15.2% weighted, 3,448
  helpful votes. It halved after the patches (17.0% of launch-half reviews, 8.5% after) **but did not
  go away.** It survives every reweighting.
- A complaint that **the final fight is a pushover** (easier than what came before it) was first named
  in this game. The page gives no count.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
Nothing on the pages. The Redfall pages do not count loot, unlocks or grind, so there is nothing to
report here.

### Runs, content and replay value
- Every session feels the same: 52 times (4.9% weighted).
- Too little content: 41 times.
- It wasted its potential: 53 times.
- The world is worth exploring: 52 times; the places are worth looking at: 39 times.
- The open world was reworked in Game Update 2 (October 2023), the patch that turned the score.

### Money and price
- **"Only buy it on sale"** is the most common thing said: 172 times, and 17.0 per 100 reviews, the
  highest rate in the whole study (next are Aliens: Fireteam Elite at 9.1 and Back 4 Blood at 8.6).
  It tripled after launch (8.7% to 24.1% of reviews).
- **"Costs too much for what it is"**: 144 times, the top complaint once weighted (18.1%), and the
  most helpful-voted (6,107 votes). It fell from 21.3% to 8.1% of reviews as the price fell.
- Price and selling are 11.9% of everything said, the most of any game in that comparison.
- **Free copies:** "came free with my graphics card" 50 times, the highest helpful votes per time said
  (79). Game Pass and bundles mean many players never paid and never reviewed.
- **Refunds:** wanted a refund and could not get one, 34 times. Bite Back Edition price-gap refunds
  (June 2024) push refund talk to 55 times.
- **The store page hides a deal-breaker** (the game may not start; support ended): 8 times. Two
  reviewers ask for the game to be taken off sale.
- **101 reviews (10.0%) show zero hours played, and 89 of them are thumbs down.** The game keeps
  selling, and each new buyer who cannot start it becomes another bad review.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Shipped broken: 99 times (12.0% weighted). 14.7% of launch-half reviews, 5.5% after.
- Buggy: 70 times. Bugs break the session: 60 times. Bugs rare and small (praise): 39 times.
- Frame rate jumps: 49 times (6.4% weighted). 8.5% of launch-half reviews, 1.7% after. Game Update 2
  added a performance mode.
- **Crashes rose after launch:** 1.7% to 5.5% of reviews.
- **Players found the crash cause themselves:** two reviewers each found one setting behind the
  crashes: *"switching off DLSS cured ALL the crashing"*, *"lower your shadow quality and turn on
  FXA."*
- **Players taught each other how to get past the server screen:** 8 times. *"DON'T use the mouse
  is the key, use the keyboard and hit enter."* *"press the space bar multiple times while pressing
  cancel and voilà - you're in."* *"if you spam enter, you can get it to work. if you do not do this,
  it will never connect."*
- **One in ten of the 2026 reviews read say the game will not start.**
- The engine is not blamed on the pages.

### The studio, updates and community
- **The studio was closed by its owner**: 60 times. Reviewers blame Microsoft, not the team. The
  study had to add a way to say "the owner, not the studio, ended it" and its partner, "the owner
  rushed it out", because no game before Redfall needed that split. Neither has a count on the pages.
- **"The people who made the old games have left; only the studio name is the same"** is another new
  idea from this game (no count). "It falls short of the studio's earlier games": 65 times.
- Patches: "the patches made it better" 61 times. The first big patch (June 2023) moved nothing; the
  October 2023 one moved everything; the last one (offline mode) came after the studio was gone.
- **Reputation is the biggest subject of all**: 356 times (9.3% of everything said). People argue
  about the game's name more than they describe it. 124 say it is judged unfairly; 9 say the bad
  name is earned.
- A review that calls the game simply "average, not good or bad" was first named here (no count).
- Some reviewers say the bad name set the bar so low that the game cleared it. The page says this is
  why it cannot tell "the game got better" from "cheaper, warned buyers arrived".
- Buyers did the support work: 8 reviews pass on the start-up trick and 2 pass on the crash setting.
- What is talked about, by area: how the game plays 25.6%; reputation and marketing 12.5%; tech
  12.3%; price and selling 11.9%; how it was made and shipped 8.4%; story 8.2%; the review itself
  6.6%; art 5.7%; updates and support 5.2%; other players and the studio 2.7%; sound 0.9%.
- Story is 8.2% of everything said, second only to Space Marine 2 (9.2%); the co-op games are under 3%.
  Lines worth quoting are rare: 0.6 reviews per 100. "Looks dated": 1.9 reviews per 100.

### What players asked for
The pages give few direct requests:
- Two reviewers ask for the game to be **delisted** (taken off sale) because it does not start.
- The fixes players wanted most are clear from what changed the score: an **offline mode / no server
  check** (21 times praised once it came), a **pause**, **steady frame rate**, and **smarter enemies**.
These come from what was praised after it arrived, not from a counted list of requests.

### Only in this game
- **The reputation argument.** Reputation as the biggest subject, and a 124-to-9 argument that the
  game is better than its name. No other game in the corpus has this.
- **A server check that outlived the company.** The game still asks a server for permission at first
  start, the studio is gone, and the store still sells it.
- **The owner, not the studio, killed it**, and **the owner rushed it out**: first needed here.
- **"It came free with my graphics card"** carries the most helpful votes per time said in the read.
- **Buyers as the support desk and the crash testers.**
- **Only the host keeps progress** and **no public matchmaking** were first named in this game.
- **The final fight is a pushover** was first named here.
- Story share (8.2%) is the highest of any co-op game read.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Do not lock the game behind an online check that can die.**
   Evidence: 18 reviews cannot reach the server; 13 came after the studio closed, 7 at zero hours,
   593 helpful votes; one in ten 2026 reviews say it will not start; 101 zero-hour reviews, 89 of them
   thumbs down. The offline mode came three weeks after the studio closed, behind the same broken
   screen.
   Dominion could: let the game start and reach the menu with no server at all. Let solo and
   friends-only (one player hosts) play run with no online service needed. If any online check is
   added (Steam, EOS, store), make it fail open: show a clear message and keep going. Test a start with
   the network cable pulled before every release.
   Strength: **strong** for the outcome; the cause is told by reviewers.
2. **The first screen must never block itself.**
   Evidence: the "connecting" box sits on top of the licence agreement you must accept; 8 reviews teach
   others to press Enter or Space to get through.
   Dominion could: never stack two pop-ups that both need an answer. Every first-run screen should
   work with mouse, keyboard and pad, and time out to something you can use.
   Strength: **medium** (8 reviews plus the 18 cannot-connect reviews).
3. **Smart enemies are the design fix that matters most in a PvE shooter.**
   Evidence: dumb enemies is the top complaint about the game itself, 126 times, 15.2% weighted,
   3,448 helpful votes. It halved after patches but stayed. *"how are vampires so stupid in A VAMPIRE
   GAME"*.
   Dominion could: give each enemy type a clear plan (rush, flank, hold, ambush) and test that the
   team sees it happen. Avoid enemies that only run forward or stand still. Budget for AI before
   launch, not after.
   Strength: **strong**.
4. **Launch is the review that sticks. A late fix changes reviews, not the score or the sales.**
   Evidence: 65% of English reviews came in launch month; the score went from 34.7% to 60.7% up after
   October 2023, but Steam still shows 38.5%. The first big patch (June 2023) did nothing. The
   cross-game page says few people were playing by the time the fix landed.
   Dominion could: hold the launch until frame rate, stability and enemy AI are solid. Use an early
   access or a playtest with honest labels instead of a big launch of an unfinished game.
   Strength: **strong**.
5. **A big patch must fix what players actually feel, not a list of systems.**
   Evidence: v1.1 ("enemy AI, graphics, stability") left June-August flat; Game Update 2 (a
   performance mode and an open-world rework) turned the score at once and for good.
   Dominion could: aim patches at the top two or three complaints players name, and say in the notes
   exactly what changes for them.
   Strength: **medium** (one game, clear timing).
6. **Fixing frame rate is not the same as fixing crashes.**
   Evidence: frame rate complaints fell from 8.5% to 1.7%, but crashes rose from 1.7% to 5.5%; two
   players found one setting (DLSS, shadows) behind the crashes.
   Dominion could: track crashes as their own number; test each upscaler and each high setting for
   crashes; give a safe-mode start (low settings, no upscaler) if the last run crashed.
   Strength: **medium**.
7. **Price must match what is in the box, on day one.**
   Evidence: "costs too much for what it is" 144 times, 18.1% weighted, 6,107 helpful votes; "only on
   sale" 172 times and 17.0 per 100 reviews, the highest in the study; too little content 41 times.
   Dominion could: set the launch price to the amount of content and polish there really is; a lower
   price at launch costs less than years of "wait for a sale".
   Strength: **strong**.
8. **Free copies bring the angriest, most-voted reviews.**
   Evidence: "came free with my graphics card" 50 times, 79 helpful votes per time said, the top rate;
   the most helpful review (1,070 votes) was a one-hour, thumbs-down review that says it came free
   with hardware.
   Dominion could: be careful with bundles and giveaways while the game is rough; a free player who
   bounces still writes a review.
   Strength: **medium**.
9. **Co-op needs to be easy to get into, or players stop talking about each other.**
   Evidence: only 2.7% of everything said is about other players, against 28.5-28.8% in the two big
   co-op hits; reviewers say there is no public matchmaking and only the host keeps progress; yet
   "much better with friends" is the third praise (54 times).
   Dominion could: offer quick join with strangers as well as friend invites; make sure every player,
   not just the host, keeps what they earn in a run (this matters doubly on a listen server where one
   player hosts).
   Strength: **medium** (the 2.7% is strong; the host-progress and matchmaking claims have no count).
10. **A strong world and look will not save weak play, but players do notice them.**
    Evidence: world worth exploring 52, mood pulls you in 51, art well directed 44, places worth
    looking at 39; praised even in thumbs-down launch reviews.
    Dominion could: keep investing in a clear sci-fi look and mood, but never in place of enemy AI,
    stability and feel.
    Strength: **medium**.
11. **A story that matters to players needs good cutscenes.**
    Evidence: story worth following 53 vs thin and forgettable 72; cutscenes badly made 41.
    Dominion could: if it tells a story, keep cutscenes short and well staged, or tell it in play
    instead.
    Strength: **medium**.
12. **Have a pause and an offline mode from day one.**
    Evidence: Game Update 4 added a pause and offline mode; "it plays offline" went from 0 to 21 times
    said, all praise.
    Dominion could: pause in solo; offline solo play from launch.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **Write the store page honestly, and keep it honest as things change.**
    Evidence: "the store page hides a deal-breaker" 8 times; refunds refused 34 times; two ask for
    delisting.
    Dominion could: if support ever ends or servers change, say so at the top of the store page.
    Strength: **weak to medium**.
14. **The climax must be the hardest fight, not the easiest.**
    Evidence: "the final fight is a pushover" was first named in this game (no count on the page).
    Dominion could: make sure the last arena or boss of a run is the peak of the run.
    Strength: **weak** (no count).
15. **A bad name sticks, and the people who come later spend their review arguing with it.**
    Evidence: reputation is the biggest subject (356 times, 9.3%); 124 say it is judged unfairly vs 9
    who say it is fair.
    Dominion could: protect the launch reputation above all; a strong launch is cheaper than any
    rescue.
    Strength: **strong** for the pattern.

### Limits
- English only: 1,645 reviews in other languages were never read.
- The sample is 48.6% up; Steam is 38.5%. Weighted it is 42.9%. The launch month (1,986 reviews,
  65%) was read at 20.3%; the other 39 months (1,085 reviews) at 56.3%. Even inside May 2023 the
  first, worst days are read thinner.
- 175 reviews (17.3%) were edited later; each is filed under the month it was first written.
- Steam's "English" label is the reader's client language: at least one review is in Portuguese.
- The pages cannot split "the game got better" from "cheaper, warned buyers arrived".
- Game Pass and bundles hide most players; only 50 reviews show the bundle.
- Server, refund, delisting and ban claims are what reviewers say, except the patch dates and the
  studio closure date, which the page took from Steam's news feed.
- The pages give no counts for loot, progression, movement, camera or matchmaking; those sections are
  thin because the pages are.
- One review in the read was not about the game and was left out.

---

## Helldivers 2

**Read:** 1,626 of 819,840 English reviews (0.20%); the pages give no margin of error, and call this the
thinnest sample in the study. Written February 2024 to August 2026. **83.3% thumbs up** in the sample
(Steam's own English figure, on the cross-game page: 83.3%; the pages give no all-languages figure and
no Steam rating word).
**Pages:** `findings/helldivers-2-english.md`, `findings/helldivers-2.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
sections 1 to 8 (and passing mentions in later sections).

**What it is:** A co-op PvE shooter with friendly fire, a satirical sci-fi setting (players fight "for
Super Earth" and "for Democracy"), sold as a paid game with a paid currency that players can also earn
by playing, and run as a live service with frequent patches and content updates (Escalation of Freedom,
an Illuminate invasion, Machinery of Oppression). Reviewers say a publisher account became required in
May 2024 and that the game then stopped being sold in many countries; the page calls it "the
publisher-account fight" and does not check the details. Some players object to extra software it
installs. Reviews start in February 2024; the Steam patch
record starts in September 2023. The Steam store page calls it *"a fast, frantic, and ferocious third-person shooter"* with *"squads of up
to four"* (checked 2026-10-03). The pages do not say who hosts a match, the engine, the price, or how
missions are structured.

**How close to Dominion:** Very close in what players do: third person, four-player co-op, sci-fi, PvE against alien hordes, friendly fire, and a strong in-world voice. It differs in being a big live service with a big publisher, a
required outside account and a paid currency, and the pages say nothing about hosting, so nothing here
speaks to a listen server directly. Most of what this game teaches is about running a game after launch,
not about its design.

### The short version (plain words)
Almost nobody complains about how the game plays. They complain about the people running it. Four of the
top five complaints are choices someone made: a patch made it worse (said 47 times), the publisher puts
players off (45), it is not sold in my country (39), and the studio weakened things players liked (39);
the fifth is crashes (37). The first complaint about the game itself, "it repeats itself", is seventh at
22. The game got better (crash complaints fell from 2.5 to 1.7 per 100 reviews from 2024 to 2026) while
the relationship collapsed: thumbs down tripled to 36.2% in 2026, and good things said about the studio
went from beating bad things 3.3 to 1 in 2024 to losing 1 to 9 in 2026. What stays loved all three years
is the war cry (398 times, one review in four), playing with friends (62), the satire (53), coming back
again and again (47), and friendly fire that makes funny stories (37).

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are times it was said (one review can say more than one thing). Per 100 = per 100 reviews read.

1. **The war cry.** Reviews say nothing but the game's slogan - "FOR SUPER EARTH", "FOR DEMOCRACY",
   "Liber-tea" - 398 times, 24.5 per 100, one review in four, 13.7% of everything said. Two reviewers wrote
   another game's chant by mistake ("FOR ROCK AND STONE ...wait wrong game" from Deep Rock Galactic, and a
   Killzone chant). The page reads this as a habit tied to having a slogan at all. It is fading: 26.3 per
   100 in 2024, 24.2 in 2025, 16.3 in 2026.
2. **The studio listens and acts** - 78 times (4.8 per 100). 4.8 per 100 in 2024, 7.1 in 2025, then 1.7 in
   2026. Publisher reversals were filed here too.
3. **Much better with friends** - 62 times (3.8 per 100), steady across all three years.
4. **Players take on the game's identity** - they call themselves by the game's own names - 54 times (3.3
   per 100).
5. **The satire lands** - 53 times (3.3 per 100), steady across all three years.
6. **It keeps pulling you back** - 47 times (2.9 per 100), steady.
7. **Best game of its kind** - 46 times (2.8 per 100), steady.
8. **Friendly fire makes stories** - 37 times (2.3 per 100), steady. Against only 3 negative ones in the
   whole study. The positives are self-inflicted, against friends, and funny: "dropping a bomb on his own
   squad and then apologising insincerely."
9. **A welcoming community** - 33 times (2.0 per 100).
10. **Patches fixed what mattered** - 26 times (1.6 per 100).
11. **The price is fair** - 25 times (1.5 per 100).
12. **Combat hits hard** - 22 times (1.4 per 100).
13. **The game is judged unfairly** - its reputation is worse than the game - 21 times (1.3 per 100). This
    became the most common opinion in the last batch read: the argument itself became the subject.
14. **The paid currency can be earned by playing** - 20 times (1.2 per 100).
15. **A patch made it better** - 20 times (1.2 per 100).
16. **Builds are deep and varied** - 16 times (1.0 per 100).
17. **Hard in a good way** - 16 times (1.0 per 100).
18. **It looks great** - 16 times (1.0 per 100).
19. **It works solo** - 15 times (0.9 per 100).
20. **The studio earned my trust** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
21. **A steady stream of updates** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
22. **The difficulty ladder is well built** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
23. **Fine with strangers** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
24. **The mission falling apart is the fun** - a swarm, a collapse, a panicked escape - 13 times (0.8 per
    100).
25. **Nothing else plays like it** - 10 times (0.6 per 100).
26. **Silly actions with no use that players love** (emotes and the like) - 6 times (0.4 per 100), against
    111 in Deep Rock Galactic.

Where the praise comes from: 37.3% of this game's praise is about the community, 18.6% about game design,
7.1% about marketing and reputation.

### What players complained about, most to least
Counts are times it was said. Per 100 = per 100 reviews read. The pages do not split complaints by thumbs
up or down, except where noted.

1. **A patch made it worse** - 47 times (2.9 per 100).
2. **The publisher (the owner) puts players off** - 45 times (2.8 per 100). 30 of the 45 in May 2024.
3. **Not sold in my country** - 39 times (2.4 per 100). 30 of the 39 in May 2024. Reviewers could not
   agree how many countries: they say 176, 177, 180, "over 200" and "about 170". Most of these English
   reviewers write about countries they do not live in.
4. **The studio weakened things players liked** - 39 times (2.4 per 100). 1.6 per 100 in 2024, 1.7 in
   2025, 7.3 in 2026 - up 4.7 times. It becomes the game's defining complaint.
5. **It crashes again and again** - 37 times (2.3 per 100). Falling: 2.5 per 100 in 2024, 2.0 in 2025,
   1.7 in 2026.
6. **A bug stops a mission, loses progress or spoils a run** - 28 times (1.7 per 100).
7. **It repeats itself** - 22 times (1.4 per 100). The first complaint about the game itself.
8. **Other players put me off** - 22 times (1.4 per 100). By 2026 it comes from both sides: one reviewer
   objects to players attacking the studio, another to players defending it.
9. **The thumbs down is a protest, not about the game** - 21 times (1.3 per 100). 14 of the 21 in May
   2024. "21 people said outright that their thumb was aimed at the company, not at the game."
10. **Buggy** - 19 times (1.2 per 100).
11. **The studio misreads what players want** - its idea of fun is not theirs - 19 times (1.2 per 100).
12. **The studio fights its own players** - 17 times (1.0 per 100).
13. **The studio lost my trust** - 16 times (1.0 per 100).
14. **The studio ignores feedback** - 15 times (0.9 per 100).
15. **A required account or platform link blocks or annoys** - 15 times. 13 of the 15 in May 2024.
16. **Disconnects** - 14 times (0.9 per 100).
17. **It damages things outside the game** - the computer, the sound or the system needs fixing after - 12
    times (0.7 per 100).
18. **It installs extra software I did not want** - 12 times (0.7 per 100). The label covers anti-cheat,
    launchers and companion apps; the page does not say which.
19. **Selling more while the game is still broken** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
20. **Known bugs never fixed** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
21. **Punishing solo** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
22. **Too grindy** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
23. **Builds barely change anything** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
24. **Trolls and griefers** - 11 times (0.7 per 100).
25. **A marketing claim was untrue** - 10 times (0.6 per 100).
26. **Updates arrive but add little** - 10 times (0.6 per 100).
27. **The studio punishes criticism** - 0 in 2024, 1 in 2025, 6 in 2026. The 2026 ones are four separate
    reviewers, none involved, reporting the same story: that a player who offered the studio help had
    private details posted online. This is hearsay in every case - the page records that four people
    believed it, not that it happened.
28. **The fights drove me away** - built from this game: "I game to relax and enjoy myself and I find there
    is more drama with this game than anything else I do in my life." (count not given)
29. **Friendly fire as a cost** - 3 negative reviews in the whole study, and all of them are done TO the
    reviewer by strangers, again and again: "I get teamkilled about 85% of my games."
30. **Shots stopped by scenery that should let them pass** - seen once here (and once in another game)
    before a later game made it a full label.

**Who could have stopped these?** Of the top 25 complaints: live-ops and publisher decisions, 202 times;
community and reputation, 131; engineering, 122; game design and production, 55 - the smallest group, by
a factor of four. As a share of all this game's complaints: engineering 21.7%, game design 19.9%,
community 15.8%, publishing 15.1%, live-ops 13.3%, marketing 4.2%, production 3.8% (898 complaints in all).
It is the only one of the first three games where the game itself is not the main target. Complaints about
money practices are 1.8 per 100 reviews.

### How it changed over time

| Year | Reviews | Thumbs down | Things said per review | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2024 | 1,093 | 13.9% | 1.72 | 110.2 | 48.0 |
| 2025 | 297 | 11.8% | 1.73 | 114.1 | 46.1 |
| 2026 | 232 | 36.2% | 2.17 | 89.7 | 101.7 |

- **2026 is the break.** Thumbs down tripled. Complaints per review doubled and passed praise for the
  first time. People wrote more (2.17 things each).
- **What players said about the studio, per 100 reviews:** "listens and acts" 4.8, 7.1, 1.7; the four bad
  ones together (misreads, fights its players, ignores feedback, punishes criticism) 1.5, 2.0, 15.5. Good
  to bad went from 3.3 : 1 (2024) and 3.5 : 1 (2025, the studio's best year) to 1 : 9 (2026) - a
  thirty-fold swing in a year. "Weakened what players liked" 1.6, 1.7, 7.3. Crashes 2.5, 2.0, 1.7.
- **May 2024 is the loudest month, not the angriest.** 309,103 English reviews on Steam that month, more
  than twice the launch month's 147,461. It is the publisher-account fight: 30 of 45 "publisher puts me
  off", 30 of 39 "not sold in my country", 14 of 21 "protest vote", 13 of 15 "account blocks me" all land
  in that one month. Yet its share of negative reviews read was 30.9%, not among the worst.
- **The three low points** (share of reviews read that were negative): August 2024, 48.1% (on only 10,632
  Steam reviews - a quiet, unhappy month, not a review bomb); May 2025, 50.8%; March to May 2026, 52.3%,
  62.3%, 56.2%. April 2026 is the most negative month in the whole 31-month record.
- **The best month:** March 2025, 4.2% negative.
- **Lined up against the Steam patch notes** (799 Steam news items, 103 with a version number, pulled
  2026-09-02; quotes are from that record):
  - February 2024: eleven versioned patches in one month, patches every two to three days. The least
    negative month of the launch period, 14.1%.
  - March 2024: 1.000.100 "nerfs its best weapons, adds new hazards" (03-06), 1.000.102 "nerfs hardest
    enemy and spawn rates" (03-12), 1.000.103 "sadly doesn't fix the highest priority issue" (03-20).
    Negative share nearly doubled, 14.1% to 25.1%. The earliest dated start of the weakening complaint.
  - April 2024: 01.000.202 "finally fixes those many crashes" (04-09); 01.000.300 "makes some massive
    changes to ammo" (04-29). Negative share 35.2%.
  - August 2024: 1.001.002 Escalation of Freedom (08-06), then an Emergency Hot Fix (08-21). 48.1%
    negative. A content release, not a removal - "a studio can ship the thing it promised and lose the
    month anyway."
  - May 2025: 01.003.000, the Illuminate invasion, 6 patches that month. 50.8% negative.
  - March to May 2026: 6.1.0 to 6.2.5, Machinery of Oppression, 6 patches at an ordinary pace of one to
    three a month. 52.3%, 62.3%, 56.2% negative. By 2026 the complaint is no longer about any one patch.
- **Edits:** many reviews carry several dated updates across years and are counted on one date (2 to 15
  per batch of 50; the cross-game page puts edited reviews at 18.4%, the highest in the study). This
  affects the 2026 numbers.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends:** much better with friends, 62 (3.8 per 100). Lower than Back 4 Blood (7.5) and Deep Rock
  Galactic (10.1).
- **Strangers:** fine with strangers, 14; a welcoming community, 33; trolls and griefers, 11; other
  players put me off, 22 (by 2026 mostly about the fight over the studio).
- **Friendly fire is the eighth best thing about the game** (37 praise, 3 against in the whole study).
  The split is not the mechanic or how much it hurts; it is who does it and how often. Praise: you did it
  to your friends, by your own hand, and it was funny. Complaint: strangers did it to you, again and again
  ("teamkilled about 85% of my games"). The page: "Removing friendly fire would delete the game's
  most-loved feature; the lever is who can do it to you and how often."
- **The mission falling apart is the fun:** 13.
- **Solo:** works solo 15, punishing solo 11. Both sides show up in all three of the first games read.
- **Every role needed:** 0.0 per 100 here (4.6 in Deep Rock Galactic).
- **Disconnects:** 14.
- **Account:** a required account or platform link, 15 (13 in May 2024).
- The pages do not cover hosting, joining a game already running, rejoining, scaling, matchmaking,
  crossplay or loot sharing.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Combat hits hard:** 22 (1.4 per 100). Lower than Back 4 Blood's 5.8.
- **Weakening weapons:** the March 2024 patch notes say a patch "nerfs its best weapons"; "the studio
  weakened things players liked" is 39 and rises to 7.3 per 100 by 2026. An ammo change in April 2024
  ("massive changes to ammo").
- **Shots stopped by scenery:** seen once here.
- **Silly actions with no use** (emotes and the like): 6, far below Deep Rock Galactic.
- The pages say nothing else about movement, controls, camera or stuns.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Hard in a good way:** 16. **The difficulty ladder is well built:** 14.
- A March 2024 patch "nerfs hardest enemy and spawn rates" and another "adds new hazards" (patch notes).
- New enemy factions arrive as content: the Illuminate invasion in May 2025 (50.8% negative that month).
- The pages name no bosses and no unfair attacks.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Builds:** deep and varied, 16; barely change anything, 11. Both sides of one design choice.
- **Grind:** 11 (0.7 per 100). The same low rate in all three of the first games (0.9, 0.7, 0.7).
- **Paid currency earnable by playing:** 20 praise.
- The pages say nothing about loot drops, luck or time gates.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Keeps pulling you back:** 47, steady all three years.
- **Repeats itself:** 22 (1.4 per 100). This rate is nearly the same in all three of the first games (1.8,
  1.4, 1.4) - the tightest match in the whole study.
- **Updates add little:** 10. **A steady stream of updates:** 14.
- **Nothing else plays like it:** 10. **The mission falling apart is the fun:** 13.
- Content updates did not protect a month: Escalation of Freedom (August 2024) and the Illuminate invasion
  (May 2025) both sit in the worst months.

### Money and price
- **Fair price:** 25 (1.5 per 100). **Paid currency can be earned by playing:** 20.
- **Selling more while the game is broken:** 11.
- **Buy on sale only:** 0.0 per 100 (Back 4 Blood 8.6).
- **Complaints about money practices** overall: 1.8 per 100 reviews (cross-game page).
- **Free updates after launch:** 0.1 per 100 say so (Deep Rock Galactic 1.5).
- **Region lock:** 39 "not sold in my country" (30 in May 2024) - a publisher decision, not a price.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is 9.3% of everything said - three times Deep Rock Galactic's share - and 21.7% of all
  complaints, more than game design.
- **Crashes again and again:** 37, falling 2.5 to 1.7 per 100 from 2024 to 2026. Patch 01.000.202 on
  2024-04-09 "finally fixes those many crashes" (patch notes).
- **A bug breaks the mission or loses progress:** 28. **Buggy:** 19. **Known bugs never fixed:** 11.
- **Damages the computer or system:** 12.
- **Unwanted extra software** (anti-cheat, a launcher or a companion app - the page does not say which): 12.
- **Disconnects:** 14.
- **Required account or platform:** 15.
- An Emergency Hot Fix followed the August 2024 content patch (patch notes).
- The engine is not named on the pages.

### The studio, updates and community
- **The whole finding:** "The game got better and the relationship got worse." A live-service game "is not
  judged on its build quality after the first year. It is judged on whether the studio is felt to be on the
  player's side."
- **Listens and acts** was the second biggest praise (78), yet the bad studio labels add up to 51 (misreads
  19, fights its players 17, ignores feedback 15) plus punishes criticism (7, mostly 2026).
- **Weakening favourite things** (39) and **patches that made it worse** (47) are the top live-ops
  complaints; **fixed what mattered** (26) and **made it better** (20) the praise.
- **Trust:** the studio earned my trust 14; lost my trust 16.
- **The publisher:** the owner puts players off 45; not sold in my country 39; protest vote 21; account
  required 15 - one dated event in May 2024. The page notes that publisher reversals were filed as the
  studio listening, and whether to split studio from publisher is an open question with Rico.
- **The community:** the war cry (398), the identity players take on (54), the satire (53), a welcoming
  community (33). Community is 28.5% of everything said - the biggest part - and game design only 18.6%.
  Back 4 Blood had none of the war cry at all; Deep Rock Galactic and Helldivers 2 both did, and the
  cross-game page calls this "the culture line" between the games that lasted and the one that did not.
- **The argument took over:** other players put me off (22, from both sides); judged unfairly (21, the most
  common opinion in the last batch); three reviewers in one 2026 batch read the same community three
  incompatible ways; "the fights drove me away".
- **Updates:** eleven patches in February 2024; one to three a month by 2026.

### What players asked for
The pages give no list of requests for this game. What the complaints ask for, without new counts: stop
weakening favourite weapons and enemies' challenge (39); sell the game in every country and drop the
required account (39 and 15); fix crashes and game-breaking bugs (37 and 28); more variety (22); do not
sell more while it is broken (11); drop the unwanted extra software (12); protect players from
strangers who teamkill (3 in the whole study, and trolls and griefers 11).

### Only in this game
The pages for this game were written before the study listed "labels used by no other game". What the
cross-game page shows as almost only here, among the first three games:
- The publisher puts players off - 2.8 per 100 (Back 4 Blood 0.5, Deep Rock Galactic 0.0).
- Not sold in my country - 2.4 per 100 (0.0 in the other two).
- Friendly fire makes stories - 2.3 per 100 (Deep Rock Galactic 0.6, Back 4 Blood 0.0).
- The thumbs down as a protest vote (21), and "the fights drove me away" - both built from this game.
- The 30-fold swing in how players see the studio in one year.

### For Dominion - our reading

1. **Lesson:** Do not weaken what players love; it becomes the complaint that defines the game.
   **Evidence:** "the studio weakened things players liked" 39 times, rising from 1.6 to 7.3 per 100 by 2026;
   the March 2024 patches that "nerf its best weapons" and "nerf hardest enemy" came in the month the
   negative share nearly doubled (14.1% to 25.1%); "a patch made it worse" is the top complaint (47).
   **Dominion could:** balance by raising weak options rather than cutting strong ones; when a cut is
   needed, explain it in the patch note and give something back in the same patch; test balance changes
   with players first. **Strength:** strong.

2. **Lesson:** After the first year, players judge the studio, not the build.
   **Evidence:** crash complaints fell (2.5 to 1.7 per 100) while thumbs down tripled (11.8% to 36.2%) and
   good-to-bad talk about the studio swung from 3.3 : 1 to 1 : 9. "The studio listens and acts" is still
   the second biggest praise (78). **Dominion could:** keep a visible loop - "you said, we did" in patch
   notes; never argue with players in public; admit mistakes fast. **Strength:** strong.

3. **Lesson:** Friendly fire is loved when you do it to friends and hated when strangers do it to you.
   **Evidence:** 37 praise it, 3 complain in the whole study; the praise is self-inflicted and funny, the
   complaints are strangers teamkilling "about 85% of my games"; trolls and griefers 11; "the mission
   falling apart is the fun" 13. **Dominion could:** keep friendly fire as a feature; give the host a
   setting for it; soften or limit it in public matchmaking; let the squad vote out a griefer; make funny
   friendly kills easy to share. **Strength:** medium-strong.

4. **Lesson:** A required outside account or a region lock can cause the biggest review wave the game ever
   gets.
   **Evidence:** May 2024 had 309,103 English reviews, twice launch; 30 of 45 publisher complaints, 30 of 39
   "not sold in my country", 14 of 21 protest votes and 13 of 15 account complaints landed that month.
   **Dominion could:** use only Steam login; if an outside account is ever needed, make it optional and
   decide it before launch, never after; sell everywhere Steam sells. **Strength:** strong (one event, huge
   volume).

5. **Lesson:** Crashes and game-breaking bugs lead the engineering complaints, and some bugs hurt the
   player's computer.
   **Evidence:** crashes 37, bugs that end a mission or lose progress 28, buggy 19, damages the system 12,
   known bugs never fixed 11; engineering is 21.7% of all complaints. **Dominion could:** on a listen
   server a host crash ends the run for all four, so save each player's run rewards often and let the
   squad recover after a host drop; keep a public known-bugs list and close it out. **Strength:** strong.

6. **Lesson:** Unwanted extra software (anti-cheat, a launcher, a companion app) is a complaint of its own.
   **Evidence:** 12 times. **Dominion could:** a PvE co-op game may not need deep anti-cheat; if it uses
   any, say what and why on the store page. **Strength:** medium.

7. **Lesson:** A slogan and an identity give players a voice, and it spreads.
   **Evidence:** the war cry 398 times (one review in four); players take on the game's identity 54; the
   satire lands 53; two reviewers wrote another game's chant by mistake. It faded from 26.3 to 16.3 per
   100 as the relationship soured. The cross-game page ties having this culture to the games that lasted.
   **Dominion could:** give the player's side a short war cry and a name players can call themselves, and
   put both in voice lines, emotes and the end-of-run screen; a tone with some humour helps. **Strength:**
   medium-strong.

8. **Lesson:** A content update can still lose the month if it lands badly.
   **Evidence:** Escalation of Freedom (August 2024) came with the worst month of the launch year (48.1%
   negative) and needed an Emergency Hot Fix; the Illuminate invasion month was 50.8% negative.
   **Dominion could:** test new content with a small group before release; do not pair new content with
   big balance cuts; have a hotfix plan ready. **Strength:** medium (the months are thinly read).

9. **Lesson:** Fast patching at launch helps.
   **Evidence:** eleven versioned patches in February 2024, the least negative month of the launch period
   (14.1%). **Dominion could:** plan a launch team that can ship fixes every few days in the first month.
   **Strength:** medium-weak (the months line up; cause is not proven).

10. **Lesson:** Play with friends is the core, and strangers need to be safe too.
    **Evidence:** much better with friends 62; fine with strangers 14; welcoming community 33; trolls and
    griefers 11. **Dominion could:** make inviting friends and joining friends' games one click; give public
    lobbies a kick vote and a mute. **Strength:** medium.

11. **Lesson:** Solo play is a real trade-off: some say it works, some say it punishes.
    **Evidence:** works solo 15, punishing solo 11; both sides show up in all three of the first games.
    **Dominion could:** decide on purpose how a solo run scales (fewer enemies, or help), and say so in
    game. **Strength:** medium.

12. **Lesson:** "It repeats itself" shows up at the same rate in every co-op game.
    **Evidence:** 22 here (1.4 per 100); 1.8, 1.4, 1.4 across the first three games. **Dominion could:**
    plan mission and arena variety from the start, mixing objectives, places and enemy sets. **Strength:**
    medium-strong (across games).

13. **Lesson:** A fair price and a currency you can earn keep money complaints low.
    **Evidence:** fair price 25; paid currency earnable by playing 20; money-practice complaints only 1.8
    per 100; "buy on sale only" 0.0. But "selling more while broken" 11. **Dominion could:** let any paid
    currency be earned in play; do not launch a new paid item in a week when a big bug is still open.
    **Strength:** medium.

14. **Lesson:** Builds must feel different, or players say they do not matter.
    **Evidence:** deep and varied 16; barely change anything 11; grindy 11. **Dominion could:** make every
    loadout choice change how a fight goes, not just a number. **Strength:** weak-medium.

15. **Lesson:** Fights among players about the studio can drive people away.
    **Evidence:** other players put me off 22, from both sides by 2026; "the fights drove me away" built
    from "there is more drama with this game than anything else I do in my life"; the doxxing story told by
    four reviewers (hearsay). **Dominion could:** keep official spaces moderated and calm; never single out
    a player in public. **Strength:** weak-medium.

16. **Lesson:** Marketing claims get checked.
    **Evidence:** "a marketing claim was untrue" 10. **Dominion could:** promise only what is in the build.
    **Strength:** weak.

17. **Lesson:** Updates that add little are noticed, and a steady stream is praised.
    **Evidence:** updates add little 10; steady stream 14; "patches fixed what mattered" 26, "made it better"
    20. **Dominion could:** make each update carry one thing players can feel. **Strength:** weak.

### Limits
- 0.20% of the English reviews - the thinnest sample in the study, five times thinner than Deep Rock
  Galactic's. Month weights reach 579 to 1; one bullet in a quiet month can move a weighted share by a full
  point. Weighted shares are direction, not measurement. A label with 10 bullets is 10 people out of
  819,840.
- 2026 rests on about 232 reviews for a whole year.
- English only. Four other language groups (184,243 reviews: Simplified Chinese 116,313, Russian 30,779,
  European Spanish 29,201, LatAm Spanish 7,950) were pulled and not read. The country-availability
  complaint is exactly where they might read differently.
- Many reviews were rewritten across years and counted on one date; this affects the 2026 figures. Open
  with Rico.
- Publisher actions were filed under "the studio"; whether to split them is open with Rico.
- The doxxing story is hearsay from four reviewers.
- Reviews do not measure how many people play; the worst 2026 months also have few reviews.
- Small mismatches between pages: the year table gives 1,093 / 297 / 232 reviews on one page and 1,096 /
  297 / 233 on the other; the crash fall is called 32% on one page and 30% on the other; bullets are 2,901
  on the game pages and 2,905 on the cross-game table; the recomputed cross-game figures are 108.0 praise
  and 55.5 complaints per 100 (1.95 : 1).
- The pages are tags, not quotes; few direct quotes are given.

---

## Deep Rock Galactic: Rogue Core

**Read:** 814 of 7,990 English reviews (10.2%), ±3.26%, written 2026-05 to 2026-09 (September only
part of the month). **70.3% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam, all languages: 60.1%, Mixed).
**Pages:** `findings/drg-rogue-core-english.md`, `findings/drg-rogue-core.md`, `findings/cross-game.md`
section 9 (also the table at the top of the page, and short mentions in sections 10, 23, 24 and 25).

**What it is:** A spin-off of Deep Rock Galactic, by the same studio, in the same world, played by the
same fans. A co-op shooter built on runs: a team goes in, picks up upgrades from a pool the whole team
shares, and a timer inside the run decides when the run ends. Paid game (one reviewer complains about
small paid extras "in a paid game"). In Early Access since 2026-05-20; every review read was written
inside Early Access, in its first five months. The camera is not stated on the pages or in the Steam store's short description. The pages do not say who hosts a
game, how many play at once, or the engine.

**How close to Dominion:** Close on the core loop: a co-op team, sci-fi setting, runs with upgrades
picked up along the way, and a clock that pushes the team out. It is the nearest thing in the study to
"a loved co-op game makes a run-based follow-up", which is a useful mirror for Dominion's runs. It is
not known from the pages to be third person, to be hosted on one player's machine, or to have an
extraction or arena mode.

### The short version (plain words)
The same studio, the same world and the same fans gave the parent game 97.1% thumbs up and this game
70.3%. Half of everything said is about how the game is designed (1,321 of 2,643 points made), and only
3.2% is about crashes, lag or speed: players are not saying it is broken, they are saying they disagree
with it. The thing named most is the run timer (112 times), and most people who mention it still
recommend the game (69 of the 112). The score fell from 74.3% thumbs up in June to 50.0% in September
after a July hotfix, and what players wrote about patches turned sour a month before the votes did.

### What players praised, most to least
1. **They defended the game against its own review page.** Said 83 times, the third most-said thing in
   the game. About one review in ten argues with the other reviews instead of describing the game.
   *"I really struggle to understand the majority of the reviews for this game."* (226862716);
   *"I think you should be required to take a written test before leaving a review for this game."*
   (227460186). Cross-game: 10.2 per 100 reviews read here, against 7.1 in Back 4 Blood, 1.3 in
   Helldivers 2 and 0.2 in Deep Rock Galactic.
2. **They used the parent game's catchphrase and salute.** Said 61 times (7.5 per 100 reviews read).
   The parent game's players do it 28.6 times per 100, so the ritual carried over at about a quarter of
   its strength (the page says 26%).
3. **Liked the timer** (see complaints, item 1): 69 of the 112 timer mentions are on thumbs-up reviews.
   *"I also like the timer, because it actually put pressure on me"* (226877922).
4. **Worth buying even unfinished.** Said 38 times: sold openly as Early Access and already worth the
   money.
5. **Hard in a good way.** Said 34 times.
6. **Shooting hits with weight and feedback.** Said 34 times.
7. **Much better with friends.** Said 34 times.
8. **Builds are deep and varied.** Said 32 times (against 79 saying the upgrades barely change
   anything - see complaints).
9. **Patches made things better.** Said 31 times that a change improved something (3.8 per 100 reviews,
   the highest of the first four games), and 30 times that a patch fixed a real complaint (3.7 per 100,
   also the highest).
10. **The studio listens and acts.** 3.1 per 100 reviews read (Helldivers 2 is 4.7, Deep Rock Galactic
    1.8, Back 4 Blood 0.4).
11. **People bought it to support the studio.** 3 of the 4 points about how the game makes money.
12. **Lines players quote back.** 0.5 reviews per 100 (cross-game section 23; Earth Defense Force 5
