    leads at 7.6, every other large game is at 0.6 or less).
13. "Good game" with nothing else: 99 times. It counts as a thumbs up but carries no detail.

### What players complained about, most to least
The pages give the thumbs split only for the timer. Overall, each thumbs-up review carries 1.17
complaints (and 1.53 praises) - people who recommend it still list faults (Immortal: Unchained page).
1. **A timer inside the run decides when you leave.** 112 times, the most-said thing in the game. This
   is a split, not a pure complaint: 69 on thumbs-up reviews, 43 on thumbs-down reviews. 61.6% of the
   people who raise it recommend the game, and that share stayed between 60.7% and 62.7% across ten
   batches, even while the thumbs-up rate fell 24 points. *"The timer makes this game impossible
   especially if solo"* (227471291). The other games in the first four have zero on this.
2. **Upgrades barely change anything.** 79 times.
3. **Luck decides whether you win, not how you played.** 53 times. Cross-game, complaints about
   randomness reach 6.6 reviews per 100 here, second only to The First Descendant (7.2) in the corpus.
4. **The store page and trailer promised a different kind of game.** 51 times (the page marks it as
   mixed in tone).
5. **Teammates can take the upgrades you wanted.** The shared upgrade pool is the most-complained-about
   system: 47 times that teammates take your things, and 15 times that the rules set teammates against
   each other - 62 in all. 5.8 per 100 reviews here; 0.2 in Back 4 Blood, zero in Deep Rock Galactic
   and Helldivers 2.
6. **Too little content for the price.** 44 times.
7. **Ammo, health or supplies run out too fast.** 43 times.
8. **Another game does it better** (mostly the parent game). 38 times.
9. **Whole kinds of choices are never worth taking.** 38 times.
10. **Repetitive.** 35 times.
11. **The game only works if the team works together.** 32 times. (The page lists this as a complaint
    here; the tag card now files it as praise. See Limits.)
12. **Not worth buying yet - come back later.** 30 times.
13. **Not finished enough to recommend, no specific fault named.** 28 times.
14. **It could have been more and settled for less.** 27 times.
15. **Moving feels slow and heavy.** 11 reviews (from cross-game section 24: it is the commonest
    movement complaint after Gunfire Reborn's 15).
16. **The studio ignores feedback.** 1.8 per 100 reviews read (the highest of the first four games).
17. **Updates to the parent game slowed while this one was made.** Named from this side too:
    *"updates for DRG came to an almost complete stop for this, it doesn't feel like it was worth the
    wait."* (227898874, 100 people marked it helpful); *"they literally pulled drg out back and had it
    shot in order to make this game"* (228545314). No count given.
18. **Politics in the cast or world put them off.** 9 times in all (4 about the characters' writing, 3
    about the world, 2 about how the characters look) - 0.4% of everything said. Loud inside a single
    review, tiny in the counts.
19. **The class decides your character's gender.** Seen in this game (ZCREW's page counts 4 sightings
    across ZCREW and this game, no split).
20. "Bad game" with nothing else: 26 times.

### How it changed over time
| Month written | Thumbs up | Thumbs down | % up | Patch points: good / bad |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-05 launch | 343 | 139 | 71.2% | 24 / 5 |
| 2026-06 Update 1 | 162 | 56 | 74.3% | 29 / 5 |
| 2026-07 hotfix 01.02 | 40 | 23 | 63.5% | 4 / 4 |
| 2026-08 | 21 | 18 | 53.8% | 2 / 2 |
| 2026-09 (part month) | 6 | 6 | 50.0% | 2 / 2 |

- Best month June (74.3%), worst September (50.0%): a 24-point fall in three months.
- The 70.3% overall is mostly the launch month, which is 59% of the sample (and 75% of all English
  reviews).
- What players wrote about patches went from 4.8 good for every bad at launch, to 5.8 to 1 in June, to
  1 to 1 in July. The thumbs-up rate turned in July and kept falling. The words moved a month before the
  votes. Players tie the turn to the July hotfix: *"Patch RC: EA: 01.02 broke the game. No one bothered
  to actually playtest their patch... Their first big patch was a great one, but they decided to
  absolutely decimate the game with a 'hotfix'."* (229967213).
- Update 1 (June) loosened the shared upgrade pool. Complaints about co-op design fell from 68% to 54%
  of what was said about co-op across the read, because of when reviews were written. Two reviewers then
  gave thumbs down because of the fix (see Co-op).

### Co-op and online play
- **The shared upgrade pool is the biggest co-op fight.** 62 complaints: teammates grab what you wanted
  (47) and the rules set teammates against each other (15).
- **But the friction had fans.** After Update 1 loosened it, two reviewers gave thumbs down over the
  fix: *"the devs bowed to people who play with randoms and took out all the teamwork and strategizing
  so now you just play by yourself with other people"* (228588560); *"they turned a fun team game into a
  solo game you can play with friends"* (228588082).
- **Better with friends:** 34 times.
- **The game only works if the team works together:** 32 times.
- **The timer is harder alone:** *"The timer makes this game impossible especially if solo"* (227471291).
- **Servers, lag and netcode:** inside the engineering share, which is only 85 points (3.2%) in all; no
  separate count for online problems.
- Hosting, joining a running game, bots, friendly fire, crossplay: the pages say nothing.

### Combat, movement and feel
- Shooting hits with weight: 34 times.
- Moving feels slow: 11 reviews (cross-game section 24).
- Ammo, health or supplies run out too fast: 43 times.
- Nothing on camera, controls or reading enemy attacks on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- Hard in a good way: 34 times.
- The timer makes it too hard alone (quote above).
- Supplies too scarce: 43 times.
- The pages say nothing about enemy types, bosses or difficulty settings.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **Upgrades barely change anything:** 79 times - the second most-said complaint.
- **Builds are deep and varied:** 32 times. The same system gets both, and the complaint is more than
  twice as common.
- **Luck decides the outcome:** 53 times.
- **Some whole kinds of choices are useless:** 38 times.
- **Shared upgrades can be taken by teammates:** 47 times.
- The class decides the character's gender: a small complaint shared with ZCREW (4 sightings across both
  games).
- Nothing on what carries over between runs.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **The run timer** is the most-said thing (112). See complaints, item 1, for the split.
- A later study of Risk of Rain 2 moved 3 Rogue Core reviews to a new praise, "the clock is the
  thrill", after this page was written - more people praise the timer than the 69 above show.
- **Too little content for the price:** 44. **Repetitive:** 35. **Settled for less than it could be:**
  27.
- Nothing on run length or level generation on the pages.

### Money and price
- Very little talk about how the game makes money: 4 points in all. 3 are people who bought it to
  support the studio; 1 complains about small paid extras in a paid game.
- **Early Access value is split:** 38 say it is worth it unfinished, 30 say wait.
- **Too little content for the price:** 44.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Only 85 of 2,643 points (3.2%) are about crashes, speed or netcode. The page says plainly: "This game
  is not being rejected for being broken."
- Stability: 9 points in 814 reviews, 4 of them repeated crashes.
- The one tech story is a patch: the July hotfix 01.02 is blamed for breaking the game (229967213).
- Language and translation: 1 point. Accessibility: 2 points.

### The studio, updates and community
- **Update 1 (June) was loved;** patch talk ran 5.8 good to 1 bad.
- **Hotfix 01.02 (July) turned it.** Patch talk went to 1 to 1, and the thumbs-up rate fell from there.
- **The studio listens and acts:** 3.1 per 100 reviews. **Ignores feedback:** 1.8 per 100.
- **Players praise the studio's response and still reject the game.** The page treats these as two
  separate things: this game leads the first four games in patch praise while getting the worst scores
  of them.
- **The parent game was starved for this one:** see complaints, item 17.
- **Defending the game against other reviews:** 83 times.
- **The parent's culture came along weaker:** the shared catchphrase and salute, 7.5 per 100 here
  against 28.6 in the parent.
- Community talk is 9.2% of everything said (243 points), close to Back 4 Blood (9.4%) and far below
  Deep Rock Galactic and Helldivers 2 (about 28.5% each). Cross-game section 10 notes the games with
  little community talk are the ones that did worse.

### What players asked for
The pages give no list of requests. The nearest things: 30 reviews say "come back later" (not ready
yet), 44 say there is too little content, and two reviewers asked for the shared-pool friction back
after it was loosened.

### Only in this game
- **The in-run timer** (13.8 per 100 reviews read; zero in Back 4 Blood, Deep Rock Galactic and
  Helldivers 2).
- **Teammates taking your upgrades** (5.8 per 100; at most 0.2 in the other three).
- **One in ten reviews defending the game against its reviews** (10.2 per 100; only Back 4 Blood is
  near, at 7.1, and later Redfall argues it 124 to 9).
- **The cleanest test in the study of carrying a culture across:** the catchphrase survived the spin-off
  at about a quarter of its strength.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **A run timer will be the most-argued feature, and that is fine if it is a real choice.**
   Evidence: the timer is 112 mentions, the top item; 69 of them are on thumbs-up reviews (61.6%), and
   the split never moved as the score fell. Dominion could: keep a clock as pressure, but test it alone
   and with four, since the solo complaint is the sharpest one (227471291); think about a softer clock
   for solo or smaller teams; and do not treat "people talk about the timer" as "people hate the
   timer". Strength: strong.
2. **Shared loot that teammates can grab is the biggest co-op fight - and some players love the
   fight.** Evidence: 62 complaints (47 + 15), the most of any system; loosening it in Update 1 drew two
   thumbs-down reviews from people who wanted the friction back. Dominion could: give each player their
   own pick of upgrades by default (protects games with strangers), and offer shared or contested picks
   as a friends-only or optional rule. Strength: strong on the complaint, weak (2 reviews) on the
   friction-lovers.
3. **Upgrades have to change how you play, not just add a few percent.** Evidence: 79 say upgrades
   barely change anything, 38 say whole kinds of choices are useless, against 32 praising deep builds.
   Dominion could: make each run upgrade change a behaviour (how a gun or ability works) and check that
   every option gets picked. Strength: strong.
4. **Luck must not decide the run.** Evidence: 53 say the draw decides the outcome; randomness
   complaints reach 6.6 reviews per 100, second highest in the corpus. Dominion could: let players
   re-roll, bank, or pick from several upgrade offers, and give a bad-luck floor. Strength: strong.
5. **Do not starve players of ammo, health or supplies.** Evidence: 43 say supplies run out too fast.
   Dominion could: tune resupply to the team size and to the timer, so scarcity is a choice, not a wall.
   Strength: medium.
6. **One rushed patch can undo the goodwill a good patch earned.** Evidence: patch talk 5.8 good to 1
   bad after Update 1, then 1 to 1 after hotfix 01.02; thumbs-up fell from 74.3% to 50.0%; "No one
   bothered to actually playtest their patch" (229967213). Dominion could: playtest every hotfix with a
   full team before release, and be ready to roll back. Strength: strong (clear time line), though the
   later months are small.
7. **Watch what players write, not only the score - the words turn first.** Evidence: patch talk went
   sour in July, the score turned a month later. Dominion could: read new reviews and community posts
   after every patch and act on the tone before the score moves. Strength: medium.
8. **A run-based follow-up to a loved game is judged against that game.** Evidence: 38 say another game
   does it better; 51 say the store framing promised something else; the parent's players are angry it
   was slowed for this one (227898874). Dominion could: describe runs, the timer and the shared rules
   plainly on the store page and in trailers, so no one buys the wrong game. Strength: medium.
9. **Early Access is fine if the price matches what is there.** Evidence: 38 say worth it unfinished,
   30 say wait, 44 say too little content for the price, 28 say not finished enough. Dominion could: if
   it goes into Early Access, say what is in and what is coming, and price for what is in. Strength:
   medium.
10. **A culture cannot be copied in; it has to grow.** Evidence: the parent's catchphrase shows up 28.6
    times per 100 there and 7.5 here. Dominion could: leave room for players to make their own rituals
    (emotes, a squad call-out, a shared line) instead of borrowing one. Strength: medium (one clean
    case).
11. **Players can like the studio and still dislike the game.** Evidence: highest patch praise of the
    first four games, worst scores among them. Dominion could: not read praise for fast fixes as proof
    the design is right. Strength: medium.
12. **Movement must feel quick.** Evidence: 11 reviews say moving feels slow (cross-game section 24).
    Dominion could: make sprint, dodge and climbing feel snappy, since a third-person shooter is judged
    on it. Strength: weak here (stronger across the corpus).
13. **Small, loud complaints are small.** Evidence: the cast and politics complaint is 9 of 2,643 points.
    Dominion could: triage by counts, not by how angry one forum post is. Strength: medium.

### Limits
- 814 reviews, ±3.26% (the plan was 1,298; Steam stopped returning reviews early). The launch month is
  read at 8.0% and every later month at about 16%; launch is 75% of the English group, so launch-month
  figures are the weakest.
- The sample says 70.3% thumbs up; Steam says 60.1%. The page puts the gap down to the launch month being
  under-read.
- English only: 7,220 reviews in other languages are unread.
- Early Access, still changing. Every figure is a snapshot taken 2026-09-02.
- 158 reviews (19.4%) were edited after posting; each is counted in the month it was first written.
- The later months are small (63, 39 and 12 reviews), so the fall in score rests on few reviews per
  month.
- The page lists "the game only works if the team works together" (32) as a complaint, but the tag card
  now files it as praise; it is unclear which reading the 32 reflect.
- 3 timer reviews were moved to a praise heading after this page was written (Risk of Rain 2 page), so
  the 112 and the 69/43 split are as of the page date.
- Reviewers' claims about what hotfix 01.02 did are their own and not checked against patch notes.

---

## Deep Rock Galactic

**Read:** 2,133 of 215,564 English reviews (1.0%; the findings pages give no margin of
error), written 2018-02 to 2026-08.
**97.1% thumbs up** in the sample (Steam's own figure in the cross-game table: 97.1%, *Overwhelmingly
Positive* per `findings/drg-rogue-core.md`; the pages give no all-languages percentage).
**Pages:** `findings/deep-rock-galactic-english.md`, `findings/deep-rock-galactic.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` sections 1 to 9 (section 9 is the Rogue Core spin-off measured against this
game). Also `Rico notes.md` (two notes from this game).

**What it is:** A co-op shooter about fighting waves of insects in caves, with classes that each have
their own job, and maps built by the computer so each mission is different. It came out in early
access in February 2018 (the studio's anniversary events count from then) and left it later; the
pages do not give the 1.0 date. It is a paid game. New content after launch has been free (seasons,
events), with cosmetic DLC on top (the Skullcrusher DLC in 2025-02 is named in the patch notes).
Season 05 "Drilling Deeper" shipped 2024-06-13 and Season 06 "Relics of Hoxxes" in 2026-01. A
roguelite spin-off, Rogue Core, came later. The Steam store page calls it *"a 1-4 player co-op FPS"* (a first-person shooter; checked 2026-10-03).
The pages do not say who hosts, which engine it uses, or how a mission ends.

**How close to Dominion:** Close in spirit, not in camera. It shares a sci-fi setting, a small squad
of friends against alien hordes, PvE only, classes that need each other, generated maps, and four
players. It differs in being first-person, in using missions rather than runs that build power, and in having no player-versus-
player risk. It is the corpus's model of a loved co-op game, so it is the best guide to what players
reward when the co-op works.

### The short version (plain words)
Almost nobody dislikes this game: 97.1% thumbs up, only 61 thumbs-down in 2,133 reviews, and good
things are said 7 times as often as bad ones (3,744 against 515). The single most common thing
players write is the community's catchphrase, 610 times, 14% of everything said - more than any
remark about the game itself. Close to half of what players praise is the people: friends, kind
strangers, and a shared culture. The few complaints are the cost of the same design: it gets
repetitive (29 times), it is hard alone (27), and you need a group (22). The game's best feature and
its main complaint are one thing seen from two sides.

### What players praised, most to least
Counts are times it was said, out of 2,133 reviews; "per 100" means times per 100 reviews.
1. **The catchphrase and salute, used as a greeting** - 610 times, 28.6 per 100. Players write it
   unprompted. It outweighs combat, art, price, progression and difficulty added together (610
   against 599). It is 13.5% of everything written about the game.
2. **Much better with friends** - 215 times, 10.1 per 100.
3. **Players describe themselves in the game's own words** - 141
   times, 6.6 per 100.
4. **The community is welcoming; strangers are kind** - 123 times, 5.8 per 100.
5. **It keeps pulling you back** - 121 times, 5.7 per 100.
6. **Silly actions with no game effect** - dancing, drinking in the bar, petting the loot bugs,
   kicking the barrel, prop hunt in the lobby, breaking gravity - 111 times, 5.2 per 100. This is the
   seventh most common thing anyone says. The whole subject of these toys (114) beats the
   difficulty (92), the enemies (75), all sound (57), the levels (20) and the story (14).
7. **Every class has a job the others cannot do** - 98 times, 4.6 per 100.
8. **It works fine alone** - 79 times, 3.7 per 100.
9. **Updates arrive regularly** - 69 times, 3.2 per 100.
10. **The game's satire lands; players get the joke** - 68 times, 3.2 per 100.
11. **The studio earned my trust; I will buy what they make next** - 63 times, 3.0 per 100.
12. **The computer-built maps keep it varied** - 59 times, 2.8 per 100.
13. **The price is fair** - 57 times, 2.7 per 100 (65 remarks about price in all).
14. **Builds and gear choices are deep** - 57 times, 2.7 per 100.
15. **The missions vary** - 56 times, 2.6 per 100.
16. **The best game of its kind** - 54 times, 2.5 per 100.
17. **The art direction looks well done** - 44 times, 2.1 per 100 (the art as a whole, 133 remarks).
18. **The difficulty levels are clear steps and you pick your own** - 42 times, 2.0 per 100.
19. **Playing with strangers is good** - 41 times, 1.9 per 100, against 4 who say it is bad, 10 to 1.
    Helldivers 2 runs 14 to 9 and Back 4 Blood 8 to 30 the other way.
20. **There is plenty of content** - 40 times, 1.9 per 100.
21. **Unlocking things feels satisfying** - 40 times, 1.9 per 100.
22. **The studio listens and the feedback changes the game** - 39 times, 1.8 per 100.
23. **The shooting has punch** - 36 times, 1.7 per 100.
24. **The game only works if the team works together** - 36 times, 1.7 per 100.
25. **A patch made it better** - 35 times, 1.6 per 100.
26. **New content after launch is free, and that is why I trust them** - 1.5 per 100 (the pages give
    the rate, not the count).
27. **Hard in a good way** - 1.2 per 100.
28. **Old season rewards can still be earned later** - 9 times (see "How it changed").
29. **Friendly fire makes funny stories** - 0.6 per 100 (a floor; the page says this row undercounts).
30. **Worth playing even with nothing to earn** - one reviewer (85232716) Rico agreed with: *"the
    missions themselves are intrinsically rewarding... I'm never playing just to reach a goal."* The
    pages give no count for this.
31. In early access: **good value while unfinished** (said from 2018-03 to 2020-12, then never again)
    and **does not feel unfinished** (2018-11 to 2023-11). No counts given.

Also: 440 thumbs-up reviews (20.6 per 100) say nothing specific, and 62 times a reviewer placed the
game against others without saying how.

### What players complained about, most to least
The pages do not split each complaint by thumbs up or down. The English page names the top four as
the complaints the 61 thumbs-down reviews agree on.
1. **It gets repetitive** - 29 times, 1.4 per 100.
2. **It is noticeably harder or worse alone** - 27 times, 1.3 per 100.
3. **I have nobody to play with, and the game suffers** - 22 times, 1.0 per 100.
4. **There is too little content** - 20 times, 0.9 per 100.
5. **The unlocks are a grind** - 15 times, 0.7 per 100.
6. **The cast leaves people out; players ask for a roster that includes them** - 9 times, 0.4 per 100.
7. **It runs out fast** - 9 times, 0.4 per 100.
8. **A patch made it worse** - 8 times.
9. **Build choices are shallow** - 8 times.
10. **Some options are useless** - 8 times.
11. **Only buy it on sale** - 7 times.
12. **Its fame is bigger than the game** - 7 times.
13. **Hard to tell where to go in the caves** - 7 times.
14. **Buggy** - 7 times.
15. **Price too high for what it is** - 6 times.
16. **One class is weaker than the rest** - 6 times.
17. **A class I expected is missing** - 6 times.
18. **Nothing left to unlock, so no reason to keep going** - 6 times.
19. **Trolls and griefers spoil runs** - 6 times.
20. **Updates come too slowly** - 5 times.
21. **The systems are too shallow** - 5 times.
22. **Ammo, health or supplies run out too fast** - 5 times.
23. **Weapons feel weak, hits feel like nothing** - 5 times.
24. **Not enough kinds of enemy** - 5 times.

Also: 17 thumbs-down reviews say nothing specific; 7 reviews have a thumb that says the opposite of
the words (not a complaint). In early access, 25 remarks waited for promised content (13 of them in
2018-19), and players worried the game would die (2018-04 to 2019-06, then never again).

**What is missing from the complaints:** no crashes, no servers, no money tricks and no fights with
the studio in the top 25. All the big complaints are trade-offs of the design, not defects. Of all
515 complaints, 46.4% are about game design, 10.5% about how much content there is, 12.4% about other
players, 7.4% about tech, 4.3% about updates, 3.9% about reputation and 2.7% about price and sales.

### How it changed over time
- **Complaints fell, then rose.** Complaints per 100 reviews: 55.5 in 2018, 43.7 in 2019, 26.4 in
  2020, 21.4 in 2021, 19.7 in 2022, **10.3 in 2023**, 11.9 in 2024, then **22.9 in 2025 and 27.1 in
  2026**. Thumbs-down went from 4.1% (2018) to 1.7% (2023) and back to 4.0% (2026). The pages do not
  know why it rose again, and the last two years are the thinnest part of the sample.
- **Praise per 100** peaked at 234.0 in 2019 and settled at 148 to 165 from 2023 on.
- **Review volume on Steam peaked in 2023** (51,740, 24% of all), then fell to 30,299 (2024), 15,811
  (2025) and 7,586 (2026 so far), while the studio kept shipping seasons and events on schedule.
- **November is the biggest month in 7 of 8 years** - the Steam autumn sale and Steam Awards. In
  2022 November had 10,316 reviews, 2.4 times the next month, with nothing shipped. The one year
  November lost was 2024, to June 2024 (4,365), the month Season 05 shipped with four patches in eight
  days. 2026's biggest month so far is January, when Season 06 shipped. Smaller things (events,
  hotfixes, cosmetic DLC, the anniversary) leave no mark on the monthly count.
- **Reviews got shorter as the game aged.** One-remark reviews went from 51.4% (2018) to 76.9%
  (2023); the catchphrase went from 3.6% of all remarks (2018) to 24.0% (2023). The biggest review
  year said the least.
- **The one shipped feature that moved a count:** letting players go back to old seasons' reward
  tracks. Announced 2024-04-14, live with Season 05 on 2024-06-13 (studio patch notes). Praise that
  missed content comes back was said 9 times in all, 4 of them in the eight months after it shipped;
  2024 holds 5 of the 9 in a remark that averages one a year. Quote: *"MASSIVE respect to the devs for
  allowing players to switch their battlepass progress to any of the previous seasons"* (233551125).
- Studio patch notes also show Season 06 announced and the Rogue Core spin-off delayed in 2025-11, a
  board game Kickstarter (2025-07) and an $85 mug (2025-02). The patch record only reaches back to
  2024-03.

### Co-op and online play
- **Friends are the core.** Much better with friends: 215 times. Needs a group: 22. Hard alone: 27.
  Fine alone: 79. The same choice makes both lists.
- **Strangers are a plus here, unlike in most games.** Good with strangers 41 against 4 bad (10 to
  1). Welcoming community 123. Trolls and griefers only 6.
- **Teamwork is built in.** Every class needed: 98. The game only works if the team works together:
  36. A class missing: 6. One class weaker: 6.
- **Players come for different things in the same run** - one reviewer with 1,790 hours (178648003)
  hand-picks teammates because some play for fun, some for rewards, some only for the mission. Rico's
  view (Rico notes): this is a signalling problem, best fixed outside the game first (Discord channels
  or a role picker: for fun, for rewards, for clears), with an in-game lobby flag as the costlier
  version.
- **Friendly fire:** praised as story-making at 0.6 per 100 (Helldivers 2: 2.3). Across the whole
  corpus only 3 people filed it as a cost.
- **Loot taken by teammates:** 0.0 per 100 here, against 5.8 in Rogue Core, where the spin-off made
  pickups shared.
- **Bots:** complaints that bots are useless are 0.1 per 100 here (Back 4 Blood 2.6).
- **Hosting, lag, joining a running game, servers:** the pages report nothing. Tech is only 3.2% of
  all remarks.

### Combat, movement and feel
- The shooting has punch: 36. Weapons feel weak: 5.
- Silly actions with no effect (dance, beer, barrel, pet the loot bug): 111 - more than any remark
  about enemies or levels.
- Movement, controls, camera, stuns and readability are not reported on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- The enemies as a subject: 75 remarks. Not enough kinds of enemy: 5.
- Difficulty as a subject: 92 remarks. Clear difficulty steps you pick yourself: 42. Hard in a good
  way: 1.2 per 100. Supplies run out too fast: 5.
- Hard to tell where to go in the caves: 7 (the levels as a subject, 20).
- Bosses and the end game are not reported on the pages.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- Deep builds: 57. Shallow builds: 8. Some options useless: 8. Systems too shallow: 5.
- Satisfying unlocks: 40. Grindy: 15. Nothing left to chase: 6. Progression as a whole: 267 remarks.
- Old season rewards can be earned later: 9 (and the feature behind it moved the count).
- Rico's note: the reviewer he agreed with says games that make you *"play to get stronger to play to
  get stronger"* go stale, and this one does not because each mission is fun on its own. Rico: *"I
  feel this way playing Helldivers 2."*
- Random drops and luck are not reported on the pages.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **The trade-off, side by side:** repetitive 29 against varied maps and missions 115; hard alone 27
  against fine alone 79; needs a group 22 against better with friends 215; grindy 15 against
  satisfying unlocks 40. People who like the design say so 449 times; people who do not, 93.
- Keeps pulling you back: 121. Runs out fast: 9. Too little content: 20. Plenty: 40.
- Repetition is the tightest complaint in the corpus: 1.8, 1.4 and 1.4 per 100 in Back 4 Blood, this
  game and Helldivers 2 - a loved, a hated and a contested game complain about it at the same rate.

### Money and price
- Price fair: 57 (2.7 per 100). Too high: 6. Buy only on sale: 7 (0.3 per 100, against Back 4
  Blood's 8.6).
- New content after launch is free, named as a reason to trust the studio: 1.5 per 100 (Back 4
  Blood 0.0, Helldivers 2 0.1).
- Price and sales are only 2.7% of all complaints. The pages report no shop or battle pass complaint;
  the seasons' reward tracks are free (one reviewer calls it a "battlepass").

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
Tech is 3.2% of everything said - for an eight-year-old online game, almost nothing. Buggy: 7. No
crash, lag or server complaint reaches the top 25. Tech is 7.4% of complaints.

### The studio, updates and community
- Steady updates: 69. Updates too slow: 5. Studio earned my trust: 63 (Back 4 Blood: 0). Listens and
  acts: 39. A patch made it better: 35 (1.6 per 100); made it worse: 8; fixed what mattered: 0.3 per
  100; ignores feedback: 0.0 per 100.
- The culture: the catchphrase 610 (28.6 per 100), the identity 141 (6.6 per 100). All culture
  remarks are 35.7 per 100 here, 29.7 in Helldivers 2, and **0.0 in Back 4 Blood**, which failed.
  The community is 32.9% of this game's praise.
- The satire lands: 68. The cast leaves people out: 9. The story as a subject: 14. Sound: 57.
- **The ritual cannot be copied.** Rogue Core, by the same studio with the same catchphrase, gets it
  at 7.5 per 100 - 26% of this game's strength.
- **Accessibility:** 10 remarks in all; two of them are about phobia.

### What players asked for
The pages do not keep a separate request list. The asks inside the complaints: more content (too
little, 20; runs out fast, 9; nothing left to chase, 6), more kinds of enemy (5), a wider cast (9), a
missing class (6), more promised content (25 waiting, 13 of them in 2018-19), faster updates (5),
and a way to earn missed season rewards (granted - 9 thanks).

### Only in this game
- **Silly actions with no effect, praised 111 times** (5.2 per 100; Helldivers 2 0.4, Back 4 Blood
  0.0).
- **Every class needed** at 4.6 per 100 (Back 4 Blood 1.7, Helldivers 2 0.0).
- **Players like the strangers the game gives them** (10 to 1, the only game where it runs this way).
- **The only shipped feature in the corpus that visibly moved a count** (old seasons coming back).
- **Studio earned my trust** at 3.0 per 100 and free post-launch content at 1.5 per 100, both near
  zero in the other early games.
- **Reviews turned into a greeting**: by 2023 a quarter of all remarks were the catchphrase.

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Make the game great with friends, and still fine alone and with strangers.**
   Evidence: better with friends 215, fine alone 79, good with strangers 41 to 4; and on the other
   side hard alone 27, needs a group 22 - three of the top four complaints are about having no group.
   Dominion could: tune solo and duo runs so they work (scale enemies by player count), make
   matchmade strangers useful to each other, and give a solo player a reason to queue with others.
   Strength: **strong**.
2. **Give players a shared ritual - a salute, a phrase, a gesture - and let them own it.**
   Evidence: the catchphrase 610 times (28.6 per 100), identity 141; Back 4 Blood, which failed, has
   0.0. But Rogue Core, with the same phrase, gets only 7.5 per 100: a ritual cannot be pasted in.
   Dominion could: put a salute or call-out button and a voice line in from day one, tie it to a
   moment that happens every run (the drop-in, the extraction), and see if players pick it up rather
   than forcing it.
   Strength: **medium** (strong count, but the cause is unclear; one failure against two survivors).
3. **Build cheap toys with no game effect into the hub and the run.**
   Evidence: dancing, the bar, petting the loot bug, the barrel - 111 times, more than enemies (75)
   and levels (20).
   Dominion could: give the pre-run hub a drink, a dance, a kickable object, a pettable creature, a
   lobby mini-game - things friends do while waiting for the host.
   Strength: **strong**.
4. **Give each class a job the others cannot do.**
   Evidence: every class needed 98; teamwork required 36; one class weaker 6; a class missing 6.
   Dominion could: design classes around a team need (lighting, mobility, defence, resupply) rather
   than damage flavours, and check none falls behind.
   Strength: **strong**.
5. **Fight repetition with generated maps, but expect it to stay the top complaint.**
   Evidence: repetitive 29 against varied 115; the same rate (about 1.4 per 100) in every game.
   Dominion could: vary objectives, modifiers and map pieces between runs, and plan a content
   pipeline for players who run out (too little 20, runs out fast 9, nothing left to chase 6).
   Strength: **strong**.
6. **Make every run fun with nothing to earn; do not lean only on unlocks.**
   Evidence: Rico's note on reviewer 85232716 (no count) and the pull-you-back praise 121; grind
   complaint 15, nothing left to chase 6.
   Dominion could: judge each run by whether it is fun at max level with all unlocks done.
   Strength: **weak** on numbers, but Rico agrees with it.
7. **Let missed time-limited rewards come back.**
   Evidence: the old-season feature is the only shipped feature in the corpus that moved a count (4 of
   9 thanks in the eight months after it shipped).
   Dominion could: never lock seasonal or event rewards away for good; let players switch to old
   reward tracks.
   Strength: **medium** (small count, clear link).
8. **Steady, free updates buy trust that lasts years.**
   Evidence: steady updates 69, studio earned trust 63, listens and acts 39, free post-launch
   content 1.5 per 100; price and sales are only 2.7% of complaints.
   Dominion could: plan a regular free update rhythm and sell only cosmetics on top, and say so.
   Strength: **strong**.
9. **A clear difficulty ladder the player picks for themselves.**
   Evidence: well-graded difficulty 42; hard in a good way 1.2 per 100.
   Dominion could: offer named difficulty steps per run, each clearly harder, chosen at the start.
   Strength: **medium**.
10. **Let players say what they want from a run before it starts.**
    Evidence: the 1,790-hour reviewer (178648003) and Rico's note: some want fun, some rewards, some
    clears. Six complain of trolls.
    Dominion could: start with Discord channels or a role picker (Rico's free version); later a lobby
    flag such as "casual / full sweep / fast clear".
    Strength: **weak** (one review, Rico's own view).
11. **A shared joke in the tone helps the culture form.**
    Evidence: the satire lands 68; it is 3.2 per 100 here and 3.3 in Helldivers 2, 0.0 in Back 4 Blood.
    Dominion could: give the corporation or the war a voice players can laugh at and quote.
    Strength: **medium**.
12. **Make strangers good for each other.**
    Evidence: strangers good 41 to 4; welcoming community 123; no loot stealing (0.0 per 100 here vs
    5.8 in Rogue Core with shared pickups).
    Dominion could: give each player their own loot or a fair split, reward reviving and helping, and
    avoid systems where one player's gain is another's loss.
    Strength: **medium**.
13. **Read the review text, not only the score, and know Steam's calendar.**
    Evidence: November sales beat every update but one season in eight years; the catchphrase turned
    reviews into a greeting; complaints per 100 doubled in 2025-26 while thumbs-down stayed under 5%.
    Rogue Core's text moved a month before its score.
    Dominion could: track complaint rates per month, not just the percentage; time big updates away
    from or into the autumn sale on purpose.
    Strength: **medium**.
14. **Widen the cast.**
    Evidence: 9 ask for a roster that includes people it leaves out; 6 miss a class.
    Dominion could: let players pick body type, voice and look for their soldier.
    Strength: **weak**.
15. **Think about phobias when designing bugs or swarms.**
    Evidence: 2 of 10 accessibility remarks are about phobia.
    Dominion could: consider an option that changes the look of the creepiest enemies.
    Strength: **weak**.

### Limits
- A 1.0% sample, with no margin of error on the pages; 20 reviews a month at the floor, so single months are rough.
- Only 61 thumbs-down reviews; their pattern is clear but small.
- English only. Russian (65,935), Simplified Chinese (36,562), Spanish (8,308) and LatAm Spanish
  (2,138) reviews were pulled and not read.
- The patch record reaches back only to 2024-03; nothing about early access, 1.0 or seasons 1 to 4 can
  be linked to the counts.
- The pages cannot say why reviews collapsed after 2023, or why complaints rose in 2025-26.
- The camera, player count, hosting and engine in "What it is" are not from the findings pages.
- Small mismatches between pages: thumbs up is 2,072 of 2,133 on one page and 2,069 of 2,130 on the
  other (both 97.1%); complaints per 100 are 24.1 in the early cross-game table and 23.7 in the later
  recount; accessibility is 10 remarks in one place and 9 in another; and the ranked complaint list on
  the master page leaves out the 17 wordless thumbs-down that the English page lists fifth.

---

## Back 4 Blood

**Read:** 1,663 of 33,766 English reviews (4.9%), the findings page prints no margin of error, written 2021-10 to 2026-08 (59 months). **69.2% thumbs up**
in the sample (Steam: 69.2%; the pages do not give Steam's label). Plus every Latin American Spanish
review: 712 of 712, a census with no margin, 81.3% thumbs up (Part B below).
**Pages:** `findings/back-4-blood-english.md`, `findings/back-4-blood.md`, `findings/back-4-blood-latam.md`,
`findings/cross-game.md` sections 1 to 8 (also the table at the top of the page, and short mentions in
sections 9, 10 and 20).

**What it is:** A four-player co-op zombie horde shooter in first person (the Steam store page calls it *"a thrilling cooperative first-person shooter"*, checked 2026-10-03), made by the studio behind an
older, much-loved co-op zombie game (Left 4 Dead) and sold on that link; published with Warner Bros. You
build a deck of cards that changes how you play, run by run. Paid, with three paid expansions (2022-04-12,
2022-08-30, 2022-12-06). Launched 2021-10-12, and was on a subscription service from day one
(2021-10-06). Announced as always-online before launch (2021-06-20); an offline solo campaign shipped
2021-12-19. The studio was bought on 2021-12-17 and announced no more content on 2023-02-02. Copy
protection (Denuvo) was removed 2024-05-16. (These dates come from the game's own news feed, 116 items,
which the page used as its patch record.) Bots fill empty seats. The pages do not say who hosts or give
the engine.

**How close to Dominion:** Shares four-player co-op PvE, bots, a build system that changes play during a
run (the cards), random rule changes per run, and a paid model with expansions. Differs in camera (first
person), setting (modern zombies, not sci-fi), structure (horde campaign, not extraction or arena runs)
and in being sold as the successor to a famous game.

### The short version (plain words)
The reviews are an argument about a different game. The second most common thing said is "this is the
successor to that older game" (241 times), and the fourth is "the comparison was unfair" (140 times):
535 points, 12% of everything said, are about a game that is not this one. Two of the top three
complaints are about money: wait for a sale (143) and too expensive (106). The shooting and the card
system are well liked (96 and 97 times). The game has no player culture at all - zero catchphrases,
in-jokes or shared rituals in 2,375 reviews across both languages - and its best year was 2025, two
years after updates stopped.

### What players praised, most to least
English counts (LatAm counts in Part B).
1. **"It is the true successor to the older game."** 241 times, the most-said thing after "good game".
   Peaked at launch (73 in 2021), then 25 to 39 a year.
2. **Much better with friends.** 125.
3. **The game is better than its reputation.** 118. It rose over time: 11 (2021), 21, 17, 28 (2024), 26,
   15 (2026). 7.1 per 100 reviews read.
4. **Card builds are deep and varied.** 97. The page calls the card deck the single most-praised design
   feature in the corpus.
5. **Shooting hits with weight.** 96 (5.8 per 100, the highest of the first three games).
6. **Looks great.** 69.
7. **The cards change how you play,** not just numbers. 51.
8. **Patches fixed what mattered.** 50; peaked at 18 in 2022, the year of the expansions.
9. **Keeps pulling you back.** 45.
10. **Levels are well built.** 33.
11. **Every class has a job the others can't do.** 28 (1.7 per 100).
12. **Hard in a good way.** 27.
13. **Fair price.** 27. Grew from 1 (2021) to 8 (2024).
14. **Randomness keeps each run fresh.** 27.
15. **Works solo.** 23. Grew from 2 in 2021 to 6 in 2024.
16. **Plenty of content.** 23.
17. **Deep once you learn it.** 22.
18. **The team has to work together.** 21.
19. **Punchy sound effects.** 18.
20. **Paid add-ons are fair.** 17.
21. **Story worth following.** 17.
22. **Satisfying progression.** 16.
23. **Difficulty levels well graded.** 16.
24. **Random rule changes each run are welcome.** 14.
25. **Runs well.** 13.
26. **Fine with strangers.** 8 (against 30 saying poor with strangers, across both languages; Deep Rock
    Galactic page).
"Good game" with nothing else: 431 (25.9 per 100 reviews).

### What players complained about, most to least
English counts. The pages do not split complaints by thumbs up and down for this game, but the whole
group is 69.2% thumbs up while its points are more negative (2,284) than positive (1,961): many
complaints come from people who still recommend it (0.77 complaints per recommending review, Immortal:
Unchained page).
1. **Buy it on sale only.** 143 (8.6 per 100). Peaked in 2024 (35), three years after launch.
2. **The successor comparison was unfair** - the framing set a bar it could not clear. 140. Never below
   17 in any year: 17, 31, 24, 28, 21, 19.
3. **Too expensive for what it is.** 106. 42 in 2021, falling to 8 to 16 a year.
4. **Updates stopped.** 78. Zero before 2023, then 28, 21, 12, 17. 4.7 per 100.
5. **No mod support.** 50 (3.0 per 100; zero in Deep Rock Galactic and Helldivers 2).
6. **Dead game.** 49.
7. **Nobody to play with, and the game suffers for it.** 45.
8. **Bots useless in combat.** 43 (2.6 per 100). The first complaint about how the game plays is this
   one, at eighth in the combined list.
9. **Runs out of reasons to replay fast.** 41.
10. **The characters are flat or annoying.** 39.
11. **Difficulty levels badly spaced** (usually a cliff at the top). 38.
12. **The successor claim backfired.** 36. Rose late: 4, 3, 6, 5, 11 (2025), 7.
13. **Too little content.** 33.
14. **Could have been more, settled for less.** 31.
15. **Repetitive.** 30 (1.8 per 100 - almost the same rate in every game).
16. **Overcomplicated.** 30.
17. **Thin, forgettable story.** 28.
18. **Shipped broken.** 28.
19. **Worse with strangers.** 27.
20. **Enemies spawn with no chance to react.** 27.
21. **The atmosphere falls flat.** 25.
22. **The cards don't belong in this kind of game.** 24.
23. **A mode is not worth playing.** 23.
24. **Luck decides the run.** 22.
25. **Recycled assets.** 20.
26. **Build choices cannot be undone.** The page gives no count of its own; it is counted with "luck decides the run" and "the cards don't belong", 66 for the three together.
27. **The studio lost my trust.** 20. And zero reviews said the studio had earned their trust (Deep
    Rock Galactic has 63, Helldivers 2 has 14).
28. **Expansion content that should have been in the base game.** 14 in five years.
29. **Works badly alone.** 5 in 2021, 1 after. Solo complaints overall: 1.1 per 100.
30. **Grindy unlocks.** 0.9 per 100.
31. **Needs an internet connection.** 2 ever, despite the always-online announcement.
32. **The owner puts players off.** 0.5 per 100.
33. **Text reads badly** (translation). 0.1 per 100.
"Bad game" with nothing else: 99.

### How it changed over time
English, by year:
| Year | Reviews on Steam | Share | Sample thumbs down | Share of points negative |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 14,391 | 42.6% | 29.9% | 57.7% |
| 2022 | 7,549 | 22.4% | 36.0% | 55.2% |
| 2023 | 3,798 | 11.2% | 33.1% | 53.8% |
| 2024 | 4,432 | 13.1% | 25.2% | 46.7% |
| 2025 | 2,657 | 7.9% | 25.8% | 44.4% |
| 2026 | 939 | 2.8% | 36.1% | 50.8% |
Both languages together:
| Year | Reviews | Thumbs down | Praise per 100 | Complaints per 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 513 | 25.9% | 104.9 | 134.7 |
| 2022 | 395 | 34.9% | 100.8 | 131.9 |
| 2023 | 350 | 30.3% | 115.7 | 135.1 |
| 2024 | 478 | 21.1% | 117.8 | 99.8 |
| 2025 | 409 | 23.0% | 124.7 | 95.8 |
| 2026 | 230 | 32.2% | 122.2 | 117.4 |
- **Launch was huge:** 7,631 reviews in 2021-10 alone, 22.6% of all the game ever got; the first three
  months are 42%.
- **The worst year was 2022** (most thumbs down); 2021 and 2023 tie for most complaints per review.
- **The solo fix is the clearest cause and effect in the study.** Playing alone earned no unlocks: 28
  complaints in 2021, 4 in 2022, 0 in 2023 and 2024, 2 in 2025, 0 in 2026. The news feed shows the studio
  discussing it three days before launch (2021-10-09), promising an offline mode on 2021-11-08, and
  shipping it 2021-12-19 - inside ten weeks of launch.
- **"No more content" (2023-02-02) shows at once:** "updates stopped" goes from 0 to 28 that year and
  never leaves.
- **2024 came back.** Review volume rose (3,798 to 4,432) for a game with no updates, and the negative
  share fell nine points, to 46.7%. That window holds deep sales, the copy protection removed
  (2024-05-16) and press about a player revival; the page cannot say which caused it.
- **2025 was the best year** (most praise, fewest complaints), two years after support ended.
- **2026 turned down again:** thumbs down back to 32.2%, complaints up 23%. The page does not know why.
- **Price talk fades, sale talk does not:** "too expensive" fell by 80% as the game was discounted, but
  "buy on sale only" peaked in 2024. The page: the discount solved the price objection and made it
  permanent - the game is now recommended only as a cheap buy.

### Co-op and online play
- **Much better with friends:** 125 (7.5 per 100).
- **Nobody to play with:** 45. **Dead game:** 49. **Worse with strangers:** 27 (and across both
  languages, 30 poor-with-strangers against 8 fine-with-strangers - the reverse of Deep Rock Galactic,
  41 to 4).
- **Bots useless in combat:** 43.
- **Solo had no progression at launch** (28 complaints in 2021), fixed with an offline mode in ten weeks.
- **Every class needed:** 28. **The team has to work together:** 21.
- **Always-online at launch** drew only 2 complaints ever.
- Nothing on hosting, joining mid-game, friendly fire or crossplay on the pages.

### Combat, movement and feel
- **Shooting hits with weight:** 96. **Punchy sound effects:** 18.
- **The atmosphere falls flat:** 25.
- **Enemies spawn with no chance to react:** 27.
- Nothing on movement, camera or controls on the pages.

### Enemies, bosses and difficulty
- **Difficulty levels badly spaced, a cliff at the top:** 38.
- **Hard in a good way:** 27. **Well graded:** 16.
- **Unfair spawns:** 27.
- **Random rule changes per run welcome:** 14.
- No findings on bosses on the pages.

### Progression, loot, randomness and grind
- **The card deck is the top design praise:** deep 97, changes how you play 51, deep once learned 22,
  satisfying progression 16.
- **But English players argue with it:** luck decides the run 22, the cards don't belong in this game
  24, choices can't be undone (66 for the three of these together), overcomplicated 30. LatAm players have zero of
  these three.
- **Randomness keeps runs fresh:** 27, against luck decides 22 - the same system, both ways.
- **Grindy unlocks:** 0.9 per 100.

### Runs, content and replay value
- **Keeps pulling you back:** 45. **Runs out fast:** 41. **Too little content:** 33. **Plenty:** 23.
- **Repetitive:** 30. **Recycled assets:** 20. **A mode falls flat:** 23.
- **Levels well built:** 33.
- **Story thin:** 28; **story worth following:** 17; **cast flat or annoying:** 39.
- **Median playtime 15 hours** - these are not people who quit after an hour.

### Money and price
- **Wait for a sale:** 143 (8.6 per 100; third in the corpus after Redfall and Aliens: Fireteam Elite).
  **Too expensive:** 106. **Fair price:** 27.
- **Paid expansions:** "should have been in the base game" only 14 in five years; "add-ons are fair" 17.
- **Day one on a subscription service** (2021-10-06): no reaction recorded.
- Money and how the game was sold (marketing plus publishing) are 23.3% of everything said in English;
  publishing alone 9.5%.

### Tech: performance, crashes, bugs
- Engineering is only 3.8% of everything said (168 points); 5.4% of complaints.
- **Shipped broken:** 28. **Runs well:** 13.
- **Always-online:** 2 complaints ever.
- **Copy protection removed in 2024** sits in the recovery window; no direct count.

### The studio, updates and community
- **No player culture at all.** Zero catchphrases, in-jokes or adopted identities in 1,663 English and
  712 LatAm reviews. Deep Rock Galactic has 35.7 per 100, Helldivers 2 29.7. The heading existed from
  round 5; this game was read from round 27, so the zero is real. In its place: 25.1 points per 100 about
  which game this is the successor to (Deep Rock 3.1, Helldivers 0.9).
- **Players praise the marketing, not each other:** 18.5% of this game's praise is about marketing and
  8.3% about the community (Deep Rock 32.9% community, Helldivers 37.3%).
- **Community talk is 9.4%** of all points; the games that did well sit near 28.5%.
- **Updates stopped:** 78, from 2023 on. **Fixed what mattered:** 50, peaking in 2022.
- **Studio lost my trust:** 20. **Studio earned my trust:** 0.
- **No mod support:** 50.
- **Listens and acts:** 0.4 per 100. **Ignores feedback:** 0.8 per 100. Patch fixed what mattered: 3.0
  per 100; patch "made it better": 0.0.

### What players asked for
The pages give no list of requests. What the complaints point at: mod support (50), more reasons to
replay and more content (41, 33), better bots (43), a fairer price or sales (143, 106), more updates
(78), and solo progression (28 in 2021, delivered).

### Only in this game
- **The successor fight:** 535 points (12% of all) about a game that is not this one; "the comparison was
  unfair" 8.4 per 100, zero in Deep Rock Galactic and Helldivers 2.
- **Zero player culture** in 2,375 reviews.
- **Zero "the studio earned my trust".**
- **The cleanest fix-and-the-complaint-stops story** (solo progression, 28 to 4 to 0).
- **No mod support** as a top-five complaint.
- **Its best year two years after support ended.**

---

**Part B - Latin American Spanish (712 of 712 reviews, every one)**

- **81.3% thumbs up** (579 of 712; 133 down). Points split 734 positive to 540 negative - more positive
  than negative, unlike English. 1.87 points per review; 20% of points raise a subject and say nothing
  more; "good game" and nothing else is 209 (16% of all points). Median playtime 10 hours.
- **Top items:** successor accepted 78; buy on sale only 69; much better with friends 67; better than its
  reputation 59; looks great 53; unfair comparison 36; successor claim backfired 35; dead game 30; too
  expensive 29; updates stopped 24; cards change how you play 22; cards deep 22; shooting impactful 21;
  hard in a good way 17; keeps pulling back 15; too little content 15; repetitive 15; no mod support 14.
- **The successor comparison** in its three forms is 149 points, 11% of everything this group wrote.
- **Said much more often than in English** (per 1,000 points): looks great 39.8 vs 15.8 (2.5 times);
  successor claim backfired 26.3 vs 8.2 (3.2 times); buy on sale only 51.9 vs 32.7; better than its
  reputation 44.4 vs 27.0; dead game 22.6 vs 11.2 (2 times); much better with friends 50.4 vs 28.6.
- **Said much less often:** needs a group 1.5 vs 10.3; luck decides 0 vs 5.0; randomness keeps it fresh
  0 vs 6.2; cards don't belong 0 vs 5.5; choices can't be undone 0 vs 4.6; studio lost my trust 0 vs 4.6;
  fair price 0.8 vs 6.2; atmosphere falls flat 0 vs 5.7.
- **Price is a condition, not a complaint.** Only 1 review in 712 said the price was fair. 3 reviewers
  said the local price is set for a richer country.
- **Nobody blames the studio.** Zero "lost my trust" (20 in English), while "better than its reputation"
  runs 1.6 times the English rate.
- **They praise the cards and never argue about them:** "changes how you play" 16.5 per 1,000 against
  English 11.7; zero for the three card complaints.
- **A small language group finds fewer teammates:** dead game at twice the English rate, much better
  with friends 1.8 times.
- **Language:** the Spanish dub is praised 6 times and never criticised. 2 complaints are about not
  sharing a language with teammates in the lobby - a design problem, not a translation one.
- **By year:**
| Year | Reviews | Thumbs down | One-point reviews | Share of points negative |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2021 | 91 | 7.7% | 57.1% | 36.2% |
| 2022 | 73 | 30.1% | 58.9% | 49.6% |
| 2023 | 102 | 23.5% | 51.0% | 47.5% |
| 2024 | 224 | 16.5% | 61.6% | 40.5% |
| 2025 | 161 | 18.6% | 57.1% | 35.9% |
| 2026 | 61 | 21.3% | 42.6% | 37.0% |
- The launch was warm (7.7% thumbs down; 81 of the 91 2021 reviews are from the first two months). 2024
  is the biggest year, 2.5 times the launch year, during the sales and copy-protection removal. Mood kept
  improving after updates stopped; 2025 was the best year by negative share.
- **Both languages together:** 2,375 reviews, 72.8% thumbs up, 2,824 negative points against 2,695
  positive. Top complaints: buy on sale only 212, unfair comparison 176, too expensive 135, updates
  stopped 102, dead game 79, successor backfired 71, no mods 64. Top praise: successor accepted 319,
  friends 192, better than its reputation 177, looks great 122, cards deep 119, shooting 117.
  Marketing and publishing together: 24.5% of everything said.

---

### For Dominion - our reading
1. **Do not sell Dominion as "the next X".** Evidence: 535 English points (12%) about a different game;
   "unfair comparison" 140 and never below 17 a year for five years; "the claim backfired" rose late; in
   LatAm the comparison is 11% of everything said. Dominion could: describe itself by what it is (runs,
   extraction, arenas, its own world), and avoid "spiritual successor" or "from the makers of"
   as the main pitch. Strength: strong.
2. **Price fair at launch, or the "wait for a sale" verdict becomes permanent.** Evidence: wait for a sale
   143 (212 both languages), too expensive 106; "too expensive" fell 80% with discounts but "sale only"
   peaked three years in; LatAm: 1 review in 712 called the price fair, 3 said the local price was set
   for a richer country. Dominion could: launch at a price that matches the content; use Steam's regional
   pricing with care for Latin America. Strength: strong.
3. **Fix the loudest early complaint fast, and say so.** Evidence: solo progression complaints went 28,
   4, 0 after an offline mode shipped inside ten weeks of launch - the clearest fix in the study.
   Dominion could: from day one, make solo and short-handed play earn the same progress, and keep a fast
   patch path for the first months. Strength: strong.
4. **A build system that changes play is the best-loved part, but give players control over luck.**
   Evidence: cards deep 97, change how you play 51; but English players complain luck decides the run
   (22), choices can't be undone (no count of its own), the cards don't belong (24), overcomplicated (30); randomness
   keeps it fresh 27. Dominion could: let run upgrades change behaviour (as the cards do), let players
   shape what they draw (pick from offers, re-roll, a small deck they choose before the run), and allow
   some undo. Strength: strong on the praise, medium on the fixes.
5. **Bots must fight.** Evidence: useless 43 English, 52 both languages (2.6 per 100); the first complaint
   about how the game plays. Dominion could: make bots useful enough to carry a solo run, or scale the
   run to the team size. Strength: medium.
6. **Plan for mods or deep custom options.** Evidence: no mod support 50 (64 both languages), a top-five
   complaint, and zero in the games that lasted. Dominion could: consider mod or custom-rule support
   (custom run modifiers, lobby options) as a way to keep a game alive after updates. Strength: medium.
7. **The end of updates shows up at once and stays.** Evidence: "updates stopped" 0 to 28 the year support
   ended, still 17 in 2026; yet 2025 was the best year. Dominion could: if support ends, leave the game in
   a complete, fair-priced state with all content unlocked by play; a well-priced finished game can still
   gain fans. Strength: medium.
8. **Player culture matters and cannot be bought.** Evidence: zero culture talk in 2,375 reviews against
   28.6 and 24.5 per 100 in the two games that lasted; the study calls this its strongest single line,
   though it cannot prove which way cause runs. Dominion could: give room for rituals - emotes, a salute,
   a squad call-out, actions with no use but fun - and let the community name things. Strength: medium
   (three games at first; the related "how much players talk about each other" line held across five
   games in cross-game section 10).
9. **Difficulty steps must be smooth.** Evidence: badly spaced 38 (usually a cliff at the top), unfair
   spawns 27; hard in a good way 27, well graded 16. Dominion could: test each step with a full and a
   short team and avoid enemies spawning out of sight next to players. Strength: medium.
10. **Random rule changes each run are welcome.** Evidence: 14 (19 both languages). Dominion could: add
    per-run modifiers to arenas and extraction runs. Strength: weak.
11. **Each class needs its own job.** Evidence: every role needed 28 (1.7 per 100). Dominion could: give
    each class a job no other can do. Strength: medium.
12. **Strangers must be good company.** Evidence: worse with strangers 27 (30 both), fine with strangers
    8; nobody to play with 45. Dominion could: build pings, quick chat and clear roles so strangers can
    play well together. Strength: medium.
13. **Characters and story are noticed when weak.** Evidence: cast flat or annoying 39, story thin 28,
    atmosphere flat 25. Dominion could: give the squad and world a voice and a reason to care. Strength:
    medium.
14. **Small language groups need help finding teammates and talking to them.** Evidence: LatAm dead game
    at twice the English rate; 2 complaints about not sharing a language in the lobby; dub praised 6 times.
    Dominion could: offer region or language filters in matchmaking, a ping system that needs no words,
    and Spanish text (and voice if possible). Strength: weak to medium.
15. **The thumb and the text disagree.** Evidence: 69.2% thumbs up but more complaints than praise in the
    text. Dominion could: read the complaints in positive reviews as the to-do list. Strength: strong.
16. **Always-online and subscription launch drew almost no complaint.** Evidence: online-only 2 complaints
    ever; day-one subscription, no recorded reaction. Dominion could: not over-worry about these, but the
    offline solo fix was still needed. Strength: weak.
17. **Paid expansions were not the problem.** Evidence: "should have been in the base game" 14 in five
    years, "add-ons are fair" 17. Dominion could: paid expansions are tolerated if the base game is full.
    Strength: weak.

### Limits
- English: 1,663 of 33,766 (4.9%); the findings page prints no margin .
- LatAm: a full census of 712, so no sampling error, but a count of 3 is still just 3 people; the group
  covers a whole continent and cannot be split by country.
- Four other language groups (23,734 reviews) are unread, including Simplified Chinese at 14,997.
- The pages do not say why 2024-2025 improved (sales, copy-protection removal and press all landed
  together), or why 2026 turned down.
- Nothing about console players or people who never reviewed.
- Most cross-game comparisons here were made against only two other games at the time (Deep Rock
  Galactic and Helldivers 2).
- Several small numbers (rates per 100) come from cross-game tables written before later fixes; the
  page says the Back 4 Blood figures still match within half a point.
- Event dates come from the game's own news feed; reviewer claims beyond those are their own.
